# Second full review: findings log (2026-09-27)

> 中文说明：这是第二轮全项目 code review 的完整问题清单，`verdicts.tsv` 里的 148 行全部收录（143 条已确认、1 条"可能存在"、4 条已排除）。每条都写明是什么问题、在哪个文件、哪个模块、影响哪个功能、怎么复现、证据、建议怎么修，以及要加的回归测试。修复时按下面的 Fix plan 分阶段进行。每修完一条，就在索引表的 Fixed 列打勾，并在该条的 Status 下写上修复它的提交。

## Scope and status

- Reviewed commit: `1e1ce31` on `main`, which is pushed and passed CI. Scope: the whole repository, frontend and backend, on 2026-09-27.
- Method: finders ran per module and per concern: correctness, code that recent commits removed, cross-module traces, contracts, Java and TypeScript idioms, efficiency, altitude, reuse, simplification, conventions, and a final sweep. A separate agent then re-verified each candidate with its own probe, often against a disposable Compose stack.
- `verdicts.tsv` lists 148 findings:
  - Verdict: 143 CONFIRMED, 1 PLAUSIBLE, 4 REFUTED.
  - Severity: 3 high, 15 medium, 130 low.
  - Kind: 85 bugs, 28 cleanup, 19 efficiency, 10 convention, 6 altitude.
  - The review's closing summary counted 137 confirmed findings after merging near-duplicates. This log keeps every index row and cross-references related rows under **Related**.
- The review's candidate registry has 227 second-round candidates:
  - 146 are the logged IDs; X70b and X71b, second verifications of X70 and X71, make up the 148.
  - 79 were verified as duplicates of a logged finding.
  - 2 were merged by the reviewers' note (X159 into X106, X174 into X54).
  - The appendix at the end maps each of these 81 candidates to the entry that covers it and quotes its note. When an entry's **Related** line names an ID without its own entry, look it up there.
- Nothing has been fixed yet.

## Evidence

The full verification records, finder reports and probe programs are archived outside the repository, at `/Users/SoooEZ/.claude/projects/-Users-SoooEZ-Developer-arc/review-evidence/2026-09-27-second/`:

- `verdicts.tsv`: the index this log was built from.
- `verdicts/<file>.md`: the verifier's record for each ID, with its summary, failure scenario and proof. Each entry below names its record file.
- `findings/<finder>.md`: the finders' original reports.
- `probes/<name>/`: probe programs in Java, TypeScript, Python and shell, with their outputs. Reuse them to write regression tests.
  - Records refer to them as `$SP/probes/…`. That path now lives under `…/review-evidence/2026-09-27-second/probes/…`.
  - Built frontend bundles were not archived. Rebuild with `npm --prefix frontend run build` when a probe needs one.

## How to work through this log

1. Before changing code, read these, plus the lessons that each entry cites:
   - `AGENTS.md`
   - `backend/AGENTS.md` or `frontend/AGENTS.md`
   - `docs/maintaining.md`
   - `docs/review-lessons.md`
2. Work phase by phase, following the Fix plan below. Within a phase, fix a shared root cause once: group findings that name each other under **Related**, or that touch the same file.
3. Give every fix a regression test that fails before the fix; each entry's **Regression test** says what to test. Never weaken an existing assertion. When a test asserts the defect, change it and say why in the commit message.
4. After each fix:
   - Record intended behavior changes under "Behavior changes in this revision" in `docs/api.md` or `docs/studio.md`.
   - Add or extend lessons in `docs/review-lessons.md`.
   - Tick the entry's `Fixed` cell in the index, and write "Fixed in `<commit>`" under its Status.
5. Verify as `AGENTS.md` requires, reporting which checks ran:
   - `python3 scripts/check_architecture.py`
   - `mvn -f backend/pom.xml verify` with Java 21: `export JAVA_HOME=/Users/SoooEZ/Library/Java/JavaVirtualMachines/openjdk-21.0.2/Contents/Home`
   - `npm --prefix frontend run format:check`, `npm --prefix frontend run test:unit` and `npm --prefix frontend run build`
   - `scripts/smoke.py`, `scripts/studio_smoke.py` and the affected Playwright specs, against a disposable stack.
6. Commit once per phase or per coherent group: a plain imperative subject and a short body. Do not add `Co-Authored-By` or any other attribution trailer; this is the user's rule. Do not push unless the user asks.

### Disposable stack for API and browser tests

Never run the smoke scripts or Playwright against ports 3080 or 8080: they belong to the user's working `arc` stack and its database, and both write fixtures. Use a separate Compose project:

```sh
COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME=arc-fix ARC_WEB_PORT=3190 ARC_API_PORT=8190 docker compose up -d --build --wait
ARC_API_URL=http://localhost:8190 python3 scripts/smoke.py
ARC_API_URL=http://localhost:8190 python3 scripts/studio_smoke.py
(cd frontend && ARC_WEB_URL=http://localhost:3190 npx playwright test)
COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME=arc-fix docker compose down -v   # removes only the arc-fix project and its volume
```

Rebuild the stack (`up -d --build --wait` again) after changing backend or frontend code.

## Fix plan

Work in this order. Each phase lists its IDs; the index below adds severity, module and location, and each detailed entry names related IDs that share a root cause.

**Phase 1 — Availability of the open API (2).** A request of about 200 bytes exhausts memory, and one of about 5 KB holds a request thread and a CPU core for minutes regardless of `timeoutMs`. [X127](#x127) (high), [X147](#x147) (high)

**Phase 2 — Rule deletion (14).** The deletion feature (commit `b8aa4ff`) assumed that no caller is published while a rule is deleted and that a rule ID never comes back; client caches, the delete dialog and the in-flight delete path all break those assumptions. X11 is PLAUSIBLE: decide whether multi-process deployments must be supported before fixing it. [X41](#x41) (high), [X09](#x09) (medium), [X42](#x42) (medium), [X44](#x44) (medium), [X81](#x81) (medium), [X01](#x01), [X02](#x02), [X11](#x11), [X13](#x13), [X28](#x28), [X45](#x45), [X51](#x51), [X91](#x91), [X107](#x107)

**Phase 3 — Medium-severity bugs: silent wrong results and lost work (10).** [X25](#x25) (medium), [X32](#x32) (medium), [X35](#x35) (medium), [X56](#x56) (medium), [X59](#x59) (medium), [X64](#x64) (medium), [X72](#x72) (medium), [X84](#x84) (medium), [X102](#x102) (medium), [X148](#x148) (medium)

# Findings in detail

## Backend: expressions, text and Excel functions

<a id="x127"></a>

### X127 · high · bug · Zeros of any scale pass the value bounds: `$TO_STRING(0e-2000000000)` allocates ~4 GB and extreme zeros answer 500
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-zero.md` — Fixed in `a0bb3d5` (X27 fixed with X127 in Phase 1)
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / engine.expression (`Expressions.bound`), engine (`ValueText`), persistence (`JsonCodec`) / decimal value bounds (literals, `$TO_NUMBER`, `^`/`*`, inputs, provider values) feeding `$TO_STRING`/`$CONCAT`/`$CONTAINS`, HTTP query parameters, API number output, draft and source storage
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/Expressions.java:129` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/ValueText.java:30`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExpressionRuntime.java:167`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/source/http/HttpSource.java:140`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/ExecutionTrace.java:29`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/persistence/JsonCodec.java:20`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/persistence/JsonCodec.java:30`, `backend/src/main/resources/application.yaml:13`, `frontend/src/domain/numericDefaults.ts:75`)
- **Problem:** `bound` rejects a BigDecimal only when both the number as written and `n.stripTrailingZeros()` exceed the precision/scale limits, and `stripTrailingZeros()` returns `BigDecimal.ZERO` for every zero, so a zero with any scale up to 2,147,483,647 passes every value bound (from the literal `0e-2000000000`, `$TO_NUMBER("0e-…")`, repeated `^` or `*` on `0.0`, JSON inputs or provider values). `ValueText.text` then calls `toPlainString()`, which allocates one character per unit of scale with no deadline or operation check (`ExpressionRuntime.function` checks the deadline only after `Functions.call` returns). Separately, Jackson (`write-bigdecimal-as-plain`) fails to write a plain number with |scale| > 9999 and fails to read back one longer than about 1,000 digits, which contradicts the B17 statement "ARC's value bounds keep numbers inside Jackson's plain-writing limit".
- **Trigger → consequence:** (1) Memory DoS on the open API: `POST /api/preview` of about 200 bytes with Output `$TO_STRING(0e-2000000000)` (or `$CONCAT("",…)`, `$CONTAINS("x",…)`, the chain `((((0.0 ^ 100) ^ 100) ^ 100) ^ 100) ^ 10`, or an HTTP-source query parameter carrying such a zero, HttpSource.java:140) builds a StringBuilder of about 2e9 bytes plus a copy of about 2e9 bytes before the 2,000-character bound rejects the string: OutOfMemoryError on any heap below about 4 GiB, and a few concurrent requests exhaust larger heaps (the review stack's heap is about 18 GiB, 75% of a 24 GiB Docker VM with no container limit); a published rule containing the expression does the same on every `/execute`; `$TO_STRING(0e-2147483647)` throws NegativeArraySizeException, which is not an ArcException, so 500. (2) A zero with |scale| > 9999 as Output or input (e.g. `0e-10000`) gives 500 instead of 422 on preview and execute (trace measuring or the response write), and 500 from the JSONB encode on rule and source save; a zero with |scale| from about 1,001 to 9,999 as a draft default or LOOKUP entry is written, but the read-back exceeds StreamReadConstraints' 1,000-digit limit, giving 500 "Could not decode stored ARC definition".
- **Evidence:** `Expressions.java:129 if (exceedsDecimalLimits(n) && exceedsDecimalLimits(n.stripTrailingZeros()))`; `ValueText.java:30 if (scalar instanceof Number) return Expressions.number(scalar).toPlainString();`. Probe `$SP/probes/v-zero/ZeroProbe.java`: "bounded rejects 1e-101", "bounded ACCEPTS 0e-10000 (scale 10000)", "ACCEPTS 0e-1000000000", "ACCEPTS 0e+2000000000"; "((((0.0 ^ 100) ^ 100) ^ 100) ^ 100) ^ 10 => BigDecimal scale=1000000000"; "$TO_STRING(0e-2147483647) => java.lang.NegativeArraySizeException: -2147483646"; with -Xmx256m "$TO_STRING(0e-400000000) => java.lang.OutOfMemoryError: Java heap space"; with -Xmx512m "$TO_STRING(0e-200000000) => ArcException: String exceeds 2,000 characters (387 ms)" (built first); Jackson with the app's number settings: "scale 1500: written 1508 chars, read back FAILS: StreamConstraintsException … maximum allowed (1000…)" and "scale 10000: write FAILS … illegal scale (10000): needs to be between [-9999, 9999]". Live on 8191 (`$SP/probes/v-zero/pz1.json`…`pz4.out`): preview Output `0e-10000` → 500; input x=0e-10000 → 500 with "IllegalStateException: Could not measure execution trace … illegal scale (10000)"; Output `1e-101` → clean 422; `$TO_STRING(0e-20000000)` → 422 "String exceeds 2,000 characters" at node out after the 20M-character string was built.
- **Suggested fix:** Bound a zero's scale as written, since a zero has no trailing digits to strip: in `Expressions.bound`, for `BigDecimal n` test `n.signum() == 0 ? Math.abs((long) n.scale()) > Limits.MAX_NUMBER_SCALE : exceedsDecimalLimits(n) && exceedsDecimalLimits(n.stripTrailingZeros())`, keeping the by-value rule for nonzero numbers (a stored `1E+100` still passes). Every producer already bounds through `bound` (literals via `ExpressionParser` `number(new BigDecimal(token))`, `$TO_NUMBER`, operator results in `ExpressionRuntime.binary`, `InputTypes.check`, `LookupSourceAdapter`, `HttpSource`), so `ValueText.text`/`toPlainString()` then only sees |scale| ≤ 100 and Jackson's ±9,999 write and 1,000-digit read limits become unreachable; fix line 131 in the same change (X27). Keep `frontend/src/domain/numericDefaults.ts:75` (`if (!digits) return true; // Zero`) in step with the server rule (B6; today the frontend always saves a typed zero as `0`, so only the mirror's branch/comment changes). Update B17 (qualify the Jackson-limit claim) and B6's bound description in docs/review-lessons.md, and docs/architecture.md:165 (a zero's scale counts as written). Behavior change (docs/api.md "Behavior changes"): a zero written with more than 100 decimal places or an exponent beyond ±100 (`0e-101`, `0.00 ^ 100`) now fails with 422 "Number exceeds supported precision or magnitude" like any other out-of-range number; a stored draft or version holding such a zero (possible only since 6fbbd07) fails until fixed.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExpressionsTest.java`: `Expressions.bounded(new BigDecimal("0E-101"))` and `0E+101` throw 422 "Number exceeds supported precision or magnitude" while `0E-100`, `0E+100` and `0.000` pass; compiling `0e-2147483647` fails with 422; `$TO_STRING(((((0.0 ^ 100) ^ 100) ^ 100) ^ 100) ^ 10)`, `$CONCAT("", 0e-2000000000)` and `$CONTAINS("x", $TO_NUMBER("0e-2000000000"))` fail with 422 inside a short `assertTimeoutPreemptively` (e.g. 1 s). `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/api/JsonNumberTest.java` (application Jackson config): preview with Output `0e-10000` and with input `x = 0e-10000` → 422, not 500. `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/source/SourceConfigurationConsistencyTest.java`: LOOKUP entry `0e-1500` → 422 at save. `scripts/smoke.py` (real PostgreSQL): POST /api/rules with NUMBER default `0e-1500` → 422 and nothing stored; the existing `1e100` default check keeps passing.
- **Related:** B17, B6, B24 (a 500 where 422 is documented); X27 (same method, line 131; fix together); X141 and X146 (duplicates: regression against 49bb365, which rejected |scale| > 100; `amount * 0E-10000` publishes cleanly and then every execute answers 500; `/api/studio/build` with `default 0e-10000;` → 500; `$IFERROR` cannot catch the NegativeArraySizeException); X227 (same Jackson 1,000-digit read-back failure for unbounded HTTP-source `entries`); introduced by 6fbbd07 (`git log -S` on the `stripTrailingZeros()` bound).

<a id="x130"></a>

### X130 · low · bug · `$GET`/`$PLUCK` path splitting drops trailing empty segments and splits fractional numbers into nested paths
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-zero.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / engine.expression (`Functions.get`, `Functions.path`) / `$GET` and `$PLUCK` paths (variable property paths share `Functions.get`)
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/Functions.java:157` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/Functions.java:144`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/Functions.java:132`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExpressionParser.java:161`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExpressionParser.java:169`)
- **Problem:** `Functions.get` splits the path with `String.split("\\.")`, which drops trailing empty segments and returns an empty array for "." or "..", so a path ending in a dot reads its prefix and a path of only dots returns the whole value instead of the fallback. `path()` turns a numeric path into text through `ValueText.key` (line 144), so a fractional number becomes dotted text ("1.5") that `get` splits into a nested path. This contradicts docs/studio.md:201 ("Empty or missing object paths return null ($GET/$PLUCK accept a fallback)") and the parser's own rule for variable paths (ExpressionParser.java:161 rejects `customer.name.`).
- **Trigger → consequence:** `$GET(customer, $CONCAT("address.", field), "missing")` with `field` = null uses the path "address." and returns the address object instead of "missing"; `$GET(customer, "name.", "missing")` returns "Ada"; `$GET(customer, ".", "missing")` returns the whole object (the finder reports the same for `".."`), while `$GET(customer, "", "missing")` and `".name"` return "missing"; `$GET(grid, 3 / 2, "missing")` uses the path "1.5" and returns grid[1][5] = 15 (a flat array correctly returns the fallback); `$PLUCK([customer], "tier.", "missing")` returns ["gold"].
- **Evidence:** `Functions.java:157 for (String part : path.split("\\.")) {` and `:144 return ValueText.key(path);`. Probe `$SP/probes/v-zero/PathProbe.java` printed each result listed above; `customer.name.` gives "ArcException: Property path needs a name after every '.'".
- **Suggested fix:** Give paths an explicit segment model, `get(Object value, List<String> segments, Object fallback)`: text paths split with `path.split("\\.", -1)` so every empty segment is kept and misses (returning the fallback unless an object really has a field named ""); a number path is one segment, `ValueText.key(number)`, naming one array index or field and never split ("1.5" is not an index, so arrays return the fallback, while an object field named "1.5" is found). `ExpressionParser.variable` already rejects empty segments, so it can pre-split its path once at parse time and call the same method (also removing the per-access split noted in X216). Behavior change (docs/api.md "Behavior changes", and the path sentence at docs/studio.md:201): `$GET`/`$PLUCK` text paths with an empty segment (`"name."`, `"."`, `"a..b"`) return the fallback, and a fractional number path no longer reads a nested element.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/TextConversionTest.java`, next to `numericPathsNameTheSameEntryHoweverTheNumberWasProduced`: `$GET(customer, "name.", "missing")`, `"."`, `".."`, `".name"` and `"a..b"` → "missing"; `$GET(grid, 3 / 2, "missing")` → "missing"; `$GET($OBJECT("1.5", "x"), 1.5)` → "x"; `$PLUCK([customer], "tier.", "missing")` → ["missing"]; the existing `10`, `1e1` and `10.0` index cases stay unchanged and `ExpressionCompatibilityTest.missingRootAndMissingObjectFieldHaveDistinctSemantics` keeps passing.
- **Related:** B17 (numeric paths through `ValueText.key`); X152 (duplicate); X216 (same function: dotted paths re-split and a regex compiled per access); X145 (compatibility note on `..` paths).

<a id="x131"></a>

### X131 · low · bug · The `$REPT` length pre-check measures `String.valueOf` text instead of the text POI repeats
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-zero.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / engine.expression (`ExcelFunctionAdapter.checkRepeatedLength`) / Excel functions ($REPT)
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExcelFunctionAdapter.java:131` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExcelFunctionAdapter.java:125`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExcelFunctionAdapter.java:140`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExcelWildcards.java:105`)
- **Problem:** `checkRepeatedLength` estimates REPT's result length from `String.valueOf(arg)` — `BigDecimal.toString()` for numbers and "null" for a blank — instead of the text POI actually repeats (POI receives `new NumberEval(n.doubleValue())` and repeats `OperandResolver.coerceValueToString` of it). Equal numbers therefore pass or fail depending on their scale, and long quotients and null are over-counted, so valid REPT calls fail with 422. This breaks B17 ("Never turn a number into text with BigDecimal.toString()") and backend/AGENTS.md.
- **Trigger → consequence:** `$REPT(100, 600)` returns 1,800 characters, but `$REPT(1E+2, 600)`, `$REPT($ROUND(149, -2), 600)` and `$REPT($TO_NUMBER("1e2"), 600)` fail with 422 "REPT result exceeds string limit" because the check measures "1E+2" (`$REPT(1E+2, 400)` shows that POI writes "100"); `$REPT(1 / 3, 100)` fails with 422 although POI repeats the 17-character "0.333333333333333" (1,700 characters in total), while `$REPT(1 / 3, 50)` returns 850 characters; with blank = null, `$REPT(blank, 600)` fails with 422 although POI returns "" (`$REPT(blank, 400)` returns length 0).
- **Evidence:** `ExcelFunctionAdapter.java:131 double length = String.valueOf(args.getFirst()).length() * count.doubleValue();`. Probe `$SP/probes/v-zero/ReptProbe.java` printed exactly the outcomes above.
- **Suggested fix:** Measure what POI will build: in `checkRepeatedLength`, compute `String text = OperandResolver.coerceValueToString(value(args.getFirst()))` (the conversion POI's REPT uses and `ExcelWildcards.databaseTexts` already uses: "" for a blank, "100" for 1E+2) and multiply by the count truncated as POI does (`(int)` of the double), keeping the existing count cap of `Limits.MAX_STRING_CHARACTERS` so an empty text cannot drive an unbounded loop. This also closes the under-count noted in X170 (`$REPT(1e10, 399)` measures "1E+10" and passes, and POI builds 4,389 characters before the generic bound rejects them). Optionally, per X151, coerce the count (and the FIXED/DOLLAR/TRUNC/COMBIN bound arguments) through POI (`OperandResolver.coerceValueToDouble`) instead of `Expressions.number`, so numeric text such as `$REPT("ab", "3")` behaves as in other POI functions. Behavior change (docs/api.md): `$REPT` accepts every call whose actual result fits 2,000 characters however a number is written, and `$REPT(1e10, 399)` now fails with "REPT result exceeds string limit" instead of the generic string-limit message.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/TextConversionTest.java` (B17 number-text coverage) or `ExcelArgumentsTest.java`: `$LEN($REPT(1E+2, 600))`, `$LEN($REPT($ROUND(149, -2), 600))` and `$LEN($REPT($TO_NUMBER("1e2"), 600))` = 1800; `$LEN($REPT(1 / 3, 100))` = 1700; `$LEN($REPT(blank, 600))` = 0 with blank null; `$REPT(1e10, 399)` fails with "REPT result exceeds string limit" (not "String exceeds 2,000 characters"); `StudioTest`'s `$REPT("x", 1000000000)` still fails with "string limit".
- **Related:** B17; X151 (duplicate; adds numeric-text arguments rejected by the REPT/FIXED/DOLLAR/TRUNC/COMBIN pre-checks: `$REPT("ab", "3")`, `$FIXED(1234.5, "1")`, `$COMBIN("5", 2)`); X156 (duplicate; `$REPT(0.50, 600)` fails while `0.5` works); X170 (duplicate; the under-count bypass `$REPT(1e10, 399)`).

<a id="x132"></a>

### X132 · low · bug · Error responses go through content negotiation, so an Accept header without JSON turns every error into an empty 500
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-zero.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / api (`Errors`) / HTTP error responses of every endpoint
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/api/Errors.java:21` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/api/Errors.java:43`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/api/Errors.java:50`)
- **Problem:** The `@ExceptionHandler` methods return a `ResponseEntity` with a `Map` body and no preset content type, so Spring negotiates the error body's media type against the Accept header. When Accept excludes both JSON and `*/*`, writing the body throws `HttpMediaTypeNotAcceptableException` inside the handler; the original exception then escapes to Tomcat, and every 404, 409, 422 and 504 becomes a 500 with a WARN and an ERROR stack trace in the log.
- **Trigger → consequence:** GET /api/rules/<missing> with Accept `text/plain` or `application/xml` returns 500 with an empty body, and with `text/html` 500 with the whitelabel HTML page; POST /api/preview `{}` with Accept `text/plain` returns 500 instead of 422; a success path with the same Accept returns 406. Browser, curl and fetch defaults include `*/*` and are unaffected, which is why the severity is low.
- **Evidence:** `Errors.java:21-31 return ResponseEntity.status(e.status()).body(Map.of(...))`; `:50-53` does the same without a content type. Live on 8191 (`$SP/probes/v-zero/acc.out`): `*/*` → 404 application/json; `text/plain` → 500, 0 bytes; `application/xml` → 500, 0 bytes; `text/html` → 500 text/html; the browser Accept string → 404; success path with text/plain → 406; preview `{}` → 500 with text/plain, 422 with JSON. Log: "WARN … Failure in @ExceptionHandler dev.arc.api.Errors#arc(ArcException)", then "ERROR … Servlet.service() … threw exception [Request processing failed: dev.arc.error.ArcException: Rule not found: rv-v-zero-nonexistent] with root cause".
- **Suggested fix:** Build every error response in `Errors` (`arc`, `malformed`, `unexpected`) through one private helper that sets `.contentType(MediaType.APPLICATION_JSON)` on the `ResponseEntity`; Spring's message-converter processor writes with a preset concrete Content-Type instead of negotiating, so the JSON error body is always written with its real status. Let the same helper write a fixed field order (`status`, `message`, `issues`, `locations`) through a `LinkedHashMap` or a small record, which also fixes X134. Behavior change (docs/api.md): error responses are JSON with their documented status whatever the Accept header; the 406 for an unacceptable success response also carries the JSON error body.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/api/ResourceErrorsTest.java` (standalone MockMvc with the real `Errors` advice): GET /api/rules/missing with Accept `text/plain`, `application/xml` and `text/html` → 404, Content-Type application/json, `$.message` "Rule not found: missing"; an ArcException 422 path with Accept `text/plain` → 422 JSON. `scripts/smoke.py`: GET of an unknown rule with `Accept: text/plain` → 404 JSON.
- **Related:** X134 (same bodies: `Map.of` field order varies per JVM, B9); B24 (`Errors` stays resource-agnostic).

<a id="x133"></a>

### X133 · low · bug · Rule names are validated before trimming and stored after it; the source-name copy of the policy never trims
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-zero.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / rule (`RuleService.metadata`), source (`SourceValidator`, `SourceService`), persistence (`StoredText`) / rule and data-source names
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleService.java:79` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleService.java:87`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleService.java:101`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleService.java:184`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/source/SourceValidator.java:26`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/source/SourceService.java:50`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/source/SourceService.java:56`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/persistence/JdbcRuleRepository.java:198`)
- **Problem:** `RuleService.metadata` validates the raw name with `isBlank()` (false for control characters) and counts padding toward the 160-character limit, then `create`/`update` store `name.trim()`, which strips every character at or below U+0020, including U+0000 and U+0001; a name made only of control characters is therefore stored empty, and the NUL check (`StoredText.requireStorable`) runs on the already trimmed text. `SourceValidator.validate` is a second copy of the name policy that never trims and words its message differently ("1–160" versus "1 to 160"), and `SourceService` stores `request.name()` unchanged.
- **Trigger → consequence:** POST /api/rules `{"id":…,"name":"\u0001\u0000","kind":"RULE"}` returns 201 and the stored name is "" (length 0), bypassing "Name must contain 1 to 160 characters" and the NUL check; a 158-character name with two spaces on each side is rejected although its stored form would fit; a rule named "  padded  " is stored as "padded", but a source with the same name keeps its spaces (the sources UI sends names untrimmed). Related: StoredText rejects only U+0000, so an unpaired surrogate is silently stored as "?" (X29).
- **Evidence:** `RuleService.java:87 request.name().trim(),` and `:184 if (name == null || name.isBlank() || name.length() > Limits.MAX_NAME_CHARACTERS)`; `SourceValidator.java:26` has the same check without a trim, with the message "Source name must contain 1–" + …. Live on 8191 (`$SP/probes/v-zero/ctl.out`, `pad.out`, `sur.out`; probe rules deleted afterwards): the "\u0001\u0000" rule → 201 and GET returned name '' (len 0); "  padded  " stored as 'padded'; the source rv-d1-java-src is listed as '  padded  '; "a\ud800b" stored as 'a?b'.
- **Suggested fix:** One display-name policy in the engine root beside `Identifiers`/`Limits` (e.g. `engine.DisplayNames.normalize(String resource, String name)`) that returns the name to store — reject null, trim, require 1 to `Limits.MAX_NAME_CHARACTERS` characters of the trimmed text, one message "<Rule|Source> name must contain 1 to 160 characters" — used by `RuleService.create/update` and by `SourceService.create/update` in place of `SourceValidator`'s copy. Decision needed: also reject control characters (`Character.isISOControl`) anywhere in a name before trimming — recommended, because names are single-line display text and it stops `trim()` from silently dropping a leading or trailing U+0000 that StoredText would otherwise reject; existing names with control characters would then need a rename at their next save. The sources editor already adopts the saved source when nothing changed after submission (`frontend/src/features/sources/sourceDocument.ts` save acknowledgement), as the rule editor adopts the trimmed rule name (F1). Behavior change (docs/api.md): source names are trimmed like rule names, a name that is empty after trimming returns 422, and the name message wording is shared.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleServiceTest.java`: create/update with "\u0001\u0000" → 422 and nothing stored; "  padded  " stored as "padded"; 158 characters plus two spaces on each side accepted and stored as 158 characters. `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/source/SourceServiceTest.java` and `SourceValidatorTest.java`: the same cases for sources with the shared message. `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/api/ResourceErrorsTest.java`: the control-character name → 422 with no JDBC write. `frontend/tests/unit/source-document.spec.ts`: a trimmed name echo is adopted after a save with no later edits.
- **Related:** X158 and X198 (duplicates: the name policy is duplicated for rules and sources and has drifted); X29 (unpaired surrogates stored as "?"); B5 (one policy read by producer and consumer); F1 (the editor adopts the server-trimmed name).

<a id="x10"></a>

### X10 · low · bug · A NUL character in a paged search or a Reference `ruleId` reaches PostgreSQL and answers 500
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-text.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / model (`PageRequest`), api (`PageParameters`), persistence (catalog SQL), engine.validation (`DefinitionShape`, `NodeValidation`) / library, source and version-history search; Reference nodes in validate, diagnostics, preview and Code studio build
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/model/PageRequest.java:20` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/persistence/JdbcRuleRepository.java:18`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/persistence/JdbcRuleRepository.java:43`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/persistence/JdbcSourceRepository.java:15`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/NodeValidation.java:108`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/DefinitionShape.java:160`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/api/PageParameters.java:11`)
- **Problem:** `PageRequest` only trims the search (`trim()` removes a leading or trailing NUL, not an interior one), and the text is bound into `strpos(lower(…), lower(?))`/`strpos(version::text, ?)`; PostgreSQL rejects U+0000 in a text parameter and the API returns 500. Separately, neither `DefinitionShape` nor `NodeValidation` checks a Reference node's `ruleId` with `Identifiers.isResourceId`, although source bindings (`InputValidation:49`) and Formula pins (`ExpressionParser:190`) are checked, so `resolver.resolve(node.ruleId(), …)` sends the NUL to SQL. Docs promise 422 for NUL text (api.md:115, :159; architecture.md:143); this is B24's class (a storage outcome reaching the client as 500) on a read path, and nothing is written, so there is no data loss.
- **Trigger → consequence:** `GET /api/rule-summaries?search=a%00b`, `/api/source-summaries?search=a%00b` or `/api/rules/apply-discount/version-summaries?search=1%002` → 500 "An unexpected server error occurred" with an ERROR log (a search of only `%00` is trimmed away and returns 200). A graph whose REFERENCE node has `"ruleId":"a\u0000b","version":1` → 500 on POST /api/validate, /api/diagnostics and /api/preview (a well-formed unknown ID gives 404, or a 200 diagnostics list). Code studio can produce this graph: `/api/studio/build` accepts `use "a\u0000b" version 1;` with 200, and the editor's diagnostics request then 500s.
- **Evidence:** `PageRequest.java:20 search = search == null ? "" : search.trim();`; `NodeValidation.java:108 Definition child = resolver.resolve(node.ruleId(), node.version());`; `DefinitionShape.java:160-163` checks only `node.version() == null || node.ruleId() != null`. Live on 8191: all three search URLs gave `{"status":500,"message":"An unexpected server error occurred","issues":[]}`, and the log shows `DataIntegrityViolationException … SQL [SELECT count(*) FROM rule_versions WHERE rule_id = ? AND (? = '' OR strpos(version::text, ?) > 0)]; ERROR: invalid byte sequence for encoding "UTF8": 0x00`; with ruleId 'a\x00b', validate, diagnostics and preview each returned (500, '{"status":500,…}'), and with 'no-such-rule' validate 404, diagnostics 200 [message], preview 404 (probe `$SP/probes/v-text/ref_nul.py`).
- **Suggested fix:** (1) In the `PageRequest` compact constructor, reject a search that contains U+0000 anywhere (checked before trimming) with `IllegalArgumentException("Text cannot contain the NUL character (U+0000)")`; `PageParameters.page` already turns it into a 422 for every paged endpoint. Share the message with `persistence.StoredText.NUL_MESSAGE` by moving that constant to `model` (persistence may import model, not the reverse). (2) In `DefinitionShape`, beside the version/ruleId check, add `require(node.ruleId() == null || Identifiers.isResourceId(node.ruleId()), declaration, node.label() + ": choose a valid rule ID")`, as `InputValidation.checkSource` does for source pins; no valid pin is rejected, because rule creation has required `[a-z][a-z0-9-]{0,79}` since b50ccb0, and draft save, /validate, /diagnostics, /preview and `/studio/build` (at the `use` statement) then answer 422 at the Reference node. Behavior change (docs/api.md): these requests return 422 instead of 500, and a search of only `%00` also returns 422 instead of every item.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/model/PageRequestTest.java`: a search with an interior, leading or lone NUL throws IllegalArgumentException with the NUL message. `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/api/CatalogControllerTest.java`: the three search URLs with `%00` → 422 and the repository is never called. `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/NodeContractsTest.java` (or `ValidatorTest`): a Reference with ruleId "a\u0000b" (and "Bad ID") → 422 shape violation at that node. `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/script/ScriptLocationTest.java`: `use "a\u0000b" version 1;` → diagnostic at the `use` statement. `scripts/smoke.py` (real PostgreSQL): `rule-summaries?search=a%00b` → 422.
- **Related:** B24, B5 (one resource-ID policy for rules, sources, pins and Formula calls); X30 and X78 (duplicates; X78 adds the Reference `ruleId` path); X29 (StoredText guards writes only).

<a id="x29"></a>

### X29 · low · bug · Unpaired UTF-16 surrogates are silently stored as "?" instead of being rejected
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-text.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / persistence (`StoredText`, `JsonCodec`, JDBC repositories) / stored names, descriptions, drafts, published versions and source definitions (the editor's save acknowledgement then adopts the altered echo)
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/persistence/StoredText.java:18` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/persistence/StoredText.java:27`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/persistence/JsonCodec.java:20`, `frontend/src/features/editor/documentState.ts:111`, `docs/openapi.yaml:11`)
- **Problem:** StoredText rejects only U+0000. A lone surrogate arrives as the valid JSON escape `\ud800`, Jackson decodes it into the Java string, `JsonCodec.encode` (`writeValueAsString`) keeps it as a raw char, and pgjdbc's UTF-8 encoding silently replaces it with '?'. Names, descriptions, drafts and source definitions are stored altered with 201/200 instead of being rejected, and nothing documents the substitution (openapi.yaml:10-11 names only NUL).
- **Trigger → consequence:** POST /api/rules with name "a\ud800b" and description "d\udc00e" → 201 with name "a?b" and description "d?e". A PUT whose input defaultValue is "k\ud83d" (half an emoji) → 200, and the response and every reload show "k?". Browser path: `stringifyJson` uses JSON.stringify, which escapes lone surrogates as `\ud800`; `acknowledgeSave` in documentState.ts then sees `sameDefinition(localDraft, response.draft)` as false and adopts `withNodePositions(response.draft)`, so the altered draft replaces the local one and a later publish freezes it into an immutable version. The same substitution applies to lookup entries, labels and raw expression text, and two keys differing only in a lone surrogate collapse to one "k?" key; the trigger is malformed Unicode, mainly from API clients that truncate strings mid-pair.
- **Evidence:** `StoredText.java:18 if (value != null && value.indexOf('\0') >= 0) throw …` and `:27 if (json.startsWith("u0000", escape + 1))` are the only checks; `JsonCodec.java:20 encoded = json.writeValueAsString(value);`; create and update return a read-back (`return get(id);`). Live on 8191 (probe `$SP/probes/v-text/sur.py`; fixture rv-v-text-sur-1790560738, since deleted): create 201 `"name":"a?b","description":"d?e"`; update 200 with inputs `[{'name': 's', …, 'defaultValue': 'k?'}]`; the reloaded raw JSON contains `k?`.
- **Suggested fix:** Reject malformed UTF-16 before any write: `StoredText.requireStorable` and `requireStorableJson` scan for a surrogate that is not part of a high+low pair (`Character.isHighSurrogate(c) && Character.isLowSurrogate(next)` skips the pair; any other `Character.isSurrogate(c)` fails) and throw 422, e.g. "Text cannot contain an unpaired UTF-16 surrogate", keeping `NUL_MESSAGE` unchanged. Scanning the encoded JSON works because Jackson writes surrogates as raw chars, not escapes; valid pairs (emoji) stay storable (B1). No frontend change is needed: the save fails with 422 and the local draft stays. Behavior change (docs/api.md "Behavior changes", the 422 row at api.md:115, openapi.yaml:10-11 and architecture.md:143): such writes return 422 and nothing is written.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/persistence/StoredTextTest.java`: "a\ud800b", "d\udc00e", a trailing "\ud83d" and a map key with a lone surrogate are rejected by `requireStorable`/`json.encode` with 422, while a valid pair ("😀") and the escape-like text "\\ud800" stay storable. `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/api/ResourceErrorsTest.java`: POST /api/rules with name "a\ud800b" → 422 and no JDBC write. `scripts/smoke.py` (real PostgreSQL): create with a lone surrogate → 422 and nothing stored; an emoji name round-trips unchanged.
- **Related:** B24 (StoredText is the storage policy); B1 ("reject malformed Unicode"; keep surrogate pairs); F1 (the editor adopts a differing save echo); X82 (duplicate; frontend adoption and publish freezing); X133 (the "a\ud800b" name example).

<a id="x22"></a>

### X22 · low · bug · The input-type error lowercases with the JVM default locale ("must be strıng" under tr/az)
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-text.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / engine (`InputTypes`) / type errors for inputs, source parameters and defaults (native dev and Maven runs)
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/InputTypes.java:26` (+ `backend/Dockerfile:14`, `Procfile.dev:1`)
- **Problem:** `InputTypes.check` builds "Input 'x' must be <type>" with `type.toLowerCase()`, which uses the JVM default locale, so under a tr or az default locale "STRING" becomes "strıng" (dotless ı). It is the only locale-less `toLowerCase`/`toUpperCase` in backend/src/main; every other case conversion uses Locale.ROOT or Locale.US, and only the Docker entrypoint pins en-US.
- **Trigger → consequence:** Native dev (`bin/dev` → Procfile.dev `mvn … spring-boot:run`, with no -Duser.language, JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS or .mvn/jvm.config) or a Maven run on a Turkish or Azerbaijani host: a non-string value for a STRING input, source parameter or default gives 422 "Input 'region' must be strıng". Callers are Parameters:89/114, SourceExecutionService:109, SourceValidator:41 and InputValidation:39; the other type names contain no 'I', so only STRING is affected, and existing tests assert only "must be number".
- **Evidence:** `throw ArcException.invalid("Input '" + name + "' must be " + type.toLowerCase());` is the only locale-less case conversion in main (grep). backend/Dockerfile:14 pins `-Duser.language=en -Duser.country=US` ("so value formatting never depends on the host's settings"). Java probe `$SP/probes/v-text/TextProbe.java`: `en_US -> Input 'region' must be string`, `tr_TR -> Input 'region' must be strıng`, `az_AZ -> Input 'region' must be strıng`. LimitsTest already asserts locale-independent messages.
- **Suggested fix:** `type.toLowerCase(Locale.ROOT)`. To keep the class from returning, add a rule to `scripts/check_architecture.py` that rejects `toLowerCase()`/`toUpperCase()` without a `Locale` argument in `backend/src/main` (the script already enforces similar source rules). No Behavior changes entry: the output on en-US hosts and in the container is unchanged.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/LimitsTest.java` (locale-independent messages) or a new `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/InputTypesTest.java`: with `Locale.setDefault(Locale.forLanguageTag("tr-TR"))` and `az-AZ` (restored in `finally`), `InputTypes.check("region", "STRING", 1)` fails with exactly "Input 'region' must be string"; the same loop covers every name in `InputTypes.NAMES`.
- **Related:** B15 (JVM defaults are shared mutable state; the container pins en-US); B5 (locale-independent messages, `LimitsTest`); X105 (type words in messages).

<a id="x27"></a>

### X27 · low · bug · `Expressions.bound` skips the precision limit for BigInteger values (lookup entries, HTTP responses, nested inputs)
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-text.md` — Fixed in `a0bb3d5` (X27 fixed with X127 in Phase 1)
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / engine.expression (`Expressions.bound`), source.lookup (`LookupSourceAdapter`), source.http (`HttpSource`) / value bounds for large integers in LOOKUP entries, HTTP provider responses and ARRAY/OBJECT inputs
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/Expressions.java:131` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/Expressions.java:125`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/Expressions.java:105`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/source/lookup/LookupSourceAdapter.java:38`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/source/http/HttpSource.java:201`, `backend/src/main/resources/application.yaml:10`)
- **Problem:** `bound` applies the precision and scale limits only inside `if (value instanceof BigDecimal n)`; any other `Number` is checked only with `Double.isFinite(n.doubleValue())`. Jackson parses a JSON integer that does not fit a long as `BigInteger` (only `use-big-decimal-for-floats` is set), so an integer of about 101–308 digits passes wherever values are bounded without conversion. `Expressions.number` converts via `new BigDecimal(value.toString())` and then bounds, so the NUMBER path rejects the same value later: Create/Test and execution disagree (the B22 class).
- **Trigger → consequence:** LOOKUP source rv-a4-source-db-bigint v1 has entry "a" = a 150-digit integer (stored, so the save was accepted). POST /sources/…/test {"key":"a"} → 200 with the value; POST /validate of a graph whose NUMBER input is sourced from it → 200 {"valid":true}; POST /preview → 422 "v: Number exceeds supported precision or magnitude", or the DEFAULT fallback when one is configured. The same digits written with ".0" are rejected by `bounded`; a directly supplied ARRAY input `[<150 digits>]` → 200 echoed back while `[<150 digits>.0]` → 422. HTTP responses (HttpSource.java:201) and nested ARRAY or OBJECT inputs bypass the limit the same way. The message is a clear 422 rather than a 500, and the trigger needs integers of more than 100 digits.
- **Evidence:** `Expressions.java:131 } else if (value instanceof Number n && !Double.isFinite(n.doubleValue())) {`. Java probe `$SP/probes/v-text/TextProbe.java`: `bounded(Map{a:BigInteger150}) -> no error`; `bounded(Map{a:BigDecimal150.0}) -> Number exceeds supported precision or magnitude`; `InputTypes NUMBER(BigInteger150) -> Number exceeds…`; `InputTypes ARRAY([BigInteger150]) -> no error`; a 308-digit integer passes and 310 digits → "Number must be finite". Live on 8191 (`$SP/probes/v-text/bigint.py`): source test 200, validate 200, preview 422 "v: Number exceeds supported precision or magnitude"; ARRAY [bigint] 200, [bigint.0] 422.
- **Suggested fix:** One numeric bound for every `Number`: in `bound`, convert a non-BigDecimal number to its decimal value and apply the same by-value rule — `BigInteger` via `new BigDecimal(bigInteger)`, `Double`/`Float` via `BigDecimal.valueOf(d)` after the finiteness check, `Integer`/`Long`/`Short`/`Byte` need no check — through one helper shared with `Expressions.number` (which today does `new BigDecimal(value.toString())`). The by-value rule (as written or without trailing zeros) keeps PostgreSQL's 101-digit spelling of `1e100`, which reads back as `BigInteger`, valid (smoke test). Apply together with X127, which changes the BigDecimal branch of the same method. Behavior change (docs/api.md): integers with more than 100 significant digits in lookup entries, HTTP responses and ARRAY/OBJECT values are rejected with 422 at save, Test or read, as decimals already are; a stored LOOKUP version holding one now fails its Test like its executions.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExpressionsTest.java`: `bounded(Map.of("a", new BigInteger("9".repeat(150))))` and `InputTypes.check("v", "ARRAY", List.of(bigint150))` → 422 "Number exceeds supported precision or magnitude"; `BigInteger.TEN.pow(100)` passes. `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/source/SourceConfigurationConsistencyTest.java` (B22 "Create, Test and execution agree"): a LOOKUP with a 150-digit integer entry is rejected at save. `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/source/http/HttpSourceTest.java`: a response holding a 150-digit integer → 422. `scripts/smoke.py`: the existing `1e100` default still saves and previews.
- **Related:** B22, B6 (the server bound the frontend mirrors); X127 (same method; fix together); X31 and X227 (source configurations with unbounded or unused fields).

<a id="x105"></a>

### X105 · low · bug · Type errors name JDK implementation classes (UnmodifiableMap, ListN, …) instead of ARC types
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-text.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / engine.expression (`Expressions.bool`, `Expressions.number`) / type error messages (Condition nodes, arithmetic; shown in the Test panel and API errors)
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/Expressions.java:191` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/Expressions.java:102`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/Expressions.java:113`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/GraphExecution.java:162`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/model/Frozen.java:18`)
- **Problem:** `type(Object o)` returns `o.getClass().getSimpleName()`, so "Expected a boolean, got …" and "Expected a number, got …" name the Java implementation class instead of the ARC type. The name for the same ARC type changes with where the value came from: `Frozen` wraps defaults in `Collections.unmodifiableMap/unmodifiableList`, Jackson produces `LinkedHashMap`/`ArrayList`, and literals produce `List.of` (`ListN`, `List12`).
- **Trigger → consequence:** A Condition whose expression is an OBJECT input `o`: when `o` is omitted, the frozen default fails with "Expected a boolean, got UnmodifiableMap"; when supplied, "got LinkedHashMap". An ARRAY default gives "UnmodifiableRandomAccessList", a supplied ARRAY "ArrayList", the literal `[1, 2]` "ListN", `List.of(1,2)` "List12", a number "BigDecimal", and `number(Map.of())` "MapN". The Test panel and API show Java internals instead of object/array/number; the frontend does not translate these messages (no match in frontend/src), and no test or doc fixes the wording.
- **Evidence:** `Expressions.java:190-191 private static String type(Object o) { return o == null ? "null" : o.getClass().getSimpleName(); }`, used at :102 and :113; `GraphExecution.java:162 boolean value = Expressions.bool(eval(node.expression(), scope));`. Live on 8191, POST /preview (`$SP/probes/v-text/cond.py`; unit probe `$SP/probes/v-text/TextProbe.java`): OBJECT default → 422 'Expected a boolean, got UnmodifiableMap'; OBJECT supplied → 'got LinkedHashMap'; ARRAY default → 'got UnmodifiableRandomAccessList'; ARRAY supplied → 'got ArrayList'; NUMBER → 'got BigDecimal'; literal [1, 2] → 'got ListN'.
- **Suggested fix:** Replace `type(Object)` with an ARC type name from the vocabulary of `InputTypes.NAMES`, lowercased: `null`, `number` (any `Number`), `string`, `boolean`, `array` (any `List`), `object` (any `Map`). Keep the helper in `engine.expression`, which may not import `InputTypes` (docs/maintaining.md:199), with a comment pointing at `InputTypes.NAMES`. Behavior change (docs/api.md): type errors read "Expected a boolean, got object" whatever the value's origin.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExpressionsTest.java`: `Expressions.bool` of a `LinkedHashMap`, `Collections.unmodifiableMap(...)` and `Map.of()` → "Expected a boolean, got object"; of `List.of(1, 2)`, an `ArrayList` and `Collections.unmodifiableList(...)` → "…got array"; of `BigDecimal`/`Integer` → "…got number"; of "x" → "…got string"; `Expressions.number(Map.of())` → "Expected a number, got object". An engine test (e.g. `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/EngineTest.java`): a Condition on an OBJECT input gives the same message for its default and for a supplied value.
- **Related:** X153 (duplicate; also notes that `Expressions.compile` trims the source (Expressions.java:89) before `ExpressionParser` reports "near character N", so positions are off by the leading whitespace); X22 (type words in input errors).

<a id="x147"></a>

### X147 · high · bug · POI's quadratic number-parsing regex runs unmetered over range text in COUNTIF/SUMIF/CORREL/FORECAST, past every deadline
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-excel.md` — Fixed in `a0bb3d5` (X27 fixed with X127 in Phase 1)
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / engine.expression (`ExcelFunctionAdapter`, `ExcelWildcards`) / Excel functions (COUNTIF and SUMIF with numeric criteria, CORREL, FORECAST) on the open preview and execute API
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExcelFunctionAdapter.java:33` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExcelFunctionAdapter.java:34`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExcelWildcards.java:80`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExcelWildcards.java:115`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExpressionRuntime.java:168`)
- **Problem:** ARC limits only POI's wildcard regexes (B14), but POI's number-parsing regex (`OperandResolver.fpPattern`, run with `matches()`) backtracks quadratically on long digit text. COUNTIF and SUMIF with a numeric criterion or numeric-looking text ("5", "=5"), CORREL and FORECAST run that regex on every text cell in one POI call that cannot be interrupted. Nothing bounds this work — `ExcelWildcards.criteriaPattern` returns `List.of()` for a non-string criterion and a numeric text criterion has no wildcard pattern — and the deadline is checked only after POI returns.
- **Trigger → consequence:** POST /api/preview with the Output `$MAP([$CONCAT($REPT("1", 1999), "x")], s, $COUNTIF($MAP(items, i, s), 5))` and `timeoutMs` 100: with 20 items (a 512-byte body) the response is 504 after 1.72 s; with 1,000 items (about 5 KB) it takes about 75 s; a 98×100 grid of the same text fits the value and operation budgets and would take about 9,800 × 73 ms ≈ 12 minutes per request. Each such request holds a request thread and a CPU core whatever `timeoutMs` says; the API has no authentication, and this is the same failure class as K01/B14, which was rated high.
- **Evidence:** `ExcelFunctionAdapter.java:33-34 ExcelWildcards.checkMatchingWork(name, args); return converted(inExcelLocale(() -> calculate(name, metadata, values)), name);`; `ExcelWildcards.java:80 if (!(criterion instanceof String text)) return List.of();` and `:115-116 getWildCardPattern(source); if (pattern == null) continue;`; `ExpressionRuntime.java:168 // POI cannot be interrupted, so a slow Excel calculation is caught as soon as it returns.` javap of POI 5.5.1: parseDouble = `fpPattern.matcher(s).matches()` with `[\x00-\x20]*[+-]?(((\p{Digit}+)(\.)?((\p{Digit}+)?)...`. Probe `$SP/probes/v-excel/ParseCost.java`: parseDouble takes 4.7 ms at 500 characters, 18.2 ms at 1,000 and 72.8 ms at 2,000, while 1,000 parses of 2,000 letters take 5 ms in total; over 10 cells of 2,000 digits: `$COUNTIF(cells, 5)` 883 ms, `"=5"` 726 ms, `"5"` 722 ms, `$SUMIF(cells, 5, nums)` 725 ms, `$CORREL` 730 ms, `$FORECAST` 720 ms, `$MATCH(5, cells, 0)` 1 ms, `">5"` 0 ms; with a 100 ms deadline `$COUNTIF(cells, 5)` ran 734 ms and then threw "Rule execution deadline exceeded". Live on 8191: HTTP 504 in 1.720637 s (`$SP/probes/v-excel/countif20.json`, `countif20-resp.txt`). `$SP/probes/v-excel/GridFits.java`: `$COUNTIF($MAP(r, i, $MAP(c, j, s)), ">5")` over 98×100 runs within budget in 198 ms, so the grid reaches COUNTIF.
- **Suggested fix:** Treat POI's number parsing like its wildcard matching (B14: "never hand user text to a third-party regex without a bound that ARC enforces first"): extend `ExcelWildcards.checkMatchingWork` (renamed, e.g., `ExcelMatchingWork`) with a second replay that runs POI's number pattern over every text cell through `MeteredText`, charged to a budget, and rejects the call with 422 before POI runs (e.g. "COUNTIF: numeric text needs more than N character comparisons; use shorter text"). Apply it to every function that coerces range text to numbers: COUNTIF and SUMIF for every criterion form POI's numeric matcher handles (a number, "5" and "=5" were measured; check "<>5" in the same test), CORREL, FORECAST, and any other function a catalog-wide probe shows calling `OperandResolver.parseDouble` on range cells. POI keeps the pattern in a private static field: copy its text into ARC and pin the copy with a test that reads `OperandResolver`'s field reflectively, so a POI upgrade cannot drift silently. Calibrate the budget so a rejected call stays within tens of milliseconds (2,000-digit text costs about 73 ms per parse). Behavior change (docs/api.md "Excel and expression functions", docs/studio.md:203 and the limits bullet at docs/architecture.md:140): such calls fail fast with 422 instead of running past the deadline.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExcelWildcardsTest.java` (B14 coverage): over 20 cells of `$REPT("1", 1999)`+"x", each of `$COUNTIF(cells, 5)`, `$COUNTIF(cells, "=5")`, `$COUNTIF(cells, "5")`, `$SUMIF(cells, 5, nums)`, `$CORREL(cells, nums)` and `$FORECAST(1, cells, nums)` fails with the 422 budget message inside a short `assertTimeoutPreemptively`, while ordinary numeric text cells ("12", " 3.5") give POI's usual results; a pinned-pattern test compares ARC's copy with POI's field; a catalog-wide test (like the finder's fuzz) runs every supported POI function with a long-digit-text range and asserts each finishes quickly or fails with 422. `scripts/smoke.py` (live): the 512-byte preview with `timeoutMs: 100` returns 422 well under a second instead of 504 after 1.7 s.
- **Related:** B14 (same failure class; its rule says new POI matching must be added to `checkMatchingWork`); K01 (the earlier wildcard ReDoS finding); X148 (another argument-handling gap in the same adapter).

<a id="x148"></a>

### X148 · medium · bug · Arrays in reference-class single-value Excel parameters (VLOOKUP/HLOOKUP index, MATCH type, D* field) silently use their first element
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-excel.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / engine.expression (`ExcelFunctionAdapter.checkSingleValues`) / Excel functions (VLOOKUP, HLOOKUP, MATCH, DSUM, DGET and the other D* functions, T)
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExcelFunctionAdapter.java:85` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExcelFunctionAdapter.java:80`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExcelWildcards.java:25`)
- **Problem:** The B16 guard rejects arrays only in parameters that POI's `FunctionMetadata` marks as value class (`Ptg.CLASS_VALUE`). Several parameters that Excel reads as one value are marked reference class, so an array there still reaches POI, which evaluates at cell (0,0) and silently uses its first element: VLOOKUP's col_index_num, HLOOKUP's row_index_num, MATCH's match_type and the field argument of the D* database functions. T's argument is debatable, because Excel's T accepts a reference and uses its top-left cell; this is what remains of the earlier K05 (medium).
- **Trigger → consequence:** `$VLOOKUP(2, [[1,10,100],[2,20,200]], [3, 2], false)` returns 200, also when the index array comes from an input; this is the Excel 365 multi-column idiom, and `$SUM(...)` over it silently totals one column. `$HLOOKUP(2, [[1,2],[10,20],[100,200]], [3, 2], false)` returns 200; `$MATCH("b", ["a","b"], [0, 1])` silently uses match type 0; `$DSUM(db, ["qty","price"], crit)` returns 3 (qty only), and `$DGET(db, [3, 2], crit)` returns 20. The documented behavior is a 422: docs/api.md:140 "An array passed to a single-value Excel parameter fails with `422` "NAME: argument N must be a single value, not an array"", and docs/studio.md:205 "Excel functions take arrays only where Excel takes a range".
- **Evidence:** `ExcelFunctionAdapter.java:85 if (parameterClass == Ptg.CLASS_VALUE && args.get(index) instanceof List<?>)`. POI metadata from probes `$SP/probes/v-excel/ArrayArgs.java` and `RefParams.java`: VLOOKUP [V R R V], HLOOKUP [V R R V], MATCH [V R R], T [R], DSUM/DGET/… [R R R]. Probe outputs: the values above plus `$T(["a","b"]) => a` (D* field cases in `$SP/probes/v-excel/DField.java`); for contrast, `$SQRT([4, 9])` and `$INDEX(..., [2, 1], 1)` fail with "argument N must be a single value, not an array". No test in ExcelArgumentsTest covers reference-class single-value parameters.
- **Suggested fix:** Add to `ExcelFunctionAdapter` an explicit table of reference-class parameters that Excel reads as one value — `VLOOKUP`/`HLOOKUP` index 2, `MATCH` index 2, and index 1 of every database function (share `ExcelWildcards.DATABASE_FUNCTIONS` as one package-level set instead of a second list) — and make `checkSingleValues` reject an array when `parameterClass == Ptg.CLASS_VALUE` or the (name, index) pair is in that table, with the same `notSingleValue` message. Decision needed for `$T`: recommend rejecting arrays there too, for consistency with `$UPPER(["a","b"])` (B16), since ARC cannot spill. Behavior change (docs/api.md; it also changes results of existing published versions that relied on the first element, so say so explicitly): these calls now fail with 422 "NAME: argument N must be a single value, not an array"; keep docs/architecture.md:165 accurate.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExcelArgumentsTest.java`: `$VLOOKUP(2, [[1,10,100],[2,20,200]], [3, 2], false)` → 422 "VLOOKUP: argument 3 must be a single value, not an array"; the HLOOKUP case and `$MATCH("b", ["a","b"], [0, 1])` → "argument 3"; `$DSUM(db, ["qty","price"], crit)` and `$DGET(db, [3, 2], crit)` → "argument 2"; the scalar forms and the range parameters keep their results. Add a catalog-wide completeness check: for every supported function and every reference-class parameter, if POI returns the same result for `[a, b]` as for `a` on varied inputs, the parameter must be in the table or explicitly allowed.
- **Related:** B16 (the rule this completes); K05 (earlier review finding); X147 (same adapter).

<a id="x149"></a>

### X149 · low · bug · POI's RATE logs an ERROR with a stack trace (IRR a WARN) even when `$IFERROR` handles the result
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-excel.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / engine.expression (`ExcelFunctionAdapter.calculate`), resources (`application.yaml` logging) / Excel functions ($RATE, $IRR); server logs and alerting
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExcelFunctionAdapter.java:57` (+ `backend/src/main/resources/application.yaml` (no `logging:` section), `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExcelTextTest.java:125`)
- **Problem:** POI's `Rate.evaluate` calls `LOG.atError().withThrowable(e).log("Can't evaluate rate function")` for any `EvaluationException` — its exception table covers bytecode 21-192, including argument coercion and `checkValue` — so it logs an ERROR with a full stack trace for everything from #NUM! non-convergence to #VALUE! for text arguments; `Irr` logs a WARN ("Returning NaN because IRR has found an fDerivative of 0", plus denominator and max-iteration variants). Both happen even when ARC hands the error to `$IFERROR` and the request returns 200, because ARC sets no log level for org.apache.poi and log4j-to-slf4j sends these lines to logback.
- **Trigger → consequence:** The preview `[$IFERROR($RATE(1e9, 1e9, 1e9), 0), $IFERROR($IRR([1, 1, 1]), 0)]` returns 200 with [0, 0], yet writes an ERROR entry of 88 lines (10,148 bytes) and a WARN to the API log; `$MAP` over 1,000 such calls writes about 10 MB of false ERRORs per request. This triggers ERROR-based alerts, buries real errors and grows the log without bound (compose sets no log rotation).
- **Evidence:** `ExcelFunctionAdapter.java:57 return FunctionEval.getBasicFunction(metadata.getIndex()).evaluate(values, 0, 0);`; `application.yaml` has no `logging:` section. Live log on 8191 (`$SP/probes/v-excel/rate-live-log.txt`, request `rate.json`): "ERROR 1 --- [arc] [io-8080-exec-10] o.apache.poi.ss.formula.functions.Rate : Can't evaluate rate function / org.apache.poi.ss.formula.eval.EvaluationException at org.apache.poi.ss.formula.functions.Rate.checkValue(Rate.java:128)", then "WARN ... Irr : Returning NaN because IRR has found an fDerivative of 0". Probe `$SP/probes/v-excel/RateLog.java`: 100 handled RATE calls → result 0, 100 ERROR entries, 3,800 lines, 317 KB; `$IFERROR($RATE("abc", 1, 1), "bad")` → "bad" and still logs an ERROR with a stack trace.
- **Suggested fix:** Silence POI's per-call function logging, since ARC reports every POI error value itself: in `backend/src/main/resources/application.yaml` set `logging.level."org.apache.poi.ss.formula.functions": "off"` (quoted so YAML does not read it as a boolean; at least `…functions.Rate` and `…functions.Irr`), with a comment that ARC turns POI error values into ArcExceptions. With the level off, POI also skips formatting the stack trace, because its log builder checks `isEnabled` first. No Behavior changes entry (logging only).
- **Regression test:** a Spring Boot test that loads application.yaml's logging levels — e.g. `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/PoiLoggingTest.java` with `@SpringBootTest(classes = <an empty @Configuration>, webEnvironment = NONE)` and `OutputCaptureExtension` — evaluates `$IFERROR($RATE(1e9, 1e9, 1e9), 0)`, `$IFERROR($IRR([1, 1, 1]), 0)` and `$IFERROR($RATE("abc", 1, 1), "bad")`, asserts 0, 0 and "bad", and asserts the captured output contains neither "Can't evaluate rate function" nor "Returning NaN because IRR".
- **Related:** B15 (library behavior around each POI call is ARC's to contain); precedent in `ExcelTextTest.java:125` (POI's per-call stack-trace logging for `$TEXT` was treated as a defect).

<a id="x150"></a>

### X150 · low · bug · Unary minus rounds its operand to 34 digits, so negative literals and NUMBER constants lose precision that positive ones keep
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-excel.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / engine.expression (`ExpressionParser.atom`) / negative number literals and NUMBER constants in Reference bindings, Outputs and Conditions
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExpressionParser.java:131` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExpressionParser.java:141`, `frontend/src/domain/expressions.ts:39`, `frontend/src/domain/valueBinding.ts:63`)
- **Problem:** `case "-" -> context -> number(operand.eval(context)).negate(MATH);` rounds the operand to DECIMAL128 (34 significant digits), while a positive literal stays exact (`number(new BigDecimal(token))`, line 141) and unary plus returns its operand unrounded. The frontend writes a NUMBER constant such as "-0.5" as a minus attached to a literal ("A directly attached minus sign is ARC's unary negation of the number"), so every negative constant goes through this rounding. `-amount` alone is not distinctive — `0 - amount` rounds the same way under the documented DECIMAL128 arithmetic (architecture.md:127) — the literal and constant asymmetry is the real defect.
- **Trigger → consequence:** A Reference binding or Output holding the NUMBER constant `-0.12345678901234567890123456789012345` evaluates to `-0.1234567890123456789012345678901234`, while `0.12345678901234567890123456789012345` keeps all 35 digits. The Condition `negative == -1234567890123456789012345678901234567890.123456789`, where the input holds exactly that value, evaluates to false, and the array constant `[-…]` is rounded as well. Values with more than 34 significant digits are rare, so the impact is small.
- **Evidence:** `ExpressionParser.java:131` and `:141` as quoted. Probe `$SP/probes/v-excel/NegateProbe.java`: the positive 49-digit literal → precision 49, scale 9; `-1234567890123456789012345678901234567890.123456789` → `-1234567890123456789012345678901234000000` (precision 34, scale -6); `negative == -<same literal>` → false; `-0.123…12345` → `-0.1234567890123456789012345678901234`. `ExpressionCompatibilityTest.scalarTypesAndDecimalLiteralPrecisionAreNotCoerced` asserts literal precision only for the positive form.
- **Suggested fix:** Negate exactly: `case "-" -> context -> number(operand.eval(context)).negate();`. Negation never adds digits and `number(...)` already bounded the operand, so the result stays within the value bounds; `-2^2` stays -4 because the operand is still parsed at priority 7, and binary subtraction keeps DECIMAL128. Behavior change (docs/api.md, and one sentence at docs/architecture.md:127 that negation is exact): negative literals, NUMBER constants and `-x` keep every digit instead of being rounded to 34 significant digits.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExpressionCompatibilityTest.java` `scalarTypesAndDecimalLiteralPrecisionAreNotCoerced`: `eval("-" + preciseLiteral)` equals `new BigDecimal("-" + preciseLiteral)` (precision 49, scale 9); `eval("-0.12345678901234567890123456789012345")` keeps 35 digits; an array constant `[-…]` keeps its digits; `-2^2` is still -4. An engine test (e.g. `EngineTest`): the Condition `negative == -1234567890123456789012345678901234567890.123456789` with that exact input takes the true branch.
- **Related:** B6 (numbers keep their decimal value end to end); F16 (the frontend classifies `-0.5` as a NUMBER constant).

<a id="x72"></a>

### X72 · medium · bug · `$IFERROR`/`$ISERROR`/`$ISERR` recover a called Formula version's preparation failure, so published callers silently return the fallback
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-callee.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / engine.expression (`ExpressionRuntime` error functions), engine.execution (`GraphExecution.run`, `callFormula`), error (`ArcException.Kind`) / published Formula calls inside error functions
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExpressionRuntime.java:185` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExpressionRuntime.java:194`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExpressionRuntime.java:242`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/error/ArcException.java:66`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/GraphExecution.java:64`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/GraphExecution.java:229`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/ExecutionPlans.java:111`)
- **Problem:** `ifError`/`isError` recover every error whose `recoverable()` is true, and `recoverable()` is true for every INVALID error. A callee version is compiled at call time (`GraphExecution.run` → `plans.prepare`), so its own preparation failure — a draft-shape error such as a property rejected since 242886a, or a compile error such as an unprefixed call rejected since f5ee56a — surfaces inside the caller's `$IFERROR` as an INVALID error and is replaced by the fallback. This contradicts docs/api.md:29 ("`$IFERROR` and the `$IS…` error functions handle value errors only") and B25 ("one that holds such a property fails until the draft is fixed by hand").
- **Trigger → consequence:** child v1 is a published FORMULA stored with an Output that sets a result variable (or a legacy Switch `expression`, or `ROUND(amount, 2)`), and parent v1 = `$IFERROR(@child:1(amount), -1)` is published. POST /rules/parent/execute with {amount: 3} now returns 200 with result -1, where it returned 6 before 242886a; `$ISERROR` and `$ISERR` return true, while a bare `@child:1(amount)` correctly fails with 422. Diagnostics, validate and publish flag nothing (X74), so published results change silently; no test covers a callee preparation failure inside the error functions (ErrorRecoveryTest uses only the runtime `1 / 0`, #N/A and limit cases).
- **Evidence:** `ExpressionRuntime.java:186-190 try { return args.get(0).eval(context); } catch (ArcException error) { rethrowUnrecoverable(context, error); return args.get(1).eval(context); }`; `:242 if (!error.recoverable()) throw error;`; `ArcException.java:67 return kind == Kind.INVALID || kind == Kind.NOT_AVAILABLE;`; `GraphExecution.java:64 CompiledGraph compiled = plans.prepare(ruleId, version, definition);`. Probe `$SP/probes/v-callee/CalleeProbe.java` (RuleExecutionService with the real Validator, Engine and SourceBindingValidator over a proxy repository): `preview @child:1(amount) -> 422 INVALID 'Result variables belong to Formula, Transform and Reference nodes'`; `preview $IFERROR(@child:1(amount), -1) -> OK -1`; `preview $ISERROR(@child:1(amount)) -> OK true`; `preview $ISERR(@child:1(amount)) -> OK true`; `preview $IFERROR(@unpre:1(amount), -1) -> OK -1`; `execute published parent v1 = $IFERROR(@child:1(amount), -1) -> OK -1`.
- **Suggested fix:** Classify "the called version cannot be prepared" by kind (B7), as a definition failure that fallbacks cannot hide: add `ArcException.Kind.DEFINITION` (not `recoverable()`; javadoc: a pinned rule version fails draft shape or compilation) and a method such as `asDefinitionFailure()` that re-kinds only recoverable errors (LIMIT/DEADLINE keep their kind; `atNode`/`inRule`/`withContext` already keep kinds), and in `GraphExecution.run` wrap only `plans.prepare(...)` with it (the root converts too; HTTP status and message are unchanged because the kind is internal). `$IFERROR`, `$ISERROR`, `$ISERR`, `$ISNA`, source `DEFAULT` and field/case context then let it through unchanged, while the callee's runtime value errors (`1 / 0`, #N/A) stay recoverable. Decision needed: whether a missing or non-Formula pin (404 "Published Formula version not found", "@ calls require a published Formula") is also a definition failure — recommended yes, as it is not a value error and the deletion race (X09) produces exactly this; with X46's lazy resolution the read happens inside `prepare` and the same wrap covers it. Behavior change (docs/api.md): error functions, including `$ISNA` (which answers false today), no longer turn a called version that cannot be prepared into a fallback; the request fails with the callee's 422 and its location. Update B7 and B25 in docs/review-lessons.md and the `ArcException.Kind` javadoc.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/ErrorRecoveryTest.java`: add published formulas `stale:1` (an Output with `.output("r")` via `GraphFixtures`) and `unprefixed:1` (`ROUND(amount, 2)`); assert `$IFERROR(@stale:1(3), -1)`, `$ISERROR(@stale:1(3))`, `$ISERR(@stale:1(3))` and `$ISNA(@stale:1(3))` fail with 422 "Result variables belong to Formula, Transform and Reference nodes" located at (stale, 1, out) and the caller, the unprefixed case fails likewise, and `$IFERROR(@broken:1(), -1)` (runtime `1 / 0`) still returns -1. `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/error/ArcExceptionTest.java`: the new kind is not recoverable and survives `atNode`/`inRule`/`withContext`. `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleExecutionServiceTest.java`: executing published parent v1 = `$IFERROR(@child:1(amount), -1)` fails with 422, not -1.
- **Related:** B7, B25; X74 (static checks accept the same broken pins); X76 (locations of the same preparation failures); X09 (the deletion race leaves dangling pins that reach this path); X46 (lazy pin reads move resolve failures into `prepare`); reachable for existing published versions since 242886a (unused properties) and f5ee56a (function prefixes).

<a id="x74"></a>

### X74 · low · bug · Validate, diagnostics and publish never check that a pinned callee version can still be prepared
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-callee.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / engine.validation (`NodeValidation.referenceParameters`, `FormulaCallValidation`), source (`SourceBindingValidator` walk), rule (`RuleDefinitionService`, `RuleService.publish`) / validate, diagnostics (Problems list) and publish of rules with References or `@id:version` calls
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/NodeValidation.java:108` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/NodeValidation.java:110`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/FormulaCallValidation.java:13`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/source/SourceBindingValidator.java:73`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleDefinitionService.java:50`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleDefinitionService.java:71`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleService.java:110`)
- **Problem:** Static checks resolve a pinned callee but read only its `inputs()` (`referenceParameters`, lines 110-117; `FormulaCallValidation.validate`), and the source-contract walk resolves callees transitively but walks only `Validator.dependencies(callee)` and source mappings, with no shape or compile check. A Reference or `@id:version` call pinned to a version that fails shape or compile therefore passes every static check, and every execution of the new parent then fails (or, under an error function, silently returns the fallback — X72). docs/architecture.md:64 lists what static call checks cover ("kind, pin and argument count"); no doc says broken callee versions are deliberately accepted, and the prefix docs only tell users to "update any pinned parents".
- **Trigger → consequence:** child v1 holds a now-rejected property, or unprefixed v1 holds `ROUND(amount, 2)`. A parent draft with a Reference to that version, or with `@child:1(amount)`, gets `/diagnostics` → [] and `/validate` → valid; publish calls the same `definitions.validate(rule.draft())` (RuleService.java:110), so it succeeds and creates an immutable version that always fails with 422 at child v1. The Problems list stays empty; only running the Test panel reveals it.
- **Evidence:** `NodeValidation.java:108 Definition child = resolver.resolve(node.ruleId(), node.version());` is followed only by checks of `child.inputs()` (lines 110-117); `FormulaCallValidation.java:13 var inputs = resolver.resolveFormula(call.id(), call.version()).inputs();`; `SourceBindingValidator.java:73-75` resolves the callee and walks only `Validator.dependencies(callee)`, with no shape check. Probe `$SP/probes/v-callee/CalleeProbe.java`: `validate(call) -> OK "valid"`, `diagnostics(call) -> OK []`, `preview(call) -> 422 ... locations=[{child,1,out},{preview,null,out}]`; the same result holds for `ref`, `call-unprefixed` and `ref-unprefixed`: valid, [], then 422.
- **Suggested fix:** Check that every reached pin can be prepared inside the one transitive pin walk that already exists (B11: no second traversal): the walk in `SourceBindingValidator.validateContracts`, which visits each reached pin once with the nesting limit, gains a callee-preparation step — `validator.compile(callee, resolver)`, or, to reuse and warm the published-plan cache, `Engine.Session.prepare(pin, () -> dependency.resolve(resolver))` — and rethrows a failure exactly as it rethrows source-contract errors, `error.inRule(dependency.ruleId(), dependency.version()).atNode(null, null, dependency.nodeId(), dependency.label())`. Enable the step from `RuleDefinitionService.validate(definition, resolver)` (→ `/validate` and publish) and `RuleDefinitionService.diagnostics` (reported once, like the source-contract problem, skipping pins the graph checks rejected), but not from `RuleExecutionService.evaluate`, whose execution prepares callees itself (and, with X72, fails unrecoverably). Coordinate with X226, which fixes the same walk's depth accounting. Behavior change (docs/api.md; update docs/architecture.md:64): validate, diagnostics and publish report a pinned version that no longer compiles — for example one holding a property its kind does not use — at the calling node.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleDefinitionServiceTest.java`: with child:1 holding an Output `output` property (and unprefixed:1 holding `ROUND(amount, 2)`), `validate(parent)` throws 422 "Result variables belong to Formula, Transform and Reference nodes" located at (child, 1, out) and the parent's Reference node; `diagnostics(parent)` returns exactly one such problem; the same holds for `@child:1(amount)`; a healthy child → valid and no problems. `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleServiceTest.java`: publishing such a parent → 422 and no version stored.
- **Related:** B11, B25; X72 (runtime side); X76 (locations of the same failures); X226 (same walk: order-dependent nesting depth); X09 (dangling pins from the deletion race); broken versions come from 242886a and f5ee56a.

<a id="x76"></a>

### X76 · low · bug · Published executions report preparation and source-contract errors without `ruleId`/`version`
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-callee.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / rule (`RuleExecutionService.evaluate`) / error locations of `POST /rules/{id}/execute`
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleExecutionService.java:104` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleExecutionService.java:105`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/GraphExecution.java:66`, `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleExecutionServiceTest.java:137`, `docs/api.md:81`, `frontend/src/domain/graph.ts:58`)
- **Problem:** For a published execution, `evaluate` calls `execution.prepare(id, version, definition)` and `definitions.validateSources(...)` outside any `inRule(id, version)` wrapper, so errors from preparing the root version and from the source-contract walk carry `ruleId` and `version` null at the root location, while runtime errors of the same version are wrapped by `GraphExecution.run` (lines 66-67).
