This contradicts docs/api.md:81 ("A published rule may also use the ID `preview`, but its locations always carry a version"; frontend/src/domain/graph.ts:58-60 says the same). The finder's editor consequence is not reachable: the editor's Test panel only calls /preview, and ApiPage renders only the message text.
- **Trigger → consequence:** POST /rules/child/execute on a current published version that fails preparation (a legacy property or unprefixed call, or a dangling pin after the deletion race) returns `locations:[{"ruleId":null,"version":null,"nodeId":"out","label":"Out"}]`, while a runtime failure returns `{"ruleId":"bad","version":1,...}`; a walk failure yields `[child v1/…, {null,null,ref}]`. API clients cannot tell which version failed when they omit `version`.
- **Evidence:** `RuleExecutionService.java:104-113 var prepared = execution.prepare(id, version, definition); definitions.validateSources(prepared.definition(), resolver, ...)` has no `inRule`, unlike `GraphExecution.java:66-67 catch (ArcException error) { throw error.inRule(ruleId, version); }`. Probe `$SP/probes/v-callee/CalleeProbe.java`: `execute child v1 (prepare fails) -> 422 ... locations=[{"ruleId":null,"version":null,"nodeId":"out","label":"Out"}]`; `execute bad v1 (runtime failure) -> 422 'Division by zero' locations=[{"ruleId":"bad","version":1,"nodeId":"out","label":"Result"}]`. Caveat: `RuleExecutionServiceTest.java:136-137` asserts the null location for execute of versions null and 1, so the fix is a deliberate contract change that must update that assertion.
- **Suggested fix:** In `RuleExecutionService.evaluate`, wrap the preparation block (`execution.prepare` and `definitions.validateSources`) in `try { … } catch (ArcException error) { throw version == null ? error : error.inRule(id, version); }`. `inRule` fills only locations whose ruleId is null, so nested child locations are kept, and preview (`version == null`) keeps today's static-check locations, which `isCurrentGraphLocation` already treats as the shown graph. Behavior change (docs/api.md): a published execution that fails while preparing its version or checking source contracts now names the rule and version in its root location, as runtime failures already do.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleExecutionServiceTest.java` `storedDraftsAndPublishedVersionsRequireExplicitFunctionPrefixesWithoutRewritingHistory`: change the expected location at line 137 to `new ArcException.Location("old-rule", 1, "output", "Result")` for both `version` null (the published pointer is 1) and 1; add a source-contract walk failure (a child pin with a missing source mapping) whose root location is `("parent", 1, ref)`; preview failures keep ruleId null.
- **Related:** X72 and X74 (the same preparation failures); X09 (dangling pins); the "Error navigation needs the whole reference context" guidance in docs/review-lessons.md (published locations carry a version; `isPreviewRoot`).

<a id="x46"></a>

### X46 · low · efficiency · Nested Reference and `@` pins are read eagerly, so a cached child plan is still read and decoded on every request
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-callee.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / engine.execution (`GraphExecution.reference`, `callFormula`, `ExecutionPlans.Session.prepare`), engine (`MemoizingRuleResolver`) / published execution with the plan cache
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/GraphExecution.java:225` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/GraphExecution.java:226`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/GraphExecution.java:232`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/GraphExecution.java:49`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/MemoizingRuleResolver.java:25`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/source/SourceBindingValidator.java:73`)
- **Problem:** `reference()` and `callFormula()` resolve the child pin before `run` and pass `() -> child`, so the lazy `Supplier` never helps nested pins: each request reads a reached nested pin through the resolver even when both plans are cached. This contradicts the `run` javadoc (lines 49-52: "A pinned definition is read only when this session and the plan cache have not compiled that version yet") and docs/review-lessons.md:102, and it is a second site beside the known E17 remainder (docs/reviews/2026-09-27-full-review.md:79/:252: the source-contract walk still re-reads child pins).
- **Trigger → consequence:** Today, in the common case, the source-contract walk reads the same pin into the per-request memo first, so the eager read is a memo hit; fixing the walk alone would still leave one read and decode per reached nested pin per request. One extra cost exists today: when the transitive graph uses the same pin through both a Reference and an `@` call, the walk resolves it once through `resolve` and skips the second use as visited, the runtime `resolveFormula` then misses `MemoizingRuleResolver`'s `formulas` map, and every request does a second repository read and decode although all plans are cached.
- **Evidence:** `GraphExecution.java:225-226 Definition child = resolver.resolve(node.ruleId(), node.version()); return run(node.ruleId(), node.version(), () -> child, inputs, depth + 1);` and `:232 Definition child = resolver.resolveFormula(call.id(), call.version());`. Probe `$SP/probes/v-callee/CalleeProbe.java` (engine session, no walk, plans cached): `session-only p2 request 2 ... reads=[resolve child:1]` from `GraphExecution$RuleRun.reference:225`, and `session-only p3 request 2 ... reads=[resolveFormula child:1]` from `callFormula:232`. `$SP/probes/v-callee/MixedPinProbe.java` through RuleExecutionService, root with a Reference to child:1 and `@child:1(amount)`: `request 2 ... delegate reads=[resolve child:1 @SourceBindingValidator.validateContracts:73, resolveFormula child:1 @GraphExecution$RuleRun.callFormula:232]` (the same for request 3). Finder probe: `$SP/probes/e1-wrap/NestedPinReadProbe.java`.
- **Suggested fix:** Pass the read as the Supplier: `reference()` calls `run(node.ruleId(), node.version(), () -> resolver.resolve(node.ruleId(), node.version()), inputs, depth + 1)`. `callFormula()` needs the callee's input names only to map positional arguments, so take them from the prepared plan: split `run` so the plan is prepared first (inside the same active-rule guard and `inRule` wrapper) and the caller then builds the input map from `compiled.definition().inputs()`, keeping the "Too many arguments" check. Keep review-lessons.md:102's guarantees: an uncached pin is read first, so a missing version still fails with 404; and the FORMULA-kind check stays valid because the caller's compile already ran `FormulaCallValidation` (`resolveFormula`), a rule's kind never changes for an ID and `Engine.forget` evicts a deleted rule's plans (or store the kind with the cached plan if the runtime check must stay for cached pins). The E17 walk remainder is separate: cache the source-contract verdict with the root's published plan (pins and source versions are immutable), so a cached root needs no reads at all. No Behavior changes entry (no API change); keep docs/maintaining.md:38 accurate for nested pins.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/FormulaCallExecutionTest.java` (or `ExecutionPlansTest.java`): one `Engine`, a new `engine.session(...)` per request, a counting resolver and a root with a Reference to child:1 and `@child:1(amount)`; only the first request reads the child pin, requests 2 and 3 read nothing, and an uncached missing pin still fails with 404. After the walk fix, `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleExecutionServiceTest.java` (next to `aCachedPublishedPlanIsExecutedWithoutReadingItsVersionAgain`): across three executions the repository reads child:1 only during the first.
- **Related:** E17 (docs/reviews/2026-09-27-full-review.md:252); the "Ambient evaluation state" contract guard (docs/review-lessons.md:102); X72 (a lazy read moves missing-pin failures into `prepare`).

## Backend: execution, draft shape, ARC Script and other backend

<a id="x03"></a>

### X03 · low · bug · "Edit test inputs" is hidden for server-side test-input errors, which also badge a valid Input card
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-exec.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/execution (ExecutionError, TestPanel) / Test panel preview errors
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/execution/ExecutionError.tsx:66` (+ `frontend/src/features/execution/TestPanel.tsx:130`, `frontend/src/features/editor/Editor.tsx:139`, `frontend/src/features/editor/Editor.tsx:211`, `frontend/src/features/editor/useGraphProblems.ts:40-43`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/GraphExecution.java:107-119`)
- **Problem:** ExecutionError renders the "Edit test inputs" action only when the problem has no locations (`{!locations.length && …}`). The server puts every server-side test-input failure (missing required input, wrong type, unknown input) on the Input node, which is the documented and intended contract (docs/api.md:107: "problems of the whole graph or its inputs are located at the Input node"), so the defect is the frontend check: the action is reachable only for client-side JSON parse errors. The same preview problem also feeds `runtimeProblems` → `nodeErrorsOf`, which marks the Input card `node-error` although the graph is fine.
- **Trigger → consequence:** Rule with a required NUMBER input `amount`. In the Test panel run `{}`, `{"amount":"x"}` or `{"amount":1,"extra":2}`. The alert reads "Missing required input: amount", "Input 'amount' must be number" or "Unknown input: extra"; its only button is "Show problem · Inputs", and the Input card gets `node-error`. Only an unparsable buffer such as `{"amount":` offers "Edit test inputs" (switch to the JSON tab and focus the buffer).
- **Evidence:** `ExecutionError.tsx:66-70` `{!locations.length && (<Button … onClick={onEditInputs}>Edit test inputs</Button>)}`; `GraphExecution.java:107-119` `resolveInputs` rethrows every `Parameters.resolve` failure with `error.atNode(ruleId, version, input.id(), input.label())`. curl `POST :8191/api/preview` → 422 `"locations":[{"ruleId":"preview","version":null,"nodeId":"input","label":"Inputs"}]` for all three inputs. Browser probe on :3191: `{} => buttons=["Show problem · Inputs"] inputCardHasNodeError=true`; `{"amount":"x"}` and `{"amount":1,"extra":2}` the same; `{"amount": => message="Unexpected end of JSON input" buttons=["Edit test inputs"] inputCardHasNodeError=false`.
- **Suggested fix:** Keep the server contract. In `ExecutionError`, offer "Edit test inputs" when there are no locations **or** when the failure surfaced at the shown graph's Input node (the last current-graph location in `local` is the Input node), next to "Show problem · Inputs" (source-read and source-mapping failures also land on the Input node, so both actions stay useful). Pass the Input node's ID from `Editor` (the single `<TestPanel>` mount, Editor.tsx:211, from `rule.draft`) through `TestPanel`/`PreviewOutput` into `ExecutionError`. Decision needed on the badge: the client cannot tell a caller-input failure from a source failure at the Input node without parsing message text. Recommended: keep `nodeErrorsOf` unchanged (the badge is truthful for source failures) and rely on the new action; if the badge must go, the server would have to add a typed marker to the error body, which is an HTTP payload change that needs a docs/api.md "Behavior changes" entry.
- **Regression test:** `frontend/tests/execution-json-editor.spec.ts`: for a rule with a required input, running `{}` (and `{"amount":"x"}`) shows both "Show problem · Inputs" and "Edit test inputs"; clicking "Edit test inputs" from the cURL tab switches to Input JSON and focuses the buffer (as the existing parse-error case asserts). Optionally extract the decision into a pure predicate unit-tested in `frontend/tests/unit/execution.spec.ts`.
- **Related:** X106 (other runtime failures located at the Input node carry no context); docs/api.md:107.

<a id="x04"></a>

### X04 · low · bug · API playground says "no published rules" while the catalog loads and after it fails
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-exec.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/execution (ApiPage, usePublishedExecution) / API playground and API docs request builder
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/execution/ApiPage.tsx:160` (+ `frontend/src/hooks/useAsyncResource.ts:49-53`, `frontend/src/features/execution/usePublishedExecution.ts:167-170`, `frontend/src/features/execution/usePublishedExecution.ts:72`, `frontend/src/features/library/LibraryPage.tsx:193`)
- **Problem:** The playground's empty-state Alert checks only `!published.length`. The catalog starts as an empty page and `useAsyncResource` resets `data` to that empty page on failure, so "Publish a rule in the library to make your first API call." shows during every first catalog load and stays after a failed catalog read; with a search typed, a failed read shows "No published rules match your search.". LibraryPage guards its empty state against loading and errors; ApiPage does not.
- **Trigger → consequence:** Open `/#/playground` while the publishedOnly catalog read is slow: the page says nothing is published until the page arrives. If the read then fails with 503, the request panel keeps telling the user to publish a first rule next to "Catalog unavailable / Retry loading". Typing a search while reads fail shows "No published rules match your search.".
- **Evidence:** `ApiPage.tsx:160-166` `{!published.length && (<Alert severity="info">{request.search ? "No published rules match your search." : "Publish a rule in the library to make your first API call."}</Alert>)}` has no loading or error guard; `useAsyncResource.ts:49-53` sets `data: initial` on failure; `usePublishedExecution.ts:167-170` `published` is `catalogRules` while nothing is selected; compare `LibraryPage.tsx:193` `{!library.loading && !library.loadError && !rules.length && (`. Browser probe (publishedOnly reads held, then 503): `while loading: empty-state visible=true`; `after 503: empty-state visible=true, error alert=["Catalog unavailable\nRetry loading"]`; `search after 503: no-match visible=true`.
- **Suggested fix:** Show the empty state only for a settled, successful, empty read: `{!request.catalog.loading && !request.catalog.error && !published.length && (…)}`. `request.catalog.loading` already includes a typed search that has not applied yet (`query !== search`, usePublishedExecution.ts:72), so neither message flashes while typing. This is the same guard LibraryPage uses; no new abstraction is needed.
- **Regression test:** `frontend/tests/execution-catalog.spec.ts`: hold the `rule-summaries?…publishedOnly=true` read and assert neither empty-state text is visible; fulfil it with 503 and assert only the error alert with "Retry loading" is shown; type a search while reads fail and assert "No published rules match your search." stays hidden; an empty 200 page still shows "Publish a rule in the library to make your first API call.".
- **Related:** F5 (explicit loading/ready/failed resource states); X50 (same paged catalog hook).

<a id="x50"></a>

### X50 · low · bug · Returning to an earlier filter or search restores its old page instead of page 1
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-exec.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / hooks (usePagedResource) / catalog paging: Library, API playground catalog and version history, data-source catalog and history, VersionHistory, Code studio Reuse search, SourceBindingEditor versions
- **Location:** `frontend/src/hooks/usePagedResource.ts:29` (+ `frontend/src/hooks/usePagedResource.ts:17`, `frontend/src/hooks/usePagedResource.ts:28`, `frontend/src/hooks/usePagedResource.ts:46`, `frontend/src/hooks/usePagedResource.ts:52-53`, `frontend/tests/catalog-pagination.spec.ts:68`)
- **Problem:** A key change computes offset 0 (`position.key === key ? position.offset : 0`) but never stores it, so `position` keeps the old key's offset. Returning to that key (filter, search or owner) brings back its old page, which contradicts the hook's contract at line 17 ("One bounded page; changing a search or owner immediately resets the offset"). It is also inconsistent: the only writers are `setOffset` (52-53) and the last-page clamp (46, only when `offset > last`), so the old page comes back only if the user did not page under the intermediate key.
- **Trigger → consequence:** Library with 45 rules: Next, Next ("41–45 of 45"), then "Formulas" ("1–20 of 45"), then "All rules" shows "41–45 of 45" instead of page 1. Likewise page 2 → search "Catalog" ("1–20") → clear the search shows "21–40 of 45". Every consumer whose key can return to an earlier value is affected.
- **Evidence:** `usePagedResource.ts:28-29` `const [position, setPosition] = useState({ key, offset: 0 }); const offset = position.key === key ? position.offset : 0;`; `catalog-pagination.spec.ts:68` tests only the one-way reset to a new search. Browser probe with a mocked 45-rule catalog: `All -> page 3 -> Formulas -> All: Previous 41–45 of 45 Next`; `page 2 -> search -> cleared: Previous 21–40 of 45 Next`.
- **Suggested fix:** Store the reset when the key changes, using React's "adjust state while rendering" pattern inside `usePagedResource`: `if (position.key !== key) setPosition({ key, offset: 0 });` before deriving `offset` (keep the derivation so the first render with the new key already reads offset 0). No consumer changes; every hook user gets the documented behavior.
- **Regression test:** `frontend/tests/catalog-pagination.spec.ts`: add "a filter or search round trip starts at page 1": with the mocked 45-rule catalog go to page 3 → Formulas → All rules and expect "1–20 of 45" and a request with `offset=0`; page 2 → search → clear expects "1–20 of 45".
- **Related:** X04 (same paged catalog); the existing test "catalog reads only requested pages and search resets to the first page" covers only the forward reset.

### X69 · low · bug · A failed `@` Formula search is silent in Code studio and both expression dialogs
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-exec.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/studio (CodeStudio, useFormulaSupport, StudioLibrary), features/expressions (ExpressionDialog, InlineExpressionEditor), features/editor (NodeExpressionDialog) / `@` Formula completion
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/studio/CodeStudio.tsx:45` (+ `frontend/src/features/studio/useFormulaSupport.ts:176-178`, `frontend/src/features/studio/useFormulaSupport.ts:293`, `frontend/src/features/expressions/ExpressionDialog.tsx:54`, `frontend/src/features/editor/NodeExpressionDialog.tsx:49`, `frontend/src/features/expressions/InlineExpressionEditor.tsx:18`, `frontend/src/features/expressions/InlineExpressionEditor.tsx:44`, `frontend/src/features/studio/StudioLibrary.tsx:154-159`)
- **Problem:** useFormulaSupport stores a failed `@` Formula search in `formulaError` (returned through useArcLanguageSupport). CodeStudio (:45), ExpressionDialog (:54) and NodeExpressionDialog (:49) destructure only `insertFormula`; only InlineExpressionEditor (:44, shown through `helpText` at :18) displays it. In Code studio and both dialogs a failed search looks exactly like "no Formula matches", even though these editors do show function-catalog failures (`catalogError`).
- **Trigger → consequence:** In Code studio, type `return price + @tax;` while `GET /api/rule-summaries?search=tax&kind=FORMULA&publishedOnly=true&offset=0&limit=8` returns 500. No suggestion list appears and no message is shown anywhere. The same failure in the inspector's inline expression editor shows "Formula suggestions unavailable: <message>".
- **Evidence:** `useFormulaSupport.ts:176-178` `} catch (failure) { if (!token.isCancellationRequested) setError(errorMessage(failure)); return { suggestions: [] }; }` and `:293` `return { insertFormula, formulaError: error };`; the dialogs render only `catalogError`/check errors (ExpressionDialog.tsx:105, NodeExpressionDialog.tsx:109-110). Browser probe on :3191: `X69 code studio: failed searches=1, 'Formula suggestions unavailable' shown=false, 'exploded' shown=false, suggest widget visible=0`; `X69 inline editor: "Formula suggestions unavailable: Formula search exploded"`; screenshot `$SP/probes/v-exec/x69.png`.
- **Suggested fix:** Render `formulaError` wherever `useArcLanguageSupport` is used, with one message rule. Move the text from `InlineExpressionEditor.helpText` into one exported helper beside useFormulaSupport (e.g. `formulaSuggestionProblem(formulaError)` → "Formula suggestions unavailable: …"), then: CodeStudio passes it to StudioLibrary, which adds it to its existing error Alert (`insertion.error || catalogError || catalog.error`); ExpressionDialog shows it in its aside next to `catalogError`; NodeExpressionDialog adds it to its error Alert. The hook already clears the error on the next successful search (`setError("")`, useFormulaSupport.ts:144).
- **Regression test:** `frontend/tests/formula-completion.spec.ts`: route the FORMULA `rule-summaries` search to 500 `{"message":"Formula search exploded"}`, type `@tax` in Code studio, in the Expression dialog and in the node code dialog, and assert "Formula suggestions unavailable: Formula search exploded" is visible in each; let the next search succeed and assert the message disappears.
- **Related:** F31 (`@` search lifecycle); InlineExpressionEditor already implements the intended presentation.

### X56 · medium · bug · One draft-shape problem anywhere makes /api/variables 422 and blanks every inspector's scope
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-shape.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend (+ Frontend) / rule (RuleDefinitionService), engine.validation (DefinitionShape, Validator); features/editor/inspector (useNodeVariables), features/expressions (ExpressionDialog, ValueBinding) / inspector variable scopes, Expression dialog Apply, value-source switching
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleDefinitionService.java:63` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/DefinitionShape.java:33-43`, `frontend/src/features/editor/inspector/useNodeVariables.ts:30-35`, `frontend/src/hooks/useAsyncResource.ts:47-55`, `frontend/src/features/expressions/ExpressionDialog.tsx:58-63`, `frontend/src/features/expressions/ExpressionDialog.tsx:139`, `frontend/src/features/expressions/ValueBinding.tsx:80-82`)
- **Problem:** POST /api/variables shape-validates the whole draft (`validator.shape(definition)`) before building the scope plan, so one shape violation on any node returns 422, even when it cannot affect scopes (a blank label, an invalid default, an unused property). useNodeVariables then falls back to empty scopes for every node, and the frontend treats that failure like an authoritative empty scope: the expression dialog of an unrelated node reports its valid variables as unavailable and disables Apply, pickers label valid bindings "unavailable", and switching a binding's value source to "Upstream variable" erases it; the value stays erased after the scope read recovers. An incomplete but well-shaped draft (a missing edge) still returns 200, so the all-or-nothing failure comes only from shape validation.
- **Trigger → consequence:** The draft has one shape violation: an ARRAY parameter default of `{}`, which the UI accepts (X57); a node label cleared while it is being renamed; or a stored draft with an unused property after 242886a. `/variables` returns 422 and every inspector gets no variables. On Formula "calc" (`amount * 2`), "Open in Editor · Expression" shows "Unavailable variables: amount" and Apply is disabled. The Output shows "total · unavailable"; switching its value source Expression → Upstream variable clears the expression, and fixing the default does not bring it back. The editor has no undo, so the edit is lost.
- **Evidence:** `RuleDefinitionService.java:62-64` `public Map<String, Set<String>> variables(Definition definition) { validator.shape(definition); return new GraphPlan(definition).available(); }`; `useAsyncResource.ts:47-55` sets `data: initial` on error and `useNodeVariables.ts:30-35` passes `noScopes` and ignores the error; `ExpressionDialog.tsx:58-63` "Unavailable variables: …" and `:139` `disabled={loading || !check?.valid || !!error}`; `ValueBinding.tsx:80-82` `const kept = choices.some((v) => v.name === value) ? value : undefined; … if (kept === undefined) onChange(undefined);`. curl on :8191 (fixtures `$SP/probes/v-shape/base.json`, `blank.json`, `legacy.json`, `arr.json`, `incomplete.json`): valid draft → 200 `{"input":["amount"],"calc":["amount"],"out":["amount","total"]}`; blank label on out → 422 "Every node needs a label of 1 to 160 characters"; `output:"legacy"` on an OUTPUT → 422 "Result variables belong to Formula, Transform and Reference nodes"; ARRAY default `{}` → 422 "Input 'items' must be array"; missing edge → 200. Browser probe on :3191: `BASELINE APPLY ENABLED: true`; after filling `{}` into the ARRAY default `VARIABLE STATUSES [200,422]`, `DIALOG ALERTS ["Unavailable variables: amount"]`, `APPLY ENABLED AFTER: false`; `OUTPUT VALUE "total · unavailable"`, empty after Expression → Upstream variable (preview read "…"); after clearing the default, statuses `[...,200]` but the Output value stays empty. The docs call for a 422 on "Invalid structure or cycles" (api.md:83) and for a failed read to offer no suggestions (architecture.md:171), not for the editor-wide loss, the false Apply refusal or the erasure.
- **Suggested fix:** Backend (root cause): make `/variables` depend only on the structure the scope plan reads. Give `DefinitionShape` a structure-only mode that runs the same checks in the same order but skips content checks: document lists and counts, node non-null, ID syntax, uniqueness and known type, Switch case entries with valid unique IDs, edges (unique IDs, existing endpoints, valid handles) and non-null input names; skip labels, positions, kind properties, expression sizes, result/Output names, fields, bindings, pins, notes, defaults and sources. Expose it as e.g. `Validator.scopeStructure(definition)` and call it from `RuleDefinitionService.variables` instead of `validator.shape`; full `shape`/`validate` stay unchanged for save, render, validate and execute. Cycles and broken structure still return 422, as api.md:83 states. Frontend (so a remaining 422, e.g. a cycle, is not destructive): let `useNodeVariables` return a discriminated scope (`{ status: "ready", variables }` or `{ status: "unknown", error }`) so each consumer handles an unknown scope explicitly; ExpressionDialog then shows "Variable scope unavailable" instead of "Unavailable variables: …" and does not block Apply on `missing` while the scope is unknown; `ValueBinding.chooseMode("variable")` keeps the current value while the scope is unknown (the picker already renders "name · unavailable") instead of calling `onChange(undefined)`. F10 still holds: no variables are offered while a read is pending or failed. Add a docs/api.md "Behavior changes" line: `/variables` returns scopes for drafts whose problems do not affect the graph structure.
- **Regression test:** Backend: `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleDefinitionServiceTest.java` — `variables()` of the valid draft and of the blank-label, ARRAY-default-`{}` and `output:"legacy"` variants return the same map; an edge to a missing node, a duplicate node ID and a cycle still throw 422. Browser: `frontend/tests/available-variables.spec.ts` — with a blank Output label (or `/api/variables` routed to 422), the Formula's Expression dialog keeps Apply enabled without an "Unavailable variables" alert, and Expression → Upstream variable on the Output keeps `total`; keep the existing test "pending and failed variable reads do not invent input choices or erase the current expression" unchanged.
- **Related:** X57 (the ARRAY-default trigger), X86 (the same 422 carries no location), X77 (refuted; its `/variables` part is this finding), X215 and X220 (other per-edit `/variables` costs), X102 (too-complex graphs make `/variables` fail too); F10, B25; the unused-property trigger exists since 242886a.

### X77 · low · bug · (Refuted) node code render/build refuse a draft whose other node holds a stale property
- **Status:** REFUTED — record: `verdicts/v-shape.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / engine.script (ArcScript) / node code editor (`/studio/node/render`, `/studio/node/build`), Code studio render
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/script/ArcScript.java:74` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/script/ArcScript.java:58`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/script/ArcScript.java:63`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/script/ArcScript.java:68-71`)
- **Problem:** The finder reported that node render, `/studio/render` and node build shape-validate the whole containing draft, so one node with a property its kind does not use blocks node-code editing everywhere. The facts are accurate, but the behavior is intended: ARC Script cannot represent a property the node's kind does not use (`ArcScriptRenderer.declarations` writes only a kind's own statements), so rendering without the shape check would silently drop the stale property on the next build, against B25 ("Stored graphs are never rewritten: one that holds such a property fails until the draft is fixed by hand, where the inspector names the properties and **Remove** clears them"). The documented repair works.
- **Trigger → consequence:** Formula `calc` with a stale `selector`: node/render for `calc` and for `out` → 422 "Selectors belong to Switch nodes" located at `calc`; node/build with the corrected fragment → 200, `definition:null`, diagnostic at 1:1. `/diagnostics` marks `calc`, so the user is pointed at the node, and Remove in the inspector clears it. The UI never reaches node/build for such a draft (useNodeExpressionDraft.ts:62 builds only after renderNode succeeded); Code view shows a spinner plus the error and can switch back to the graph (useRuleDocument.ts:232), so no edit is lost.
- **Evidence:** `ArcScript.java:68-71` "an invalid containing graph or unknown node is reported at the start of the fragment"; docs/review-lessons.md B25; docs/studio.md:292; `frontend/tests/unused-node-properties.spec.ts` tests the repair; the do-not-report list covers "stored graphs with unused node properties fail until fixed by hand".
- **Suggested fix:** None required. Optional maintainability improvement: when `buildNode` fails because of another node's shape violation, prefix that node's label to the 1:1 diagnostic so the dialog names the node to repair.
- **Regression test:** None new; keep `frontend/tests/unused-node-properties.spec.ts` and `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/NodeContractsTest.java`.
- **Related:** X56 (the `/variables` part of the original report); B25.

<a id="x86"></a>

### X86 · low · bug · Input and document shape problems come back unlocated from save, create, render and /variables
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-shape.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / engine.validation (DefinitionShape, Validator, Problems) / error locations of draft save/create, Code studio render, node render and `/api/variables`
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/DefinitionShape.java:52` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/DefinitionShape.java:61-64`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/Validator.java:24-26`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/GraphValidation.java:33-34`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/Problems.java:21-22`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleService.java:84`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleService.java:98`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/script/ArcScript.java:58`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/script/ArcScript.java:63`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleDefinitionService.java:63`)
- **Problem:** `Validator.shape` throws `DefinitionShape.located()` errors without applying `Problems.onInputNode`, so violations whose element has no owning node return `locations: []` from draft create/save, /studio/render, /studio/node/render and /variables. /validate (GraphValidation.compile wraps with onInputNode) and /diagnostics (Problems.add) put the same fault on the Input node, which is what docs/api.md:107, maintaining.md:48 and B11 promise. The record also names node and edge counts; in HEAD an over-limit node or edge list is reported at its first extra node/connection, so the unlocated cases are document-level violations (schemaVersion, missing lists, no nodes), the input list and each input declaration or source, and notes.
- **Trigger → consequence:** `PUT /api/rules/{id}` or `POST /api/rules` with two inputs named `amount` → 422 "Duplicate input: amount", `locations: []`; /validate on the same draft → `[{nodeId:"input",label:"Inputs"}]`. A source binding with an empty ID gives save `[]` and diagnostics Input node. API clients get no node for save failures; the editor is barely affected, because its live diagnostics already mark the Input node with the same message.
- **Evidence:** `DefinitionShape.java:52` `return owner == null ? error : error.atNode(...)` with `:63-64` `case InputDeclaration declaration -> null; case InputSource source -> null;`; `Validator.java:24-26` never calls onInputNode, unlike `GraphValidation.java:33-34` `throw Problems.onInputNode(error, definition);`. curl on :8191: save dup → `{"status":422,"issues":["Duplicate input: amount"],"locations":[]...}`; create, render and variables dup → `[]`; validate → `[{"nodeId":"input","label":"Inputs"}]`; diagnostics → same; bad source: save `[]` vs diagnostics Input node.
- **Suggested fix:** Give owner-less violations the Input node in one place: in `DefinitionShape.located`, return `Problems.onInputNode(error, definition)` when `owner == null` (it already leaves the error unlocated when the draft has no Input node, and `Definition.inputNode()` tolerates unvalidated documents). `Validator.shape`, `GraphValidation.compile` and `GraphDiagnostics` then agree without extra wrapping (onInputNode is a no-op on an already located error). Update the `located` Javadoc ("Document and input problems have no node" → "…appear on the Input node when the draft has one"). Add a docs/api.md "Behavior changes" line: save, create, render, node render and variables now locate input and whole-document shape problems at the Input node, as /validate does.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/ValidatorTest.java`: `validator.shape` on drafts with duplicate inputs, an empty source ID and 51 inputs (over `Limits.MAX_INPUTS` = 50) throws with location `(null, null, "input", "Inputs")`; a draft without an Input node keeps `[]`. `scripts/smoke.py`: `PUT /api/rules/{id}` with a duplicate input returns 422 whose `locations[0].nodeId` is the Input node's ID.
- **Related:** B11 ("Whole-graph and input problems appear on the Input node (`Validator.onInputNode`)"); X57 (common trigger), X56 (same shape call in `/variables`).

<a id="x57"></a>

### X57 · low · bug · ARRAY/OBJECT parameter defaults accept wrong-type and out-of-limit JSON as valid
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-shape.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/editor/inspector (InputDefaultValue), components (JsonField), domain (numericDefaults) / Input parameter defaults
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/editor/inspector/InputDefaultValue.tsx:17` (+ `frontend/src/components/JsonField.tsx:58-67`, `frontend/src/domain/numericDefaults.ts:71`, `frontend/src/domain/numericDefaults.ts:107-108`, `frontend/src/features/editor/inspector/NumericDefaultField.tsx:46-54`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/InputTypes.java:16-27`)
- **Problem:** ARRAY and OBJECT defaults go to JsonField, which accepts any parseable JSON, forwards it to the draft and marks itself valid. This includes the wrong type (`{}` or `5` for ARRAY) and numbers beyond server limits (`[1e400]`), so a shape-invalid default enters the draft. The NUMBER field instead rejects out-of-limit values before they reach the draft. Correction from the record: the Input node does get a diagnostics badge, so the user is pointed at the node, though not at the field; the save 422 itself has no location (X86).
- **Trigger → consequence:** Typing `{}` into an ARRAY default leaves the field `aria-invalid="false"` with helper "JSON". Diagnostics show "Input 'items' must be array" on the Input node. Save fails with 422 `locations: []`, Code view and node-code dialogs refuse to render, and /variables returns 422, which causes the editor-wide loss of variables in X56. `[1e400]` gives "Number exceeds supported precision or magnitude" in the same way.
- **Evidence:** `JsonField.tsx:58-67` `parsed = raw.trim() ? parseJson(raw) : null; … acceptedValue.current = parsed; onChange(parsed); setError(""); onValidity(true);` has no type or limit check; compare `numericDefaults.ts:107-108` `if (!withinServerLimits(savedToken)) return { valid: false, error: unsupportedNumber };`; server rule `InputTypes.check` `case "ARRAY" -> value instanceof List<?>;` → "Input 'items' must be array". /api/diagnostics: `{}`, `5`, `"x"` → "Input 'items' must be array" at input; `[1e400]` → "Number exceeds supported precision or magnitude" at input; `[1,2]` → `[]`. Browser probe: `JSON FIELD aria-invalid: false`; helpers include "JSON"; Input node "Node errors (1)"; `/variables [200,422]`. docs/studio.md:177 promises field errors only for unfinished JSON and out-of-limit numeric defaults.
- **Suggested fix:** Validate the parsed value against the declared type before it reaches the draft, with the NumericDefaultField contract. Add an optional `check?: (value: unknown) => string | null` prop to JsonField: on a problem it shows the message as the field error, calls `onValidity(false)` and does not call `onChange`, so the existing invalid-default gate keeps blocking Save, node changes and code view. InputDefaultValue passes a pure domain helper (e.g. `inputDefaultProblem(type, value)` in `domain/numericDefaults.ts` or a new `domain/inputDefaults.ts`) that mirrors `InputTypes.check` (ARRAY → array; OBJECT → plain object, not an array) and applies the exported `withinServerLimits` to every nested number token (`DecimalNumber.text`, otherwise `JSON.stringify(number)`), mirroring `Expressions.bounded`. Update docs/studio.md:177 and add a docs/studio.md "Behavior changes" line (wrong-type and out-of-limit JSON defaults show a field error and block saving).
- **Regression test:** `frontend/tests/json-defaults.spec.ts`: typing `{}` or `5` into an ARRAY default (and `[]` into an OBJECT default) sets `aria-invalid="true"` with the type message, blocks saving and sends no draft change (no `/api/variables` 422); `[1e400]` shows the number-limit error; `[1,2]` is accepted and saves. Unit-test the pure helper in `frontend/tests/unit/numeric-defaults.spec.ts` (or a new `frontend/tests/unit/input-defaults.spec.ts`).
- **Related:** X56 (consequence), X86 (unlocated save 422), X183 (JsonField buffer logic duplicated), X62 (decimal grammar duplicated); F27, F34 (fields must not accept what the server rejects), B6.

<a id="x103"></a>

### X103 · low · bug · Diagnostics without a scope plan drop scope-free node checks; structure checks report only the first problem
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-shape.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / engine.validation (GraphDiagnostics, GraphValidation, NodeValidation) / editor diagnostics (`/api/diagnostics`)
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/GraphDiagnostics.java:41` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/NodeValidation.java:29-63`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/NodeValidation.java:69-76`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/NodeValidation.java:90`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/GraphValidation.java:79-86`, `docs/maintaining.md:48`, `docs/architecture.md:180`)
- **Problem:** Without a scope plan (a cycle, or branch analysis that is too complex), diagnostics run only `NodeValidation.syntax`, which compiles expressions. That silently drops node checks that need no scope: a Switch needs a case, Reference pin and binding checks, a valid result variable, and not overwriting an input — although `NodeValidation.validate` already accepts a null scope (`check()` skips the variable check at :90). Separately, `checkStructure` reports only the first connection or reachability problem. Partly by design and long-standing (maintaining.md:48: "A cyclic graph without a scope plan still gets labelled syntax and Formula checks"; GraphValidation.java:79-80: "The first input-cycle, connection or reachability problem"; baseline 49bb365 had the same syntax-only fallback), hence low severity; architecture.md:180 promises "collecting errors across nodes".
- **Trigger → consequence:** An acyclic draft reports 4 problems: "F: provide a valid result variable", "W: cannot overwrite input amount", "Published rule version not found: nope v3", "Route: add at least one case". Adding one edge out→f leaves only "Decision graphs cannot contain cycles" and "Out: connect no outgoing branches"; the 4 node problems vanish until the cycle is fixed, and the canvas does not prevent cycles. 3 Conditions missing their false edges plus unreachable Outputs → 1 problem, "c1: connect [true, false]", so the editor marks one node at a time. Publish still validates fully; only the completeness of diagnostics is affected.
- **Evidence:** `GraphDiagnostics.java:41` `if (plan == null) nodeValidation.syntax(node, pins, expressions);`; `NodeValidation.java:69-76` (syntax only compiles) vs `:29-63` validate; `GraphValidation.java:82-86` checkStructure throws at the first problem. Java probe `$SP/probes/v-shape/VShapeDiagProbe.java` (acyclic, cyclic and structure cases above).
- **Suggested fix:** (1) In `GraphDiagnostics.diagnose`, always call `nodeValidation.validate(definition, node, plan == null ? null : plan.available().get(node.id()), pins, expressions)` and delete `NodeValidation.syntax` (its only caller); a null scope still skips only variable availability, and labels stay as today. (2) Let `GraphValidation.checkInputCycles`, `checkConnections` and `checkReachability` report through a `Consumer<ArcException>`, like `checkSourceMappings` already does: `compile` passes `GraphValidation::fail` (first problem, unchanged), diagnostics pass `problems::add` and get one problem per offending node (missing handles, duplicate connections, unreachable nodes); "exactly one Input node" can stay a stop condition. Update maintaining.md:48, the GraphValidation Javadoc (:79-80) and add a docs/studio.md "Behavior changes" line (the editor marks every broken node, also in cyclic graphs).
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/DiagnosticsTest.java`: the acyclic 4-problem draft plus an out→f edge reports the cycle error and all four node problems with their labels; the 3-Condition draft reports "connect [true, false]" for c1, c2 and c3 plus each unreachable Output; keep `cyclicGraphsKeepTheLabelOfEveryBrokenExpression`. `ValidatorTest`: executable validation still stops at the first problem with the same message.
- **Related:** B11; maintainability lesson "Separate executable/incomplete-graph validation paths…" (diagnostics and executable validation share the steps; validation stops at the first problem); X102 (too-complex graphs also lose the scope plan).

### X145 · low · convention · $CONTAINS on structured values and `..` property paths changed for published versions without a behavior-change note
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-be-misc.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Docs (backend behavior) / docs/api.md, docs/studio.md; engine.expression (Functions, ExpressionParser) / expression functions ($CONTAINS) and property paths
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/Functions.java:113` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/Functions.java:114-116`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExpressionParser.java:161`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/BuiltinFunctionCatalog.java:205`, `docs/api.md:141`, `docs/api.md:142`, `docs/studio.md:139`)
- **Problem:** Two intended, tested compatibility changes for stored and published expressions are missing from the "Behavior changes in this revision" lists. `$CONTAINS` now fails with 422 when it searches an object, or when it searches text for an array or object; it used to return a boolean from Java `toString`, and no doc mentions this. A property path containing `..` is now a syntax error (it used to return null silently); architecture.md:127 and studio.md:201 say "A property path needs a name after every dot", but the change list in api.md:142 names only the trailing-dot case (`customer.`). AGENTS.md: "existing published versions are compatibility boundaries. Make intended behavior changes explicit".
- **Trigger → consequence:** A version published before 6fbbd07 with `$CONTAINS(order, "open")`, where order is an OBJECT, returned 200 true (`"{status=open}".contains("open")`); every execution now returns 422 "CONTAINS searches text or an array". `$CONTAINS(text, ["a"])` now returns 422 "CONTAINS can only search text for a scalar value". `customer..name` now returns 422 "Property path needs a name after every '.'" instead of null. api.md:141 says only "`$CONTAINS` is false when either side is null", studio.md:139 covers only null and array membership, and the catalog signature "CONTAINS(text or array, value)" still allows a structured value to find in text. The trigger needs an object searched, a structured needle in text, or a typo `..`, so it is narrow.
- **Evidence:** HEAD `Functions.java:113-116` `if (!ValueText.isScalar(searched)) throw ArcException.invalid("CONTAINS searches text or an array"); if (!ValueText.isScalar(value)) throw ArcException.invalid("CONTAINS can only search text for a scalar value");`; `ExpressionParser.java:161` `if (path.endsWith(".") || path.contains(".."))`; baseline 49bb365 `Functions.java:93` `: String.valueOf(first).contains(String.valueOf(args.get(1)));`. Deliberate: `TextConversionTest:69-72` and `ExpressionSyntaxTest:62-66` assert them. Probe `$SP/probes/v-be-misc/ContainsPathProbe.java`: `$CONTAINS(order, "open") => ArcException: CONTAINS searches text or an array`, `$CONTAINS(text, ["a"]) => ArcException: CONTAINS can only search text for a scalar value`, `customer..name => ArcException: Property path needs a name after every '.': customer..name`; replaying the baseline expression gave `baseline order: true`.
- **Suggested fix:** Documentation only; keep the behavior. In docs/api.md "Behavior changes in this revision" → "Excel and expression functions", add: "`$CONTAINS` rejects an object as the searched value (`422` "CONTAINS searches text or an array") and an array or object as the value to find in text (`422` "CONTAINS can only search text for a scalar value"); both used to compare Java text such as `{status=open}`." Extend the api.md:142 sentence to "A property path ending in a dot or containing `..` (`customer.`, `customer..name`) is a syntax error instead of reading null". Mirror the `$CONTAINS` rule in docs/studio.md:139 and in the studio.md "Behavior changes" list. Optional: state "the value must be a scalar when searching text" in the `CONTAINS` catalog help (BuiltinFunctionCatalog.java:205) and update the catalog fixtures explicitly.
- **Regression test:** Behavior already pinned by `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/TextConversionTest.java` (`containsSearchesCanonicalTextAndNullIsNeverText`) and `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExpressionSyntaxTest.java` (`customer.`, `customer..name`, `items.0.`). For the docs change, validate local links and check the new examples against those tests.
- **Related:** B17 (structured values have no text form; "objects joined as `{a=1}`"), B18; introduced in 6fbbd07.

<a id="x144"></a>

### X144 · low · efficiency · Rule deletion prefilter matches every stored definition for common IDs and decodes them all under the row lock
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-be-misc.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / persistence (JdbcRuleRepository), rule (RuleService.delete) / rule deletion caller check
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/persistence/JdbcRuleRepository.java:52` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/persistence/JdbcRuleRepository.java:45-57`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/persistence/JdbcRuleRepository.java:261-273`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/persistence/JdbcRuleRepository.java:178`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleService.java:122-123`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleService.java:137-149`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleRepository.java:42-46`)
- **Problem:** The deletion prefilter `strpos(draft::text, ?)` / `strpos(definition::text, ?)` matches every other stored draft and version when the rule ID is a substring of the stored JSON structure, e.g. `id`, `type`, `label`, `input`, `version`, `position`, `source`, `cases`, `a`, `e`. Stored definitions write every node key, even null ones (`"cases": null`, `"ruleId": null`, …), so for these IDs the "cheap" filter narrows nothing. DELETE then decodes all stored definitions into one list and recompiles every `@` expression while it holds the rule's `FOR UPDATE` lock.
- **Trigger → consequence:** `DELETE /api/rules/type` reads every other draft plus the whole version history through `jdbc.query` and Jackson-decodes all of it into memory; `callersOf` then runs `Validator.draftDependencies` on each row. The cost is O(total stored JSON) per delete. It only matters on large installations, and deletes are rare.
- **Evidence:** `JdbcRuleRepository.java:49-58` `WHERE id <> ? AND strpos(draft::text, ?) > 0 UNION ALL … WHERE rule_id <> ? AND strpos(definition::text, ?) > 0`; `:261-268` decode every returned row; `RuleService.java:122-123` `store.lock(id); List<String> callers = callersOf(id);` (lock is `SELECT … FOR UPDATE`, :178). Read-only count on the disposable DB (arc-review-database-1): 15 drafts and 7 versions in total; for each of 'type', 'id', 'a', 'input' and 'label', all 15 drafts and all 7 versions matched. Stored text sample: `{"edges": [{"id": "input-next-discount", "source": "input", "target": "discount", "sourceHandle": "next"}, ...], "nodes": [{"id": "input", "type": "INPUT", "cases": null, "label": ...`.
- **Suggested fix:** Prefilter on the two ways a definition can call a rule instead of the bare ID, and keep the exact `Validator.draftDependencies` check in `RuleService.callersOf`. A Reference pin: JSONB containment `draft @> jsonb_build_object('nodes', jsonb_build_array(jsonb_build_object('ruleId', ?::text)))`, which does not depend on JSON text formatting and matches with or without a version; OR a Formula call: `strpos(draft::text, '@' || ? || ':') > 0` (the parser's token is `@id:version` with no spaces, and rule-ID characters are never escaped in JSON). Apply the same predicates to `rule_versions.definition`. The new filter still returns version-less Reference callers (X107) and `@` calls in source mappings (X28). Update the `DEFINITIONS_MENTIONING` Javadoc and the `RuleRepository.definitionsMentioning` contract ("drafts and versions that may call the rule").
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/persistence/JdbcRuleRepositoryTest.java` (mocked JdbcTemplate): `definitionsMentioning("type")` issues the containment and `@…:` predicates with those bound values, not `strpos(draft::text, ?)` with the bare ID. `scripts/smoke.py` against a disposable PostgreSQL: the existing deletion scenario (409 listing draft and version callers, 204 after removal) keeps passing, extended with an `@callee:1(...)` call inside an Input source mapping so both predicates run on real JSONB.
- **Related:** B26 (a called rule is kept), X09 (delete vs. concurrent publish on the same path), X107 and X28 (caller-check exactness the new predicates must preserve); maintainability lesson on catalog paging ("a page never costs work proportional to all stored drafts"); the query was added with rule deletion in b8aa4ff (`git log -S DEFINITIONS_MENTIONING`).

<a id="x106"></a>

### X106 · low · bug · Runtime errors in Reference bindings and source-mapping arguments do not name the binding or mapping
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-be-misc.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / engine.execution (GraphExecution, Parameters) / runtime error messages in the Test panel, preview and execute
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/GraphExecution.java:223` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/GraphExecution.java:222`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/GraphExecution.java:210-213`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/Parameters.java:103-105`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/Parameters.java:115-119`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/NodeValidation.java:143-150`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/NodeValidation.java:183-195`)
- **Problem:** When a Reference binding fails at runtime, the error is the bare message at the Reference node without the binding name. When a source-mapping argument fails, the error is the bare message at the Input node and names neither the input nor the mapping key, because Parameters evaluates the arguments (:103-105) outside the try that adds `parameter.name() + ": "`. The record cites that prefix at :116; in HEAD it is at :119, inside the catch that starts at :115 — the arguments are outside it either way. By contrast Transform fields and Switch cases add "Field x:" / "Case y:", a failed source read adds "rate:", and static diagnostics label the same expressions "Reuse / amount" and "rate source / region". This gap already existed at the 49bb365 baseline; it is not a regression.
- **Trigger → consequence:** Reference "Reuse" with bindings `{amount: "1 / zero", rate: "2"}` and input `zero=0` fails with 422 "Division by zero" at reuse/Reuse; the same expression as a Transform field gives "Field amount: Division by zero". A sourced input `rate` whose mapping `region` is `"1 / zero"` fails with "Division by zero" at the Input node, while a failed read of the same input says "rate: Division by zero". With several bindings or several sourced inputs, the Test panel cannot show which one failed.
- **Evidence:** `GraphExecution.java:222-223` `for (var binding : node.bindings().entrySet()) inputs.put(binding.getKey(), eval(binding.getValue(), scope));` has no context; compare `:210-213` `throw inContext(error, "Field " + field.name());`. Probe `$SP/probes/v-be-misc/BindingContextProbe.java`: `reference binding -> Division by zero [Location[... nodeId=reuse, label=Reuse]]`, `transform field -> Field amount: Division by zero [...nodeId=shape...]`, `source mapping argument -> Division by zero [...nodeId=in, label=Input]`, `source read failure (for comparison) -> rate: Division by zero [...nodeId=in...]`.
- **Suggested fix:** Wrap each binding evaluation in `GraphExecution.reference` and each mapping-argument evaluation in `Parameters.Resolution.read` with its position label, through the recoverable-only gate so LIMIT and DEADLINE keep their plain message (B7). Take the wording from NodeValidation's owned-expression enumeration instead of retyping it; the shared change (gate inside `ArcException.withContext`, position labels exposed from `NodeValidation.OwnedExpression`) is described under X194 and fixes both. Decision needed on the runtime wording; recommended: the static label without the node prefix — `amount: Division by zero` for a binding (location stays the Reference node) and `rate source / region: Division by zero` for a mapping (location stays the Input node) — leaving the existing `Selector`, `Case x` and `Field x` texts unchanged. Add a docs/api.md "Behavior changes" line for the new prefixes.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/ErrorRecoveryTest.java`: a Reference binding `1 / zero` fails with "amount: Division by zero" at the Reference node, and a limit error inside a binding keeps its plain message. `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/ParametersTest.java`: a mapping argument `1 / zero` fails with "rate source / region: Division by zero" at the Input node, and a failed read still says "rate: …".
- **Related:** X194 (same root cause and shared fix), X159 (registry duplicate merged into X106); B7, B11.

### X134 · low · convention · Error-body field order follows Map.of hash order and changes per JVM start
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-be-misc.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / api (Errors) / HTTP error responses
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/api/Errors.java:23` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/api/Errors.java:23-31`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/api/Errors.java:52`, `docs/api.md:104`)
- **Problem:** Error bodies are built with `Map.of`, whose iteration order is salted per JVM start. So the JSON field order of every ArcException body, and of the 3-field `response()` body at :52, changes across restarts and replicas and does not match the documented order. This contradicts B9: "Do not let Set.of/Set.copyOf/Map.of or HashSet iteration decide an observable order". It already existed at baseline.
- **Trigger → consequence:** The disposable API currently returns `{"status":404,"issues":["Rule not found: rv-v-be-misc-missing"],"locations":[],"message":"Rule not found: rv-v-be-misc-missing"}`, while docs/api.md:104 documents `{"status":..,"message":..,"issues":..,"locations":..}`. Six fresh JVMs that serialized the same Map.of with Jackson produced four different orders. Clients that parse by key are unaffected; byte-level snapshots and log diffs across replicas are unstable. PayloadSnapshotTest ("field names, field order ...") pins order for success payloads only.
- **Evidence:** `Errors.java:23-31` `Map.of("status", e.status(), "message", e.getMessage(), "issues", e.issues(), "locations", e.locations())`; `Errors.java:52` `Map.of("status", status, "message", message, "issues", issues)`; application.yaml has no ORDER_MAP_ENTRIES setting. Probe `$SP/probes/v-be-misc/ErrorOrderProbe.java`, six runs: `{"issues",..."status",..."message",..."locations"}`, `{"status","message","locations","issues"}`, `{"locations","issues","status","message"}`, `{"locations","message","status","issues"}` ...
- **Suggested fix:** Replace both `Map.of` bodies with one typed record in `api`, e.g. `@JsonPropertyOrder({"status", "message", "issues", "locations"}) record ErrorBody(int status, String message, List<String> issues, @JsonInclude(JsonInclude.Include.NON_NULL) List<ArcException.Location> locations)`, used by `arc()` (with locations) and `response()` (locations null, so 400/500 bodies keep exactly their three fields). Field names and values stay the same; only the order becomes the documented one.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/api/PayloadSnapshotTest.java`: exact-string snapshots, through the application's ObjectMapper/MockMvc, of a 404 ArcException body (`{"status":404,"message":"…","issues":["…"],"locations":[]}`) and of the 400 malformed-JSON body (`{"status":400,"message":"Request contains malformed JSON or an invalid value","issues":[]}`); before the fix they fail in most JVM runs (six runs gave four orders).
- **Related:** B9; X132 (same handler: a non-JSON `Accept` turns every error into 500).

<a id="x47"></a>

### X47 · low · cleanup · (Refuted) the non-caching `Engine.execute(…, Definition, …)` path is not dead code
- **Status:** REFUTED — record: `verdicts/v-be-misc.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / engine.execution (Engine, ExecutionPlans) / embedded execution entry point
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/Engine.java:126` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/Engine.java:112-130`, `docs/maintaining.md:52`)
- **Problem:** The finder called the non-caching session mode and the two public `Engine.execute(…, Definition, …)` overloads dead production surface, since no main-code caller uses them. It is true that no main code calls them, but they are a documented, intended embedding entry point (docs/maintaining.md:52: "Embedded `Engine.execute` callers retain request-only behavior because their supplied definitions need not represent immutable database versions"), and the engine tests depend on that behavior.
- **Trigger → consequence:** None. `ValueSwitchTest.evaluatesSelectorOnceAndSkipsLaterCasesAfterAMatch` runs a modified graph (`equals 1 / 0`) and then `choice()` under the same pin "test"@1 on one Engine; through the caching `engine.session(...)`, the second run would reuse the first plan and fail. The production path is still covered: GraphExecution is identical in both modes, only the published-plan lookup differs, and the cache has its own tests (ExecutionPlansTest, and RuleExecutionServiceTest through the service).
- **Evidence:** `Engine.java:126` `// Embedded callers may provide changing definitions under the same ID/version.`; probe `$SP/probes/v-be-misc/CachedPinProbe.java` (same pin test@1, first the modified graph, then the original): `embedded (non-caching) second graph: string` / `caching session second graph: Case Number: Division by zero`.
- **Suggested fix:** None.
- **Regression test:** None new; the engine tests and `ExecutionPlansTest` keep covering both modes.
- **Related:** docs/maintaining.md:52; maintainability lesson "Ambient evaluation state…" (cached published plans keyed by immutable pins).
