### X194 · low · altitude · Error context is decided per call site: runtime bindings/mappings are unlabeled, and NodeValidation.check prefixes deadline errors
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-be-small.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / engine.validation (NodeValidation), engine.execution (GraphExecution, Parameters), error (ArcException) / error messages of static checks, execution preparation and runtime
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/NodeValidation.java:100` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/NodeValidation.java:99`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/error/ArcException.java:97-104`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/GraphExecution.java:223`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/GraphExecution.java:295-298`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/Parameters.java:104-105`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleExecutionService.java:141-145`)
- **Problem:** Two separate defects come from deciding error context at each call site. (1) GraphExecution prefixes Switch selector/case and Transform field errors ("Case Gold: …", "Field cost: …") behind a `recoverable()` gate, but Reference bindings (GraphExecution:223) and source-mapping arguments (Parameters:103-105) get no label at runtime, even though static diagnostics name them ("Apply discount / rate", "rate source / key"); no doc promises runtime binding or mapping labels, so this is a diagnostic-quality and ownership gap, not a contract break. (2) `NodeValidation.check` wraps every error with `withContext` and has no `recoverable()` gate, and `ArcException.withContext` (97-104) has none either; check also runs during execution preparation with the deadline-checking resolver, so a 504 raised while an `@` pin inside a Switch case, Transform field or source mapping is being resolved comes back as "Route / Case Gold: Rule execution deadline exceeded", contradicting docs/api.md:131 and docs/maintaining.md:32. Correction from the record: only DEADLINE can reach this path, because static checks raise no LIMIT; status 504, kind and node location stay correct — only the message and issues text drift.
- **Trigger → consequence:** (1) A Reference "Apply discount" binds `rate = 1 / amount`; running it with amount=0 returns 422 "Division by zero" located at `ref`, with no binding named, while a Transform field fails as "Field cost: Division by zero" and a Switch case as "Case Gold: Division by zero"; a sourced input with mapping `key = 1 / amount` fails as bare "Division by zero" at the Input node. (2) POST /api/preview (or /rules/{id}/execute) with `timeoutMs=300`, for a draft whose Switch "Route" case "Gold" is `@f:1() > 0`: if the rule_versions read outlives the deadline (slow or contended DB), the response is 504 with message "Route / Case Gold: Rule execution deadline exceeded"; the same wait on a Reference pin returns the plain "Rule execution deadline exceeded".
- **Evidence:** `NodeValidation.java:99-100` `} catch (ArcException error) { throw error.withContext(expression.label()); }`; `GraphExecution.java:297` `return error.recoverable() ? error.withContext(context) : error;`; `GraphExecution.java:223` and `Parameters.java:104-105` have no context; `RuleExecutionService.java:141-145` `deadline.check(); … rules.resolveFormula(…); deadline.check();`. Java probe `$SP/probes/v-be-small/VbsContextProbe.java`: "runtime Reference binding: status=422 … message=\"Division by zero\" … nodeId=ref", "runtime Transform field: … \"Field cost: Division by zero\"", "static Reference binding: Apply discount / rate: …", "runtime source mapping: … \"Division by zero\" … nodeId=in", "static source mapping: rate source / key: …"; with a 100 ms deadline and a slow resolver: "Route / Case Gold: Rule execution deadline exceeded" (504, DEADLINE), "Shape / Field cost: Rule execution deadline exceeded", "rate source / key: Rule execution deadline exceeded", plain text for a Reference pin. Live probe `$SP/probes/v-be-small/live_deadline_lock.py` (1.2 s ACCESS EXCLUSIVE lock on rule_versions in arc-review-database-1, `timeoutMs` 300 on :8191): Switch draft → 504 `{"issues":["Route / Case Gold: Rule execution deadline exceeded"],"locations":[{"nodeId":"route","label":"Route"}],"message":"Route / Case Gold: Rule execution deadline exceeded"}`; Reference draft → 504 `{"message":"Rule execution deadline exceeded"}`. `ErrorRecoveryTest.limitsKeepTheirMessageWhileValueErrorsNameTheirFieldOrCase` covers only the runtime path.
- **Suggested fix:** One owner per half. (a) Gate: make `ArcException.withContext` return the error unchanged unless `recoverable()` (only two callers exist: NodeValidation.java:100 and GraphExecution.java:297) and delete `GraphExecution.inContext`; update the method's Javadoc. This intentionally changes the unit contract asserted by `ArcExceptionTest.locationsAndContextKeepTheKind`, which today expects the prefix for LIMIT and DEADLINE too — change that assertion to the documented rule (maintaining.md:32, api.md:131) rather than weakening it. (b) Wording: give `NodeValidation.OwnedExpression` a position part (`Selector`, `Case Gold`, `Field cost`, binding `rate`, mapping `rate source / key`) from which `label()` is built (node label + " / " + position for node expressions; the position alone for Input source mappings), and expose the enumerations to `engine.execution` (for example through `CompiledGraph` or a public accessor on `Validator`), so `GraphExecution.selector/matches/transform/reference` and `Parameters.read` wrap evaluation with `error.withContext(position)` instead of retyping strings. This also resolves X106 (see there for the recommended runtime wording and the docs/api.md "Behavior changes" line).
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/error/ArcExceptionTest.java`: `withContext` prefixes INVALID and NOT_AVAILABLE and returns LIMIT and DEADLINE errors unchanged, with kind, status and locations kept. `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/ErrorRecoveryTest.java`: during preparation, a resolver whose `resolveFormula` throws `ArcException.deadline("Rule execution deadline exceeded")` for `@f:1()` in a Switch case, a Transform field and a source mapping yields 504 with exactly "Rule execution deadline exceeded" at the right node; runtime binding and mapping labels as in X106. `RuleExecutionServiceTest` can reproduce the slow-read case with its deadline-checking resolver.
- **Related:** X106 (same root cause), X159 (registry duplicate of X106); B7 ("`LIMIT` … and `DEADLINE` bypass … Transform/Switch context"; "`withContext` keep[s] the kind"), B11 (one enumeration names every expression's position), maintainability lesson "Ambient evaluation state…" (deadline and budgets are not recoverable: "field/case context keeps their message").

<a id="x196"></a>

### X196 · low · bug · Reference ruleId is never checked as a resource ID: malformed IDs save, and a NUL ID returns 500
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-be-small.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / engine.validation (DefinitionShape), engine (Identifiers) / Reference node pins (graph editor, Code studio `use`, API drafts)
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/DefinitionShape.java:159` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/DefinitionShape.java:159-167`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/InputValidation.java:48-51`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExpressionParser.java:190-191`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/Identifiers.java:30-36`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/NodeValidation.java:108`, `docs/maintaining.md:34`)
- **Problem:** Draft shape never checks a Reference node's `ruleId` for syntax or length, while source pins and `@id:version` calls both use `Identifiers.isResourceId`, so every save, Script build and static check accepts it. Malformed IDs ("Bad ID!", "") and 5,000-character ones save and build with no diagnostic and fail only later as 404 "Published rule version not found"; an ID containing U+0000 reaches SQL and returns 500 from /validate, /diagnostics and /preview. git history shows Reference pins were never checked (not a regression). A draft save of the NUL ID is 422 via StoredText, and publishing any of these drafts fails with 404, so no published version can hold such a pin.
- **Trigger → consequence:** `PUT /api/rules/{id}` with a Reference `{"ruleId":"Bad ID!","version":1}` returns 200 and is stored; the same holds for `"x"*5000` (stored ruleId length 5000) and for `""`. `/api/studio/build` of `use "Bad ID!" version 1;` returns 200 with diagnostics `[]`. /validate and /preview return 404 "Published rule version not found: Bad ID! v1", whereas `@Bad:1()` gets 422 "Formula call needs a valid rule ID" and a source pin gets "Source needs an ID and version". A Reference ruleId `"a\u0000b"` (sent directly, or produced by the Code studio escape `use "a\u0000b" version 1;`) makes /validate, /diagnostics and /preview return 500 "An unexpected server error occurred"; the diagnostics 500 breaks the editor's problem list.
- **Evidence:** `DefinitionShape.java:159-167` checks only `node.version() == null || node.ruleId() != null` and `node.version() == null || node.version() > 0`; compare `InputValidation.java:48-51` and `ExpressionParser.java:190-191`; docs/maintaining.md:34 lists isResourceId only for "rule and source creation, source pins and @id:version calls". Java probe `$SP/probes/v-be-small/VbsPinProbe.java`: "\"Bad ID!\": script build diagnostics=[] … draft shape accepts the Reference pin … Published rule version not found: Bad ID! v1"; "5000-char id: draft shape accepts the Reference pin"; "\"\": draft shape accepts"; "@Bad:1(): Formula call needs a valid rule ID"; "source pin \"Bad ID!\": Source needs an ID and version". Live probe `$SP/probes/v-be-small/live_pin.py` on :8191 (fixtures deleted afterwards): "Bad ID! PUT draft -> 200"; "5000-char PUT draft -> 200 stored ruleId length 5000"; "empty PUT draft -> 200"; "Bad ID! /validate -> 404 … Published rule version not found: Bad ID! v1"; "studio build use \"Bad ID!\" -> 200 diagnostics []"; "NUL PUT draft -> 422 Text cannot contain the NUL character"; "NUL /validate -> 500", "NUL /diagnostics -> 500", "NUL /preview -> 500"; the API log shows DataIntegrityViolationException on `SELECT * FROM rule_versions WHERE rule_id = ? AND version = ?`: invalid byte sequence for encoding "UTF8": 0x00.
- **Suggested fix:** Make draft shape own the syntax of every pin form. In `DefinitionShape.checkNode`, next to "choose a rule before its version", add `require(node.ruleId() == null || Identifiers.isResourceId(node.ruleId()), declaration, node.label() + ": choose a valid rule ID")` (null keeps meaning "no rule chosen yet"; new Reference nodes in the editor have no ruleId, and the editor never writes ""). The pin is then rejected at its node on save, build (at the node's declaration via ScriptLocations), render, validate, diagnostics, preview and execute, and the NUL ID becomes a 422 or a diagnostic before any SQL. Update the `Identifiers.isResourceId` Javadoc and docs/maintaining.md:34 to include Reference pins, and add a docs/api.md "Behavior changes" line: such drafts now fail with 422 at the node instead of saving; a stored draft that holds one fails until the rule is chosen again in the inspector (stored graphs are not rewritten, as in B25).
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/NodeContractsTest.java` (or `ValidatorTest`): shape rejects "Bad ID!", "", an 81-character ID (over `Limits.MAX_RESOURCE_ID_CHARACTERS` = 80) and "a\u0000b" at the Reference node, and accepts a null ruleId with a null version. `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/IdentifiersTest.java` ("one resource-ID policy for rules, sources, pins and Formula calls") gains Reference pins. `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/script/ScriptLocationTest.java`: `use "Bad ID!" version 1;` builds with a diagnostic at that node's declaration. `scripts/smoke.py` (or `ResourceErrorsTest`): /validate and /preview with the NUL ruleId return 422 and /diagnostics returns 200 with the problem at the node, never 500.
- **Related:** X10 (the same NUL-ruleId 500 on validate/diagnostics/preview), B5 (one resource-ID policy), B24 (storage failures must not reach clients as 500), B25.

<a id="x12"></a>

### X12 · low · bug · Comments typed into a node's code editor are silently dropped on Apply; removing `at (…)` moves the node to (0, 0)
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-script.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / engine.script (ArcScript.buildNode, ArcScriptNodeParser) / node code editor (`/studio/node/build`)
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/script/ArcScript.java:135` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/script/ArcScript.java:118-135`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/script/ArcScriptNodeParser.java:113-116`, `frontend/src/features/editor/useNodeExpressionDraft.ts:95`, `docs/studio.md:9`, `docs/architecture.md:178`)
- **Problem:** A `//` comment typed into a node's code editor is parsed into the fragment's notes, but `ArcScript.replaceNode` always returns the containing graph's `definition.notes()` and never looks at `fragment.definition().notes()`, so the comment is discarded with no diagnostic. `useNodeExpressionDraft.apply` then calls `onApply` and closes the dialog, so the text is lost. The same comment written in Code studio is kept as a note ("Comments use `//` and are retained", docs/studio.md:9), so the two editors behave differently for the same text. Minor related effect: the node parser gives a node without `at (…)` the grid position of its index, which is (0, 0) in a one-node fragment, so deleting a fragment's `at (…)` moves the node to (0,0).
- **Trigger → consequence:** Open `</>` on a Formula, add `// TODO: confirm the factor with finance` and click Apply to graph: `/studio/node/build` returns an empty diagnostics list and a definition whose notes are unchanged; the dialog closes and the comment is gone with no warning. Deleting the fragment's `at (300.0, 40.0)` puts the node at `Position[x=0.0, y=0.0]` instead of keeping its position.
- **Evidence:** `ArcScript.java:118-119` "A fragment owns only its node and outgoing edges. Incoming edges and notes belong to the containing graph"; `:130-135` `return new Definition(…, edges, definition.notes());`; `ArcScriptNodeParser.java:113-116` `new Position((nodeIndex % 3) * 300, (nodeIndex / 3) * 170)` when `at` is absent; `useNodeExpressionDraft.ts:95` `if (built.definition && onApply(built.definition)) onClose();`. Live probe `$SP/probes/v-script/live_script.py` on :8191: node/build diagnostics `[]`, notes unchanged, returned and re-rendered source without the TODO; without `at(...)` the position is (0,0); the same comment in `/studio/build` is kept as a note. Finder's Java probe: `diagnostics: []`, `notes after apply: [keep me]`.
- **Suggested fix:** Keep the ownership rule (a fragment owns only its node, outgoing edges and, for Input, parameters; graph notes stay with the containing graph — docs/maintaining.md:54, docs/architecture.md:178) and make the refusal visible. In `ArcScript.replaceNode`, when the fragment has notes, throw a build error located at the first comment, e.g. `parsed.locations().error(new ShapeViolation("Comments belong to the whole graph; add them in Code studio", new ShapeViolation.Note(0)))`; the dialog then stays open with its buffer, because failed builds keep the text. Decision needed only if node comments should be kept instead: appending fragment comments to the graph notes is the alternative; recommended is rejecting, since notes render only at the top of the whole-graph code. For the position: let `ArcScriptNodeParser` leave `position` null when the header has no `at`, apply the existing grid default in `ArcScriptParser` for whole-graph builds, and in `replaceNode` use `original.position()` when the fragment's node has none. Add a docs/studio.md "Behavior changes" line.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/script/NodeEditorTest.java`: `buildNode` with a fragment containing `// TODO` returns no definition and one diagnostic at the comment's line; a fragment without `at (…)` keeps the original position. `DraftRoundTripTest` or `ArcScriptContractTest`: a whole-graph build of a node without `at` still gets the grid position. Optional browser check in `frontend/tests/node-editor.spec.ts`: the dialog stays open and shows the diagnostic.
- **Related:** X14 and X15 (other ARC Script round-trip gaps); maintainability lesson "A node editor could accidentally acquire ownership of the containing graph…" ("graph notes remain intact … Problems in a node fragment point at the fragment's own statements").

<a id="x14"></a>

### X14 · low · bug · Notes with line breaks or padding do not survive graph → code → graph; a 600-line note makes the code unbuildable
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-script.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / engine.script (ArcScriptRenderer, ArcScriptScanner), engine.validation (DefinitionShape.checkNotes) / Code studio round trip of graph notes
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/script/ArcScriptRenderer.java:28` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/script/ArcScriptScanner.java:50-51`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/DefinitionShape.java:94-103`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleService.java:84`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleService.java:98`, `docs/architecture.md:151`)
- **Problem:** The renderer splits each note on `\R` (any line terminator, including CR, VT, form feed, NEL, U+2028/U+2029) and writes one `// ` line per piece, while the scanner ends a comment only at `\n` and `strip()`s it. So a note with a line break comes back as several notes, surrounding whitespace is removed, and a note with many line breaks renders more comments than `Limits.MAX_NOTES` (500) allows. Draft shape checks only note count and length, so API/JSON drafts can hold such notes; this breaks the renderer's contract that building the text gives the same draft back, and docs/architecture.md:151 ("comment text … retained"). Correction from the record: switching to Code view and back without editing does not rebuild; the notes change only on the next build after an edit to the code.
- **Trigger → consequence:** A draft saved through the API with `notes:["first\nsecond"]` (or `"a\rb"`, or `"  padded  "`) rebuilds, after any code edit, as `["first","second"]` / `["a","b"]` / `["padded"]`, so the draft changes although nobody edited the notes. A stored note with 600 line breaks renders to code that will not build ("Too many or oversized comments"). A Code studio comment containing a pasted form feed (`// pasted \f text and   more`) is stored as one note and split into two on the next rebuild, a rare path from inside the UI.
- **Evidence:** `ArcScriptRenderer.java:28` `for (String line : note.split("\\R", -1)) out.append("// ").append(line).append('\n');` versus `ArcScriptScanner.java:51` `comments.add(new Statement(source.substring(start, index).strip(), …))`; `DefinitionShape.checkNotes` checks only count and length. Live probes on :8191: `$SP/probes/v-script/live_roundtrip.py` (fixtures rv-v-script-note-lines and rv-v-script-note-many, deleted afterwards) and `$SP/probes/v-script/live_ff.py` (form feed). Finder probe RoundTrip.java: `"notes":["first\nsecond"]` → `["first","second"]`; CR note → `["a","b"]`; padded → `["padded"]`.
- **Suggested fix:** Decision needed on the note contract. Recommended: a note is one line without surrounding whitespace — the only form an ARC Script comment can carry. Normalize at the write boundary (`RuleService.create`/`update`, before `validator.shape`): split each note on `\R`, `strip()` each line, then apply `Limits.MAX_NOTES`/`MAX_NOTE_CHARACTERS` to the result, so a draft that would render more comments than the parser accepts is rejected with the existing "Too many or oversized comments" at the note (B5: check limits across producer and consumer). Keep the renderer's per-line output. Stored drafts and published versions are not rewritten (notes never affect execution), and their code round trip becomes a documented normalization. Alternative: extend the ARC Script grammar so one note can span lines exactly (parser, renderer and round-trip tests). Document the chosen rule in docs/api.md and docs/studio.md "Behavior changes" and in docs/architecture.md:151.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/script/DraftRoundTripTest.java`: notes that are single trimmed lines render and build back identically. `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleServiceTest.java`: saving `["first\nsecond", "  padded  ", "a\rb"]` stores `["first","second","padded","a","b"]`, and a note with 600 line breaks is rejected with "Too many or oversized comments". `scripts/studio_smoke.py`: graph → code → graph keeps notes identical.
- **Related:** B12 (building the rendered text returns the same draft, up to documented normalizations), B5; X12, X15 (other round-trip gaps).

<a id="x15"></a>

### X15 · low · bug · A draft with duplicate same-handle connections renders to ARC Script that cannot be built
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-script.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / engine.script (ArcScriptNodeParser) / Code studio and node code editor round trip of API-created drafts
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/script/ArcScriptNodeParser.java:153` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/script/ArcScriptNodeParser.java:150-160`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/script/ArcScriptRenderer.java:77-85`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/DefinitionShape.java:250-272`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/GraphValidation.java:134-139`)
- **Problem:** Draft shape requires only unique edge IDs, so a draft saved through the API can hold two connections from the same handle to the same target with different edge IDs; only executable validation rejects "Duplicate connection". The renderer writes both statements, each with its explicit `edge "id"`, but the parser rejects the second with `unique(handle + ":" + target, …)`, so render works and build fails. The node editor also cannot apply that Input node's unchanged fragment. The graph editor cannot create such a draft, and the build error points at the line, so the user can recover.
- **Trigger → consequence:** `PUT /rules/{id}` with edges `e1` and `e2`, both from the Input to `out` on `next`, saves. `/studio/render` writes `next -> "out" edge "e1";` and `next -> "out" edge "e2";`; building that exact text fails with "Duplicate statement: next:out", so the code view of a saved draft does not round-trip. `/studio/node/build` of the unchanged Input fragment returns diagnostics and no definition.
- **Evidence:** `ArcScriptNodeParser.java:153` `unique(handle + ":" + target, statement);`; `DefinitionShape.checkEdges` requires only unique edge IDs; `GraphValidation.java:134-139` rejects duplicates only at validate/publish. Live probes `$SP/probes/v-script/live_roundtrip.py` (fixture rv-v-script-dup-edge) and `$SP/probes/v-script/live_dup_node.py`; finder probe RoundTrip.java: `duplicate connection: BUILD FAILED [Diagnostic[message=Duplicate statement: next:out, line=9, column=3]]` for the renderer's own output.
- **Suggested fix:** Make the parser accept exactly what the renderer writes (B12). In `ArcScriptNodeParser.parseEdge`, apply the `handle:target` uniqueness check only when the statement has no explicit `edge "id"` (two such statements would generate the same `source-handle-target` ID); with explicit IDs, rely on draft shape's unique-edge-ID check, which ScriptLocations already reports at the offending statement, and leave "Duplicate connection" to executable validation at the source node, as for JSON drafts. The renderer always writes `edge "id"`, so every saved draft then round-trips. Alternative (an API compatibility change that needs a docs/api.md "Behavior changes" entry): move the duplicate-connection rule into draft shape so such drafts cannot be saved.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/script/DraftRoundTripTest.java`: a draft with the `e1`/`e2` duplicates renders and builds back to the same two edges, and `Validator.validate` still fails with "Duplicate connection" at the Input node. `NodeEditorTest`: `buildNode` of the unchanged Input fragment succeeds. `ScriptLocationTest`: two identical connection statements without `edge "id"` still fail at the second statement.
- **Related:** B12; X14, X12 (other round-trip gaps).

<a id="x102"></a>

### X102 · medium · bug · Branch analysis passes or fails with "too complex" depending only on node IDs
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-script.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / engine.graph (BranchScopes, GraphTopology, BooleanConditions) / validate, publish, preview and `/api/variables` of branching graphs
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/graph/BranchScopes.java:25` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/graph/BranchScopes.java:17`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/graph/GraphTopology.java:26`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/graph/BooleanConditions.java:26`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/graph/BooleanConditions.java:68`)
- **Problem:** BranchScopes numbers the BDD test variables in topological order (`variableIndex++`), and GraphTopology breaks ties by node ID (`PriorityQueue<String>`), so the variable order — and with it the BDD size — depends on how nodes are named. When the size exceeds BooleanConditions' caps (50,000 branches, 200,000 cached operations), the graph is rejected with "Branch analysis is too complex; split this graph into reusable rules". The same structure is therefore valid or invalid by naming alone, and the editor's random node IDs (`shortId("node-")`) make publishability a matter of chance.
- **Trigger → consequence:** 32 nodes and 71 edges, "approve if any of 14 two-part checks passes" (Input → check_i; check_i.true → confirm_i; confirm_i.true → a shared approve Formula; both false exits → a shared reject Output; no variable written twice). Named `check_NN`/`confirm_NN`, validate, preview and variables all return 422; named `rule_NN_a`/`rule_NN_b`, the same graph is valid and preview returns a result. With the editor's random node IDs, rejection rises from 1 in 60 graphs at 44 nodes to 15/60 at 64 nodes and 51/60 at 82 nodes. A copy/paste or rebuild that regenerates IDs can make a publishable graph unpublishable, or the reverse. (The finder's own example had every branch write the same variable, which would fail at runtime anyway; the verifier's graph avoids that and is the more realistic trigger.)
- **Evidence:** `BranchScopes.java:17` `for (Node node : topology.order())`, `:25` `int test = logic.variable(variableIndex++);`; `GraphTopology.java:26` `Queue<String> ready = new PriorityQueue<>();`; `BooleanConditions.java:26` `if (branches.size() >= 50_000)`. Probes: `$SP/probes/v-script/VsBdd2.java` (grouped vs interleaved IDs, and 60 random `node-%08x` ID assignments per size), `$SP/probes/v-script/VsBdd3.java`, `$SP/probes/v-script/VsBdd.java`, `$SP/probes/v-script/live_bdd.py` (live /studio/build, /validate, /variables, /preview on :8191). Finder probe: `n=14 nodes=44 edges=84 ids=grouped -> REJECTED` vs `ids=interleaved -> valid`.
- **Suggested fix:** Number the BDD variables in a structural order that does not depend on node IDs, and keep GraphTopology's ID tie-break for execution and trace order. In `BranchScopes.analyze`, assign each branching node's test indices from a depth-first preorder that starts at the Input node and follows outgoing edges in `node.handles()` order (edges of one handle in document order), then any nodes the Input cannot reach in topological order; keep computing gates in `topology.order()` with those precomputed indices. A child test then directly follows its parent (check_0, confirm_0, check_1, …), which keeps OR-of-AND structures linear, and renaming nodes can no longer change the verdict. Add a docs/api.md "Behavior changes" line (graphs rejected only because of their node IDs are now accepted) and one sentence to the `BranchScopes` description in docs/maintaining.md.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/graph/GraphPlanTest.java`: the 14-pair graph gives identical `available()` maps with `check_NN`/`confirm_NN` and with `rule_NN_a`/`rule_NN_b` IDs; 60 seeded random `node-%08x` ID assignments of the 39-pair (82-node) graph all validate through `Validator.validate`; a genuinely exponential graph still reports "Branch analysis is too complex; split this graph into reusable rules".
- **Related:** X103 (a too-complex graph falls back to syntax-only diagnostics), X56 (`/variables` then fails for every inspector); B9 (by analogy: an incidental ordering must not decide an observable result).

## Backend: rule deletion, data sources and the HTTP transport

<a id="x09"></a>

### X09 · medium · bug · Rule deletion races a concurrent publish of a new caller, so an immutable version pins a deleted rule
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-del-be.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / rule (RuleService), persistence (JdbcRuleRepository) / rule deletion and publication
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleService.java:122` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleService.java:123`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleService.java:131`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleService.java:108`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleService.java:98`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/persistence/JdbcRuleRepository.java:52`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/persistence/JdbcRuleRepository.java:55`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/persistence/JdbcRuleRepository.java:251`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/persistence/JdbcRuleRepository.java:277`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/persistence/JdbcRuleRepository.java:285`)
- **Problem:** `RuleService.delete` locks only the rule being deleted (`store.lock(id)`, `SELECT … FOR UPDATE`) and then finds callers with an unlocked text scan (`definitionsMentioning`, plain SELECTs over `rules.draft` and `rule_versions.definition`). `publish` locks only the rule being published (:108) and validates its pins with plain SELECTs (`resolveFormula` JOIN at JdbcRuleRepository.java:285, `version` at :251); draft save runs only `validator.shape` (:98). No code locks a dependency, so under READ COMMITTED a caller saved and published between the deletion's scan and its commit is invisible to the scan, while its own validation still sees the callee whose deletion has not committed yet.
- **Trigger → consequence:** Callee A has v1 published and no callers. T1 sends `DELETE /api/rules/A`: lock(A) FOR UPDATE (:122), `callersOf(A)` finds nothing (:123), the DELETEs run (:131, JdbcRuleRepository.java:277-278) and are not committed yet. T2 sends `PUT /api/rules/B` with an Output `@A:1(amount)` → 200, then `POST /api/rules/B/publish`, which locks only B and reads A v1 with plain SELECTs → B v1 inserted, 200. T1 commits with 204. B v1 now fails every execution with 404; once someone creates and publishes a new A v1, B v1 silently runs the new logic. Breaks "pinned references remain immutable" and api.md:99 ("a rule that other rules call is kept"). Needs two users acting on the same rule inside the scan-to-commit window, which grows with stored data because the strpos scan reads every draft and version.
- **Evidence:** Live probe `$SP/probes/v-del-be/race.sh` against :8191; a psql transaction held `SELECT … FROM rule_versions WHERE rule_id='rv-v-del-be-callee' FOR UPDATE; pg_sleep(6)` to pause the deletion after its scan. Output: "save caller during deletion 200", "publish caller during deletion 200" (publishedVersion 1), "delete callee: 204", caller v1 expression "@rv-v-del-be-callee:1(amount)", "GET callee: 404", "execute caller v1: 404 … Published Formula version not found: rv-v-del-be-callee v1"; after re-creating the callee as `amount * 1000` and publishing, "execute caller v1 after ID reuse: result 10000" (the original callee computed amount * 0.9 = 9). e1-wrap reproduced it without an artificial lock (150 rules mentioning the ID, ~55 ms window; `$SP/probes/e1-wrap/race_run.py`).
- **Suggested fix:** Make every write that adds a caller hold a lock that conflicts with the deletion, as a foreign key would. (1) Add `RuleRepository.lockCallees(Collection<String> ruleIds)` → `SELECT id FROM rules WHERE id IN (…) ORDER BY id FOR KEY SHARE` (missing IDs are simply not returned). (2) Call it with `Validator.calledRuleIds(draft)` (the draft-tolerant enumeration proposed under X28) in `publish` after the revision check and before `definitions.validate`, and in `update`/`create` before writing, so the documented "any draft" guarantee also holds. (3) Lock the edited rule with `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` in update/publish (compatible with other writers' `FOR KEY SHARE`, so two drafts that pin each other cannot deadlock) and keep `FOR UPDATE` only for deletion (a separate `lockForDeletion(id)`). If the deletion locks first, the publish waits, then finds the callee gone and fails validation with 404; if the publish locks first, the deletion waits and its scan (a new statement under READ COMMITTED) sees the caller and returns 409. Update the comment at RuleService.java:121, the publish transaction rule in docs/maintaining.md:36 and lesson B26. No API contract change.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleServiceTest.java`: extend `publicationLocksAndValidatesBeforeSnapshotting` to assert the order `lock("example")` → `lockCallees([callee IDs])` → `definitions.validate` → `publish`, and the same lock before the write in `update`/`create`. `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/persistence/JdbcRuleRepositoryTest.java`: the callee statement uses `FOR KEY SHARE` ordered by ID, the edit lock `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, the deletion lock `FOR UPDATE`. Mocks cannot prove locking, so add a real-PostgreSQL race check to `scripts/smoke.py`'s deletion section, modeled on its concurrent-update check: repeatedly run `DELETE callee` concurrently with `PUT caller` (Output `@callee:1(amount)`) plus publish, optionally after saving ~150 drafts that mention the callee ID to widen the window; assert the outcome is never both `204` and a published caller, and that when the publish wins the delete is `409` and caller v1 executes.
- **Related:** B26; docs/review-lessons.md:110 (keep lock → revision check → validation → publication in one transaction); duplicates X24, X40, X73, X104, X128, X143; X28 and X107 (same caller enumeration, `Validator.calledRuleIds`); X11; introduced with rule deletion in b8aa4ff.

<a id="x11"></a>

### X11 · low · bug · Engine.forget evicts a deleted rule's cached plans only in the API process that served the DELETE
- **Status:** PLAUSIBLE — record: `verdicts/v-del-be.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / engine.execution (ExecutionPlans, Engine), rule (RuleService, RuleExecutionService) / rule deletion and the published-plan cache
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleService.java:132` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/ExecutionPlans.java:26`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/ExecutionPlans.java:59`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/ExecutionPlans.java:116`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleExecutionService.java:61`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/Engine.java:51`, `docs/maintaining.md:38`)
- **Problem:** The published-plan cache is a per-JVM field of the singleton Engine and treats `(ruleId, version)` as immutable; deletion ends that, and `afterCommit(() -> engine.forget(id))` edits only the local map. A cached pin runs without any database read (`if (plan == null && cachePublished) plan = cached(pin);`), so another API process on the same database never learns of the deletion. Multi-process deployment is not a shipped or documented configuration (compose.yaml:26 has one `api` service with the fixed host port `"${ARC_API_PORT:-8080}:8080"`, so `--scale api=2` cannot bind; README.md:375 lists production deployment under Next phases), but maintaining.md:38 states the guarantee without a single-process caveat ("so an ID used again never runs a deleted rule's plan"), and lesson B9 mentions replicas.
- **Trigger → consequence:** Processes X and Y share one database and Y has cached plan (A,1). `DELETE /api/rules/A` on X runs `forget` on X only. On Y, `POST /api/rules/A/execute {"version":1}` skips the pointer read (RuleExecutionService.java:61 `request.version() == null ? rules.publishedVersion(id) : request.version()`) and answers 200 with the deleted rule's result instead of 404. After A is re-created and v1 published, `execute {}` on Y reads pointer 1 and runs the old plan until LRU eviction; References and `@A:1()` calls from other rules hit the same stale plan (duplicate X129).
- **Evidence:** ExecutionPlans.java:26 `private final Map<Pin, Entry> published = new LinkedHashMap<>(…)`; :59-67 `synchronized void forget(String ruleId) { generation++; … entries.remove(); }`; Engine.java:51 `this.plans = new ExecutionPlans(validator, json);`. Nothing notifies other processes. Not run with two processes; the duplicate X142's probe `$SP/probes/b1-removed-be/ReplicaProbe.java` (two Engine instances over one resolver) printed "replica A after re-create: new rule v1" / "replica B after re-create: old rule v1".
- **Suggested fix:** Decision needed: whether several API processes may share one database. Recommended now: state the single-process assumption where the guarantee is made (docs/maintaining.md:38, the "Bounds and deployment" section of docs/architecture.md, lesson B26); no code change. If replicas become supported, stop relying on local eviction: give each rule incarnation an identity that changes when an ID is reused, for example a new Flyway migration adding `rules.incarnation UUID NOT NULL DEFAULT gen_random_uuid()` (PostgreSQL 17 in compose), read it with the publication pointer and, for explicit-version and nested pins, with one indexed row read per distinct pin through the per-request `MemoizingRuleResolver`, and add it to `ExecutionPlans.Pin`. A cached plan still skips the JSONB read and decode, but the "cached pins skip the version read" assertion in RuleExecutionServiceTest must then be updated deliberately.
- **Regression test:** Documentation option: none. Incarnation option: `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/ExecutionPlansTest.java` with two plan caches (or Engines) over one resolver, as in ReplicaProbe: after the rule is deleted through one and re-created with a different graph, the other runs the new v1, and a deleted pin fails with 404.
- **Related:** B26, B9; duplicates X129, X142; X09; introduced with rule deletion in b8aa4ff.

<a id="x13"></a>

### X13 · low · bug · Deleting every rule makes the next API start re-seed and republish the sample rules
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-del-be.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / rule (RuleSamples), persistence (JdbcRuleRepository) / sample rule seeding after rule deletion
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleSamples.java:101` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleSamples.java:97`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/persistence/JdbcRuleRepository.java:114`, `README.md:161`, `docs/maintaining.md:38`)
- **Problem:** `RuleSamples.run`, an ApplicationRunner on every start that no setting in application.yaml gates, treats an empty `rules` table as a new database (`if (store.hasRules()) return;` → `SELECT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM rules)`). Since b8aa4ff a user can empty that table with DELETE, and no marker records that seeding already happened. The stated intent is a fresh database (RuleSamples Javadoc, maintaining.md:38 "seeded only into an empty database", RuleSamplesTest `anEmptyDatabaseReceivesThePublishedExamples`); README.md:161 ("only if the `rules` table is empty") was written 2026-09-17 (8daccce), before deletion existed.
- **Trigger → consequence:** The user deletes free-shipping and order-pricing (the callers go first), then apply-discount and any rules of their own, then restarts or redeploys the API (compose restart or bin/dev). `hasRules()==false`, so `store.create` plus `service.publish` run for all three samples: the deleted rules reappear in the library, and `POST /api/rules/apply-discount/execute {"version":1}` returns 200 again. Contradicts api.md:97 ("Afterwards every request for the rule returns `404`") and README.md:161 ("Restarting an initialized workspace preserves existing rules").
- **Evidence:** RuleSamples.java:97-101 `/** Seeds the examples into an empty database only. */ … if (store.hasRules()) return;`; JdbcRuleRepository.java:114-116 `SELECT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM rules)`; `RuleService.delete` can empty the table. Not run live (it would empty and restart the shared stack); the code path is unambiguous.
- **Suggested fix:** Record seeding once instead of inferring it from table contents. Add a new Flyway migration (V3; V1/V2 stay unchanged) that creates a marker table, e.g. `workspace_seeds(name VARCHAR(40) PRIMARY KEY, seeded_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now())`, and inserts `'rule-samples'` when `EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM rules)`, so existing workspaces count as seeded. Replace `RuleRepository.hasRules()` with `claimSampleSeeding()` (`INSERT … VALUES ('rule-samples') ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, true when a row was inserted); `RuleSamples.run` seeds only when the claim succeeds, inside its existing transaction, so a failed seed rolls back the marker and two starting processes cannot both seed. Remove `hasRules` once nothing calls it. Update the RuleSamples Javadoc, README.md:161 ("seeded once, into a new database; deleting every rule does not bring them back"), docs/maintaining.md:38, and add a docs/api.md "Behavior changes" line. Accepted edge: a workspace emptied before the migration runs is seeded one last time.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleSamplesTest.java`: a refused claim (seeded earlier, now empty) creates and publishes nothing (`verifyNoInteractions(service)`); a successful claim publishes apply-discount, order-pricing and free-shipping at revision 1; startup still never loads stored rules. `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/persistence/JdbcRuleRepositoryTest.java`: the claim is an `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` insert whose row count decides the result.
- **Related:** B26; duplicate X75; docs/review-lessons.md:111 (sample seeding uses `EXISTS`, covered by RuleSamplesTest); introduced with rule deletion in b8aa4ff.

<a id="x28"></a>

### X28 · low · bug · Deletion caller check ignores source-mapping @calls in drafts without an Input node
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-del-be.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / engine.validation (NodeValidation, Validator), rule (RuleService) / rule deletion caller check
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/NodeValidation.java:210` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/NodeValidation.java:202`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/Validator.java:68`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleService.java:136`)
- **Problem:** `RuleService.callersOf` → `Validator.draftDependencies` → `NodeValidation.dependencies` adds input source-mapping calls only while visiting a node of kind INPUT (`if (node.kind() == NodeKind.INPUT) for (var mappings : sourceMappings(definition).values()) owned.addAll(mappings);`). That attribution suits validation locations, but the deletion check reuses the same list, so a stored draft without an Input node (allowed for unfinished drafts) does not count as a caller of the rules its source mappings call.
- **Trigger → consequence:** A draft has no Input node (the Input statement removed in Code studio, or saved through the API), and its input `amount` has source country-tax v1 with the mapping key = `$TO_STRING(@a:1(1))`. `DELETE /api/rules/a` returns 204 instead of the documented 409 (api.md:99 covers "an `@id:version` call in any draft"). When the Input node comes back, the draft calls a missing rule; if the ID has been reused, it silently calls the unrelated new rule and can be published that way.
- **Evidence:** Live probe `$SP/probes/v-del-be/callers.py` on :8191. Control (same draft with an Input node): "DELETE callee: 409 … Other rules call this rule: rv-v-del-be-src-caller (draft)". Case (no Input node): "save: 200", "DELETE callee: (204, None)", the caller draft still holds "{'key': '$TO_STRING(@rv-v-del-be-src-callee:1(1))'}", and GET callee returns 404.
- **Suggested fix:** Give the deletion check its own draft-tolerant enumeration instead of the validation dependency list. Add `Validator.calledRuleIds(Definition)` (backed by a package-private `NodeValidation.calledRuleIds`) returning the IDs of every rule a stored draft or version names: each well-formed `@id:version` call in `NodeValidation.expressions(node)` of every node and in every entry of `NodeValidation.sourceMappings(definition)`, whether or not an Input node exists, plus every Reference `ruleId` with or without a version (X107); malformed expressions call nothing, as `wellFormedFormulaCalls` does now. `callersOf` tests `Validator.calledRuleIds(stored.definition()).contains(id)`; `NodeValidation.dependencies` stays unchanged for validation and keeps its Input-node locations. X09's callee locks use the same set. Optionally extend api.md:99 to say drafts without an Input node are covered.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleServiceTest.java`: a `StoredDefinition("pricing", null, …)` without an Input node whose input source maps `key` to `$TO_STRING(@example:1(1))` makes `delete("example")` fail with 409 naming "pricing (draft)", and `repository.delete` is never called; `aRuleThatOnlyMentionsTheDeletedOneDoesNotKeepIt` (malformed `@example:1(`) still passes. `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/ValidatorTest.java`: `calledRuleIds` for drafts with zero, one and two Input nodes. `scripts/smoke.py` deletion section: the same draft saved through the API keeps its callee (409).
- **Related:** B26; B11 (one enumeration of owned expressions, `NodeValidation.expressions`/`sourceMappings`); X107 (same fix); X09; introduced with rule deletion in b8aa4ff, whose caller check reused `NodeValidation.dependencies`.

<a id="x107"></a>

### X107 · low · bug · Deletion caller check skips a Reference that chose the rule without a version
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-del-be.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / engine.validation (NodeValidation, Validator), rule (RuleService) / rule deletion caller check
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/NodeValidation.java:206` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/NodeValidation.java:198`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/Validator.java:108`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/DefinitionShape.java:159`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleService.java:139`)
- **Problem:** `NodeValidation.dependencies` counts a Reference only when it has both `ruleId` and `version` (`Validator.Dependency.version` is an `int`), because it was written for source-contract checks that need a version ("complete Reference pins", NodeValidation.java:198, which predates deletion: c307729). The deletion check reuses it, yet a version-less Reference is a well-formed, documented draft state (DefinitionShape.java:159 "A draft may choose a rule before its version (ARC Script `use "rule-id";`)"; studio.md:252), and api.md:99 says a rule called "through a Reference node … in any draft … is kept". RuleServiceTest only exempts malformed `@example:1(` text, so this is not an intended exclusion.
- **Trigger → consequence:** Checkout's draft has a Reference `{ruleId: "discount", version: null}` (ARC Script `use "discount";`; the inspector always sets both fields, so this comes from Code studio or the API). `DELETE /api/rules/discount` returns 204 rather than 409. The draft's Reference then names a missing rule; after an unrelated rule is created as "discount", the Reference points at it and the version picker offers that rule's versions. No executable or published effect, because a version-less Reference cannot validate.
- **Evidence:** NodeValidation.java:205-207 `for (Node node : definition.nodesOf(NodeKind.REFERENCE)) if (node.ruleId() != null && node.version() != null) dependencies.add(Validator.Dependency.reference(node));`. Live probe `$SP/probes/v-del-be/callers.py` on :8191: control (Reference pinned to v1) "DELETE callee: 409 … rv-v-del-be-ref-caller (draft)"; case (version null) "save: (200, 'ok')", "DELETE callee: (204, None)", caller reference "rv-v-del-be-ref-callee None", GET callee → 404.
- **Suggested fix:** The shared change described under X28: `Validator.calledRuleIds(Definition)` includes every Reference `ruleId` whether or not a version is chosen, and `RuleService.callersOf` uses it, so the delete returns 409 "Other rules call this rule: checkout (draft). …". `Validator.Dependency` and `NodeValidation.dependencies` stay version-complete for validation. Optionally name this case in api.md:99.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleServiceTest.java`: a draft whose Reference is built with `nodeOf(…, "REFERENCE", …).rule("example", null)` makes `delete("example")` fail with 409 naming "checkout (draft)" and never calls `repository.delete`. `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/ValidatorTest.java`: `calledRuleIds` includes the version-less Reference. `scripts/smoke.py` deletion section: a caller whose Reference has `"version": null` keeps the callee (409).
- **Related:** B26; B12 (`use "rule-id";` keeps a rule chosen before its version); X28 (same fix); X09; X196 (Reference `ruleId` never checked as a resource ID); introduced with rule deletion in b8aa4ff.

<a id="x32"></a>

### X32 · medium · bug · JSON codec drops trailing fraction zeros, so UI round trips change results that depend on decimal scale
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-src.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / domain (json.ts codec, numericDefaults.ts) / lossless numbers: lookup source saves, draft defaults, Test panel and playground inputs
- **Location:** `frontend/src/domain/json.ts:285` (+ `frontend/src/domain/json.ts:64`, `frontend/src/domain/json.ts:9`, `frontend/src/domain/numericDefaults.ts:101`, `frontend/src/features/sources/model.ts:42`, `docs/maintaining.md:115`, `docs/maintaining.md:141`)
- **Problem:** `JsonReader.numberValue` keeps a double whenever `sameDecimalValue(token, value)` holds, and `canonicalDecimal` ignores trailing zeros (`digits.replace(/0+$/, "")`), so 10.50, 0.070, 2.50 and 1.0 become 10.5, 0.07, 2.5 and 1; `parseNumericDefault` (numericDefaults.ts:101) uses the same predicate for typed defaults. The engine keeps scale on purpose (application.yaml `use-big-decimal-for-floats: true`; ValueText.java:24-25 "numbers keep their scale", `toPlainString()` at :30), so `$CONCAT`/`$TO_STRING` results change. docs/maintaining.md:115 and json.ts:9 accept "1.0 becomes 1" as spelling, but maintaining.md:141 promises "an unchanged lookup table saves as a new version with every digit", and nothing accepts the effect on results.
- **Trigger → consequence:** (1) A LOOKUP source created through the API has v1 entries `{"US":{"price":10.50,"rate":0.070}}`; the user opens it in Data sources, only renames it and saves: v2 stores `{"price":10.5,"rate":0.07}`, so a rule pinned to v1 returns "$10.50" and the same rule pinned to v2 returns "$10.5". (2) A draft loaded with default 2.50 is re-serialized as 2.5 on the next save, even one that only moves a card, so the next publish changes `$CONCAT`/`$TO_STRING` output. (3) Typing `{"price":2.50}` in the Test panel sends `{"price":2.5}` and the preview shows "$10.5"-style text, while an API client sending the same JSON gets the scaled text.
- **Evidence:** Live preview of `$CONCAT("Price ", price)` on :8191 (`$SP/probes/v-src/preview.sh`): input 2.50 → "Price 2.50", 2.5 → "Price 2.5", default 2.50 → "Price 2.50", input 0.070 → "Price 0.070". Codec, reducer and API probe `$SP/probes/v-src/zz-v-src-codec.spec.ts`: v1 raw `"entries":{"US":{"rate":0.070,"price":10.50}}`, UI buffer `"rate": 0.07, "price": 10.5`; after a rename and PUT through `sourceCandidate` + `stringifyJson`, v2 raw `{"rate":0.07,"price":10.5}`; preview pinned to v1 "$10.50", to v2 "$10.5"; Test-panel body `{"price":2.5}` gives "$10.5" while the raw client gets `"result":"$10.50"`.
- **Suggested fix:** Keep a double only when it reproduces the server's text of the token: the same decimal value and the same number of decimal places (what `BigDecimal.toPlainString()` shows, `max(0, fraction digits − exponent)`); otherwise keep `DecimalNumber(token)`. Implement this once in `domain/json` as a named helper, e.g. `decimalKey(text)` returning the canonical value plus decimal places, computed with BigInt from the token without expanding exponents, and use `decimalKey(token) === decimalKey(JSON.stringify(double))` in `JsonReader.numberValue` and `parseNumericDefault` instead of `sameDecimalValue`; X33's `canonicalJson` reuses the same helper, which also removes the duplicated decimal grammar of X62. `stringifyJson` already writes DecimalNumber tokens verbatim; audit parsed-value checks such as `typeof value === "number"` (e.g. `features/studio/formulaCalls.ts:26` already handles DecimalNumber first). This is an intentional contract change: rewrite json.ts:9-10 and docs/maintaining.md:115 ("decimal places are kept: 2.50 stays 2.50; only exponent spelling may change") and add a docs/studio.md "Behavior changes" (Numbers) entry.
- **Regression test:** `frontend/tests/unit/json.spec.ts`: `2.50`, `0.070`, `1.0` and `-0.0` round-trip unchanged through `parseJson`/`stringifyJson`, while `1e3` may still become `1000`; update the existing "values, not spelling" assertions deliberately. `frontend/tests/unit/numeric-defaults.spec.ts`: `parseNumericDefault("2.50")` keeps `2.50`. `frontend/tests/lossless-numbers.spec.ts` ("Code studio, lookup source and preview round trips"): an API-created lookup with `{"US":{"price":10.50,"rate":0.070}}`, renamed and saved in the UI, stores `10.50` and `0.070` in v2's raw response text, and a Test panel run with `{"price":2.50}` shows the same result text as the raw API call.
- **Related:** B6 (lossless numbers), B17 (`ValueText.text` keeps scale), F27; X33 and X62 (same shared decimal helper); X121 (same codec).

<a id="x33"></a>

### X33 · low · bug · sameDefinition compares DecimalNumber spelling, so a respelled save echo replaces the local draft
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-src.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / domain (graph.ts), features/editor (documentState.ts, usePreviewExecution.ts) / draft save acknowledgement and preview session
- **Location:** `frontend/src/domain/graph.ts:28` (+ `frontend/src/features/editor/documentState.ts:120`, `frontend/src/features/editor/usePreviewExecution.ts:34`)
- **Problem:** `canonicalJson` writes a DecimalNumber as its token (`if (isDecimalNumber(value)) return value.text;`) and a double as `JSON.stringify(value)`, so `sameDefinition` compares numbers by spelling, not decimal value. The server echoes numbers in plain notation, so an exponent-form DecimalNumber comes back respelled, `sameDefinition(localDraft, response.draft)` is false, and `acknowledgeSave` adopts `withNodePositions(response.draft)`: the F1 symptom `sameDefinition` exists to prevent. `sameJsonNumber` already compares by value.
- **Trigger → consequence:** The user types `1.23456789012345678901e5` as a NUMBER default (or an exponent number with more digits than a double holds in an ARRAY/OBJECT default) and saves; the server echoes `123456.789012345678901`. The echo replaces the local draft object and `semanticGraphKey` changes: the Test panel result and trace are discarded, diagnostics and variables are fetched again, and an untouched sample buffer is respelled. Edited Test inputs survive and no data is lost. Narrow trigger: exponent notation beyond double precision; plain tokens and doubles echo identically.
- **Evidence:** documentState.ts:120-122 `draft: sameDefinition(localDraft, response.draft) ? localDraft : withNodePositions(response.draft)`; usePreviewExecution.ts:34-36 keys the result on `JSON.stringify([graphKey, input, trace, timeoutMs])`. Live probe (`$SP/probes/v-src/zz-v-src-codec.spec.ts`, "X33"): parseNumericDefault gives DecimalNumber(1.23456789012345678901e5); PUT echo `"defaultValue":123456.789012345678901`; "sameDefinition false", "sameJsonNumber true", "kept local draft object: false", "semanticGraphKey unchanged: false"; control (default 5) "kept local draft object: true". The fixture rule was deleted.
- **Suggested fix:** In `canonicalJson`, write every JSON number, double or DecimalNumber, through the shared `decimalKey` helper proposed in X32 (canonical value plus decimal places, the form that PostgreSQL's numeric output and `toPlainString` share). Then `1.23456789012345678901e5` equals `123456.789012345678901` and `1e400` equals its 401-digit plain echo, while `2.5` and `2.50` still differ. Update the comment above `sameDefinition` to "key order, explicit nulls and number notation".
- **Regression test:** `frontend/tests/unit/document.spec.ts` ("Document transitions and save echoes"): a save acknowledged with an echo that spells the local DecimalNumber `1.23456789012345678901e5` as `123456.789012345678901` (and `1e400` as its plain digits) keeps the local draft object and its `semanticGraphKey`; an echo that differs in value or scale (2.5 vs 2.50) does not. Optionally add such a default to `frontend/tests/preview-session.spec.ts` ("typed inputs and result survive Save").
- **Related:** F1 (save echo replaced the draft), B6; X32 and X62 (same shared decimal helper).

<a id="x121"></a>

### X121 · low · bug · parseJson lists integer-like object keys first, so displayed results differ in member order from the response
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-src.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / domain (json.ts codec), features/execution (ExecutionResult.tsx, ApiPage.tsx) / Test panel result, trace and playground response display
- **Location:** `frontend/src/domain/json.ts:177` (+ `frontend/src/domain/json.ts:197`, `frontend/src/features/execution/ExecutionResult.tsx:25`, `frontend/src/features/execution/ApiPage.tsx:273`)
- **Problem:** `parseJson` builds plain JS objects (`addEntry(container.value, container.key, value)` into `value: {}`), and JavaScript enumerates array-index-like keys ("10", "2024") first in ascending order. Displayed results, traces and playground responses therefore list members in a different order from the server's response; values are never affected, and this matches the earlier `JSON.parse` behavior. Correction to the finder: the "saved order" consequence is not caused by the frontend, because JSONB normalizes key order, so typed order never survives storage whatever the client sends, and no ARC computation depends on member order.
- **Trigger → consequence:** A Transform with fields "2025", "2024", "b", "10" gets the API response `{"2025":1,"2024":2,"b":3,"10":4}`; the Test panel and playground show `{"10":4,"2024":2,"2025":1,"b":3}`. Cosmetic.
- **Evidence:** The live preview returned `"result":{"2025":1,"2024":2,"b":3,"10":4}`; the codec showed `{"result":{"10":4,"2024":2,"2025":1,"b":3}}`, same as JSON.parse (`$SP/probes/v-src/zz-v-src-codec.spec.ts`, "X121 index-like keys"). Saved order: a live POST of entries `{"US":1,"90210":{…},"10001":2}` came back as `{"US":1,"10001":2,"90210":{"2024":0.06,"2025":0.07}}` in both the create echo and the GET (V2__data_sources.sql:10 `definition JSONB`). Order never matters to values (Functions.java:152; ValueText has no text form for objects).
- **Suggested fix:** Decision needed: accept or preserve display order. Recommended: accept and document it: the json.ts header and docs/maintaining.md:115 state that parsed objects follow JavaScript property order (integer-like keys first, ascending, as with `JSON.parse`) and that stored order is JSONB's. If server order must be shown, change displays only: pretty-print the raw response text with a token-level formatter in `domain/json` (re-indents without building objects) for the playground "Response" pane and the Test panel result, which needs `api/http` to hand those views the response text; do not introduce an ordered-object type into drafts.
- **Regression test:** `frontend/tests/unit/json.spec.ts`: pin the documented order (`Object.keys(parseJson('{"b":1,"10":2}'))` is `["10","b"]`) so a codec change is deliberate. With the display option, `frontend/tests/execution-result-display.spec.ts` asserts the playground shows `{"2025":1,"2024":2,"b":3,"10":4}` in server order.
- **Related:** B6 (displays use the codec); X32, X33 (same codec).

<a id="x34"></a>

### X34 · low · bug · Provider switch replaces declared source parameters with a template; switching back does not restore them
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-src.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/sources (sourceDocument.ts) / Data sources provider switch
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/sources/sourceDocument.ts:226` (+ `frontend/src/features/sources/sourceDocument.ts:210`, `frontend/src/features/sources/SourceConfigurationFields.tsx:51`, `frontend/tests/unit/sources.spec.ts:218`)
- **Problem:** The `provider` reducer case overwrites the shared parameters buffer with a hard-coded one-parameter template (`name: action.kind === "LOOKUP" ? "key" : "customerId"`, `type: "STRING"`, `required: true`, `defaultValue: null`) and keeps no copy of the previous text, while url, timeout, entries and secret headers survive. Correction to the finder: this is not literally an F7 violation, because the parameters buffer is always active (SourceConfigurationFields.tsx:51-57 renders it for both kinds) and installing a template on a forward switch is deliberately tested (tests/unit/sources.spec.ts:218 `.toBe("customerId")`). The defect is the unrecoverable loss of the user's declarations.
- **Trigger → consequence:** A LOOKUP v3 declares `key` as NUMBER, optional, default 1. After switching to HTTP and back to LOOKUP, the buffer holds `key` STRING, required, default null, and the chip shows "v3 · edited". Saving creates v4 with a different lookup contract; the change is visible only in "Source parameters · JSON" and can be undone only by discarding the whole draft.
- **Evidence:** sourceDocument.ts:210-226 (template, then `parameters: stringifyJson(parameters, 2)`). Reducer probe (`$SP/probes/v-src/zz-v-src-codec.spec.ts`, "X34 provider round trip"): before `[{"name":"key","type":"NUMBER","required":false,"defaultValue":1}]`, after `[{"name":"key","type":"STRING","required":true,"defaultValue":null}]`, "entries preserved: true dirty: true".
- **Suggested fix:** Keep the parameters text per provider in the document, as F7 already allows for inactive buffers: add `parametersByKind: Partial<Record<SourceConfig["kind"], string>>` to `SourceDocument`, empty in `openSource` and excluded from `sourceSnapshot`. In the `provider` case, ignore a switch to the current kind, stash `buffers.parameters` under the current kind, and restore the target kind's stash, installing the template only when the target kind has none. A round trip then restores the original text, and the document is clean again when nothing else changed. Keep the per-kind template with the other provider facts (X38's descriptor) rather than in the reducer.
- **Regression test:** `frontend/tests/unit/sources.spec.ts`: LOOKUP → HTTP → LOOKUP restores the exact parameters buffer and `sourceIsDirty` is false; an HTTP source with `customerId`/`region`/`tier` → LOOKUP → HTTP restores all three; the first forward switch still installs `customerId` (line 218). Browser: `frontend/tests/source-editor.spec.ts` (active provider payloads): switching away and back leaves Save disabled for an otherwise unchanged source.
- **Related:** F7 (inactive raw buffers stay available for switching back); X38 (provider-kind facts scattered, no per-kind descriptor).

<a id="x35"></a>

### X35 · medium · bug · A new source draft that takes a pending create's ID adopts the created source, and its next Save overwrites it
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-src.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/sources (sourceDocument.ts, useSourceEditor.ts, SourcesPage.tsx) / Data sources create and save
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/sources/sourceDocument.ts:265` (+ `frontend/src/features/sources/sourceDocument.ts:283`, `frontend/src/features/sources/useSourceEditor.ts:74`, `frontend/src/features/sources/SourcesPage.tsx:48`)
- **Problem:** `save/success` matches the open document only by source ID and "version not newer" (`document.source.id !== action.source.id || document.source.version > action.source.version`); when the selection or request differs, the reopen branch adopts the saved version and baseline for any dirty document with that ID, including a brand-new version-0 draft that never was that source. The pending-save flag is also keyed by ID (`savingIds.includes(document.source.id)`), so the new draft's ID field locks while it matches the pending create's ID. This contradicts docs/maintaining.md:141 ("They cannot select another source or replace newer edits"; only "Reopening the same source" may adopt the revision) and sourceDocument.ts:167 ("never to whichever source is open later").
- **Trigger → consequence:** The user clicks Create source for ID X and the POST is slow. They click New source, which stays enabled, and accept "Discard unsaved source changes?". They enter unrelated content and type X (typing "X-2" locks at "X"). When the create returns, the foreign draft becomes "v1 · edited" with its ID disabled, and "Save new version" sends PUT revision 1, publishing the unrelated content as X v2 where a create would have returned 409.
- **Evidence:** sourceDocument.ts:283-288 `{ ...document, source: { ...document.source, version: action.source.version }, viewedVersion: …, baseline: saved.baseline }`; SourcesPage.tsx:48-54 (New source has no `disabled`). Browser probe on :3191 (`$SP/probes/v-src/zz-v-src-x35.spec.ts`, create response held): "New source enabled during pending create: true", then the dialog "Discard unsaved source changes?"; "Source ID disabled while the create is pending: true"; after release: heading "Other table", chip "v1 · edited", Source ID disabled; Save sent `PUT /api/sources/rv-v-src-x35-… {"name":"Other table","revision":1,…"entries":{"FR":1}}` → 200, and GET v2 → `"name":"Other table","version":2,…"entries":{"FR":1}`. Control: a direct POST with the same ID → 409 "This source ID already exists". Reducer version: "X35 new draft adopts a pending create" in `$SP/probes/v-src/zz-v-src-codec.spec.ts`.
- **Suggested fix:** In `sourceDocumentReducer` `save/success`, adopt a save from another selection or request only when the open document is a stored copy of that source (`document.source.version > 0`, i.e. opened from the server); a version-0 draft keeps its state, so its own Create later receives the server's 409. In `useSourceEditor`, derive a version-0 draft's pending state from its own `document.saving` (selection and request) instead of `savingIds`, so typing through a pending create's ID no longer freezes the ID field; stored sources and the navigation guard keep `savingIds`.
- **Regression test:** `frontend/tests/unit/source-document.spec.ts` (or `frontend/tests/unit/sources.spec.ts`): a version-0 document with the same ID but another selection receives `save/success` for v1 and stays unchanged (version 0, same baseline, still dirty); the reopen case (version 1, other selection) still adopts v2 without losing edits. Browser: `frontend/tests/source-editor.spec.ts` (source editor races), replaying the held-create sequence: the new draft stays "Unsaved", its ID stays editable, and Create sends a POST that shows 409 "This source ID already exists"; no PUT is sent.
- **Related:** F2 (source A overwrote source B); docs/maintaining.md:141.

### X36 · low · bug · Input inspector hides a source mapping for a parameter the pinned version does not declare
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-src2.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/sources (SourceBindingEditor.tsx, sourceBindings.ts) / Input node source mapping inspector
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/sources/SourceBindingEditor.tsx:158` (+ `frontend/src/features/sources/SourceBindingEditor.tsx:116`, `frontend/src/features/sources/sourceBindings.ts:5`)
- **Problem:** The Input inspector's source section renders one `ValueBinding` per parameter of the pinned contract (`config?.parameters.map(...)`) and never lists `source.bindings` keys that contract lacks, while diagnostics flag the Input node for them. Correction to the finder: the mapping can be removed in the graph view through the Node expression dialog (docs/studio.md:250 "The Input node includes the rule's parameters and source mappings"); the form's own escape routes (Caller / default value then re-pick; a round trip through another version, which exists only for multi-version sources) discard other state, because re-picking resets the other mappings, pointer and onError, and MUI Select does not fire onChange when the current version is picked again. This conflicts with F7 ("Hiding a field must not leave an invalid hidden payload") and maintaining.md:149 ("hidden obsolete parameters must not remain in the submitted graph").
- **Trigger → consequence:** A draft pins lookup source v1, which declares only `key`, with bindings `{"key":"\"US\"","region":"\"EU\""}` (typed in Code studio's `source price = {...}` or sent through the API). Draft save returns 201 because shape does not check contracts; `/api/diagnostics` returns "Unknown source parameter: region" at node `input`. The graph inspector shows only "Source key", nothing in the form names `region`, and the node turns red for a mapping the form never displays.
- **Evidence:** Curl on :8191: create 201; diagnostics `[{'message': 'Unknown source parameter: region', 'locations': [{… 'nodeId': 'input'}]}]`; `/studio/node/render` includes `"bindings":{"key":"\"US\"","region":"\"EU\""}`. Browser probe on :3191 (`$SP/probes/v-src2/zz-v-src2-probe.spec.ts`, fixture `$SP/probes/v-src2/rule36.json`): "X36 FORM 'Source region' controls: 0", "FORM text contains 'region': false", the input card class contains `node-error`; the Node expression dialog showed `["Unknown source parameter: region"]`, and after deleting `region` there, Apply and Save, the stored bindings were `{"key":"\"US\""}`.
- **Suggested fix:** Add one shared "undeclared bindings" capability for both binding editors (also X58), modeled on B25's `inspector/UnusedProperties.tsx`: a pure `undeclaredBindings(bindings, declaredNames)` in `domain/valueBinding.ts` beside `withBinding` (own keys only, in stored order, prototype-safe per F22; `bindSourceVersion` can reuse it), and a presentational `features/expressions/UndeclaredBindings.tsx` error Alert that names the keys (e.g. "Also maps region, which v1 does not declare. Validation and publishing reject it until it is removed.") with a **Remove** action that read-only views omit. In SourceBindingEditor, render it once the pinned detail (`config`) has loaded, with the declared names from `config.parameters`; Remove calls `onChange` with those keys removed via `withBinding(…, undefined)`, keeping the other mappings, pointer and onError.
- **Regression test:** `frontend/tests/unit/value-binding.spec.ts`: `undeclaredBindings` for ordinary names and for `constructor`/`__proto__`. Browser: `frontend/tests/frontend-refactor.spec.ts` ("Pinned source parameter mapping") or `frontend/tests/source-binding-names.spec.ts`: the draft above shows an alert naming `region`; Remove + Save stores `{"key":"\"US\""}`, diagnostics no longer report "Unknown source parameter: region", pointer and onError are unchanged; a read-only version shows the alert without Remove.
- **Related:** F7, F22, B25 (UnusedProperties and its Remove); X58 (same root cause and shared fix).

### X58 · low · bug · Reference inspector hides a binding for a parameter the pinned callee does not declare
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-src2.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/editor/inspector (ReferenceFields.tsx) / Reference node inspector
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/editor/inspector/ReferenceFields.tsx:152` (+ `frontend/src/features/editor/inspector/ReferenceFields.tsx:137`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/NodeValidation.java:46`)
- **Problem:** The Reference inspector renders parameter cards only for the pinned callee's declared inputs (`child.definition.inputs.map(...)`), and nothing iterates `node.bindings`, so a stored binding for an undeclared parameter never appears while diagnostics flag the node. Correction to the finder: the binding can be removed through the inspector's Node expression button, which shows `bind ghost = …;`. The form's only way to clear it is re-pinning another version or rule, which resets all bindings, and choosing the same version does nothing (`version.version !== node.version` guard, lines 137-140).
- **Trigger → consequence:** A parent draft has a REFERENCE to callee v1 (input `a`) with bindings `{a:"amount", ghost:"3"}`, after editing `use … version N` in Code studio, or through the API or an import. Save returns 201, and diagnostics report "Reuse: unknown parameter ghost" at node `reuse`. The inspector shows only "Parameter a", so the flagged problem has no matching card.
- **Evidence:** NodeValidation.java:46-49 `require(referenceParameters.contains(expression.bindingName()), node.label() + ": unknown parameter " + …)`. Curl on :8191: create 201; diagnostics `[{'message': 'Reuse: unknown parameter ghost', … 'nodeId': 'reuse'}]`; node render `bind a = amount;\n  bind ghost = 3;`. Browser probe on :3191 (`$SP/probes/v-src2/zz-v-src2-probe.spec.ts`): `X58 FORM parameter groups: ["Parameter a"]`, `FORM text contains 'ghost': false`; after removing the line in Node expression, Apply and Save, the stored bindings were `{"a":"amount"}`.
- **Suggested fix:** Reuse the shared `undeclaredBindings` helper and `UndeclaredBindings` alert from X36: in ReferenceFields, once `child` has loaded, render the alert in "Parameters for Rule" with the declared names from `child.definition.inputs`; Remove patches `{ bindings }` with those keys removed via `withBinding(node.bindings, name, undefined)`, keeping the declared bindings.
- **Regression test:** Browser: `frontend/tests/value-binding-editing.spec.ts` (Reference parameters) or `frontend/tests/reference-picker.spec.ts`: the parent above shows an alert naming `ghost`; Remove + Save stores `{"a":"amount"}` and diagnostics no longer report "Reuse: unknown parameter ghost"; a published version shows the alert without Remove. Unit coverage of the helper is shared with X36.
- **Related:** F7 pattern, F22, B25; X36 (same root cause and shared fix).

### X37 · low · bug · A second Input node cannot be deleted in the graph view
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-src2.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / domain (nodeKinds.ts, graph.ts) / graph node deletion
- **Location:** `frontend/src/domain/nodeKinds.ts:102` (+ `frontend/src/domain/graph.ts:108`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/GraphValidation.java:115`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/script/ArcScript.java:107`, `docs/studio.md:244`)
- **Problem:** Every INPUT node is non-removable (`removable: false`, read by `canRemoveGraphNode` and every delete control), which assumes a draft has one Input. ARC Script build and draft save both accept a draft with a second Input node (DefinitionShape does not count Input nodes), validation then requires exactly one (GraphValidation.java:115, architecture.md:90), and Node expression cannot delete nodes, so Code studio is the only repair. docs/studio.md:244 ("The Input node can be renamed but cannot be deleted") assumes a single Input.
- **Trigger → consequence:** The user writes a second `node "input2" INPUT …` in Code studio (or sends it through the API): `/studio/build` returns `diagnostics: []`, draft save returns 201, and diagnostics report "A rule must have exactly one Input node" on node `input`. In the graph view there is no "Delete node" button, the context-menu Delete is disabled, and Delete/Backspace leave input2 in place; Node expression refuses a fragment without the node: "Keep exactly this node, with the same ID and type. Use Code studio to edit the whole graph." (ArcScript.java:107-108).
- **Evidence:** graph.ts:108 `!!node && nodeKinds[node.type].removable && definition.nodes.length > 1`. Curl on :8191: build `diagnostics []`, nodes `[('input','INPUT'),('input2','INPUT'),('out','OUTPUT')]`, create 201, diagnostics at `input`. Browser probe on :3191 (`$SP/probes/v-src2/zz-v-src2-probe.spec.ts`): "inspector Delete node buttons: 0", "context menu Delete disabled: true", "input2 card still present after Delete/Backspace: 1", the input card class contains `node-error`.
- **Suggested fix:** Keep only the graph's entry Input non-removable. The entry is the first INPUT in document order, as the server's `Definition.inputNode()` defines it. State the fact in the descriptor (for example INPUT `removable: "extra"`, meaning only an Input after the draft's first one, with other kinds `true`) and let `canRemoveGraphNode` apply it together with the one-node minimum; every delete path (Inspector.tsx:114, GraphCanvas.tsx:105, useGraphCommands.ts:55) already goes through it. Update docs/studio.md:244 ("the first Input node cannot be deleted; an extra one can"). Decision needed: whether draft shape and ARC Script build should also reject a second Input (recommended, as B25 did for unused properties: 422 at the second Input's node or statement, with a docs/api.md "Behavior changes" entry). The frontend change is needed either way, because stored drafts may already hold two Inputs.
- **Regression test:** `frontend/tests/unit/graph-nodes.spec.ts` ("result kinds and deletion"): `canRemoveGraphNode` is true for `input2`, false for the first Input and for a lone node; `frontend/tests/unit/node-kinds.spec.ts` covers the descriptor value. Browser: `frontend/tests/node-context-menu.spec.ts` or `frontend/tests/graph-authoring.spec.ts`: an API draft with two Inputs enables context-menu Delete on input2, which removes it and its edges, and the Input error clears after saving. With the backend option: `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/NodeContractsTest.java` and `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/script/ScriptLocationTest.java`.
- **Related:** F15 (one-node minimum; drafts without an Input); docs/review-lessons.md:106 (frontend node-kind facts, "Input cannot be deleted" once restated four times); B25.
