### X39 · low · bug · Data sources catalog error alert cannot be dismissed and offers no retry
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-src2.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/sources (useSourceEditor.ts, SourcesPage.tsx) / Data sources list and the embedded source manager
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/sources/useSourceEditor.ts:286` (+ `frontend/src/features/sources/useSourceEditor.ts:303`, `frontend/src/features/sources/SourcesPage.tsx:108`)
- **Problem:** The Data sources error Alert shows `document?.error || listError || catalog.error`, but its close button (`dismissError`) clears only the document error and `listError`. `catalog.error` comes from the `usePagedResource` read of the source summaries and changes only when that read runs again, so a catalog failure shows an alert that × cannot dismiss, and nothing offers a retry.
- **Trigger → consequence:** `/api/source-summaries` fails (500 or a network drop); the list empties (useAsyncResource returns `data: initial` on error) and the Alert shows the message. Clicking × dispatches `error/clear` and `setListError("")`, neither of which touches `catalog.error`, so the alert stays. Recovery needs a search change, a save that bumps catalogRevision, or leaving and remounting the page. The embedded source manager, which reuses SourcesPage, behaves the same.
- **Evidence:** useSourceEditor.ts:303-306 `dismissError: () => { dispatch({ type: "error/clear" }); setListError(""); }`; SourcesPage.tsx:108-112 `<Alert severity="error" onClose={editor.dismissError}>`. Browser probe on :3191 (`$SP/probes/v-src2/zz-v-src2-probe.spec.ts`, route answering 500 "probe catalog failure"): `alert still visible after clicking Close: true "probe catalog failure"`, `retry buttons: 0`.
- **Suggested fix:** Separate the two kinds of error in `useSourceEditor`: `error` becomes `document?.error || listError` (dismissible as now), and the hook also returns `catalogError: catalog.error` and `retryCatalog()`, which bumps a counter passed as `usePagedResource`'s `refresh` option (reloads the current page without resetting the offset). SourcesPage renders `catalogError` in its own Alert with a **Retry** action and no close button, like the existing `versionsError` alert.
- **Regression test:** Browser: `frontend/tests/source-editor.spec.ts`: route `/api/source-summaries` to 500 once; the catalog alert shows Retry and no close button; Retry issues the read again, the list appears and the alert disappears; a document save error on the same page is still dismissible with ×.
- **Related:** F5 (explicit failed read states with retry).

### X126 · low · bug · Global Snackbar closes on any click outside it, so error notices vanish at once
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-src2.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / app shell (App.tsx) / global notices
- **Location:** `frontend/src/App.tsx:199` (+ `frontend/src/app/useCodeStudioTarget.ts:56`, `frontend/src/features/execution/usePublishedExecution.ts:164`)
- **Problem:** `<Snackbar open={!!notice} autoHideDuration={4000} onClose={() => setNotice("")} …/>` ignores MUI's `reason`, and MUI calls `onClose(event, 'clickaway')` for any click outside the notice (MUI 9.4.0 useSnackbar.js:61-62). The notice therefore closes immediately instead of after the intended 4 s, including the error notices "Could not open Code studio: …" and "Clipboard unavailable. Select and copy the example below."
- **Trigger → consequence:** A notice is showing (for example "Could not open Code studio: <error>" after a failed rule-summaries lookup, or "Draft saved"). The user clicks anywhere else, such as the page heading or the canvas, about 0.3 s later, and the notice disappears at once; without a click it stays for the full 4 s. Cosmetic, because notices are transient either way.
- **Evidence:** Browser probe on :3191 (`$SP/probes/v-src2/zz-v-src2-probe.spec.ts`, control `$SP/probes/v-src2/zz-v-src2-control.spec.ts`): the notice was gone 558 ms after it appeared, following a click on `main` (autoHide 4000); control: `visible after 2.5 s without clicking: true`, then `after a click on the heading, snackbar count: 0`.
- **Suggested fix:** `onClose={(_event, reason) => { if (reason !== "clickaway") setNotice(""); }}` (MUI's documented pattern), so only the timeout or Escape closes a notice. Optionally keep error notices until dismissed by passing a severity with `notify` and rendering an `Alert` with a close action inside the Snackbar.
- **Regression test:** Browser, in `frontend/tests/app-resilience.spec.ts` or a new `frontend/tests/notices.spec.ts`: show a notice (Create rule → "Rule created. Make it yours.", or a routed failure of the Code studio target lookup), click the page heading, assert the notice is still visible about 1 s later and gone after the 4 s timeout.
- **Related:** —

### X25 · medium · bug · HTTP source validation accepts raw non-ASCII URLs, sent as Latin-1 bytes or '?', and ports above 65535
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-http.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / source.http (HttpDestinationPolicy, HttpSource) / HTTP data sources: URL validation and the request line
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/source/http/HttpDestinationPolicy.java:56` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/source/http/HttpDestinationPolicy.java:60`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/source/http/HttpSource.java:100`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/source/http/HttpSource.java:162`, `frontend/src/features/sources/SourceConfigurationFields.tsx:37`)
- **Problem:** `validate()` parses with `URI.create(definition.url())` and then checks only the scheme, a non-blank host, userinfo, fragment and length ≤ 2000; `java.net.URI` accepts raw non-ASCII characters in the path and query and any numeric port. The transport builds the request from that URI (`new HttpGet(…)`, rebuilt from `configured.toString()` at :162-164), which sends each Latin-1 character as one raw byte and every other non-ASCII character as a literal '?'. The configured path and query are therefore silently changed, contradicting studio.md:209 ("Query text already in the configured URL is sent exactly as written") and maintaining.md:67 / B21 ("byte for byte"), and a port above 65535 saves but can never connect.
- **Trigger → consequence:** A user types `https://api.example.com/cities/Zürich?q=東京` in the source form, which sends `event.target.value` unchanged (SourceConfigurationFields.tsx:37; `sourceSaveProblem` checks only the ID and timeout). The save returns 200, but Test and every execution send `GET /cities/Z<0xFC>rich?q=??`: an invalid UTF-8 path and the query value "??", while appended parameters are sent as `%E6%9D%B1%E4%BA%AC`. Because a character above U+00FF becomes '?', a path such as `/users/東京/orders` reaches the provider as path `/users/` plus query `?/orders`: a different resource, with no error. Separately, `https://api.example.com:99999/x` saves with 200, and every Test/execution returns 422 "HTTP source failed: destination unavailable, blocked, timed out, or response is not valid JSON". Port 0 is also accepted, and the request still reached an HTTP server (example.com answered 403; HttpClient likely treats port 0 as unset and uses 80).
- **Evidence:** Probe `$SP/probes/v-http/NonAsciiProbe.java` (public HttpSource with a raw-socket server): "validate: ok", then "REQUEST LINE bytes: GET /cities/Z<FC>rich?q=?? HTTP/1.1" with no parameters and "GET /cities/Z<FC>rich?q=??&city=%E6%9D%B1%E4%BA%AC HTTP/1.1" with one; the pre-encoded URL is sent intact; "http://example.com:99999/x -> validate ok / fetch: HTTP source failed: …", and the same for 65536. Live on :8191: POST /api/sources with the Zürich/東京 URL → 200 (URL stored verbatim); with `:99999` → 200; POST /api/sources/rv-v-http-port/test → 422 with the generic message.
- **Suggested fix:** Reject in `HttpDestinationPolicy.validate` what cannot be sent as written: (a) raw non-ASCII, `!uri.toString().equals(uri.toASCIIString())` → 422 such as "Percent-encode non-ASCII characters in the URL as UTF-8 (Zürich → Z%C3%BCrich)"; (b) an explicit port outside 1–65535, `uri.getPort() != -1 && (uri.getPort() < 1 || uri.getPort() > 65535)` → 422 "Use a port from 1 to 65535". Do not silently send `toASCIIString()`: that rewrites the configured text and breaks the byte-for-byte rule. `HttpSource.fetch` calls `validate` on every read, so stored versions with such URLs then fail Test and execution with that 422 (or use their DEFAULT fallback) instead of requesting a different resource; list this in docs/api.md "Behavior changes" (Data sources) and mention percent-encoding in docs/studio.md's HTTP section. Optionally mirror both checks in `sourceSaveProblem` so the form blocks Save (F34).
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/source/http/HttpDestinationPolicyTest.java`, beside `rejectsUnsupportedUrlForms`: the Zürich/東京 URL and ports `0`, `65536` and `99999` are rejected with the new messages; `https://api.example.com/cities/Z%C3%BCrich?q=%E6%9D%B1%E4%BA%AC`, `:1` and `:65535` are accepted. `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/source/http/HttpSourceTest.java`: the percent-encoded URL reaches a local server byte for byte, with and without an appended parameter. `scripts/smoke.py`: POST /api/sources with the raw URL → 422.
- **Related:** B21 (configured query byte for byte), B5 (distinguish source characters from encoded HTTP bytes), F34 (source fields accepted what the server rejects).

<a id="x26"></a>

### X26 · low · bug · Blocking DNS lookup is not cancelled by the source timeout or the execution deadline (documented limitation)
- **Status:** REFUTED — record: `verdicts/v-http.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / source.http (HttpDestinationPolicy, HttpSource) / HTTP data sources: timeouts and deadlines
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/source/http/HttpDestinationPolicy.java:96` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/source/http/HttpDestinationPolicy.java:44`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/source/SourceAdapter.java:16`, `docs/maintaining.md:58`, `docs/architecture.md:107`)
- **Problem:** The mechanism is real: httpclient5 5.5.2 `DefaultHttpClientConnectionOperator.connect` calls `DnsResolver.resolve(String,int)` synchronously, which reaches `lookup.addresses(host)` → `InetAddress.getAllByName`, and `request.cancel()` cannot interrupt it. The project documents that the deadline does not interrupt DNS calls, so this is a stated limitation, not a defect. At most, SourceAdapter.java:16-18 and maintaining.md:58 ("A provider that blocks on IO must stop when the deadline expires") are worded more broadly than this documented exception, which is a doc-wording nit.
- **Trigger → consequence:** When a host's DNS server stalls, neither the source timeout nor the execution deadline cuts the lookup short; the request returns 504 or the fallback only after the lookup ends (finder's probe: a lookup sleeping 3 s with timeoutMs 200 and deadline 1000 → "Rule execution deadline exceeded" after 3051 ms). No crash or data loss beyond the stated limitation was shown.
- **Evidence:** docs/architecture.md:107: "A shared `timeoutMs` deadline … cancels active HTTP requests, and is checked at computation/IO boundaries. It does not forcibly interrupt arbitrary database/DNS calls or JVM pauses." docs/reviews/2026-09-25-storage-execution-exploration.md:138 says the same. javap of httpclient5-5.5.2 `DefaultHttpClientConnectionOperator` shows `invokeinterface DnsResolver.resolve:(Ljava/lang/String;I)Ljava/util/List;` inside connect. Finder probe: `$SP/probes/a4-source-db/SlowDnsProbe.java`.
- **Suggested fix:** No code change required. Optional wording fix: add to SourceAdapter.java:16-18 and docs/maintaining.md:58 "except DNS resolution, which the JVM resolver cannot interrupt (see architecture.md)". If the limitation should ever be lifted (Decision needed; not recommended now), resolve in `HttpDestinationPolicy.resolve` on a small bounded executor and wait at most the request's remaining timeout (`Future.get`), accepting that the resolver thread itself stays blocked.
- **Regression test:** None for the documented behavior. With the optional resolver change: `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/source/http/HttpSourceTest.java` with a `HostLookup` that sleeps 3 s and timeoutMs 200: the fetch fails, or falls back, within about the timeout.
- **Related:** docs/review-lessons.md:113 (blocking providers stop their IO when the execution deadline expires).

<a id="x31"></a>

### X31 · low · convention · Source providers accept configuration fields they do not use (HTTP `entries`, LOOKUP `url`)
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-http.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / source.http (HttpSourceAdapter), source.lookup (LookupSourceAdapter), source (SourceValidator) / data source configuration validation
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/source/http/HttpSourceAdapter.java:29` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/source/lookup/LookupSourceAdapter.java:25`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/source/SourceValidator.java:44`, `docs/maintaining.md:60`, `docs/openapi.yaml:824`)
- **Problem:** maintaining.md:60 and lesson B22 state "A provider rejects configuration fields it does not use", but only LOOKUP secret headers are rejected. `HttpSourceAdapter.validate` checks `http.validate(definition)` and the timeout range and never looks at `entries` (no `Expressions.bounded`, no 1,000-entry cap, although openapi.yaml:824 declares `maxProperties: 1000` for every kind); `LookupSourceAdapter.validate` checks parameters, `secretHeaders` and the entries count/bounds and never looks at `url`. Correction to the finder: LOOKUP's negative `timeoutMs` is not a violation, because the timeout range is HTTP-only by design (studio.md:231 "`timeoutMs` (HTTP: 100–10,000)"; LookupSourceAdapterTest:106-110 asserts that a LOOKUP with timeoutMs 0 validates; the UI (model.ts:116) and the V2 seed send 3000).
- **Trigger → consequence:** An API client saves an HTTP source with `entries` of any size up to the 1 MiB body limit, including numbers beyond the decimal limits, or a LOOKUP with `url: "file:///etc/passwd"`. Both return 200 and are stored as immutable versions; GET returns them, and `SourceDefinition.detached()` (SourceExecutionService.java:68 → `Frozen.values`) deep-copies the unused entries into every execution session that pins the version. No functional failure, since nothing reads these fields, and the UI never sends them (`sourceCandidate` strips `url` for LOOKUP and `entries` for HTTP). Extension X227: HTTP entries `{"a":1e5000}` pass validation, and the in-transaction read-back then fails Jackson's number-length limit, so the write rolls back with 500 instead of 422.
- **Evidence:** Live on :8191: HTTP with `"entries":{"unused":{"deep":[1,2,3],"big":1e400}}` → 200, stored as a 401-digit integer (a LOOKUP with the same number fails `Expressions.bounded`); LOOKUP with `"url":"file:///etc/passwd","timeoutMs":-5` → 200, and its Test → 200 `{"result":1}`.
- **Suggested fix:** Make "which optional fields a provider uses" one declared fact rather than a check each adapter must remember: add `Set<SourceDefinition.Field> fields()` to `SourceAdapter` (HTTP: URL, SECRET_HEADERS; LOOKUP: ENTRIES) and have `SourceValidator.validate` reject any other field that is set (non-null and non-empty) before calling `adapter.validate`, with messages built from the adapter's noun: keep the existing "Lookup tables do not use secret headers" and add "HTTP sources do not use lookup entries" and "Lookup tables do not use a URL". Remove LookupSourceAdapter's hand-written secret-header check; `timeoutMs` (a primitive) stays outside this rule. The minimal alternative is one local check in each adapter. Stored versions are unaffected, because this runs on save and HTTP's fetch-time `validate` checks only the URL, allowlist and secret headers. Document it in docs/api.md "Behavior changes" (Data sources: 422 for HTTP `entries` and LOOKUP `url`; X227's 500 becomes 422), mark `url` HTTP-only and `entries` LOOKUP-only in docs/openapi.yaml `SourceConfig`, and name `fields()` in maintaining.md:60.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/source/SourceValidatorTest.java` and `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/source/SourceConfigurationConsistencyTest.java`: HTTP with `entries` `{"unused":{"deep":[1,2,3]}}` or `{"a":1e5000}` → 422 "HTTP sources do not use lookup entries" (never 500); LOOKUP with `url` "file:///etc/passwd" → 422; HTTP with `entries` null or `{}` and LOOKUP with `url` null or "" still validate; LOOKUP with timeoutMs 0 still validates (LookupSourceAdapterTest:106-110); the LOOKUP secret-header message is unchanged. `scripts/smoke.py`: both payloads through POST /api/sources → 422.
- **Related:** B22; X227 (HTTP entries `{"a":1e5000}` → 500; same fix); X193 (duplicate from the altitude review: providers do not declare the fields they use); X27 (B22 class: BigInteger escapes `Expressions.bounded`); X38 (frontend provider-kind facts).

<a id="x17"></a>

### X17 · low · bug · nginx caps bodies at exactly Java's 1 MiB, so the web origin returns an HTML 413 without CORS instead of the JSON 413
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-http.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Infra / frontend/nginx.conf (web server, port 3080) / request body limit on the web origin
- **Location:** `frontend/nginx.conf:6` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/api/RequestLimitFilter.java:28`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/api/RequestLimitFilter.java:52`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/api/RequestLimitFilter.java:55`, `frontend/src/api/http.ts:49`, `docs/architecture.md:142`)
- **Problem:** `client_max_body_size 1m;` equals `RequestLimitFilter.MAX_BODY_BYTES = 1024 * 1024`, so no body can pass nginx and then be rejected by Java: on the web origin every oversized request gets nginx's HTML 413 without Access-Control-Allow-Origin, never the documented JSON 413 "Request body exceeds 1 MiB" that the filter writes with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`. The equal caps are documented (architecture.md:142; RequestLimitFilter.java:18 "same bound … as on the Nginx proxy"), but the HTML/no-CORS consequence is not. The project already raised `proxy_read_timeout` so that the API's JSON 504 wins over nginx's HTML one (api.md "Web server" section); the body cap does not follow that rule.
- **Trigger → consequence:** In the Compose deployment (UI on the nginx port), saving a draft, a LOOKUP source or a Code studio build larger than 1 MiB shows "Request failed (413)", because http.ts:49-52 falls back when the error body is not JSON and nothing in frontend/src handles 413; under Vite dev the same save shows "Request body exceeds 1 MiB". The copyable endpoint (`ruleApi.executeUrl`, built from `window.location.origin`: rules.ts:72, TestPanel.tsx:110, usePublishedExecution.ts:154) points external clients at nginx, so a cross-origin browser client posting more than 1 MiB gets an unreadable, CORS-blocked response instead of a readable 413. docs/api.md:3 names the proxied base URL and api.md:114/:154 describe the 413 message; smoke.py:79/:104 test 413 only on the API port.
- **Evidence:** Live 1,048,577-byte POST /api/rules with `Origin: http://example.test`: :3191 → "HTTP/1.1 413 Request Entity Too Large", "Content-Type: text/html", the nginx/1.28.3 HTML page, no Access-Control-Allow-Origin; :8191 → 413, "Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *", `{"status":413,"message":"Request body exceeds 1 MiB","issues":[]}`. An exactly 1,048,576-byte body passes both and reaches the controller (422). Body fixtures: `$SP/probes/a10-infra/body-1mib-plus1.json`, `$SP/probes/a10-infra/body-1mib.json`.
- **Suggested fix:** Make nginx's own 413 identical to the API's, since nginx should still stop oversized bodies before buffering them: at server level in nginx.conf add `error_page 413 = @request_too_large;` and a named location that returns 413 with `default_type application/json`, `add_header Access-Control-Allow-Origin * always;` and the body `{"status":413,"message":"Request body exceeds 1 MiB","issues":[]}`, with a comment that it mirrors `RequestLimitFilter.rejectOversized`. The alternative, raising the `/api/` cap above 1 MiB so Java answers (the `proxy_read_timeout` precedent), still returns HTML above the raised cap. Optionally, as defense in depth, `requestFailure` in `frontend/src/api/http.ts` maps a non-JSON 413 to "Request body exceeds 1 MiB". Document it in the docs/api.md "Web server (port 3080)" behavior changes and docs/architecture.md:142, and verify against the built image, not Vite.
- **Regression test:** `scripts/smoke.py`: when `ARC_WEB_URL` is set, POST 1,048,577 bytes to `$ARC_WEB_URL/api/rules` with an `Origin` header and assert 413, `Content-Type: application/json`, the message "Request body exceeds 1 MiB" and `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`; an exactly 1,048,576-byte body still reaches the API (422). With the http.ts fallback: `frontend/tests/unit/http.spec.ts` asserts that an HTML 413 yields that message.
- **Related:** B23 (Java body limit for every method and path spelling); the nginx 504 precedent in docs/api.md "Web server (port 3080)".

## Backend and infrastructure: duplication, efficiency, CI and tooling

### X154 · low · cleanup · Collection-function and decimal-aggregate names are listed once but dispatched by string switches that silently fall through
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-clean-be1.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / engine.expression / Collection functions (`$MAP`, `$FILTER`, `$ALL`, `$ANY`, `$REDUCE`) and decimal aggregates (`$SUM`, `$MIN`, `$MAX`, `$AVG`, `$AVERAGE`, `$COUNT`, `$MUL`)
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExpressionRuntime.java:260` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExpressionRuntime.java:271`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExpressionRuntime.java:274-280`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/Functions.java:47-48`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/Functions.java:71-88`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/BuiltinFunctionCatalog.java:14-35`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExpressionParser.java:179`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExpressionParser.java:217`, `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/FunctionCatalogTest.java:78-79`, `docs/maintaining.md:46`)
- **Problem:** `BuiltinFunctionCatalog` lists the names (`DECIMAL_AGGREGATES`, `ITEM_FUNCTIONS`, `REDUCE`), but each name's behavior is typed again in string switches whose fallthrough is silent. `ExpressionRuntime.collection` has a per-item switch statement with no default and ends with `default -> result` (the empty result list); `Functions.aggregate` sends every name it does not know to the SUM branch. No code links a listed name to a runtime case, which contradicts docs/maintaining.md:46 ("the catalog, parser, runtime and Functions read the same lists"). "REDUCE" is also retyped at ExpressionParser.java:217 and ExpressionRuntime.java:271/276 instead of using `BuiltinFunctionCatalog.REDUCE`.
- **Trigger → consequence:** A developer adds "FIND" to `ITEM_FUNCTIONS` or "MEDIAN" to `DECIMAL_AGGREGATES` and updates catalog.json/arity.json, as any intentional capability change requires. The parser, arity check, catalog and FunctionCatalogTest then all accept the name (the test counts list membership as "evaluated by ARC", and `arcFunctions()` registers every `DECIMAL_AGGREGATES` name in a loop). At runtime `$FIND(...)` returns `[]` for every input and `$MEDIAN(1,2,10)` returns 13, the sum. Nothing is wrong today because every current name has its own case.
- **Evidence:** Probe `$SP/probes/v-clean-be1/CleanProbe.java` (reflection against HEAD classes): `aggregate(SUM,[1,2,10]) = 13`, `aggregate(MEDIAN,[1,2,10]) = 13`, `aggregate(STDEV,[1,2,10]) = 13`; `collection(FILTER,[1,2,3],item,true) = [1, 2, 3]`, `collection(FIND,…) = []`, `collection(SOME,…) = []`. Code: ExpressionRuntime.java:260-279, Functions.java:47-48 and :81-85, FunctionCatalogTest.java:78-79.
- **Suggested fix:** Make each name own its implementation, so a listed name without code cannot compile. (1) Add a package-private `enum DecimalAggregate { SUM, MIN, MAX, AVG, AVERAGE, COUNT, MUL }` in `engine.expression` (declaration order = today's list order, so the catalog order is unchanged) with `BigDecimal apply(List<BigDecimal> numbers)` and `boolean acceptsNoValues()` (true for SUM, MUL, COUNT) written as exhaustive switch expressions over `this`, no `default`. `Functions.arcFunctions()` registers `for (DecimalAggregate aggregate : DecimalAggregate.values()) functions.put(aggregate.name(), args -> aggregate.apply(numbers(args)))`; delete `Functions.aggregate`; `BuiltinFunctionCatalog.DECIMAL_AGGREGATES` becomes the names derived from the enum. (2) Add a package-private `enum CollectionFunction { MAP, FILTER, ALL, ANY, REDUCE }` with `static CollectionFunction named(String name)` (null when the name is not a collection function) and `boolean bindsAccumulator()` (exhaustive switch). `ExpressionParser.functionCall`/`collectionCall` use `CollectionFunction.named(name)` and `bindsAccumulator()` instead of `isCollectionFunction` and `name.equals("REDUCE")`; `ExpressionRuntime.collection(CollectionFunction function, …)` uses exhaustive switch expressions (not switch statements, which Java does not check for exhaustiveness over an enum, review-lessons.md:105) for the per-item step and for the final value. Derive `ITEM_FUNCTIONS`, `REDUCE` and `isCollectionFunction` from the enum so help generation keeps its order. Update docs/maintaining.md:46 and the "Function help" guard at docs/review-lessons.md:103 to name the two enums. No behavior change; catalog.json and the arity fixture stay byte-identical.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/FunctionCatalogTest.java`: remove the crediting by list membership at :78-79; assert every "Collections" catalog entry is a `CollectionFunction` constant and every `DecimalAggregate` name is in `Functions.arcFunctionNames()`; golden fixtures pass unchanged. `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExpressionsTest.java`: a behavior table with one row per constant (`$SUM(1,2,10)=13`, `$MIN=1`, `$MAX=10`, `$MUL=20`, `$COUNT=3`, `$AVG`/`$AVERAGE` = 13/3 at `Expressions.MATH`, and `$MAP`/`$FILTER`/`$ALL`/`$ANY`/`$REDUCE` over `[1,2,3]`), plus "MIN requires values" for `$MIN([])` while `$SUM([])`, `$MUL([])` and `$COUNT([])` still succeed.
- **Related:** docs/review-lessons.md:103 (guard "Function help, arity and runtime support"), docs/review-lessons.md:105 (exhaustive switch expressions); earlier C21 ("one dispatch table per evaluation style", 2026-09-27 review); merged duplicates X163 (s1#2) and X195 (l1#3).

### X160 · low · cleanup · "An empty name means unset" is restated in five places; BranchScopes misses it and /api/variables lists "" as a variable
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-clean-be1.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / engine.graph (BranchScopes), with model, engine.script, engine.validation, engine.execution (frontend: domain/graph.ts, features/editor/inspector) / Variables in scope (POST /api/variables), result variable and Output name handling
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/graph/BranchScopes.java:58` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/script/ArcScriptRenderer.java:158-161`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/script/ArcScriptNodeParser.java:257`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/DefinitionShape.java:143`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/DefinitionShape.java:146-151`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/GraphExecution.java:277`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/GraphExecution.java:283`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleDefinitionService.java:62-65`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/model/Definition.java:86-148`, `frontend/src/domain/graph.ts:228`, `frontend/src/features/editor/inspector/ResultFields.tsx:19`, `frontend/src/features/editor/inspector/OutputValueFields.tsx:43`)
- **Problem:** The rule "an empty result/Output name means unset" is written separately in the renderer (`name == null || name.isEmpty() ? null : name`), the parser (`name.isEmpty() ? null : …`), draft shape (DefinitionShape.java:143 and :146-151), execution (GraphExecution.java:277 and :283, `outputName` only) and the frontend (`node.output &&` at graph.ts:228). BranchScopes checks only `node.output() != null`, so a Formula, Transform or Reference whose `output` is "" adds a variable named "" to every downstream scope, and the same graph yields different scopes before and after a code round trip.
- **Trigger → consequence:** Clearing "Result variable" in the inspector saves `output: ""` (IdentifierField accepts "" per identifiers.ts:31; ResultFields calls `patch({ output })`). Draft shape accepts "". POST /api/variables then lists "" at every downstream node: output "" → `{"in":["amount"],"calc":["amount"],"out":["","amount"]}`; output null → `{"in":["amount"],"calc":["amount"],"out":["amount"]}`. /api/studio/render writes `let = amount * 2;` (unset); /api/studio/build of that text gives `calc.output = None`, and the rebuilt draft's variables are `"out":["amount"]`. API clients receive a name that is not an identifier, although docs/api.md:83 promises "variable names guaranteed to be available". The editor UI is unaffected only because `availableVariables` filters `node.output &&` (another copy of the rule); execution is unaffected because NodeValidation.java:55 rejects "".
- **Evidence:** Live on :8191 (Input→Formula→Output) as quoted above; fixtures `$SP/probes/v-clean-be1/empty-output.json`, `$SP/probes/v-clean-be1/rendered.json`, `$SP/probes/v-clean-be1/body.json`. Code: BranchScopes.java:58 `if (node.storesResult() && node.output() != null) scope.put(node.output(), active);` versus ArcScriptRenderer.java:160 `return name == null || name.isEmpty() ? null : name;`.
- **Suggested fix:** Give the rule one owner on the model. Add to `Definition.Node` `public String resultName()` (the `output`, or null when it is null or empty) and `public String outputFieldName()` (the same for `outputName`), documented as "empty and missing both mean no name is chosen yet". Keep the names free of `get`/`is` prefixes, like `kind()` and `storesResult()`, so Jackson does not add them to graph JSON (PayloadSnapshotTest pins it). Migrate every copy: BranchScopes.java:58 → `String name = node.resultName(); if (node.storesResult() && name != null) scope.put(name, active);`; `GraphExecution.outputField`/`namedOutput` → `node.outputFieldName()`; `ArcScriptRenderer` passes `node.resultName()`/`node.outputFieldName()` to `let(...)` and `statement("as", …)` and the private `name(String)` helper is deleted; DefinitionShape.java:143 and :146-151 → `node.resultName() == null || Identifiers.isValid(node.resultName())` (same for `outputFieldName()`). The parser at :257 already reads an empty `let` name as null; keep it. Optional frontend consistency: `ResultFields` patches `output: output || null`, as OutputValueFields.tsx:43 already does for `outputName`, so the editor stops creating "". Behavior change to list in docs/api.md "Behavior changes": POST /api/variables no longer lists "" for a node whose result name is empty.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/graph/GraphPlanTest.java`: a Formula, a Transform and a Reference with `output: ""` contribute no variable downstream, exactly as with null (`available().get("out")` is `["amount"]`). `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/script/DraftRoundTripTest.java`: the scopes of `graph(formula("", "amount * 2"))` equal the scopes of the graph rebuilt from its rendered text. `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/model/DefinitionTest.java`: `resultName()`/`outputFieldName()` return null for null and "". Optional API check in `scripts/studio_smoke.py`: POST /api/variables with `output: ""` returns `"out": ["amount"]`.
- **Related:** B12 (render only what the draft holds; empty names read back as unset), docs/review-lessons.md:104 (empty unfinished names vs malformed names), F10; earlier K18 (empty output rendered unparseable script); merged duplicate X164 (s1#3); X215 and X56 (same /api/variables endpoint).

### X171 · low · convention · ArcScriptNodeParser.accepts is a keyword switch with `default -> false`, not an exhaustive switch over NodeKind
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-clean-be1.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / engine.script (ArcScriptNodeParser) / ARC Script node parsing: Code studio build, node code editor, graph → code → graph round trip
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/script/ArcScriptNodeParser.java:237` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/script/ArcScriptNodeParser.java:206-234`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/script/ArcScriptNodeParser.java:225-226`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/script/ArcScriptRenderer.java:90-101`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/model/NodeKind.java:9-12`, `backend/AGENTS.md:20`, `docs/maintaining.md:77`)
- **Problem:** `accepts(String keyword)` is the per-kind table of the statements each node kind owns. It is a `switch (keyword)` with `kind == NodeKind.X || …` chains and `default -> false`, not an exhaustive switch over NodeKind. backend/AGENTS.md:20 requires per-kind behavior to be "an exhaustive switch expression without a default branch", and NodeKind.java:9-12 promises that adding a kind fails to compile until every ARC Script switch decides its behavior. Lines 225-226 make a second unchecked per-kind decision: `if (kind == NodeKind.OUTPUT) outputName = name; else output = name;`.
- **Trigger → consequence:** Someone adds a NodeKind. The compiler forces `ArcScriptRenderer.declarations` (exhaustive, lines 90-101) to emit statements for it, but `accepts` compiles unchanged and returns false for each of them, so building the renderer's own text fails with "Unsupported statement for <KIND>: …" and graph → code → graph breaks at runtime. An `as` on the new kind silently sets `output`. No ARC Script test loops over `NodeKind.values()` (ArcScriptContractTest.everyNodeKindRendersItsCanonicalStatementsAndConnections lists the kinds by hand); the only guard is the manual checklist at maintaining.md:77. Practical risk is low; nothing fails today.
- **Evidence:** ArcScriptNodeParser.java:237-248 `private boolean accepts(String keyword) { return switch (keyword) { case "let" -> kind == NodeKind.FORMULA || kind == NodeKind.TRANSFORM; … case "as" -> kind == NodeKind.REFERENCE || kind == NodeKind.TRANSFORM || kind == NodeKind.OUTPUT; default -> false; };` and :225-226; review-lessons.md:105 says the same as NodeKind.java:9-12; check_architecture.py checks strings only, not exhaustiveness.
- **Suggested fix:** Replace `accepts` with an exhaustive switch expression over the kind, no `default`, e.g. `private static Set<String> declarationKeywords(NodeKind kind) { return switch (kind) { case INPUT -> Set.of(); case FORMULA -> Set.of("let"); case CONDITION -> Set.of("when"); case SWITCH -> Set.of("select", "case"); case TRANSFORM -> Set.of("let", "field", "as"); case REFERENCE -> Set.of("use", "bind", "as"); case OUTPUT -> Set.of("return", "as"); }; }`, and have `parseDeclaration` test `declarationKeywords(kind).contains(keyword)` with the same "Unsupported statement for …" message. Route `as` through a second exhaustive switch expression instead of `kind == OUTPUT`, e.g. choosing the target property: `case OUTPUT -> Property.OUTPUT_NAME; case TRANSFORM, REFERENCE -> Property.OUTPUT; case INPUT, FORMULA, CONDITION, SWITCH -> throw new IllegalStateException("as is not declared for " + kind);` (unreachable, because the keyword table rejects it first). The `default -> throw new IllegalStateException("Accepted but unparsed statement")` in the keyword dispatch (:232) stays as an internal assertion. In docs/maintaining.md:77, list the new method among the exhaustive per-kind owners instead of "review `ArcScriptNodeParser.accepts`". No grammar or behavior change.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/script/ArcScriptContractTest.java`: a test that loops over `NodeKind.values()` and, for each kind, builds a complete representative node through a helper that is itself an exhaustive switch over the kind (so a new kind fails to compile there too; Transform twice, with fields and with a whole-value expression), renders it, builds it back and asserts an equal node with no diagnostics; assert that `as` sets `outputName` only for OUTPUT and `output` for TRANSFORM and REFERENCE. `DraftRoundTripTest` and `NodeEditorTest` pass unchanged.
- **Related:** docs/review-lessons.md:105 (node kinds guard), backend/AGENTS.md:20; X79 (same parser, list normalization at :141-143); X19 (the guard checks strings, not exhaustiveness).

<a id="x79"></a>

### X79 · low · cleanup · NodeKind.Slot/slots()/owns() duplicate properties(); only the ARC Script parser still reads them
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-clean-be1.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / model (NodeKind), engine.script (ArcScriptNodeParser) / Node kind facts; ARC Script normalization of bindings, cases and fields
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/model/NodeKind.java:104` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/model/NodeKind.java:23-41`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/model/NodeKind.java:67-73`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/model/NodeKind.java:114-116`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/model/NodeKind.java:122-136`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/script/ArcScriptNodeParser.java:141-143`, `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/model/NodeKindTest.java:40-52`, `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/model/NodeKindTest.java:88-95`, `docs/maintaining.md:77`, `docs/maintaining.md:79`, `docs/review-lessons.md:105`)
- **Problem:** `NodeKind.Slot`/`slots()`/`owns()` is a second exhaustive per-kind table. For every kind it equals `properties()` limited to EXPRESSION/SELECTOR/CASES/FIELDS/BINDINGS, and `storesResult()` equals `uses(Property.OUTPUT)`. Since 242886a moved DefinitionShape and NodeValidation to `Property`, its only production caller is ArcScriptNodeParser.java:141-143. AGENTS.md: "Remove dead paths when their callers have been migrated."
- **Trigger → consequence:** docs/maintaining.md:77 tells a new kind to answer both `slots` and `properties`, so every kind or property change is written twice. NodeKindTest checks only one direction (slots ⊆ properties), so a kind can gain Property.CASES/FIELDS/BINDINGS without the matching Slot and the test still passes. If `accepts` is also updated, the parser then drops that list (`kind.owns(Slot.X) ? … : null`) while draft shape accepts it, so a code round trip silently loses it. `kind.uses(Property.BINDINGS/CASES/FIELDS)` gives identical answers at the three call sites.
- **Evidence:** `git grep` at 242886a~1 finds `owns(Slot)` in DefinitionShape.java:157/159/167 and NodeValidation.java:138 as well as the parser; at HEAD only ArcScriptNodeParser.java:141-143 remains. Probe `$SP/probes/v-clean-be1/CleanProbe.java` compares `owns(Slot.X)` with `uses(Property.X)` for all 7 kinds × 5 slots, plus `storesResult()` with `uses(OUTPUT)`: `slot/property/storesResult mismatches = 0`.
- **Suggested fix:** Make `properties()` the single table. In `ArcScriptNodeParser.parse` use `kind.uses(Property.BINDINGS) ? bindings : null`, `kind.uses(Property.CASES) ? cases : null` and `kind.uses(Property.FIELDS) && !fields.isEmpty() ? fields : null`. Delete `NodeKind.Slot`, `slots()` and `owns()`, and the Slot sentence in the `Property` javadoc (NodeKind.java:37-41). Define `storesResult()` as `return uses(Property.OUTPUT);` so the fact has one source. While there, stop `uses()` from allocating: compute each kind's property set once (e.g. a static `EnumMap<NodeKind, Set<Property>>` filled from the existing exhaustive switch, which becomes a private `declaredProperties()`), because `uses()` builds a new `Set.of` per call today (see X213). Update docs/maintaining.md:77 (facts: `storesResult`, `handles`, `choosesOneExit`, `properties`), docs/maintaining.md:79 and docs/review-lessons.md:105 (drop "expression `slots`"). No JSON or behavior change.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/model/NodeKindTest.java`: remove the Slot cross-check (:40-52) and slot assertions (:88-95); keep `onlyFormulasTransformsAndReferencesStoreAResult` and the explicit `properties()` expectations per kind. The ARC Script round-trip tests (`DraftRoundTripTest`, `ArcScriptContractTest`, `NodeEditorTest`) must pass unchanged, which shows the list normalization is identical (Switch cases, Transform fields including an empty list → null, Reference bindings).
- **Related:** B25 (`NodeKind.properties()` ownership), docs/review-lessons.md:105; commit 242886a (migrated the other callers and left this path); X171 (same parser), X213 (per-call `Set.of` in `uses()`); merged duplicates X109 (a2#8), X157 (r1#4), X162 (s1#1), X197 (l1#5).

<a id="x16"></a>

### X16 · low · cleanup · Rule kinds are loose string literals in several places, with no enum or exhaustive switch
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-clean-be1.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / rule (RuleService, RuleSamples), persistence (JdbcRuleRepository), model / Rule kinds: create rule, library kind filter, blank templates, `@id:version` Formula calls
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleService.java:27` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleService.java:47`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleService.java:80-81`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleSamples.java:72-94`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleSamples.java:125`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleSamples.java:169`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleSamples.java:177-178`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/persistence/JdbcRuleRepository.java:287`, `backend/src/main/resources/db/migration/V1__rules.sql:5`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/RuleResolver.java:10-13`, `frontend/src/types.ts:1`)
- **Problem:** The closed set of rule kinds is written as loose strings: `RuleService.KINDS` (used by create and by the catalog filter at :47), a hand-written copy in the message "Choose DECISION_TREE, FORMULA, or RULE", `RuleSamples.blank` (a one-branch `kind.equals("RULE")` that sends every other kind to the Formula template), `JdbcRuleRepository.resolveFormula`'s `"FORMULA".equals(row.getString("kind"))`, and the V1 CHECK. The sample seeding also passes kind strings to `store.create` (RuleSamples.java:125, :169, :177-178; observed while writing this entry). The backend has no enum and no exhaustive switch; the frontend `Kind` union is typed.
- **Trigger → consequence:** Adding a rule kind means editing KINDS and, separately, the message "Choose DECISION_TREE, FORMULA, or RULE"; it also needs a new Flyway migration, which is unavoidable because V1 is immutable. `RuleSamples.blank` still compiles and silently gives the new kind the Formula template (DECISION_TREE already takes that branch by design). The compiler offers no help. Nothing is wrong today, and rule kinds have not changed since b50ccb0.
- **Evidence:** RuleService.java:27 `private static final Set<String> KINDS = Set.of("DECISION_TREE", "FORMULA", "RULE");` and :80-81; RuleSamples.java:73 `if (kind.equals("RULE"))`, else :86-94 the Formula template; JdbcRuleRepository.java:287; V1__rules.sql:5 `CHECK (kind IN ('DECISION_TREE', 'FORMULA', 'RULE'))`; frontend/src/types.ts:1 `export type Kind = "DECISION_TREE" | "FORMULA" | "RULE";`.
- **Suggested fix:** Add `dev.arc.model.RuleKind` beside `NodeKind`: `enum RuleKind { DECISION_TREE, FORMULA, RULE }` with `static Optional<RuleKind> parse(String name)` (exact name, like `NodeKind.parse`), `boolean callableByFormula()` (exhaustive switch: FORMULA → true, the others false) and `static String choices()` that renders "DECISION_TREE, FORMULA, or RULE" from `values()`. Keep the HTTP payloads as strings (`Rule.kind`, `RuleSummary.kind`, `RuleService.Create.kind`) and parse inside `RuleService.create` and `RuleService.catalog`, so unknown values still return 422 with today's messages ("Choose DECISION_TREE, FORMULA, or RULE" from `choices()`, "Unknown rule kind") instead of a Jackson 400. Change `RuleSamples.blank(RuleKind kind)` to an exhaustive switch expression (`case RULE -> approvalTemplate(); case FORMULA, DECISION_TREE -> formulaTemplate();`), which makes DECISION_TREE's template an explicit decision, and pass `RuleKind` constants (their `name()`) in the seeding. In `JdbcRuleRepository.resolveFormula` replace the literal with `RuleKind.parse(kind).filter(RuleKind::callableByFormula)`; optionally (merged duplicate X199) move the "only Formulas are callable" policy out of the row mapper into the rule layer (the query returns kind and definition, a `RuleRepository` default method decides). A future kind still needs a new migration that replaces the V1 CHECK; never edit V1. No payload change.
- **Regression test:** New `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/model/RuleKindTest.java`: `parse` is exact, `choices()` equals the current message text, only FORMULA is callable. `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleServiceTest.java`: unknown kind → 422 "Choose DECISION_TREE, FORMULA, or RULE"; unknown catalog filter → 422 "Unknown rule kind". `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleSamplesTest.java`: RULE gets the Condition template, FORMULA and DECISION_TREE the Formula template. The JDBC kind check has real-database coverage in `scripts/studio_smoke.py:226-227` (an `@` call to a published DECISION_TREE is invalid), which must stay green; `FormulaCheckTest` only mocks `resolveFormula`, so add a `JdbcRuleRepositoryTest` case if the policy moves into the rule layer (X199).
- **Related:** docs/review-lessons.md:105 (the NodeKind counterpart); merged duplicate X199 (l1#7, callable-kind policy inside the JDBC row mapper); X19 (a broader vocabulary guard must tell the rule kind "FORMULA" apart from the node kind).

### X155 · low · cleanup · Source save checks lock and revision inside the JDBC adapter after validation; a missing source has three wordings
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-clean-be2.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / source (SourceService, SourceRepository), persistence (JdbcSourceRepository) / Data source save (PUT /api/sources/{id}) and version reads
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/persistence/JdbcSourceRepository.java:171` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/persistence/JdbcSourceRepository.java:115-116`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/persistence/JdbcSourceRepository.java:169-190`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/persistence/JdbcSourceRepository.java:200-201`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/source/SourceService.java:53-57`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/source/SourceRepository.java:23`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleService.java:93-104`, `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/source/SourceServiceTest.java:84`, `backend/AGENTS.md:16`, `docs/maintaining.md:36`)
- **Problem:** `SourceService.update` validates the configuration first and then calls `repository.update`, whose JDBC implementation takes the row lock (`SELECT version FROM data_sources WHERE id=? FOR UPDATE`) and checks the revision. Sources therefore run validation → lock → revision, while rules run lock → revision → validation, the order backend/AGENTS.md:16 and review-lessons.md:110 prescribe. The adapter's 404 says "Data source not found" instead of the class's own `sourceNotFound()` ("Source not found"), and the `SourceRepository` port (SourceRepository.java:23) does not document that `update` performs the 404/409 checks, although maintaining.md:36 says replacements must preserve 409 conflicts.
- **Trigger → consequence:** Tab B holds a source at revision 1 while tab A saves it. Tab B then edits the LOOKUP parameter away from `key` and saves: 422 "Lookup tables require exactly one parameter named key". After the user fixes that, the next save gets 409, so the fix was wasted work before a reload. A stale and invalid rule save gets 409 immediately. PUT to an unknown source answers "Data source not found", GET …/versions answers "Source not found", and GET …/versions/1 answers "Data source version not found: …" — three wordings for the same missing source. A replacement `SourceRepository` must re-implement a 409 check its port never mentions.
- **Evidence:** Live probe on :8191 after creating rv-v-clean-be2-src v1: PUT revision 99 with a LOOKUP config without a `key` parameter → HTTP422 "Lookup tables require exactly one parameter named key"; PUT revision 99 with a valid config → HTTP409; PUT rules/rv-v-clean-be2-rule with revision 99 and a blank name → HTTP409; PUT sources/rv-v-clean-be2-missing → HTTP404 "Data source not found"; GET …-missing/versions → HTTP404 "Source not found". Code: SourceService.java:55-56, JdbcSourceRepository.java:171-176 vs :200-201, RuleService.java:95-98.
- **Suggested fix:** Move the transaction policy into `SourceService`, mirroring `RuleService.update`. Add to the port `int lock(String id)` ("locks the source row for the current transaction and returns its current version; an unknown source is 404 'Source not found'") and narrow the write to e.g. `DataSource appendVersion(String id, String name, int currentVersion, SourceDefinition definition)` (inserts version `currentVersion + 1` and moves the pointer, no checks), documenting that it runs inside the caller's transaction after `lock`. `SourceService.update` becomes: `int current = repository.lock(id); if (current != request.revision()) throw new ArcException(409, "Source changed in another editor; reload before saving"); validator.validate(request.name(), request.definition()); return repository.appendVersion(id, request.name(), current, request.definition());`. `JdbcSourceRepository.lock` uses the existing `FOR UPDATE` query and `sourceNotFound()`. Decide whether `get(id, version)` for an unknown source should also say "Source not found" (B24: a missing parent is the same 404 on every read path); recommended yes, checking `requireSource(id)` only when the version row is missing. Behavior changes for docs/api.md "Behavior changes" and docs/openapi.yaml: a stale and invalid source save returns 409 instead of 422; PUT for an unknown source says "Source not found" instead of "Data source not found" (and, if adopted, so does the version read).
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/source/SourceServiceTest.java`: with Mockito `InOrder`, `lock` runs before validation; a stale revision with an invalid config → 409 and the validator/adapter are never called; an invalid config at the current revision → 422 and `appendVersion` is never called. Replace `verifyNoInteractions(repository)` at :84 with `verify(repository, never())` on the write methods, because an invalid update now locks first. `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/api/ResourceErrorsTest.java` (real controllers and JDBC): PUT to an unknown source → 404 "Source not found". `scripts/smoke.py` (real PostgreSQL): a stale and invalid source PUT → 409.
- **Related:** docs/review-lessons.md:110 (lock → revision check → validation → publication), backend/AGENTS.md:16, docs/maintaining.md:36, B24 (one 404 per missing parent); X133 and its merged duplicate X158 (rule vs source metadata policy drift in the same write paths).

### X161 · low · convention · Enforced limits are retyped as text: REPT "2,000", ROUND ±12 three times, "1 MiB" twice
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-clean-be2.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / engine.expression, source.http, api / Function help (/api/functions, Monaco hovers, function library: `$REPT`, `$ROUND`/`$ROUNDDOWN`/`$ROUNDUP`); HTTP source response and request body limit messages
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExcelFunctionHelp.java:93` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/Limits.java:99`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExcelFunctionAdapter.java:133-134`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/Functions.java:97`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/BuiltinFunctionCatalog.java:159`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/source/http/HttpSource.java:39`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/source/http/HttpSource.java:199`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/api/RequestLimitFilter.java:28`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/api/RequestLimitFilter.java:55`, `backend/src/test/resources/expression/functions/catalog.json:329`, `backend/src/test/resources/expression/functions/catalog.json:334-336`, `docs/studio.md:201`)
- **Problem:** The REPT help writes `Limits.MAX_STRING_CHARACTERS` as the literal "2,000". The ±12 bound of ROUND/ROUNDDOWN/ROUNDUP is typed separately in the check and its message (Functions.java:97) and in the catalog help (BuiltinFunctionCatalog.java:159). HttpSource and RequestLimitFilter restate their `1024 * 1024` constants as "1 MiB" in hand-written messages, and the filter writes the whole `Errors` envelope as a JSON string literal. This breaks backend/AGENTS.md:18 ("read shared sizes and budgets from `engine.Limits` instead of retyping them") and B5 ("messages state the limit from it (`Limits.format` for grouped numbers such as "2,000"). Do not retype a number.").
- **Trigger → consequence:** If `Limits.MAX_STRING_CHARACTERS` is raised, REPT enforcement (ExcelFunctionAdapter.checkRepeatedLength) changes, but /api/functions, Monaco hovers and the function library keep saying "2,000"; FunctionCatalogTest still passes because golden catalog.json:329 holds the same literal. Changing the ROUND bound means editing Functions, BuiltinFunctionCatalog, catalog.json:334-336 and docs/studio.md:201. Changing MAX_RESPONSE_BYTES or MAX_BODY_BYTES leaves the "exceeds 1 MiB" messages stale. No wrong output today: every text matches the current value.
- **Evidence:** Live GET :8191/api/functions returns `$REPT | Repeats text. ARC limits the result to 2,000 characters.` Code: ExcelFunctionHelp.java:93; Limits.java:99 `MAX_STRING_CHARACTERS = 2_000;`; ExcelFunctionAdapter.java:133-134; Functions.java:97 `if (scale < -12 || scale > 12) throw ArcException.invalid("Round precision must be -12 to 12");`; BuiltinFunctionCatalog.java:159; HttpSource.java:39 and :199; RequestLimitFilter.java:28 and :55.
- **Suggested fix:** Build every message from the constant it states. (1) ExcelFunctionHelp.java:93 → `"Repeats text. ARC limits the result to " + Limits.format(Limits.MAX_STRING_CHARACTERS) + " characters."`. (2) Name the ROUND bound once, e.g. a package-private `static final int MAX_ROUND_DIGITS = 12` in `Functions`, used by the check and message in `Functions.round` and by the help text at BuiltinFunctionCatalog.java:159. (3) Add a byte-size formatter next to `Limits.format`, e.g. `Limits.formatBytes(long bytes)` that renders 1,048,576 as "1 MiB", and use it at HttpSource.java:199 and RequestLimitFilter.java:55; moving the two byte limits into `Limits` is optional (`api` and `source.http` may both import `engine`). (4) Write the 413 body through the same error-body builder `Errors` uses (one shared static method or record serialized by the application's ObjectMapper) instead of a JSON string literal, so the envelope has one owner; coordinate with X134, which fixes that envelope's field order. All rendered text stays byte-identical, so catalog.json and the docs need no change. The candidate report (s1#6) lists the same pattern for the exponent ±100 bound, COMBIN's 10,000 and the ±100 decimal places of FIXED/DOLLAR/TRUNC; the verifier did not assess those, but the same named-constant treatment applies.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/FunctionCatalogTest.java` (or `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/LimitsTest.java`): the REPT description equals the string built from `Limits.format(Limits.MAX_STRING_CHARACTERS)`, and the ROUND/ROUNDDOWN/ROUNDUP descriptions and the "Round precision must be …" message contain the named bound (expectations derived from the constants, not literals); golden catalog.json unchanged. `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/api/RequestLimitTest.java`: the 413 body is still `{"status":413,"message":"Request body exceeds 1 MiB","issues":[]}` (or the agreed shared envelope). `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/source/http/HttpSourceTest.java`: an oversized response still fails "HTTP source response exceeds 1 MiB".
- **Related:** B5, backend/AGENTS.md:18, docs/maintaining.md:34; earlier C02/C04/C05 (limit literals copied across classes); merged duplicates X167 (s1#6) and X173 (v1#4); X17 (nginx `client_max_body_size 1m` is a third copy of the body limit), X134 (Errors envelope field order).

### X165 · low · cleanup · ArcScript.ExpressionCheck record left in the ARC Script facade after checkExpression was removed
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-clean-be2.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / engine.script (ArcScript), rule (RuleDefinitionService), api (StudioController) / Expression check (POST /api/studio/expression/check, expression dialog)
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/script/ArcScript.java:30` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/script/ArcScript.java:4`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/script/ArcScript.java:15`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/script/ArcScript.java:29-34`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleDefinitionService.java:7`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleDefinitionService.java:39-47`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/api/StudioController.java:25-28`)
- **Problem:** The `ArcScript.ExpressionCheck` record stayed in the ARC Script facade after 6fbbd07 (C01) removed `ArcScript.checkExpression`. ArcScript neither produces nor reads the record; its only producer is `RuleDefinitionService.checkExpression` and its only consumer is `StudioController`. `rule.RuleDefinitionService` imports `engine.script.ArcScript` only for this record (import at :7, uses at :39/:43/:46), and ArcScript imports `Expressions` (:4) and `Set` (:15) only to declare it. The contract of /studio/expression/check sits in a class documented as "build and render graphs without evaluating expressions or fetching data".
- **Trigger → consequence:** No behavior impact. Someone looking for the /studio/expression/check contract finds it in the Script grammar facade, and the rule layer depends on engine.script for a DTO it owns.
- **Evidence:** ArcScript.java:29-34 `/** Result of {@code /studio/expression/check}; the application layer produces it. */ public record ExpressionCheck(boolean valid, Set<String> variables, String error, List<Expressions.FormulaCall> formulaCalls) {}`. `git grep ExpressionCheck` → only ArcScript.java:30, StudioController.java:26 and RuleDefinitionService.java:39/43/46, with no tests. `git show 6fbbd07` removed `public ExpressionCheck checkExpression(String expression)` from ArcScript and added the "application layer produces it" comment instead of moving the record.
- **Suggested fix:** Move the record next to its producer as `RuleDefinitionService.ExpressionCheck`, the pattern of `GraphController.Validation` and `SourceController.TestResult`, with the same components in the same order (`valid`, `variables`, `error`, `formulaCalls`) so the JSON stays byte-identical. Change `StudioController.checkExpression`'s return type, delete the record and its two now-unused imports from ArcScript, and delete the `ArcScript` import from RuleDefinitionService. No API change.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/api/FormulaCheckTest.java` must pass unchanged (it asserts `valid`, `variables`, `error` and `formulaCalls[0].id/version/argumentCount` over HTTP); optionally add one assertion on the exact response body of a valid check to pin field order. The expression-check calls in `scripts/studio_smoke.py` stay green.
- **Related:** earlier C01 (dead `ArcScript.checkExpression`, commit 6fbbd07); docs/maintaining.md:153 (the application layer validates Formula pins for the check).

### X166 · low · cleanup · The deadline "check, call, check" policy is hand-written at six sites; Parameters.readWithin shadows its own field
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-clean-be2.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / engine (ExecutionDeadline), rule (RuleExecutionService), engine.execution (Parameters), source (SourceExecutionService), engine.expression (Expressions) / Execution deadline (504) around rule-pin reads, source configuration reads, provider reads and expression evaluation
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleExecutionService.java:134` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleExecutionService.java:108-113`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleExecutionService.java:134-137`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleExecutionService.java:142-145`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/Parameters.java:52`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/Parameters.java:111`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/Parameters.java:128-135`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/source/SourceExecutionService.java:83-87`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/Expressions.java:76-80`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/ExecutionDeadline.java:30-32`)
- **Problem:** The documented policy (review-lessons.md:113: "checks the deadline before and after every read, so a value that arrives after expiry is never used") is written by hand as check / call / check / return at six sites: RuleExecutionService 109-112 (source configuration lookup lambda), 134-137 (resolve), 142-145 (resolveFormula), Parameters.readWithin 131-134, SourceExecutionService.fetch 83-87 and Expressions.Compiled.evaluate 76-80. `Parameters.Resolution.readWithin` takes an `ExecutionDeadline deadline` parameter that shadows the `Resolution` field of the same name (:52), and its only caller passes that field.
- **Trigger → consequence:** No wrong behavior today. Any new resolver method or source hook has to remember both checks by hand; the anonymous RuleResolver already repeats two identical five-line bodies.
- **Evidence:** RuleExecutionService.java:134-137 `deadline.check(); Definition definition = rules.resolve(id, version); deadline.check(); return definition;`, the same shape at :142-145 (`rules.resolveFormula`) and :109-112 (`sourceSession.definition(sourceId, sourceVersion)`); Parameters.java:111 `value = readWithin(deadline, source, argumentValues);` and :129-134; SourceExecutionService.java:83-87; Expressions.java:76-80.
- **Suggested fix:** Give the policy one owner on `engine.ExecutionDeadline`: `public <T> T within(Supplier<T> work) { check(); T value = work.get(); check(); return value; }`, with the javadoc of review-lessons.md:113 (no work starts after expiry, a value that arrives after it is discarded, JDBC already in flight is not interrupted). Migrate all six sites: the RuleExecutionService resolver → `return deadline.within(() -> rules.resolve(id, version));` and `… rules.resolveFormula(id, version)`; the source configuration lambda → `(sourceId, sourceVersion) -> deadline.within(() -> sourceSession.definition(sourceId, sourceVersion))`; `Parameters.read` → `value = deadline.within(() -> sources.read(source, argumentValues, deadline));` and delete `readWithin`; `SourceExecutionService.fetch` → after `normalizeInputs`, `return deadline.within(() -> adapters.require(definition.kind()).fetch(source.id(), definition, values, deadline));`; `Expressions.Compiled.evaluate` → `return deadline.within(() -> bounded(expression.eval(new ExpressionRuntime.Context(scope, deadline, formulas))));`. The check order and exception propagation stay the same. Leave `ExpressionRuntime.formula`/`function` (tick → call → check) alone: `tick()` also spends the operation budget, so it is a different policy. `expression` may already import `ExecutionDeadline` (docs/maintaining.md:199), so no boundary changes.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/ExecutionDeadlineTest.java`: `within` does not invoke the supplier when the deadline already expired (DEADLINE, 504); a supplier that outlives a 100 ms deadline has its value discarded with DEADLINE; an `ArcException` thrown by the supplier propagates unchanged. Existing `RuleExecutionServiceTest` (expired preparation stops later queries), `ParametersTest` (late values, source deadline propagation), `SourceExecutionServiceTest` (provider deadlines) and `ExecutionTraceTest` (expression deadline propagation) pass unchanged.
- **Related:** docs/review-lessons.md:102 and :113 (deadline before and after every read); earlier C07/C17 (one deadline-aware read); X169 (the source configuration lookup this wraps).

### X169 · low · cleanup · Same-arity SourceBindingValidator.validate overloads switch the dependency set and the configuration source by argument type
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-clean-be2.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / source (SourceBindingValidator), rule (RuleDefinitionService, RuleExecutionService) / Static source-contract checks for execute/preview, diagnostics and validate/publish
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/source/SourceBindingValidator.java:47` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/source/SourceBindingValidator.java:26-28`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/source/SourceBindingValidator.java:30-41`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/source/SourceBindingValidator.java:43-50`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/source/SourceBindingValidator.java:52-57`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleDefinitionService.java:52`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleDefinitionService.java:55-60`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleDefinitionService.java:77`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleExecutionService.java:105-113`)
- **Problem:** The two public overloads `validate(Definition, RuleResolver, X)` have the same arity, and the static type of X switches two behaviors at once. With a `BiFunction`, the method visits all of the definition's own dependencies and reads configurations through the supplied lookup (the execution's source session). With a `List<Validator.Dependency>`, it visits only the given dependencies and reads configurations directly from the repository (`pinnedSources()`). Only the second overload has a javadoc, and it does not mention the repository read. (A third overload, `validate(Definition, RuleResolver)`, also reads the repository.)
- **Trigger → consequence:** RuleDefinitionService.java:59 `sources.validate(definition, resolver, lookup);` (execution) and :77 `sources.validate(definition, resolver, diagnosis.dependencies());` (diagnostics) look the same at the call site. A reader or a new caller cannot tell which configuration snapshot (request session or repository) or which dependency set applies without resolving the overload. No wrong behavior today: each existing caller reaches the intended overload by static type, and none passes null.
- **Evidence:** SourceBindingValidator.java:30-41 `public void validate(Definition definition, RuleResolver resolver, BiFunction<String, Integer, SourceDefinition> configurations) { validateContracts(definition, Validator.dependencies(definition), resolver, configurations, new HashSet<>(), 0); }`; :47-50 `public void validate(Definition definition, RuleResolver resolver, List<Validator.Dependency> dependencies) { validateContracts(definition, dependencies, resolver, pinnedSources(), new HashSet<>(), 0); }`; :52-57 `pinnedSources()` reads `sources.get(id, version).definition()`.
- **Suggested fix:** Name the variants by what differs and document both inputs, e.g. `validatePinnedContracts(Definition, RuleResolver)` (repository configurations, all dependencies; `RuleDefinitionService.validate` for validate/publish), `validateForExecution(Definition, RuleResolver, SourceConfigurations session)` (the execution's session snapshot, all dependencies) and `validateRemainingPins(Definition, RuleResolver, List<Validator.Dependency> unreported)` (repository configurations, only the pins the graph checks did not reject; diagnostics). Replace the raw `BiFunction<String, Integer, SourceDefinition>` with a named functional interface `SourceConfigurations { SourceDefinition get(String id, int version); }` in `dev.arc.source`, used by SourceBindingValidator, `RuleDefinitionService.validateSources` and the lambda in `RuleExecutionService.evaluate`; `pinnedSources()` returns a memoizing `SourceConfigurations` over the repository. `validateContracts` stays private and unchanged. No behavior change.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/source/SourceBindingValidatorTest.java`: `validateForExecution` reads configurations only through the supplied `SourceConfigurations` (`verifyNoInteractions(repository)`); `validateRemainingPins` reads the repository and never resolves a dependency that was left out of the list. `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleDefinitionServiceTest.java` and `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/source/FormulaSourceContractsTest.java` keep their expectations (each fault reported once).
- **Related:** B11 (the source-contract check skips pins the graph checks rejected, `Validator.Diagnosis.dependencies`); E17 (the source-contract walk re-reads child pins per execution); X226 (same class: order-dependent depth check with a global visited set); X166 (deadline wrapper around the execution lookup).
