### X108 · low · cleanup · "A declared input needs a caller value" is typed three times; runtime resolution twice
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-clean-be2.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / engine.validation (NodeValidation, FormulaCallValidation), source (SourceBindingValidator, SourceExecutionService), engine.execution (Parameters), model (Definition.Input) / Callee input contracts: Reference bindings, `@id:version` Formula-call arguments, source mappings
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/FormulaCallValidation.java:19` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/NodeValidation.java:104-116`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/source/SourceBindingValidator.java:94-99`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/source/SourceValidator.java:37`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/Parameters.java:71-91`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/source/SourceExecutionService.java:90-112`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/GraphExecution.java:234-236`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/model/Definition.java:59-73`, `docs/maintaining.md:34`)
- **Problem:** The rule "a declared Input needs a caller value when it is required, has no default and has no source" is typed three times, each spelled differently: NodeValidation.referenceParameters (Reference bindings), FormulaCallValidation (`@id:version` arguments) and SourceBindingValidator.validateSourceMappings (source mappings; the `source` term is left out there because SourceValidator:37 forbids sourced source parameters). The runtime resolution ("an unknown name fails, a supplied key wins over the default, a required null fails, then InputTypes.check") is written twice: in Parameters.Resolution and in SourceExecutionService.normalizeInputs.
- **Trigger → consequence:** All five copies agree today; the verifier found no divergent input (sourced callee inputs, defaults, explicit-null bindings and omitted trailing Formula arguments — GraphExecution.java:234-236 maps positional arguments onto the first declared inputs — are treated the same by every copy). Any new way to satisfy an input, such as an expression default or a new binding kind, must be edited in three static checks and two resolvers. Missing one makes publish validation and execution disagree: a publish passes and execution then fails "Missing required input", or the reverse.
- **Evidence:** NodeValidation.java:110-116 `require(!parameter.required() || parameter.defaultValue() != null || parameter.source() != null || bindings.containsKey(parameter.name()), …)`; FormulaCallValidation.java:19 `if (input.required() && input.defaultValue() == null && input.source() == null)`; SourceBindingValidator.java:95-97; SourceValidator.java:37; Parameters.java:72-73 and :84-89; SourceExecutionService.java:93-95 and :101-109.
- **Suggested fix:** Put the static predicate on the model: `Definition.Input.needsCallerValue()` returning `required && defaultValue == null && source == null`, javadoc "a caller must bind or pass this input". Name it without a `get`/`is` prefix so Jackson does not add a property to graph or source JSON (PayloadSnapshotTest guards this). Use it in the three static checks, messages unchanged: NodeValidation.referenceParameters → `require(!parameter.needsCallerValue() || bindings.containsKey(parameter.name()), …)`; FormulaCallValidation → `if (input.needsCallerValue()) throw …`; SourceBindingValidator.validateSourceMappings → `if (parameter.needsCallerValue() && !binding.bindings().containsKey(parameter.name()))`. The last is exactly equivalent for all stored data: sourced source parameters have been rejected at save since sources were introduced (1bfe348, SourceService.java:129 at that commit). Decision needed on the runtime resolvers: recommended to keep `Parameters` and `normalizeInputs` separate for now, because docs/maintaining.md:34 keeps required/missing/explicit-null/default handling with the caller and `Parameters` also owns source reads, read order and cycle detection; pin their agreement with a parity test instead. If they are merged later, the shared piece is "unknown name → error; supplied key wins over the default; required null → error; InputTypes.check", parameterized by the two message families.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/model/DefinitionTest.java`: truth table for `needsCallerValue()` (required/optional × default/none × source/none). A parity test (e.g. in `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/NodeContractsTest.java` or `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/FormulaCallExecutionTest.java`): for the same callee input declarations, a Reference with no binding, an `@id:version` call that omits the argument and runtime `Parameters` resolution all accept or all reject; likewise a missing source mapping in `SourceBindingValidatorTest` versus `normalizeInputs` in `SourceExecutionServiceTest`. `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/api/PayloadSnapshotTest.java` keeps graph JSON unchanged.
- **Related:** docs/maintaining.md:34; merged duplicates X168 (s1#7) and X200 (l1#8, which proposes the shared runtime resolver); X74 (same method, NodeValidation.referenceParameters: pinned callee never checked for preparation).

<a id="x210"></a>

### X210 · low · efficiency · Every ArcException and each rewrap captures a stack trace that nothing reads
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-eff-be1.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / error (ArcException) / Error handling: `$IFERROR`/`$IS…` fallbacks, source DEFAULT fallbacks, /api/diagnostics with many problems
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/error/ArcException.java:47` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/error/ArcException.java:45-52`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/error/ArcException.java:78-104`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/api/Errors.java:19-31`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/api/Errors.java:46`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/DefinitionShape.java:322`)
- **Problem:** Every ArcException is built through a private constructor that calls `super(message)`, so it records a full stack trace. That covers each factory call and each `atNode`/`inRule`/`withContext` rewrap (lines 82, 86, 98), and nothing ever reads the trace: the error handler (`Errors`) returns only status, message, issues and locations, it logs only non-Arc exceptions (Errors.java:46), no model constructor throws an ArcException that could be logged as a cause, and no main or test code calls getStackTrace. Recording the trace takes most of the time in fallback-heavy collections and in diagnostics that report many problems.
- **Trigger → consequence:** Live POST /api/preview (8191) of `$SUM($MAP(xs, x, $IFERROR(1 / x, 0)))` over 1,000 zeros measured 3,007 µs median engine time, against 196 µs for the same result written as `$IF(x == 0, 0, 1 / x)` (15× slower). In-process A/B, JIT-warmed, HEAD vs the class compiled with `super(message, null, false, false)`, with 100 extra frames to imitate the servlet stack: IFERROR loop 3,401–3,477 µs → 280 µs (about 92% is trace capture); `$IFERROR($VLOOKUP(miss), 0)` ×1,000: 8.4–8.6 ms → 5.3 ms; diagnose() on a 100-node draft with 98 problems (3 exceptions per problem): 1,657–1,702 µs → 721–724 µs; 30-node draft with 5 problems: 185–188 µs → 140–143 µs. About 3 µs per exception at server stack depth.
- **Evidence:** Probes `$SP/probes/v-eff-be1/ArcTraceProbe.java` (run with and without `$SP/probes/v-eff-be1/patched-arc/` on the classpath) and `$SP/probes/v-eff-be1/live_preview.py`. grep for getStackTrace/printStackTrace/addSuppressed in backend main and test: none. Precedent: DefinitionShape.java:322 `super(violation.message(), null, false, false);`.
- **Suggested fix:** Construct ArcException without a writable stack trace: in the private constructor call `super(message, null, true, false)` (or `null, false, false` like `DefinitionShape.Violated`), with a comment that an ArcException is an expected, user-facing outcome whose HTTP body never carries a trace and which `Errors` never logs. Every public constructor, factory (`invalid`, `notAvailable`, `limit`, `deadline`) and rewrap funnels through that constructor, so all of them become stackless; status, kind, message, issues and locations are unchanged. Note the tradeoff in the same comment: an ArcException that reaches a logger elsewhere (e.g. a failure in the `RuleSamples` startup runner) is logged without frames, which is acceptable because its message and locations identify the problem. No payload change.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/error/ArcExceptionTest.java`: `getStackTrace()` is empty for each factory, for the public constructors and after `atNode`/`inRule`/`withContext`; the existing kind/status/issues/location assertions stay. `ErrorRecoveryTest`, `DiagnosticsTest` and `ExpressionsTest` pass unchanged. Record the before/after timing of `ArcTraceProbe.java` in the fix report (efficiency fixes are measured, not asserted).
- **Related:** B7 (error kinds; fallbacks test `recoverable()`); precedent `DefinitionShape.Violated` (DefinitionShape.java:322); X209 (the `$IFERROR($VLOOKUP(miss), 0)` case also pays the lookup cost).

<a id="x213"></a>

### X213 · low · efficiency · Draft shape builds every "X belong to … nodes" message eagerly, for 8 properties of every node
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-eff-be1.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / engine.validation (DefinitionShape), model (NodeKind) / Draft-shape validation, which runs in /api/diagnostics, /api/variables, preview, validate, save/create/publish, studio build/render and uncached plan compiles
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/DefinitionShape.java:137` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/DefinitionShape.java:135-137`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/DefinitionShape.java:274-282`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/model/NodeKind.java:122-136`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/Limits.java:10-12`)
- **Problem:** The property-ownership check added in 242886a builds its failure message `belongsTo(property)` eagerly, as an argument to `require`, for all 8 properties of every node. Each call walks all 7 NodeKinds (`uses()` builds a new `Set.of` each time), title-cases the names and joins a message that is thrown away when the check passes. This is about 87% of draft-shape time, and contradicts Limits.java:10-12 (eagerly built validation messages stay compile-time constants).
- **Trigger → consequence:** Measured on a valid 100-node draft, HEAD vs the same class changed only to `if (!kind.uses(p) && node.sets(p)) throw violation(declaration, belongsTo(p));`: validator.shape() 194–195 µs → 26–27 µs (−168 µs, about 1.7 µs per node); /api/variables work in-process (shape + GraphPlan) 494–527 µs → 323–342 µs; diagnose() 625–655 µs → 462–491 µs. Live round trips on 8191 for the same draft are 1,751 µs for /api/diagnostics and 1,513 µs for /api/variables; the editor posts to both on edits (350 ms and 150 ms debounce), so this is about 10% of each request. The lazy form reports the same first violation with the same message. The other per-node message concatenations are negligible (the whole lazy-variant shape pass takes 26 µs).
- **Evidence:** Probes `$SP/probes/v-eff-be1/ShapeProbe.java` (with and without `$SP/probes/v-eff-be1/patched-shape/`) and `$SP/probes/v-eff-be1/live_shape.py`. Code: DefinitionShape.java:136-137, :275-282; NodeKind.java:122-135.
- **Suggested fix:** The messages depend only on static kind facts, so build them once: `private static final Map<Property, String> BELONGS_TO`, an `EnumMap` filled from `belongsTo(property)` for every `Property` at class initialization, and write the loop as `for (Property property : Property.values()) if (!kind.uses(property) && node.sets(property)) throw violation(declaration, BELONGS_TO.get(property));`. Also stop `NodeKind.uses()` from allocating: compute each kind's property set once (a static `EnumMap<NodeKind, Set<Property>>` built from the existing exhaustive switch; see X79). Messages and the first-violation order are unchanged.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/NodeContractsTest.java` ("every kind with every property it does not use") must pass unchanged; add explicit assertions, if missing, for the four messages documented in docs/api.md ("Parameter bindings belong to Reference nodes", "Rule references belong to Reference nodes", "Result variables belong to Formula, Transform and Reference nodes", "Expressions belong to Formula, Condition, Transform and Output nodes"). Record the before/after `ShapeProbe.java` timing in the fix report.
- **Related:** B25 (property ownership, introduced in commit 242886a); Limits.java:10-12; X79 (NodeKind table and per-call `Set.of`), X215 (shape runs twice per edit).

<a id="x211"></a>

### X211 · low · efficiency · Each trace step is serialized once to count its bytes and again for the response
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-eff-be1.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / engine.execution (ExecutionTrace, Engine), rule (ExecutionResponse) / Execution trace for POST /api/rules/{id}/execute and /api/preview (trace is on by default)
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/ExecutionTrace.java:29` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/ExecutionTrace.java:24-40`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/Engine.java:27-35`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/Engine.java:93-106`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/GraphExecution.java:145`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleExecutionService.java:39-40`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleExecutionService.java:123`, `docs/api.md:23`)
- **Problem:** When trace is on, which is the default for execute and preview, `ExecutionTrace.add` Jackson-serializes each step into a byte-counting stream only to measure its size and then keeps the `Step` object. The kept steps are serialized again when the HTTP response is written (`Engine.Result` inside `ExecutionResponse` via `@JsonUnwrapped`), so every traced request pays for one extra full serialization of the trace. GraphExecution.java:145 builds the Step objects whether or not trace is on, so the overhead is the measuring pass.
- **Trigger → consequence:** Live /api/preview on 8191: 40 Formulas each holding a 200-item array (traceBytes 64,569): executionMicros 1,768 with trace vs 1,398 without (+370 µs, +26%), round trip +878 µs; 98 scalar Formulas (traceBytes 12,565): 182 vs 144 µs (+38 µs, +26%). In-process, one Jackson pass over the same 64.5 KB of steps costs 506–511 µs, which matches the trace-on overhead (455–503 µs).
- **Evidence:** Probes `$SP/probes/v-eff-be1/TraceProbe.java` and `$SP/probes/v-eff-be1/live_trace.py`. Code: ExecutionTrace.java:27-29 `var counter = new ByteCounter(maximumBytes - bytes - separator); try { json.writeValue(counter, step);` and :38 `steps.add(step);`; Engine.java:99 `trace.steps(),`; RuleExecutionService.java:123 `requestedTrace == null || requestedTrace`; docs/api.md:23 "`trace` defaults to `true`".
- **Suggested fix:** Serialize each retained step once and reuse those bytes in the response. `ExecutionTrace` writes each step into one growing buffer bounded by `maximumBytes` (with the separators), still counting exactly and still stopping at the first step that does not fit, and keeps the `Step` objects for Java callers. `Engine.Result.trace` keeps its declared type `List<Step>` (29 test call sites read it), but its value becomes a small read-only list (e.g. `RetainedTrace extends AbstractList<Step>`) that also carries the serialized array bytes; annotate the `trace` record component with a serializer that writes those bytes with `JsonGenerator.writeRawValue` when present and falls back to normal list serialization otherwise. Constraint: the bytes must come from the same ObjectMapper that writes the response — the Spring bean injected into `Engine` (with `write-bigdecimal-as-plain`); embedded `new Engine(validator)` callers never write HTTP. `traceBytes`, field order and the payload stay byte-identical.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/ExecutionTraceTest.java`: for a traced run whose step values include a decimal such as `1E+2`, and for a truncated trace, the JSON written for `Result` by the application's ObjectMapper has a `trace` array byte-identical to serializing the steps normally, and its UTF-8 length equals `traceBytes`. `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/api/PayloadSnapshotTest.java` (flat field order) and `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/api/JsonNumberTest.java` (plain decimals through the application's Jackson configuration) pass unchanged. Record the before/after `TraceProbe.java` timing.
- **Related:** docs/review-lessons.md:102 ("Disabling trace retention must not disable the step budget"); docs/maintaining.md:32 (256 KiB trace budget) and :38 (`ExecutionResponse` @JsonUnwrapped, PayloadSnapshotTest).

<a id="x209"></a>

### X209 · low · efficiency · Wildcard metering collects every range text before checking for a wildcard; ranges are re-converted to POI values on every call
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-eff-be1.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / engine.expression (ExcelWildcards, ExcelFunctionAdapter, ExpressionRuntime) / Excel lookups and criteria (`$VLOOKUP`, `$HLOOKUP`, `$MATCH`, `$LOOKUP`, `$COUNTIF`, `$SUMIF`, database `D*` functions), especially inside `$MAP`
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExcelWildcards.java:52` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExcelWildcards.java:46-73`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExcelWildcards.java:111-123`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExcelWildcards.java:141-157`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExcelFunctionAdapter.java:32-33`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExcelFunctionAdapter.java:138-160`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExpressionRuntime.java:163-171`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/Functions.java:35-38`)
- **Problem:** Two separate costs on every call. (1) For COUNTIF/SUMIF/MATCH/LOOKUP/VLOOKUP/HLOOKUP, `texts(...)` (with `firstColumn`/`firstRow`) is an eager argument to `replay`, so the whole range is flattened, with one ArrayList per cell in `cells()`, before `replay` finds that the criterion has no wildcard (`pattern == null`) or is not text at all. (2) `ExcelFunctionAdapter.value` converts the whole range to POI values (a new `CacheAreaEval`) on every call, so a lookup inside `$MAP` converts the same unchanged table again each time.
- **Trigger → consequence:** `$SUM($MAP(keys, k, $VLOOKUP(k, table, 2, FALSE)))` with 1,000 keys and a 1,000×2 table measured 70,518 µs of engine time on live /api/preview; `$SUM($MAP(keys, k, 1))` measured 146 µs. In-process, one `$VLOOKUP("k500")` takes 57 µs: about 24 µs of text collection (A/B against a variant that checks for a wildcard before collecting: 57 → 32–34 µs; numeric key 53 → 32 µs; `$COUNTIF(1,000 numbers, ">500")` 41–43 → 23–24 µs), 29–30 µs for `ExcelFunctionAdapter.value(table)`, and 2.2–3.9 µs for POI's own lookup. The wildcard reorder alone takes the whole `$MAP` evaluation from 58.4–58.9 ms to 34.5–34.8 ms; converting the range once per evaluation would remove most of the remaining ~30 ms.
- **Evidence:** Probes `$SP/probes/v-eff-be1/LookupRangeProbe.java` (with and without `$SP/probes/v-eff-be1/patched-wild/`) and `$SP/probes/v-eff-be1/live_lookup.py`. Code: ExcelWildcards.java:52-57, :115-116, :149-156; ExcelFunctionAdapter.java:32 and :157; ExpressionRuntime.java:167.
- **Suggested fix:** (1) In `ExcelWildcards.checkMatchingWork`, compile the criteria first and return before touching the range when none is a wildcard pattern: e.g. `List<Pattern> patterns = wildcardPatterns(criteria)` (dropping null results of `Countif.StringMatcher.getWildCardPattern`), `if (patterns.isEmpty()) return;`, and only then collect the texts (pass them as a `Supplier<List<String>>` or collect after the check). Replace the recursive `cells()`, which allocates one list per element, with a single accumulator (`collectTexts(Object value, List<String> into)`). B14's guarantee holds, because a criterion without a wildcard does no regex work in POI either. (2) Convert each distinct range argument once per evaluation: add a per-evaluation memo (e.g. `RangeValues`, an `IdentityHashMap<List<?>, ValueEval>`) to `ExpressionRuntime.Context`, shared by child contexts like the operation budget and created fresh for every top-level evaluation, and pass it through `Functions.call(name, args, ranges)` (its only caller is ExpressionRuntime.java:167) to `ExcelFunctionAdapter.evaluate`, which asks it for `value(list)`. Keep it strictly per evaluation — never process-wide (review-lessons.md:102 forbids reusing library objects that cache per input across calls; maintaining.md:42: no independent global expression cache); it holds only immutable converted values of lists the evaluation already holds. Results are unchanged.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExcelWildcardsTest.java`: every existing wildcard-budget case still rejects past 10,000,000 character reads, and non-wildcard, numeric and comparison criteria over a large range still pass. `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExcelArgumentsTest.java` (B16 range rules, empty ranges) unchanged. `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExpressionsTest.java`: `$MAP(keys, k, $VLOOKUP(k, table, 2, FALSE))` returns the same values as separate calls, and two evaluations of one compiled expression with different `table` values give different results (the memo does not outlive an evaluation). Record the before/after `LookupRangeProbe.java` timing.
- **Related:** B14 (`ExcelWildcards` work bound), B16 (range conversion rules), docs/review-lessons.md:102 (no cross-call library caches); X210 (`$IFERROR($VLOOKUP(miss), 0)`).

<a id="x212"></a>

### X212 · low · efficiency · Every execution re-reads, decodes and deep-copies the whole immutable LOOKUP source version
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-eff-be2.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / source (SourceExecutionService, JdbcSourceRepository), rule (RuleExecutionService) / Inputs sourced from LOOKUP tables (execute, preview) and the static source-contract check
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/source/SourceExecutionService.java:65` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/source/SourceExecutionService.java:55-69`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleExecutionService.java:102`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleExecutionService.java:105-113`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/source/lookup/LookupSourceAdapter.java:52`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/ExecutionPlans.java:17-80`, `docs/architecture.md:107`, `docs/maintaining.md:38`, `docs/maintaining.md:52`, `docs/maintaining.md:63`)
- **Problem:** Every execute or preview of a rule with a sourced input selects the immutable source version again, decodes the whole LOOKUP table and deep-copies it with `detached()`, although the provider then returns one entry. The only cache is the per-request `Session`. The first load comes from the static source-contract check (`validateSources`, RuleExecutionService.java:105-110), which needs only `parameters`. This is separate from E17, which covers child rule pins.
- **Trigger → consequence:** A published rule has an input sourced from a LOOKUP table with 1,000 entries of 2 fields each (54 KB JSON, at the MAX_COLLECTION_ITEMS limit) and is executed repeatedly. Against the same rule on a 3-entry table (JIT warmed, 600 interleaved runs, medians): server totalMicros 916 vs 258 µs (+658 µs, 3.5×); preparation 892 vs 239 µs; client round trip 1,602 vs 894 µs. When the caller supplies the sourced value no read happens, but the cost is the same (907 vs 254 µs), because validateSources loads the whole table to check parameter names. Breakdown: decode 230 µs, `detached()` 64 µs, the remaining ~360 µs is the JDBC fetch of the JSONB text. Only a cache that outlives the request removes the bulk; freezing only the returned entry saves just the 64 µs copy.
- **Evidence:** Probes `$SP/probes/v-eff-be2/time_lookup.py` (live :8191, fixtures rv-v-eff-be2-*; rules deleted afterwards, sources cannot be deleted) and `$SP/probes/v-eff-be2/LookupLoadProbe.java`. Code: SourceExecutionService.java:60-61 and :65-68; RuleExecutionService.java:102 `var sourceSession = sources.openSession();`; LookupSourceAdapter.java:52.
- **Suggested fix:** Decision needed: allow a bounded process cache of immutable source versions. backend/AGENTS.md forbids "global caching as incidental refactoring", and architecture.md:107 and maintaining.md:52 describe source configuration as request-local, so this is an explicit design change. Recommended: yes — versions are immutable (architecture.md:157), sources cannot be deleted (api.md:97), and `ExecutionPlans` already caches immutable rule pins. Implementation: `SourceExecutionService` owns a bounded cache of frozen `DataSource` values keyed by (id, version) (`detached()` once on insert; entry and weight bounds, weight = serialized size); `Session.load` consults it before `repository.get`, and the Session keeps its per-request map so one request still sees one snapshot; a missing version (404) is never cached. Do not write a second hand-rolled LRU: extract the entry/weight-bounded access-order `LinkedHashMap` from `ExecutionPlans` into one shared helper (e.g. `dev.arc.engine.BoundedCache<K, V>` with `get`, `put(key, value, weight)` and `removeIf` for `forget`), used by both; add it to `ENGINE_DEPENDENCIES` for `execution` in scripts/check_architecture.py and to docs/maintaining.md:199. Update architecture.md:107 and maintaining.md:38, :52 and :63 to describe the cache. Without the decision, only the 64 µs copy can be saved (freeze just the entry `fetch` returns).
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/source/SourceExecutionServiceTest.java`: two sessions reading the same (id, version) call `repository.get` once; different versions are separate entries; the cache evicts past its bounds; providers still receive a frozen copy (mutating it throws, B22 "snapshot copies"); a missing version still fails 404 and is read again next time. `scripts/smoke.py`: repeated executions of a LOOKUP-sourced rule return identical results. Record the before/after `time_lookup.py` timing.
- **Related:** E17 (child pins, distinct), X46 (child pins still read per request), B22 (request snapshots), docs/review-lessons.md:102 (bounded shared plan cache keyed by immutable pins), backend/AGENTS.md (no incidental global caching).

<a id="x215"></a>

### X215 · low · efficiency · Each semantic edit runs draft shape and GraphPlan twice, via /api/variables and /api/diagnostics
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-eff-be2.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / rule (RuleDefinitionService), engine.validation (GraphDiagnostics) (frontend: features/editor/inspector, features/editor/useGraphProblems.ts) / Inspector variables and graph diagnostics while editing
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleDefinitionService.java:63` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleDefinitionService.java:62-65`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/GraphDiagnostics.java:30`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/GraphDiagnostics.java:38`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/GraphDiagnostics.java:66`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/api/GraphController.java:34`, `frontend/src/features/editor/inspector/Inspector.tsx:74`, `frontend/src/features/editor/inspector/useNodeVariables.ts:30-35`, `frontend/src/features/editor/useGraphProblems.ts:87-93`, `frontend/src/features/editor/Editor.tsx:332`, `frontend/src/features/editor/nodeSelection.ts:16-23`, `frontend/src/domain/graph.ts:11`, `docs/architecture.md:180`)
- **Problem:** In graph view the Inspector is always mounted (Editor.tsx:332; nodeSelection.ts always yields a node), so each semantic edit posts the same draft to /api/variables (150 ms debounce) and /api/diagnostics (350 ms debounce), both keyed by `semanticGraphKey`. The server runs draft shape and `new GraphPlan` for both, which duplicates about 1 ms of compute per edit on large drafts, plus a whole second HTTP request.
- **Trigger → consequence:** A 98-node, 129-edge draft (32 sequential Condition diamonds) opened on :3191; renaming the selected node by one character produced exactly one /api/variables and one /api/diagnostics request with byte-identical 33,236-byte bodies (`{"afterEdit":["variables","diagnostics"],"sameBody":true}`). In process (JIT warmed): shape 231 µs + GraphPlan 871 µs = 1.11 ms duplicated, while all of diagnose takes 1.26 ms. Live medians: /api/variables 2.33 ms, /api/diagnostics 2.55 ms. For a 20-node draft the duplication is 76 µs of compute and the extra request 0.71 ms. No user-visible latency: both are debounced background reads. The candidate's fix ("add a field to the /api/diagnostics response") does not fit: that response is a top-level JSON array (GraphController.java:34; architecture.md:180 "returns `{message, locations}[]`"), so folding scopes in would change a documented payload or need a new endpoint, and would couple the 150 ms and 350 ms debounces.
- **Evidence:** Probes `$SP/probes/v-eff-be2/ScopeDupProbe.java`, `$SP/probes/v-eff-be2/time_scope.py` and fixture `$SP/probes/v-eff-be2/diamonds-32.json`; a temporary browser spec zz-v-eff-be2-dup.spec.ts (deleted).
- **Suggested fix:** Decision needed; recommended order. (1) Frontend, no API change: key the variables read by what scopes depend on instead of the full semantic key. `GraphPlan`/`BranchScopes` read only node IDs, kinds, result names, handles (Switch case IDs), edges and input names — not labels or expression text. Add a pure `scopeGraphKey(definition)` in `frontend/src/domain/graph.ts` beside `semanticGraphKey` and use it in `useNodeVariables`; label and expression edits (the common case, including the measured rename) then send only /api/diagnostics. This must be combined with the X56 fix: today /api/variables shape-validates the whole draft, so with a structural key a 422 caused by an unrelated field (e.g. a blank label or a legacy property) would stick after that field is fixed; /api/variables must stop failing on shape problems that do not affect scopes, or the key must include them. Keep F10 (a pending or failed scope offers no variables). (2) Only if structural edits must also avoid the duplicate: add a new endpoint (e.g. `POST /api/studio/analysis` returning `{problems, variables}`), documented in docs/api.md and docs/openapi.yaml, and keep /api/variables and /api/diagnostics unchanged for API clients. Do not change the /api/diagnostics array payload.
- **Regression test:** `frontend/tests/unit/` (e.g. a new `graph-keys.spec.ts` or `document.spec.ts`): `scopeGraphKey` is unchanged by label and expression edits and changes with node add/delete, kind, result name, edge, handle/case ID and input name changes. A browser test (e.g. `frontend/tests/available-variables.spec.ts`): renaming the selected node sends one /api/diagnostics and no /api/variables request; adding a connection sends both. For option (2): API tests in `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/api/` and `scripts/studio_smoke.py`.
- **Related:** E15 (earlier duplicated diagnostics work, fixed with `ExpressionCache`), F10; X56 (/api/variables fails on any shape problem — same line RuleDefinitionService.java:63), X220 (variables keyed by the full semantic key; label edits blank the scope), X213 (shape cost), X160 (same endpoint).

<a id="x216"></a>

### X216 · low · efficiency · Binary operators allocate `Set.of` per evaluation; index path segments compile a regex per access
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-eff-be2.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / engine.expression (ExpressionRuntime, ExpressionParser, Functions) / Expression operators (`== != = <> < <= > >= + - * / % ^`) and property/index paths (`r.tags.0`, `$GET`, `$PLUCK`)
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExpressionRuntime.java:113` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExpressionRuntime.java:112-161`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExpressionRuntime.java:117`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExpressionParser.java:39-50`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExpressionParser.java:99-109`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExpressionParser.java:159-171`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/Functions.java:156-165`)
- **Problem:** Every evaluated comparison or arithmetic operator allocates one or two `Set.of` sets (ExpressionRuntime.java:113 and :117) to classify an operator the parser already classified in its `priority()` switch. `Functions.get` runs `part.matches("\\d{1,6}")` for each segment that indexes an array, which compiles a regex on every access, and then parses the segment twice. The `split("\\.")` uses String's single-character fast path, so re-splitting costs only allocations. The other `.matches` calls (ExpressionParser.java:151, 194) run only at compile time.
- **Trigger → consequence:** A/B, HEAD vs the same two classes patched only to use static final sets and a character-loop digit test (JIT warmed, best of 9, two alternating runs): `$MAP(items, x, x * 1.1 + 2 - x)` over 1,000 items 307–323 µs vs 231–238 µs (−26%, about 27 ns of about 93 ns per arithmetic op); `$FILTER(items, x, x > 10 && x != 50)` 169–172 vs 93–98 µs (−44%, about 38 ns per comparison); a scalar 10-operator expression 1.40–1.42 vs 1.09–1.12 µs (−21%); `$MAP(rows, r, r.tags.0)` 193–195 vs 102–145 µs; `$PLUCK(rows, "tags.0")` 167–171 vs 107–115 µs (about 55–90 ns per index segment). The finder's "3–4× slower index paths" is only partly the regex: after the patch `r.tags.0` is still 1.7–2.4× slower than `r.n` (60 µs) because of the extra segment and the parseInt calls. The absolute cost is tens of µs per maximum-size collection evaluation.
- **Evidence:** Probe `$SP/probes/v-eff-be2/ExprAbProbe.java`, run with the HEAD classpath and with `$SP/probes/v-eff-be2/patched/` first; the patched sources are in `$SP/probes/v-eff-be2/patch-src/` and differ from HEAD only in those lines.
- **Suggested fix:** Classify each operator once, at parse time. Add a package-private `enum BinaryOperator` in `engine.expression` (OR, AND, EQUAL, NOT_EQUAL, LESS, LESS_OR_EQUAL, GREATER, GREATER_OR_EQUAL, ADD, SUBTRACT, MULTIPLY, DIVIDE, REMAINDER, POWER) with `static BinaryOperator of(String token)` (mapping "==" and "=" to EQUAL, "!=" and "<>" to NOT_EQUAL, "&&"/"AND" and "||"/"OR") and `priority()`/`rightAssociative()` as exhaustive switches. `ExpressionParser.priority` and the operator switch at :99-109 read it, and `ExpressionRuntime.binary(BinaryOperator op, Object a, Object b)` becomes an exhaustive switch expression with no `Set.of` and no "Unknown operator" default. (Minimum alternative: hoist the two sets to `static final` constants.) For paths: split the dotted variable path once in `ExpressionParser.variable` and add `Functions.get(Object value, List<String> segments, Object fallback)`, used by compiled paths and by `$GET`/`$PLUCK`; test an index segment with a static helper `arrayIndex(String part)` that accepts 1–6 ASCII digits (`c >= '0' && c <= '9'`, not `Character.isDigit`, to keep `\d`'s ASCII-only meaning; leading zeros stay accepted) and parses once. Coordinate with X130, which changes how `Functions.get` splits paths. Semantics unchanged.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExpressionsTest.java` and `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExpressionCompatibilityTest.java`: every operator keeps its result, precedence and associativity (including the `=`/`<>` aliases, infix `AND`/`OR` and right-associative `^`); index paths `r.tags.0` and `$PLUCK(rows, "tags.0")`, a 6-digit index vs a 7-digit segment (fallback), a leading-zero segment (`tags.01`), a non-ASCII digit segment (fallback) and an out-of-range index (fallback). Record the before/after `ExprAbProbe.java` timing.
- **Related:** X130 (`$GET`/`$PLUCK` path-splitting bugs in the same `Functions.get`); earlier E12/E13 (expression hot-path fixes).

### X19 · low · convention · The node-kind/handle vocabulary guard matches only seven exact spellings
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-infra.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Tooling / scripts/check_architecture.py (CI step "Check module boundaries") / Architecture guard: no node-type or handle strings outside dev.arc.model
- **Location:** `scripts/check_architecture.py:32` (+ `scripts/check_architecture.py:30-40`, `scripts/check_architecture.py:77-79`, `docs/maintaining.md:201`, `backend/AGENTS.md:20`, `docs/review-lessons.md:105`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/persistence/JdbcRuleRepository.java:36`)
- **Problem:** `HAND_WRITTEN_NODE_VOCABULARY` (lines 32–40) matches only seven exact spellings: `.type().equals("K"`, `"K".equals(x.type()`, `case "K" ->`, a two-kind table, a `K|K` alternation, `next|true|false` and `"case:"`. Every other way of spelling a node kind or handle passes CI, although backend/AGENTS.md:20 says "the architecture script rejects node-type and handle strings elsewhere" and review-lessons.md:105 says it "rejects hand-written kind/handle vocabularies". No other enforcement exists: there is no ArchUnit or source-scanning test in backend/src/test.
- **Trigger → consequence:** Code outside dev.arc.model using any of these forms passes `python3 scripts/check_architecture.py` ("Architecture boundaries passed") and CI stays green: `case "CONDITION":`, `Objects.equals(node.type(), "SWITCH")`, `node.type().equalsIgnoreCase("OUTPUT")`, `type == "SWITCH"`, `Set.of("REFERENCE")`, `"default".equals(edge.sourceHandle())`, `edge.sourceHandle().equals("next")`, `Map.of("true", t, "false", f)`, `Set.of("next","true","false","default").contains(...)`. The string drift that lesson 105 removed from 13 files can come back unflagged: putting the pre-review (49bb365) rule/RuleSamples.java back unchanged, with `node("input", "INPUT", …)` and `edge("input", "condition", "next")`, passes the HEAD guard. A fix must stay context-sensitive: "FORMULA" is a rule kind (RuleService.java:27, JdbcRuleRepository.java:287), "SWITCH" is a function name (ExpressionRuntime.java:92, BuiltinFunctionCatalog.java:124), and "true"/"false" appear in Identifiers, ExpressionParser and the RuleSamples output expressions.
- **Evidence:** Private copy of the script and backend sources, `$SP/probes/v-infra/root`, with one-line probe files: exit 0 (pass) in dev/arc/rule for `case "CONDITION": break;`, `java.util.Objects.equals(node.type(), "SWITCH")`, `Objects.equals(type, "SWITCH")`, `"true".equals(handle) || "default".equals(handle)`, `"default".equals(edge.sourceHandle())`, `edge.sourceHandle().equals("next")`, `node.type().equalsIgnoreCase("OUTPUT")`, `java.util.Set.of("REFERENCE")`, `type == "SWITCH"`, `node.type().equals(("INPUT"))`; exit 0 in engine/validation for DefinitionShape's baseline `Set.of("next", "true", "false", "default").contains(edge.sourceHandle())` and BranchScopes' baseline `Map.of("true", test, "false", x)` on their own; exit 1 controls: `case "CONDITION" -> {}`, `node.type().equals("INPUT")`, `"INPUT".equals(node.type())`, `Set.of("INPUT", "OUTPUT")`, `"case:" + handle`. `git show 49bb365:backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleSamples.java` in the copy → "Architecture boundaries passed"; `$SP/probes/v-infra/baseline_vocab.py` shows RuleSamples.java is the one genuine vocabulary the HEAD regex misses among the 49bb365 files (`$SP/probes/v-infra/RuleSamples.head.java`).
- **Suggested fix:** Replace the spelling list with a literal-based rule plus a small, explained allowlist. After stripping comments, reject any Java string literal outside `dev/arc/model` whose content is exactly a node-kind name (`"INPUT"` … `"OUTPUT"`) or starts with `case:`, and any handle literal (`"next"`, `"true"`, `"false"`, `"default"`) in a comparison or collection context (argument of `equals`/`equalsIgnoreCase`/`Objects.equals`, operand of `==`/`!=`, a `case` label with `:` or `->`, an argument of `Set.of`/`Map.of`/`List.of`). Keep an allowlist keyed by file and literal, each with a reason: `engine/expression/ExpressionRuntime.java` and `engine/expression/BuiltinFunctionCatalog.java` → `"SWITCH"` (the `$SWITCH` function); `engine/expression/ExpressionParser.java`, `engine/expression/DataFunctions.java` and `engine/Identifiers.java` → `"true"`/`"false"` (boolean literals, reserved words). The rule-kind literals disappear once X16 moves them into `model.RuleKind`. The broadened rule also flags the SQL JSONPath `(@.type == "REFERENCE")` at JdbcRuleRepository.java:36 (observed while writing this entry: a node type spelled outside model today); build it from `NodeKind.REFERENCE.name()` or allowlist it with a reason. Make the script testable by moving the checks into functions that take a root path and return the violations (`main` prints and exits as today), and update the wording in docs/maintaining.md:201.
- **Regression test:** New `scripts/check_architecture_test.py` (stdlib `unittest`), run in the CI "Check module boundaries" step and listed in the AGENTS.md verification table: it builds a temporary tree with one-line probe files and asserts a violation for each spelling in the record, no violation for each allowlisted legitimate use, a violation for the 49bb365 RuleSamples.java content, and no violation for the current tree.
- **Related:** docs/review-lessons.md:105, docs/maintaining.md:201, backend/AGENTS.md:20; X16 (rule kinds as strings), X171 (the guard cannot check switch exhaustiveness), X20 (same script and test file).

### X20 · low · convention · The architecture check skips unknown top-level packages and the engine root package, and misses template-literal and glob imports
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-infra.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Tooling / scripts/check_architecture.py; backend engine root (ValueText, InputTypes) / Architecture guard: Java package boundaries and pure frontend modules
- **Location:** `scripts/check_architecture.py:74` (+ `scripts/check_architecture.py:74-75`, `scripts/check_architecture.py:86-90`, `scripts/check_architecture.py:115`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/ValueText.java:3`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/ValueText.java:30`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/ValueText.java:42`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/InputTypes.java:3`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/InputTypes.java:27`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/Functions.java:4`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/DataFunctions.java:4`, `docs/maintaining.md:34`, `docs/maintaining.md:198-199`)
- **Problem:** The Java check skips every top-level package it does not know (lines 74–75: `if module not in ALLOWED_JAVA: continue  # Spring application composition root.`), although maintaining.md:198 exempts only "The Spring composition root ArcApplication". Line 86 (`if module == "engine" and len(parts) > 2`) also skips every root `dev.arc.engine` class, so imports from root contracts into engine features are never checked. One such import already forms a root↔expression package cycle: engine/ValueText.java:3 imports `expression.Expressions` (used at :30 and :42), while expression/Functions.java:4 and DataFunctions.java:4 import `engine.ValueText`; engine/InputTypes.java:3 → Expressions is a one-way edge, not a cycle by itself. ValueText is new in 6fbbd07. Frontend imports written as template literals (``import(`…`)``) or `import.meta.glob(...)` also bypass the import regex (line 115); there is no current use of either in src.
- **Trigger → consequence:** (1) A new `dev/arc/web/WebConfig.java` (for example, the CORS bean moved out of ArcApplication) passes CI while it imports `dev.arc.persistence.JdbcRuleRepository`, `java.sql.Connection` and `org.springframework.jdbc.core.JdbcTemplate` and compares `node.type().equals("INPUT")`; the same imports in dev.arc.api are rejected. (2) A root contract importing `engine.execution.Engine`, `engine.script.ArcScript` and `engine.validation.Validator` passes, while the same import from engine.graph is rejected; such an import would make `expression` (allowed to use root contracts) depend on validation through the root package, contradicting maintaining.md:34. (3) A `domain/` module that loads ``import(`@xyflow/react`)`` or `import.meta.glob("../features/…")` passes; `import("@xyflow/react")` is rejected. The finder's `components/ → ../App` case also passes, but the documented rule names only features/, app/ and api/, so that part is a gap in spirit only.
- **Evidence:** Private copy of the script (`$SP/probes/v-infra/root`): `dev/arc/web/WebConfig.java` → [0] "Architecture boundaries passed"; the same imports in `dev/arc/api/ZzProbe.java` → [1] "api cannot depend on persistence … controllers must call application services …"; `engine/ProbeContract.java` importing execution/script/validation → [0]; `engine/graph/ZzProbe.java` importing execution.Engine → [1] "engine.graph cannot depend on engine.execution"; `domain/zzProbe.ts` with ``import(`@xyflow/react`)`` → [0], with `import.meta.glob("../features/editor/*.tsx")` → [0], with `import("@xyflow/react")` → [1] "pure domain and HTTP modules cannot import UI libraries". The dev.arc tree also has empty, untracked `bridge/` and `store/` directories, harmless today but skipped the same way.
- **Suggested fix:** (1) Exempt exactly `ArcApplication.java` by path and reject any other file whose top-level package is not in `ALLOWED_JAVA` ("unknown package X: add it to ALLOWED_JAVA and the backend table in maintaining.md"). (2) Check root engine classes: add an explicit root entry (other root contracts, `model`, `error`) and apply it when `len(parts) == 2`. (3) Resolve the existing cycle instead of allowlisting it: move the value-bound policy ValueText and InputTypes need (`Expressions.number`, `Expressions.bounded`, their private bound checks and the `type(value)` helper used in the message) into a root contract such as `dev.arc.engine.ValueBounds` beside `Limits`; keep `Expressions.number/bounded` as delegating entry points or migrate their other callers (GraphExecution.java:216 and :265, HttpSource.java:201, LookupSourceAdapter.java:38 and :64); add `ValueBounds` to the root-contract lists in `ENGINE_DEPENDENCIES` and docs/maintaining.md:199. (4) Frontend: match both quoted and template-literal specifiers in `import(...)`, and reject a non-literal `import(` argument and any `import.meta.glob` in pure, transport and shared-UI modules; optionally extend the shared-controls rule to root files such as `App.tsx`/`main.tsx`. Update docs/maintaining.md:198-199 to state the exemptions.
- **Regression test:** The new `scripts/check_architecture_test.py` (see X19): an unknown package importing JDBC fails, ArcApplication passes, a root engine contract importing `engine.execution` fails, a root contract importing only `Limits`/`model` passes, and a `domain/` file with a template-literal `import()` or an `import.meta.glob` fails; the current tree passes after the ValueBounds move. `mvn -f backend/pom.xml verify` proves the move; `ValueTextTest`, `ExpressionsTest` and `LookupSourceAdapterTest` stay green.
- **Related:** docs/maintaining.md:34 and :198-199; X19 (same script and test file); commit 6fbbd07 (introduced ValueText).

### X21 · low · convention · Frontend tests are never type-checked; HEAD already has 8 type errors in 6 test files
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-infra.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Tooling / frontend (tsconfig.json, package.json scripts), CI / Type checking of frontend unit specs and Playwright workflows
- **Location:** `frontend/tsconfig.json:17` (+ `frontend/package.json:8`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml:35-40`, `README.md:339`, `frontend/tests/reference-picker.spec.ts:14`, `frontend/tests/reference-picker.spec.ts:64`, `frontend/tests/studio-reuse.spec.ts:96`, `frontend/tests/unit/branches.spec.ts:31-32`, `frontend/tests/unit/records.spec.ts:28`, `frontend/tests/unit/switch.spec.ts:110`, `frontend/src/types.ts:29`)
- **Problem:** `"include": ["src"]` limits `npm run build` (`tsc -b && vite build`) to type-checking src, and Playwright strips types without checking them. So no gate type-checks any of the 108 tracked .ts files under frontend/tests (the finder said 74; there are 69 top-level specs and 37 unit specs). HEAD already has 8 type errors in 6 test files, and 3 more files need @types/node, which is not installed.
- **Trigger → consequence:** After a change to a types.ts contract or a domain/transport signature, stale test fixtures still run and stay green. At HEAD: tests/reference-picker.spec.ts:64 sends `bindings: { amount: 7 }` although `RuleNode.bindings` is `Record<string, string> | null` (types.ts:29), and reference-picker.spec.ts:14 omits the required `defaultValue`; the fixtures have silently drifted from the contract. CI step "Check frontend formatting, unit tests, types and build" (ci.yml:35) and README.md:339 ("type check") refer to this src-only check: accurate for src, but nothing type-checks the tests.
- **Evidence:** `tsc -p $SP/probes/v-infra/tsconfig.tests.json` (extends frontend/tsconfig.json, includes tests/**/*.ts), output in `$SP/probes/v-infra/tsc-tests.out`: reference-picker.spec.ts(14,7) TS2741 and (64,21) TS2322; studio-reuse.spec.ts(96,13) TS2769 and (96,21) TS7006; unit/branches.spec.ts(31,15) TS2550 `toReversed` and (32,13) TS7006; unit/records.spec.ts(28,3) TS2322; unit/switch.spec.ts(110,16) TS2550 `isWellFormed`; TS2307/TS2580 for node:fs, process and Buffer in style-cascade, style-equivalence and style-snapshot. A unit spec containing `const count: string = 5;` and a RuleNode with `bindings: { amount: 7 }`, run with `npx playwright test tests/unit/zz-v-infra-types.spec.ts`, gave "1 passed (307ms)" (spec deleted afterwards).
- **Suggested fix:** Add `frontend/tsconfig.tests.json` extending tsconfig.json with `include: ["tests", "src/vite-env.d.ts", "playwright.config.ts"]` and `lib: ["ES2024", "DOM", "DOM.Iterable"]` (the tests run in Node 24 and Chromium, which provide `toReversed` and `isWellFormed`; `src` stays on ES2022). Add a script `"typecheck:tests": "tsc -p tsconfig.tests.json"` and run it in the CI frontend step (ci.yml:35-40), the AGENTS.md verification table (Frontend logic/UI row) and README.md:339. Decision needed for Node typings: add `@types/node` as a devDependency (recommended: type-only, no runtime or bundle effect; AGENTS.md requires the user's approval for a new dependency) or exclude the three style specs and `tests/style-snapshot.ts` from the tests config. Fix the 8 errors to match the contract, not to silence it: `defaultValue: null` at reference-picker.spec.ts:14; `bindings: { amount: "7" }` at :64 (bindings are expression text); two explicit `page.on("requestfinished", …)`/`page.on("requestfailed", …)` calls instead of the loop over event names at studio-reuse.spec.ts:96; in records.spec.ts:28 write the prototype-named key through a cast or `Object.defineProperty` (the test deliberately uses `valueOf`); the branches/switch errors disappear with the ES2024 lib.
- **Regression test:** The CI step is the guard: `npm --prefix frontend run typecheck:tests` must pass on the fixed tree. Verify once locally that a deliberately wrong fixture (e.g. `const count: string = 5;` in a unit spec) makes it fail.
- **Related:** .github/workflows/ci.yml:35, README.md:339, AGENTS.md verification table; F22 (its coverage in unit/records.spec.ts is one of the affected files).

### X18 · low · cleanup · Refuted: nginx `proxy_set_header Host $host` does not create a Docker-only CORS asymmetry
- **Status:** REFUTED — record: `verdicts/v-infra.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Infra / frontend/nginx.conf, frontend/vite.config.ts / Docker web proxy: Host header and CORS for UI writes
- **Location:** `frontend/nginx.conf:24` (+ `frontend/vite.config.ts:10`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/ArcApplication.java:18-25`, `scripts/smoke.py:285-288`)
- **Problem:** The candidate claimed that `proxy_set_header Host $host` drops the published port, so Spring treats the bundled UI's writes as CORS requests only behind nginx, while native dev (Vite) treats them as same-origin; a method missing from `allowedMethods` would then fail only in Docker. The verifier refuted the asymmetry: `$host` does drop the port, but the native dev proxy also sends a Host that differs from the page's Origin, so the same requests are CORS in native dev too.
- **Trigger → consequence:** None. A method missing from `allowedMethods` gets 403 in native dev exactly as in Docker; the DELETE entry in b8aa4ff was needed in both environments and for real cross-origin clients, which smoke.py:285–288 tests with Origin https://example.com. The backend builds no absolute URL from Host (no UriComponentsBuilder, getRequestURL, getServerName or Location use). CORS allowing any origin is an intentional product choice. The proposed `$http_host` would create a Docker-vs-dev asymmetry rather than remove one.
- **Evidence:** frontend/vite.config.ts:10 uses the string shorthand `"/api": apiTarget`; Vite 8.3.0 turns it into `{ target: opts, changeOrigin: true }` (node_modules/vite/dist/node/chunks/node.js:19375–19378), and `changeOrigin` sets the forwarded Host to the target (`localhost:8080`, node.js:18439) while the browser's Origin stays `http://localhost:3080`; `rewriteOriginHeader` runs only for WebSockets with `rewriteWsOrigin`. `$SP/probes/v-infra/vite-proxy-probe.mjs` (Vite createServer with the same proxy): the echo target received `{"host":"127.0.0.1:3198","origin":"http://127.0.0.1:3197","method":"DELETE"}`; pointed at the real API (:8191), a DELETE from the Vite page origin returned "404 ACAO = *". curl DELETE :8191/api/rules/rv-v-infra-missing with `Origin: http://localhost:3080` → `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`; with `Origin: http://localhost:8191` → no ACAO; via :3191 with Origin http://localhost:3191 → ACAO.
- **Suggested fix:** No change. Keep `proxy_set_header Host $host` and do not switch to `$http_host`. The rule that already applies in both environments stands: add every new HTTP method to the CORS mapping in `ArcApplication` and test it with an Origin header and a preflight (B26).
- **Regression test:** None needed. Existing coverage: `scripts/smoke.py:285-288` (OPTIONS preflight and DELETE with `Origin: https://example.com`) and `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/api/RuleDeletionWebTest.java`.
- **Related:** B26 (CORS methods); X23 and X17 (other findings in frontend/nginx.conf).
