### X96 · low · bug · A no-op node-code Apply adopts the server's build echo, marking a clean draft dirty and dropping the preview
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-editor1.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/editor (`Editor.tsx` node-code `onApply`, `documentState`), with backend engine.script (`ArcScript.replaceNode`) / Node expression dialog "Apply to graph"
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/editor/Editor.tsx:384` (+ `frontend/src/features/editor/useNodeExpressionDraft.ts:95`, `frontend/src/features/editor/documentState.ts:50`, `frontend/src/features/editor/documentState.ts:120`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/script/ArcScript.java:120`, `frontend/tests/unit/document.spec.ts:406`)
- **Problem:** Node-code Apply replaces the whole draft with the `/studio/node/build` echo (`edit(() => built)`). The echo writes out every node with explicit nulls in record key order, and the server's `replaceNode` moves the applied node's outgoing edges to the end of `edges` (ArcScript.java:120-124: the loop over edges, then `edges.addAll(fragment.edges())`). The reducer short-circuits only on identity (documentState.ts:50), and unlike `acknowledgeSave` (documentState.ts:120, `sameDefinition`) this path has no semantic comparison; `sameDefinition` alone would not suffice, because it respects array order (document.spec.ts:406). So an unchanged Apply marks a clean draft dirty and changes `semanticGraphKey`: the preview result and trace are dropped, and diagnostics and variables are fetched again. This is the F1/K57 symptom through a path that skips `sameDefinition`, and the trigger is broader than the finder said: it also happens on a freshly loaded rule, not only after local node creation.
- **Trigger → consequence:** (a) Open a server-loaded rule input→calculate→output with edges [a,b]. Test rule → Run test gives 20. Click `</>` on Inputs → Apply to graph without editing: the header shows "Unsaved changes", Save is enabled, the test result is gone, and /api/diagnostics and /api/variables are fetched again; the echo's edges are [b,a]. (b) Add node → Save draft → a no-op Apply on Result gives "Unsaved changes": acknowledgeSave kept the locally created node without null keys, and the echo returns it with nulls in record order. Control: a no-op Apply on the Output of a server-loaded draft stays "All changes saved" and keeps the result.
- **Evidence:** Editor.tsx:384 `onApply={(built) => edit(() => built)}`; documentState.ts:50 `if (draft === state.rule.draft) return state;`. API probe `$SP/probes/v-editor1/echo.py` on :8191: the node build of "input" changed the edges from ['e1','e2'] to ['e2','e1']; a local node `{"id":"spare",…,"position":{"x":400,"y":200}}` came back as `{…,"position":{"x":400.0,"y":200.0},"expression":"1","output":"spare","ruleId":null,…,"outputName":null}`. Browser probe on :3191 (reads made after the Apply click): control `FormulaAll changes saved`, result shown 1, reads ["diagnostics"] (the dialog's own check); Inputs no-op Apply `FormulaUnsaved changes`, result shown 0, reads ["diagnostics","variables","diagnostics"], Save enabled true; add node → save → no-op Apply on Result `FormulaUnsaved changes`, reads ["diagnostics","variables","diagnostics"].
- **Suggested fix:** Apply only what the fragment owns, and keep the local draft when nothing changed. Add a pure `applyNodeFragment(current, nodeId, built)` to `domain/graph` (applied through `edit`, or as a `node/applied` document action) that mirrors the server's ownership rule: take the built node for `nodeId`, but keep the local node object when the two are `sameDefinition`-equal (canonical JSON, ignoring key order and nulls); replace the node's outgoing edges in place, keeping the local edge objects and their order when the set is unchanged; replace `inputs` only for an INPUT fragment and only when they changed; keep every other node, incoming edge and note as the local object; and return `current` itself when nothing changed, so the reducer's identity check keeps the draft clean and `semanticGraphKey` stable. Optionally make `ArcScript.replaceNode` insert the fragment's edges where the first replaced edge was instead of appending them, so the echo preserves order for API clients too.
- **Regression test:** frontend/tests/unit/document.spec.ts (document transitions and save echoes): a build echo with explicit nulls and edges [b,a] for an unchanged fragment returns the same draft object and leaves the document clean; a changed fragment replaces only that node and its outgoing edges. Browser: frontend/tests/preview-session.spec.ts: Run test, `</>` on Inputs, Apply without editing → "All changes saved", the result is still shown, and no /api/variables request follows the Apply. If `replaceNode` changes, backend `NodeEditorTest` asserts that a no-op build keeps the edge order.
- **Related:** lesson F1 and K57 of the previous review (`sameDefinition`), F3 (preview identity uses `semanticGraphKey`); maintainability lesson "A node fragment replaces only its own node and outgoing edges, plus Input parameters"; X90 (same Apply path writes a stale fragment).

<a id="x97"></a>

### X97 · low · bug · A route-driven switch to the graph is dropped while another command runs; a failed build then shows code under the graph URL with an inverted toggle
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-editor1.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/editor (`useRuleDocument` route-arrival effect, `EditorHeader`) / graph/code view switching (Code studio ↔ graph)
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/editor/useRuleDocument.ts:309` (+ `frontend/src/features/editor/useRuleDocument.ts:155`, `frontend/src/features/editor/useRuleDocument.ts:305`, `frontend/src/features/editor/editorCapabilities.ts:115`, `frontend/src/features/editor/EditorHeader.tsx:98`, `frontend/src/features/editor/Editor.tsx:246`, `docs/maintaining.md:131`)
- **Problem:** The route-driven graph-switch effect runs only on `[mode]`. It calls `runTask("switch")`, which returns silently while another command holds the lock (line 155, `if (!signal || signal.aborted || !idle) return;`), and it is never retried. If that command's build fails, `sourceDirty` stays set, `shownView` keeps the code view (editorCapabilities.ts:115), and the header toggle, driven by the route `mode` (Editor.tsx:246 → EditorHeader.tsx:98), is inverted. docs/maintaining.md:131's "unbuilt code builds before the graph shows (a failed build returns to the code)" never happens. The do-not-report item covers only refusing a route switch up front; this is a separate failure of the on-arrival reconciliation.
- **Trigger → consequence:** Code studio with code that does not build → click Build graph while the request is pending → browser Back to #/rules/X (no prompt) → the build fails. The URL is #/rules/X, the code editor is shown, the canvas is absent, and the header button reads "Code editor". Clicking the toggle navigates to /studio without a build. A later successful Build or Save flips to the graph unexpectedly. No data is lost.
- **Evidence:** useRuleDocument.ts:305-317, an effect with deps `[mode]` that calls `latest.runTask("switch", …)`; line 155; editorCapabilities.ts:115 `if (sourceDirty) return "code";`; EditorHeader.tsx:98 `{mode === "code" ? "Graph view" : "Code editor"}`, fed by Editor.tsx:246 `mode={mode}`. Browser probe with /api/studio/build held: `url: #/rules/rv-v-editor1-x97-…`, `build requests total: 1` (the switch never built), `code editor visible: 1`, `canvas visible: 0`, `header toggle: Code editor`, alert `Expected inputs { ... } or node id TYPE "Label" …`, prompts [].
- **Suggested fix:** Apply the arrival rule whenever it is due, not once: re-run the effect when the lock is released (deps `[mode, busy]`, returning early while `busy !== ""`), so a graph arrival with unbuilt code builds after the running command ends and bounces to the code on failure (through X48's corrective redirect). To avoid a second build of a buffer that has just failed, remember the source whose build failed and bounce directly for that same buffer; that also cannot loop, because a direct bounce changes no dependency. Drive EditorHeader's toggle label and icon, and `switchView`'s direction, from the shown `view` (`shownView`) instead of the route `mode`, so the header always describes what is on screen. Note in docs/maintaining.md:131 that the arrival rules apply once the running command ends.
- **Regression test:** frontend/tests/editor-commands.spec.ts: hold /api/studio/build, click Build graph on code that does not build, go Back to #/rules/X, release the failing response → the URL returns to #/studio/X, and the header reads "Graph view" whenever the code editor is visible; with a succeeding build the canvas shows. frontend/tests/unit/editor-capabilities.spec.ts: if the header derives its label from `shownView`, route `graph` with `sourceDirty` gives "Graph view".
- **Related:** X48 (the same arrival rules bounce with a history push), X100 (the header Test toggle also runs through the lock and a build); lesson F3 (builds belong to their editor session); do-not-report item "route-driven switches cannot be refused up front" (this is a new consequence).

<a id="x90"></a>

### X90 · low · bug · Node code opens during Arrange with a stale buffer, and Apply writes the pre-arrange position back
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-editor1.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/editor (`Editor.openNodeCode`, `useNodeExpressionDraft`) / Node expression dialog during Arrange graph
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/editor/Editor.tsx:123` (+ `frontend/src/features/editor/Editor.tsx:193`, `frontend/src/features/editor/useNodeExpressionDraft.ts:40`, `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/GraphNode.tsx:61`)
- **Problem:** `openNodeCode` (the card `</>` button and the inspector's Node expression) checks only invalid defaults, not the command lock, so the node-code dialog opens read-only while Arrange runs; `openNodeEditor` (193-196) checks `!can.edit`, and the GraphNode `</>` button has no disabled state. The dialog's buffer is set once from the first render (`current ?? renderedSource`) and never refreshed. When Arrange lands, the dialog becomes editable but still shows the pre-arrange `at (x, y)`, and Apply writes that stale position back.
- **Trigger → consequence:** Click Arrange graph. While the lazy graphLayout chunk is pending, click a card's `</>`. The dialog opens read-only with `at (700.0, 500.0)`. Arrange moves the card to (30, 204), and the dialog becomes editable still showing (700.0, 500.0). Apply runs buildNode(arranged draft, stale source): that node jumps back to (700, 500) while the others keep the arranged layout, and Save persists the mixed layout. Only positions are affected, because Arrange changes nothing else. The window is the layout chunk load or layout run.
- **Evidence:** Editor.tsx:121-126 `if (!blockedByInvalidDefault("opening node code")) openCode(id);`, versus 193-196 `if (!can.edit || blockedByInvalidDefault(...)) return;`; useNodeExpressionDraft.ts:40 `setSource((current) => current ?? renderedSource);`. Browser probe with `assets/graphLayout-*.js` held: `dialog during arrange: … at (700.0, 500.0)`, `apply button during arrange: 0`, `dialog after arrange: … at (700.0, 500.0)`, `arranged transform: translate(30px, 204px)`, `after apply transform: translate(700px, 500px)`, `saved positions: [["input",{"x":30,"y":30}],["calculate",{"x":700,"y":500}],["output",{"x":30,"y":378}]]`.
- **Suggested fix:** Fix the snapshot buffer with the rule F25 already uses for test inputs: an untouched buffer follows its source, and an edited one is kept. In `useNodeExpressionDraft`, record whether the user edited the buffer (in `changeSource`), and while it is untouched replace `source` with every new render of the current `definition` (lines 37-41); a dialog opened during Arrange then shows and applies the arranged position. Also refuse opening node code while a command holds the lock (check `commandsIdle`/`can.validate` in `openNodeCode`, not `can.edit`, because published versions must still open it read-only) and disable the card `</>` while busy, matching the context-menu rule.
- **Regression test:** frontend/tests/editor-commands.spec.ts (it already holds the layout chunk in "leaving the graph while Arrange waits for its layout module cannot keep the editor locked"): hold `assets/graphLayout-*.js`, click Arrange, click a card's `</>` (or assert it is disabled), release; the dialog shows the arranged `at (x, y)`, Apply keeps the arranged transform, and the saved positions equal the arranged layout.
- **Related:** lessons F8 (Arrange and the command lock), F25 (untouched buffers follow their source); maintainability lesson on the command gate ("every entry point re-checks the same rules against the running command"); X96 (same Apply path); the finder (b2) reports it pre-existing at 49bb365.

<a id="x48"></a>

### X48 · low · bug · A refused route-driven view switch bounces with a history push, trapping browser Back
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-editor1.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/editor (`useRuleDocument` arrival effects) and app (`useWorkspaceNavigation`) / graph/code view switching and browser history
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/editor/useRuleDocument.ts:313` (+ `frontend/src/features/editor/useRuleDocument.ts:264`, `frontend/src/app/useWorkspaceNavigation.ts:116`, `frontend/src/app/useWorkspaceNavigation.ts:55`)
- **Problem:** When a route-driven view switch is refused after the fact (a failed build at :313, or an invalid default at :264), the editor bounces with `navigate()`, which pushes a new history entry (`window.location.hash = path`, useWorkspaceNavigation.ts:116). Each single-step browser Back then lands on the refused route again and bounces, so Back cannot get past the rule while the condition lasts. This is a new consequence of the known "cannot be refused up front" limitation, and it goes against lesson F24 (do not damage history).
- **Trigger → consequence:** (1) #/library → #/rules/X → Code editor (#/studio/X) → type code that does not build → Back three times. Each time the URL returns to #/studio/X, the history stays at index 3 with the same entries, and no prompt appears. The push keeps #/rules/X as the Back target; replacing the arrival entry would let the next Back reach #/library and its leave prompt. (2) Set an invalid ARRAY default "[" → sidebar Code studio. The bounce pushes an extra #/rules/X after #/studio/X, and every later Back re-enters #/studio/X and bounces; `history.go(-1)` there would avoid the trap. The user must fix the code or default, or leave through in-app links. No data is lost.
- **Evidence:** useRuleDocument.ts:313 ``if (!signal.aborted) latest.navigate(`/studio/${latest.ruleId}`);`` and :264 ``navigate(`/rules/${rule.id}`);``; useWorkspaceNavigation.ts:116 `window.location.hash = path;`. CDP probe (1): before, `{"entries":["","#/library","#/rules/X","#/studio/X"],"index":3}`; after Back #1, #2 and #3 the same entries, index 3, URL #/studio/X, prompts []. Probe (2): after the sidebar click `[…,"#/rules/X","#/studio/X","#/rules/X"]` at index 4; after Back #1–#3 still index 4 and URL #/rules/X.
- **Suggested fix:** Give `useWorkspaceNavigation` one corrective operation for refused arrivals, e.g. `redirect(path)`, separate from `navigate`: it never asks and never adds an entry. The hashchange handler already knows how the shown entry was reached (no recorded position means a fresh push, a recorded one means a traversal); keep that in `shown.current`. If the refused entry was just pushed (sidebar link, typed URL), undo it with `history.go(-1)`, the traversal-undo mechanism of F24, marking the target as confirmed so it does not ask; if it was reached by Back or Forward, replace the arrival entry with the shown view (`history.replaceState({ ...state, arcEntry: position }, "", "#" + path)` plus `setRoute`), so the next Back continues past the rule. Use it at useRuleDocument.ts:264 and :313. Record in F24 and docs/maintaining.md that a refused arrival is corrected in place, while cancelled prompts keep using `history.go(delta)`.
- **Regression test:** frontend/tests/workspace-navigation.spec.ts (CDP `Page.getNavigationHistory`, as for F24): (1) code that does not build, Back from #/studio/X → no extra entry is created and a second Back reaches #/library with its leave prompt; (2) invalid default + sidebar Code studio → after the bounce the entries equal those before the click, and one Back reaches the previous page. Extend frontend/tests/editor-buffer-guards.spec.ts ("unfinished defaults survive canvas, outline, node-code and sidebar view changes") to check the history, not only the URL.
- **Related:** X97 (same arrival reconciliation); lessons F24, F23; do-not-report item "route-driven switches cannot be refused up front" (this is a new consequence).

<a id="x98"></a>

### X98 · low · bug · "Delete connection" stays visible after the selected connection is removed with its node
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-editor2.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/editor/canvas (`GraphCanvas`, `useGraphCommands`) and `Editor.tsx` selection state / graph canvas connection deletion
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/GraphCanvas.tsx:312` (+ `frontend/src/features/editor/Editor.tsx:117`, `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/useGraphCommands.ts:59`, `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/GraphCanvas.tsx:114`, `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/GraphCanvas.tsx:120`, `frontend/src/features/editor/documentState.ts:50`)
- **Problem:** `selectedEdge` (EditorContent state, Editor.tsx:117) is never checked against the draft. When the selected connection is removed along with its node, through the context-menu Delete or the Inspector's "Delete node", the canvas keeps showing "Delete connection". Clicking it runs `removeEdge`, whose updater returns `current`, so the reducer returns the same state, but `edit` still returns true and the button is hidden. This is the edge counterpart of the F5/K55 node-selection fallback (`nodeSelection.ts`), which was only applied to nodes. A code build that drops the selected edge ID (the user deletes that `edge "id"` line) should have the same effect after returning to the graph, because `selectedEdge` survives the view switch; that path was not probed.
- **Trigger → consequence:** Click edge calc→out ("finish"), then right-click node calc and choose Delete; alternatively select calc, click the edge, then use the Inspector's Delete node. `removeGraphNode` drops both edges, `removeNode` only moves the node selection, and nothing calls `setSelectedEdge(null)`, so the button stays for a connection that no longer exists; clicking it does nothing except hide it. Impact is limited because a later pane or node click clears the state.
- **Evidence:** GraphCanvas.tsx:312 `{selectedEdge && !readOnly && (` has no check against `definition.edges`; useGraphCommands.ts:59-60 `if (edit((current) => removeGraphNode(current, id))) selectNode(next?.id || "");` does not reset the edge; GraphCanvas.tsx:114 `if (!current.edges.some((edge) => edge.id === id)) return current;` and :120 `if (removed && selectedEdge === id) setSelectedEdge(null);`; documentState.ts:50 `if (draft === state.rule.draft) return state;`. Browser probe on :3191 with the real API: context-menu path `X98a edges left: 0; Delete connection buttons: 1`, and after clicking the button `edges 0, nodes 2, buttons 0`; Inspector path `X98b edges left: 0; Delete connection buttons: 1`.
- **Suggested fix:** Derive the selected connection from the current draft, like the selected node: add `selectedEdgeId(definition, requestedId): string | null` beside `selectedNode` in `nodeSelection.ts` (it returns the ID only while `definition.edges` contains it), compute it once in EditorContent and pass the derived value to `useGraphCanvas` (edge highlight) and `GraphCanvas` (the button). Every path that removes an edge (node deletion, code builds, version loads) is then covered without each one calling `setSelectedEdge(null)`.
- **Regression test:** frontend/tests/edge-context-menu.spec.ts: select a connection, then delete its source node through the node context menu and, separately, through the Inspector → "Delete connection" disappears at once. frontend/tests/unit/document.spec.ts, next to "the selection falls back to the default node when a loaded version or build removes the selected node": `selectedEdgeId` returns null for an edge that is missing from the draft.
- **Related:** lesson F5 and K55 of the previous review (selection derived from the current draft); X99 (same node-deletion command).

<a id="x99"></a>

### X99 · low · bug · Deleting an unselected node moves the selection to the Input node
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-editor2.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/editor/canvas (`useGraphCommands.removeNode`, `GraphCanvas` context menu) / graph canvas node deletion
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/useGraphCommands.ts:60` (+ `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/useGraphCommands.ts:56`, `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/GraphCanvas.tsx:109`, `frontend/src/features/editor/Editor.tsx:327`, `frontend/src/features/editor/nodeSelection.ts:16`)
- **Problem:** After any accepted deletion, `removeNode` selects the Input node (or the first other node), even when the deleted node was not selected. The canvas context menu reuses this helper for any node, so deleting another node moves the Inspector away from the node being edited. At 5bab5f0 the only caller was the Inspector's `onDelete={removeNode}`, which always deletes the selected node; the context menu (10c2437) added `onDeleteNode={removeNode}` for any node. `selectedNode()` in nodeSelection.ts already falls back when the selected node disappears, so the unconditional move is not needed even for the selected-node case. The docs only say "Delete removes the node and its connections" (studio.md:244, architecture.md:82), and no test asserts the jump.
- **Trigger → consequence:** Select "Calculate" so the Inspector shows it. Right-click the unselected "Spare" and choose Delete. The Inspector and the canvas selection jump to "Inputs". No data is lost, because Inspector edits are already in the draft, but the user loses their place and the Inspector's UI state (collapsed sections, pinned variable panel).
- **Evidence:** useGraphCommands.ts:56-60 `const next = definition.nodes.find((node) => node.type === "INPUT") || definition.nodes.find((node) => node.id !== id); if (edit((current) => removeGraphNode(current, id))) selectNode(next?.id || "");`; GraphCanvas.tsx:109 `if (menuNode) onDeleteNode(menuNode.id);`; Editor.tsx:327 `onDeleteNode={removeNode}`. Browser probe: `X99 selected after deleting unselected Spare: Inputs; selected card: input` (before the delete, the Node name field was "Calculate").
- **Suggested fix:** Move the selection only when the deleted node is the selected one: pass `selected` into `useGraphCommands` and call `selectNode(next?.id || "")` only if `id === selected` after an accepted edit. This keeps today's Inspector-delete behavior (Input is selected) and fixes the context menu. The alternative, dropping the explicit move and relying on `selectedNode()`, would select `defaultSelection` (the first Condition, else the first node) instead of Input after an Inspector delete; if chosen, state that single fallback in nodeSelection.ts.
- **Regression test:** frontend/tests/node-context-menu.spec.ts ("node context menu edits in a roomy form, cancels safely, and deletes its target and connections"): with "Calculate" selected, deleting "Spare" from the context menu keeps "Node name" = "Calculate"; deleting the selected node from the Inspector still selects Input.
- **Related:** X98 (same deletion path, selection reconciliation); lesson F5 and K55 of the previous review (selection derived from the draft); introduced when the context menu (10c2437) reused the Inspector-only helper from 5bab5f0.

<a id="x100"></a>

### X100 · low · bug · The header's "Hide test" builds pending code first, so unbuildable code keeps the Test panel open
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-editor2.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/editor (`useRuleDocument.toggleTest`, `EditorHeader`) / Test panel (preview) in Code studio
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/editor/useRuleDocument.ts:397` (+ `frontend/src/features/editor/useRuleDocument.ts:394`, `frontend/src/features/editor/Editor.tsx:259`, `frontend/src/features/execution/TestPanel.tsx:64`, `frontend/src/features/editor/editorCapabilities.ts:39`, `docs/maintaining.md:139`)
- **Problem:** The header Test toggle awaits `buildCode()` before toggling, in both directions. The only reason given (useRuleDocument.ts:394, "since preview runs the graph") applies to opening; closing runs nothing. In the code view with unbuildable edits, "Hide test" fails with the build error and the panel stays open, while the panel's own X (`preview.close`) closes it without building. When the buffer does build, hiding the panel commits it into the graph as a side effect. This became reachable with F25 (133ca20), which keeps the panel visible while code is unbuilt; at 49bb365 the panel was not rendered while `sourceDirty` (`{!sourceDirty && testPanel}`).
- **Trigger → consequence:** Open /studio/<id> and click Test rule; the panel opens. Edit the code into an unfinished statement and click "Hide test". The alert "Unfinished statement or quoted string" appears and the panel stays open; the X closes it at once. The comment at editorCapabilities.ts:39 says "Open or close the Test panel, building pending code first", while docs/maintaining.md:139 describes closing with no build precondition ("Closing the panel discards the result and a pending run but keeps inputs and options").
- **Evidence:** useRuleDocument.ts:395-399 `const toggleTest = (toggle: () => void) => runTask("test", async () => { await buildCode(); toggle(); });`; Editor.tsx:259 `onToggleTest={() => void toggleTest(preview.toggle)}` versus TestPanel.tsx `aria-label="Close test panel" ... onClick={preview.close}`. Browser probe: `X100 after Hide test: test panel open: 1; editor alerts: ["Unfinished statement or quoted string"]`, then `X100 after panel X: test panel open: 0`.
- **Suggested fix:** Build only when opening. Replace `toggleTest(toggle)` with a command that knows the direction, e.g. `toggleTest(preview)`: when the panel is open it calls `preview.close()` directly (no lock, no build, the same as the panel's X); when it is closed it keeps `runTask("test", async () => { await buildCode(); preview.toggle(); })`. Update the capability comment (editorCapabilities.ts:39) to "Open the Test panel, building pending code first; closing never builds."
- **Regression test:** frontend/tests/preview-session.spec.ts ("preview inputs, options and result survive view switches and unbuilt code"): in the code view with the panel open and an unbuildable edit, "Hide test" closes the panel, shows no error and sends no /api/studio/build request; with a buildable edit, "Hide test" leaves the code unbuilt and the graph unchanged.
- **Related:** lesson F25; X97 (header controls routed through the command lock).

<a id="x89"></a>

### X89 · low · bug · "Show problem" from the code view is overridden by the remounted canvas's initial fitView
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-editor2.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/editor/canvas (`useGraphFocus`, `useGraphCanvas`, `GraphCanvas`) / "Show problem" jump from Code studio to the graph
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/useGraphFocus.ts:63` (+ `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/useGraphFocus.ts:58`, `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/useGraphCanvas.ts:39`, `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/GraphCanvas.tsx:227`)
- **Problem:** The pending-focus gate `measurements.has(pendingFocus)` reads card sizes that useGraphCanvas keeps in EditorContent across the canvas unmount. Once the graph has been shown, "Show problem" from the code view focuses on the first frame of the remounted canvas, and React Flow's queued initial `fitView` then replaces the viewport with the whole graph, so the problem node is not centered and can end up off-screen. Mechanism: GraphCanvas unmount resets the editor's ReactFlowProvider store (StoreUpdater cleanup `reset()`). On remount, StoreUpdater applies 'nodes' before 'fitView', so the fit stays queued until the node ResizeObserver calls `updateNodeInternals`; that happens after the rAF-scheduled `setCenter` in the same frame, and d3-zoom's immediate transform interrupts the 350 ms setCenter transition. This predates the previous review: 49bb365 had `!measurements[pendingFocus]`.
- **Trigger → consequence:** A rule whose failing Output "Far result" (`1 / 0`) sits at (3000, 2400). Open /rules/<id> in the graph, click Code editor, Test rule, Run test, then "Show problem · Far result". The graph ends at `translate(137.25px, -76.75px) scale(0.25)`, identical to pressing Fit View; the node box (y 732.75) sits below the canvas bottom (679), with the Test panel open. The Inspector does select the node. Opening /studio/<id> directly (no earlier measurements) and doing the same ends centered at scale(1), as do the in-graph jump and `?node=far`.
- **Evidence:** useGraphFocus.ts:58-71 (`!measurements.has(pendingFocus)`, then `requestAnimationFrame(() => { focusNode(pendingFocus); setPendingFocus(null); })`); useGraphCanvas.ts:39 `const [measurements, setMeasurements] = useState<NodeSizes>(() => new Map());`; GraphCanvas.tsx:227-228 `fitView fitViewOptions={initialFit}`. Frame-by-frame viewport probe after clicking Show problem (spec deleted): graph-first went `translate(0px, 0px) scale(1) @89ms → translate(137.25px, -76.75px) scale(0.25) @101ms` and stayed there (`far inside canvas: false`); code-first went `... scale(0.25) @112ms`, then animated to `translate(-2574px, -2215px) scale(1) @486ms` (`far inside canvas: true`); in-graph jump `scale(1)`; deep link `translate(-2574px, -2068px) scale(1)`.
- **Suggested fix:** Give each canvas mount one initial viewport. (1) Gate the pending focus on measurements reported by the current mount rather than the persistent map: use React Flow's store-scoped `useNodesInitialized()` (the store resets on unmount), or keep a per-mount measured set in useGraphCanvas that GraphCanvas resets from its mount effect. (2) When a focus is pending as the canvas mounts, skip the initial fit (`fitView={!initialFocus}`, with the pending node passed to GraphCanvas), so the fit and the focus cannot race whatever the frame order. Keep the persistent measurements for routing and Arrange (earlier graph fix "UI measurements must survive without entering the saved graph").
- **Regression test:** frontend/tests/editor.spec.ts ("settings modal and runtime/validation errors jump to the failing node from graph and studio"): add a failing Output at (3000, 2400); open the graph first, switch to the Code editor, Run test, click "Show problem · Far result"; after the transition, assert that the node's bounding box lies inside `.react-flow` and that the viewport transform has scale(1). Keep the studio-first path and a `?node=` deep link as controls.
- **Related:** lesson F8 (viewport fits are optional presentation and must not race commands); earlier graph fix "UI measurements must survive without entering the saved graph" (the persistence that makes the gate stale).

## Frontend: Code studio, inspector and other frontend

<a id="x64"></a>

### X64 · medium · bug · Code studio commands erase undo history and reset the caret to 1:1 when adopting canonical text
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-studio1.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/studio (CodeStudio) + features/editor (useRuleDocument) / "Code studio Build, Save, Validate and Publish"
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/studio/CodeStudio.tsx:116` (+ `frontend/src/features/studio/CodeStudio.tsx:111`, `frontend/src/features/editor/Editor.tsx:292`, `frontend/src/features/editor/useRuleDocument.ts:248`, `frontend/src/features/editor/useRuleDocument.ts:341`, `frontend/src/features/editor/useRuleDocument.ts:376`, `frontend/src/features/editor/documentState.ts:88`)
- **Problem:** CodeStudio renders @monaco-editor/react as a controlled editor (`value={source}`, CodeStudio.tsx:111) whose `readOnly` comes from Editor.tsx:292 `readOnly={!can.edit}`, which is true while any command holds the document lock. Build, Save, Validate and Publish call `buildCode`, which dispatches `source/built` with the server's canonical `result.source` (useRuleDocument.ts:248-253) while the lock is still held (build then awaits `studioApi.validate` at :376, save awaits `ruleApi.save` at :341). @monaco-editor/react 4.7.0 applies a new value with `editor.setValue(value)` when the editor is read-only (otherwise `executeEdits` + `pushUndoStop`), and `setValue` clears the undo stack and puts the caret at line 1, column 1. Test is not affected because `toggleTest` releases the lock in the same render, so the library takes its executeEdits branch; the problem predates 49bb365, where the prop was `readOnly || !!busy`.
- **Trigger → consequence:** In a draft in Code studio, make any edit that the canonical renderer changes, e.g. a `// note` at the bottom (it gets hoisted to the header) or a snippet's `next -> "output";` (it gets an edge ID), then press Cmd/Ctrl+Enter, Cmd/Ctrl+S, Validate or Publish. The buffer becomes the canonical text and Cmd/Ctrl+Z can no longer reach the text from before the command. The next keystrokes, and any library chip inserted at the cursor, land before `schema 1;`; in a long script that happens off-screen and breaks the next build.
- **Evidence:** Browser probe on :3191, spec kept at `$SP/probes/v-studio1/zz-v-studio1-a.spec.ts`: Build with Ctrl+Enter → `buildChangedText:true, undoRestoredPreBuild:false, afterUndo1SameAsBuilt:true, afterUndo2SameAsBuilt:true, afterTypingFirstLine:"QQschema 1;"`; Save draft → `qqText:"QQschema 1;", undoLeftBuilt:true`; Test rule → `qqText:"QQ"` on the last line, `undoLeftBuilt:false`; control, a build that leaves the text unchanged, keeps undo (`return 12;` → `return 1;`). Library value effect: `if (editorRef.current.getOption(...EditorOption.readOnly)) { editorRef.current.setValue(value); } else if (value !== editorRef.current.getValue()) { ...executeEdits(...); editorRef.current.pushUndoStop(); }`.
- **Suggested fix:** Take value synchronization for Code studio away from @monaco-editor/react: render the editor with `defaultValue={source}` instead of `value`, and add one helper beside `useArcEditor` (e.g. `adoptSource(editor, text)` in `features/studio/useArcEditor.ts`) that CodeStudio calls from an effect when `source !== model.getValue()`. The helper replaces only the changed span (common prefix and suffix trimmed) through `model.pushEditOperations`, wrapped in `model.pushStackElement()` calls. Model edits are allowed while the editor option `readOnly` is set, so the command lock can keep the editor read-only; undo then returns to the pre-command text, and the caret follows Monaco's normal edit tracking instead of resetting to 1:1. Typing keeps flowing `onChange` → `editSource` (the effect is a no-op for it). Add a docs/studio.md "Behavior changes" line: Build, Save, Validate and Publish keep the caret and undo history when the server rewrites the code into canonical form.
- **Regression test:** `frontend/tests/studio.spec.ts`: for each of Ctrl/Cmd+Enter, Ctrl/Cmd+S, Validate and Publish, append `// note` at the end of a draft, run the command and wait for its notice, press Ctrl/Cmd+Z and assert the buffer equals the pre-command text; then type `QQ` and assert line 1 is still `schema 1;`. Keep the control case (a canonical buffer keeps undo). The probe spec above is a ready starting point.
- **Related:** X118 (duplicate report of this defect, d2#1), X65 (same read-only flag during commands); lessons F1 and F4; AGENTS.md contract "late async work must not erase newer edits".

<a id="x65"></a>

### X65 · low · bug · Cmd/Ctrl+S falls through to the browser's Save Page dialog while Code studio is read-only
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-studio1.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/studio (CodeStudio) / "Code studio keyboard save (Cmd/Ctrl+S)"
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/studio/CodeStudio.tsx:64` (+ `frontend/src/features/studio/CodeStudio.tsx:60`, `frontend/src/features/studio/CodeStudio.tsx:66`)
- **Problem:** The `arc-save` action declares `precondition: "!editorReadonly"`, so Monaco leaves the Cmd/Ctrl+S keybinding unmatched whenever Code studio is read-only (a published version, or any running command; see X64). Monaco then does not call `preventDefault`, and the browser runs its native Save Page action. The `run` body already refuses when `latest.current.readOnly` is set (the F4 guard), so without the precondition the key would be swallowed and nothing written; a grep found no other Ctrl/Cmd+S handler in frontend/src.
- **Trigger → consequence:** (a) On `#/studio/<id>?version=1`, click into the code and press Cmd/Ctrl+S: the browser's "Save Page As" dialog opens. (b) In a draft, press Cmd/Ctrl+S twice within the build+PUT latency: the first press saves, and the second, made while the lock holds the editor read-only, opens the browser dialog. Impact is minor: the read-only footer does not advertise Cmd+S ("Read-only · ⌘/Ctrl Enter to validate"), and the graph view has no Cmd+S handler at all.
- **Evidence:** CodeStudio.tsx:60-67 `keybindings: [monaco.KeyMod.CtrlCmd | monaco.KeyCode.KeyS], precondition: "!editorReadonly", run: () => { if (!latest.current.readOnly) latest.current.onSave(); }`. Browser probe with a capture-phase listener that reads `event.defaultPrevented` after dispatch (`$SP/probes/v-studio1/zz-v-studio1-a.spec.ts`, test "X65 Cmd/Ctrl+S default handling"): `X65 RESULT {"published":[false],"draft":[true,false],"puts":1}` — the press during the held PUT was not prevented, and only one PUT was sent.
- **Suggested fix:** Remove `precondition: "!editorReadonly"` from the `arc-save` action so Monaco always consumes Cmd/Ctrl+S in Code studio, and keep the `run` guard, which already makes a read-only press a no-op (F4: keyboard actions obey the same capability as buttons). Optionally show a short notice when a press is refused on a published version.
- **Regression test:** Extend the historical/draft keyboard-save test in `frontend/tests/studio.spec.ts` (it presses `ControlOrMeta+s` on `?version=1`): register a capture-phase `keydown` listener that records `defaultPrevented`, and assert it is `true` on the published version and for a second press while the save PUT is held with `page.route`; keep the assertions that the published version sends no PUT/POST and that the double press sends exactly one PUT.
- **Related:** X64 (read-only flag held during commands), lesson F4.

<a id="x66"></a>

### X66 · low · bug · A late Reuse insertion steals the caret, so the user's next keystrokes become the new node's result name
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-studio1.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/studio (StudioLibrary, arcCompletion) / "Code studio Reuse pane"
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/studio/StudioLibrary.tsx:57` (+ `frontend/src/features/studio/StudioLibrary.tsx:56`, `frontend/src/features/studio/arcCompletion.ts:60`, `frontend/src/features/studio/CodeStudio.tsx:42`, `frontend/src/features/studio/useFormulaSupport.ts:270`, `docs/maintaining.md:147`)
- **Problem:** When a Reuse card's pinned-version read finishes after the user has resumed editing, the handler checks only `signal.aborted || latest.current.readOnly` and still calls `onInsert(referenceSnippet(...), true)`. `insertSnippet` focuses the editor, moves the caret to the end and starts a snippet session with the `reusedResult` placeholder selected. The published-Formula path refuses in the same situation (useFormulaSupport.ts:270-280 throws "The expression changed while the formula loaded. Select the formula again." on a changed model version or selection). The documented contract (maintaining.md:147: reads "abort on unmount and consult the current draft/read-only state on completion") does not cover edits made meanwhile, and nothing documents this as intended.
- **Trigger → consequence:** GET /api/rules/<child>/versions/1 is slow. The user clicks a Reuse card, clicks back into `return 10;` and types ` * 2`. The read completes, and the user's next keystrokes ` + 3` go into the new REFERENCE node, giving `as  + 3;`; the build then fails, and the caret jumped away from where the user was typing.
- **Evidence:** StudioLibrary.tsx:56-61 `const version = await ruleApi.version(rule.id, pinned, { signal }); if (signal.aborted || latest.current.readOnly) return; latest.current.onInsert(referenceSnippet(...), true);`; arcCompletion.ts:60-71 `editor.focus(); ... editor.setPosition({ lineNumber: model.getLineCount(), ... }); ...insert(snippet)`. Browser probe with the read held (`$SP/probes/v-studio1/zz-v-studio1-b.spec.ts`, test "X66 late reuse insertion while typing"): `X66 RESULT {"returnLine":"  return 10 * 2;","asLine":"  as  + 3;"}`.
- **Suggested fix:** Apply the Formula path's staleness rule to Reuse through one shared helper. In `features/studio/arcCompletion.ts` add e.g. `captureEditorState(editor)` (model version ID and selection) and `unchangedSince(editor, state)`, and use it in `insertFormula` in place of its inline check. For Reuse, CodeStudio captures the state when the card is clicked (e.g. StudioLibrary calls `onBeginInsert()` before the read and receives an insert function); on completion that function throws `Error("The code changed while the rule loaded. Choose the rule again.")` when the version or selection changed, which `useLibraryInsertion.run` already shows as the card's error. Update maintaining.md:147 to state that completions also check for newer edits.
- **Regression test:** `frontend/tests/studio-refactor.spec.ts` (late reuse responses): hold the version read with `page.route`, click the card, type into `return 10;`, release the read; assert no `REFERENCE` node was added, the typed text is intact, further typing lands after it, and the "The code changed while the rule loaded" error is shown. Keep the existing "a late reuse response cannot insert into a newly mounted code editor" test.
- **Related:** X136 and X124 (same Reuse insertion path); lessons F3 ("Apply the same rule to node builds, diagnostics and reuse insertion") and F30.

<a id="x136"></a>

### X136 · low · bug · An ordinary double-click on a Reuse card still inserts two Reference nodes (K63/F30 fix incomplete)
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-studio1.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/studio (useLibraryInsertion, StudioLibrary) / "Code studio Reuse pane (library card insertion)"
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/studio/useLibraryInsertion.ts:48` (+ `frontend/src/features/studio/useLibraryInsertion.ts:38`, `frontend/src/features/studio/StudioLibrary.tsx:130`, `frontend/src/features/studio/PublishedFormulaLibrary.tsx:87`, `frontend/tests/studio-reuse.spec.ts:78`, `docs/studio.md:296`)
- **Problem:** The F30/K63 guard suppresses a second click only while the first version read is still pending: `run` aborts a previous read, marks the card busy, and clears `busy` right after the insert (`pending.current = null; setState(error ? {...} : idle);`, useLibraryInsertion.ts:48-50), while the card is `disabled={readOnly || insertion.busy === rule.id}`. On a local stack the read takes about 5 ms, so the card is enabled again before the second click of an ordinary double-click, which starts a second read and inserts a second snippet. This contradicts the hook comment ("so a double click inserts once") and docs/studio.md:296 ("a Reuse card inserts once"). The finder also noted that the published-Formula cards (PublishedFormulaLibrary.tsx:87) use the same hook; the verification probed only Reuse.
- **Trigger → consequence:** In the Code studio Reuse pane, double-click a card with 120–250 ms between clicks (well inside a normal OS double-click interval). Two `REFERENCE` nodes with different random IDs are appended, both `as reusedResult;`. The regression test studio-reuse.spec.ts:78 passes only because `locator.dblclick()` sends both clicks within the read latency.
- **Evidence:** Browser probe with mouse down/up, a wait, then down/up with clickCount 2 (`$SP/probes/v-studio1/zz-v-studio1-b.spec.ts`): gap 0 ms → `references:1, reads:["9ms"]`; gap 120 ms → `references:2, reads:["4ms","5ms"], asLines:["  as reusedResult;","  as reusedResult;"]`; gap 250 ms → `references:2, reads:["5ms","5ms"]`.
- **Suggested fix:** Make "one insertion per click sequence" explicit instead of relying on read latency. `useLibraryInsertion` exposes a card click handler (e.g. `insertion.onCardClick(event, key, insert)`) that ignores clicks with `event.detail > 1` (the second and later clicks of a double or triple click; keyboard activation has `detail` 0 and still inserts); StudioLibrary and PublishedFormulaLibrary both use it. Keep abort-previous and the busy card, and update the hook comment and lesson F30 to name the repeat-click rule.
- **Regression test:** `frontend/tests/studio-reuse.spec.ts`: replace or extend the `dblclick()` test with a real gap: `page.mouse.down()/up()`, wait until the first version read has settled (`started === settled`), then `down/up` with `clickCount: 2`; assert the built source has exactly one ` REFERENCE `. Add the same case for a published-Formula card in `frontend/tests/formula-calls.spec.ts` (exactly one `@id:version(` call inserted).
- **Related:** K63, lesson F30, X66 and X124 (same insertion path).

<a id="x124"></a>

### X124 · low · bug · The Reuse snippet always names its result `reusedResult`, so two insertions write the same variable
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-studio1.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/studio (snippets, StudioLibrary) / "Code studio Reuse snippet"
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/studio/snippets.ts:56` (+ `frontend/src/features/studio/StudioLibrary.tsx:54`, `frontend/src/features/studio/StudioLibrary.tsx:59`, `frontend/src/domain/graph.ts:156`, `docs/studio.md:296`)
- **Problem:** `referenceSnippet` makes its node ID unique (`reuseNodeId`) but always defaults the result to the constant tab stop `${1:reusedResult}`, so two Reuse insertions that keep the default produce two Reference nodes writing the same variable, the shape lesson F19 warns about; docs/studio.md:296 also says new result variables take the first free `result_N`. Caveats from the record: the name is a selected placeholder that invites the user to overwrite it, and the Modules snippets also use constant defaults. `latest.current.definition` is the last built graph, so a fix based only on `uniqueName` over it would miss an earlier insertion that has not been built yet; it needs the buffer's names or a suffix like the node ID's.
- **Trigger → consequence:** Insert two Reuse cards and keep the selected default name; both builds succeed. In sequence (A → B → Output `return reusedResult`), validation passes and the result is B's value; A's is silently replaced. With fan-out (Input → A and B → Output), validation also returns `{"valid":true}`, so the draft can be published, but every preview or execution fails with 422 "Conflicting upstream values for 'reusedResult'".
- **Evidence:** snippets.ts:56 `"  as ${1:reusedResult};",` next to StudioLibrary.tsx:54 `const nodeId = reuseNodeId(rule.id);`. The X136 probe produced two `as reusedResult;` nodes. API probe on :8191 (build, then validate, then preview; fixtures deleted): SEQ `validate: {"valid":true}`, `preview: {"result":2,...}`; PAR `validate: {"valid":true}`, `preview: {"status":422,"message":"Conflicting upstream values for 'reusedResult'; use distinct result variable names before merging"}`.
- **Suggested fix:** Decision needed on the default's spelling; recommended: follow the documented `result_N` rule. Give `referenceSnippet(rule, version, caller, nodeId, resultName)` a generated name and emit `as ${1:<resultName>};` (keep the tab stop so the user can rename). Compute the name at insertion time from every name in use: export `variableNames` from `domain/graph.ts` (inputs and node results of the last built definition) and add a pure `scriptVariableNames(source)` to `domain/expressionSymbols.ts` (names declared in the current buffer, via the existing script classifier), then `uniqueName("result_", [...both])`, the same generator `createGraphNode` uses. CodeStudio passes its current `source` to StudioLibrary through `latest`. Simpler alternative: reuse the node ID's random suffix (`reusedResult_9f86`). Update tests that look for `reusedResult` (studio-reuse.spec.ts, studio-refactor.spec.ts). The Modules snippets' result defaults (`total`, `normalized`) are the same class and can use the same generator.
- **Regression test:** `frontend/tests/unit/studio-snippets.spec.ts`: `referenceSnippet` emits the given result name; the name generator skips names declared only in an unbuilt buffer (`let result_1 = …;`) and names in the definition. Browser `frontend/tests/studio-reuse.spec.ts`: insert two Reuse cards without building in between, build, and assert the two REFERENCE nodes have different `as` names, and that a fan-out graph built from them validates and previews without 422.
- **Related:** lesson F19, X63 (another generated name that ignores names in use), X136 and X66 (same Reuse path).

<a id="x67"></a>

### X67 · low · bug · Code studio completion lists a branch-shared result twice, and hover names only the first producer
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-studio2.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/studio (useArcLanguageSupport) + domain/graph / "Code studio and node-code completion and hover"
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/studio/useArcLanguageSupport.ts:71` (+ `frontend/src/features/studio/useArcLanguageSupport.ts:74`, `frontend/src/features/studio/useArcLanguageSupport.ts:217`, `frontend/src/domain/graph.ts:238`)
- **Problem:** `declaredVariables` feeds Code studio and the node-code dialog; it emits one VariableOption per producing node and never groups by name, unlike `domain/graph.availableVariables` (graph.ts:238-250 groups by name and returns `label: [...labels].join(" / ")`). A result that mutually exclusive branches both assign, which architecture.md allows ("Mutually exclusive branches can assign the same result variable."), therefore appears twice in completion, and the hover (`latestVariables.current.find(...)`) names only the first producer. Duplicates cannot come from word suggestions, because `arcEditorOptions` sets `suggest: { showWords: false }`.
- **Trigger → consequence:** Build a valid graph (/api/diagnostics returns []): Condition → "High fee" `let feeValue = 10` / "Low fee" `let feeValue = 20` → Output `return feeValue`. In Code studio, typing `feeV` lists `feeValue, Variable` twice; hovering `feeValue` shows "From: High fee", while the inspector's grouped option for the same variable reads "High fee / Low fee". The node-code dialog passes `{kind: "node", definition}` and takes the same path.
- **Evidence:** useArcLanguageSupport.ts:74-77 `...definition.nodes.flatMap((node): VariableOption[] => node.output ? [{ name: node.output, type: "RESULT", label: node.label }] : [])`; hover at :217-219 `latestVariables.current.find((entry) => entry.name === name)`. Browser probe on :3191 (fixture rv-v-studio2-*, deleted with 204): `SUGGESTIONS ["feeValue, Variable","feeValue, Variable"]`; `HOVER TEXT "feeValue\n\nNode result\n\nFrom: High fee"`; /api/variables scope of the Output = ["amount","feeValue","order"].
- **Suggested fix:** Move the grouping out of `availableVariables` into an exported pure helper in `domain/graph.ts` (e.g. `groupVariablesByName(options)`: the first option's type, producer labels joined with " / "), and add `declaredVariables(definition)` there as `groupVariablesByName([...inputVariables(definition), ...node results])`. `availableVariables` and `useArcLanguageSupport` both use it, so there is one derivation of "the variables of a definition".
- **Regression test:** `frontend/tests/unit/document.spec.ts` (where `availableVariables` is tested): `declaredVariables` returns one `feeValue` option labelled "High fee / Low fee". Browser `frontend/tests/editor-language-context.spec.ts`: in Code studio, typing `feeV` lists `feeValue` once and its hover shows "From: High fee / Low fee".
- **Related:** lesson F31 (variables read from a ref), X71 (same classifier/hover area).

<a id="x68"></a>

### X68 · low · bug · Monarch colors keyword, type and constant fragments inside identifiers (`order.format` shows `at` as a keyword)
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-studio2.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/studio (arcLanguage) / "Syntax coloring in every ARC editor (inspector inline editors, dialogs, Code studio)"
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/studio/arcLanguage.ts:49` (+ `frontend/src/features/studio/arcLanguage.ts:52`, `frontend/src/features/studio/arcLanguage.ts:53`, `frontend/src/features/studio/arcLanguage.ts:55`)
- **Problem:** The Monarch root state has no rule that consumes a plain identifier; the only generic name rule, :55 `/[A-Za-z_][\w.]*(?=\s*\()/`, applies only before `(`. When nothing matches, Monarch advances one character and tests each `^(?:…)` rule against `line.substr(pos)`, where `\b` always matches at the start of that remainder. The keyword (:49), type (:52) and constant (:53) rules therefore color the tails of property segments and other names that semantic tokens do not classify; semantic tokens deliberately cover only the path root ("classify only a dotted path's root", maintaining.md).
- **Trigger → consequence:** In every "arc" editor, with OBJECT input `order`: `order.format` shows `at` in keyword purple rgb(135,82,149); `order.lowercase` shows `case`, `order.reuse` shows `use` and `order.wallet` shows `let`; `order.isnull` shows `null` in constant red rgb(221,0,0); `order.ACCOUNT_NUMBER` shows `NUMBER` in type green rgb(50,121,102). The keyword purple is close to the key's "Node result" purple (#7B3F98). Only colors are affected; no data changes.
- **Evidence:** arcLanguage.ts:49 `/\b(schema|inputs|node|at|let|…|case|…|use|…)\b/`, :52 types, :53 `/\b(true|false|null)\b/`. monaco-editor 0.56 monarchCompile.js:376 builds `'^(?:' + sregex + ')'`; monarchLexer.js:503-506 `const restOfLine = line.substr(pos); … restOfLine.match(rule.resolveRegex(state))`, :524 `matches = [line.charAt(pos)];`. Browser probe, painted spans in Code studio: `["order","rgb(32, 95, 166)"],[".form","rgb(0, 0, 0)"],["at","rgb(135, 82, 149)"] … [".lower",…],["case","rgb(135, 82, 149)"] … [".re",…],["use",…] … [".is",…],["null","rgb(221, 0, 0)"] … ["  ACCOUNT_",…],["NUMBER","rgb(50, 121, 102)"] … [".wal",…],["let","rgb(135, 82, 149)"]`; the inspector's inline Condition "Expression" editor painted the same spans.
- **Suggested fix:** Replace the three `\b(...)\b` rules with one identifier rule placed after the `$`-function and call rules: `[/[A-Za-z_]\w*(?:\.\w+)*/, { cases: { "@keywords": "keyword", "@typeNames": "type", "@constants": "constant", "@default": "identifier" } }]`, with `keywords`, `typeNames` and `constants` as arrays on the Monarch language definition. A dotted path such as `order.format` is then one token that never matches a keyword, while statements such as `let` or `case:premium` keep their keyword color. Leave `identifier` uncolored in the theme so semantic tokens still color path roots.
- **Regression test:** `frontend/tests/expression-colors.spec.ts` (painted colors): an expression using `order.format`, `order.lowercase`, `order.reuse`, `order.isnull`, `order.ACCOUNT_NUMBER` and `order.wallet` paints no keyword, type or constant color on any fragment, in Code studio and in an inline inspector editor, while standalone `let`, `NUMBER`, `null` and `case:` in a script keep their colors.
- **Related:** lesson F12, X71 (Monarch type names are upper-case literals), X70 (role colors).

<a id="x70"></a>

### X70 · low · cleanup · Symbol-role colors are restated in the Monaco theme, the color key and the variables-list CSS
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-studio2.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/studio (arcLanguage, ExpressionColorKey) + features/expressions (AvailableVariables) + styles / "Expression color key, Monaco arc-light theme and Available variables list"
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/studio/ExpressionColorKey.tsx:5` (+ `frontend/src/features/studio/arcLanguage.ts:80`, `frontend/src/styles/expressions.css:117`, `frontend/src/features/expressions/AvailableVariables.tsx:19`)
- **Problem:** The four symbol-role colors (function, formula, input/parameter, node result/variable) are hard-coded independently in the Monaco arc-light theme (arcLanguage.ts:80-83), the color key's inline styles (ExpressionColorKey.tsx:5-8) and the Available variables list CSS (expressions.css:117-122, used by AvailableVariables.tsx:19). Nothing ties the copies together, although they are shared by design and maintaining.md Styles says "`tokens.css` holds the values that several rules share by design … A color used once, or twice by coincidence, stays a literal in its rule". This is not in the "preserved as found" list, which covers only the separate `.expression-variable-popover` rule, and the 9e5b177 token migration did not cover it.
- **Trigger → consequence:** Changing a role color in the theme, for example parameter at arcLanguage.ts:82, leaves the legend (ExpressionColorKey.tsx:7) and the AvailableVariables `code[data-kind="input"]` rule (expressions.css:118) on the old color, so the legend misdescribes the editor it exists to explain. No test compares legend and editor; specs assert literal rgb values (expression-colors.spec.ts:12-14, formula-context.spec.ts:187-188/263, editor-language-context.spec.ts:79).
- **Evidence:** arcLanguage.ts:80-83 `{ token: "function", foreground: "8B682F" }, { token: "formula", foreground: "007C83" }, { token: "parameter", foreground: "205FA6" }, { token: "variable", foreground: "7B3F98" }`; ExpressionColorKey.tsx:5-8 `style={{ color: "#8B682F" }}` … `"#7B3F98"`; expressions.css:117-122 `.expression-variable-list code[data-kind="input"] { color: #205fa6; }` / `[data-kind="result"] { color: #7b3f98; }`.
- **Suggested fix:** Give the role colors one owner. Decision needed: TS owner or CSS token; recommended: TS owner, because Monaco's `defineTheme` needs literal hex values and the legend exists only to describe that theme. Add `features/studio/symbolColors.ts` (no Monaco import, so AvailableVariables does not pull the lazy Monaco chunk) exporting `symbolRoleColors: Record<"function" | "formula" | "parameter" | "variable", string>`; `defineTheme` builds its four rules from it (`foreground: color.slice(1)`), ExpressionColorKey renders its entries from it, and AvailableVariables sets the code color from it (inline style or a custom property on the element), so the two literal rules in expressions.css:117-122 are deleted. The alternative is `--color-symbol-*` tokens in tokens.css mirrored in the theme with a "change both together" note, as tokens.css does for app/theme.ts.
- **Regression test:** `frontend/tests/expression-colors.spec.ts`: assert each color-key entry's computed color equals the painted Monaco color of a symbol with that role, and that the Available variables input/result entries use the same colors, instead of only literal rgb values. Stylesheet change: also run `frontend/tests/style-cascade.spec.ts`.
- **Related:** X70b (second verification, logged by another group: edge-label mask repeats `--color-canvas`, Monaco `editor.background` repeats `--color-code-background`, preview arrowhead repeats the connection stroke), X181 (duplicate report, r2#5), X68, X71; maintainability lesson on style ownership and tokens.

<a id="x71"></a>

### X71 · low · cleanup · Input type names are restated in three files and matched case-sensitively, unlike the backend grammar
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-studio2.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / domain (expressionSymbols, types, executionInputs) + features/studio (arcLanguage) + features/editor/inspector (InputParameterCard) / "Script parameter coloring and the inspector Type dropdown"
- **Location:** `frontend/src/domain/expressionSymbols.ts:26` (+ `frontend/src/domain/expressionSymbols.ts:125`, `frontend/src/features/studio/arcLanguage.ts:10`, `frontend/src/features/editor/inspector/InputParameterCard.tsx:80`, `frontend/src/types.ts:10`, `frontend/src/domain/executionInputs.ts:12`)
- **Problem:** The five input type names are independent literals in expressionSymbols.ts:26, arcLanguage.ts:12-16 and InputParameterCard.tsx:80, alongside the `InputType` union (types.ts:10); only `placeholderLiteral: Record<InputType, string>` is compiler-checked, and without `noImplicitReturns` in tsconfig `sampleValue`'s switch is not checked either. Node types already avoid this by deriving `nodeTypes` from the descriptor (arcLanguage.ts:11). The copies have drifted from the server: the Script declaration check (`inputTypes.has(tokens[index + 2]?.text)`, :125-127) is case-sensitive, while the backend keeps one list (`InputTypes.NAMES`) and ArcScriptParser.java:24-29 matches it with `Pattern.CASE_INSENSITIVE | Pattern.DOTALL`, normalizing with `toUpperCase(Locale.ROOT)` at :125.
- **Trigger → consequence:** Cost: a new InputType compiles cleanly while the inspector Type dropdown, Monarch type coloring and Script parameter-declaration coloring silently leave it out. Existing drift: a typed `amount: number required;` builds on the server, which normalizes it to NUMBER with no diagnostics; until the next build re-renders canonical upper-case text, neither the declaration nor `return amount * 2` is colored as a parameter, and Monarch does not color `number` as a type. Cosmetic only.
- **Evidence:** POST :8191/api/studio/build with `amount: number required;` (`$SP/probes/v-studio2/build.json`) returned inputs `[{"name":"amount","type":"NUMBER",…}]`, diagnostics [], and a re-rendered source containing `amount: NUMBER required;`. Unit probe of `expressionSymbols(script, [], true)`: `[["amount","parameter"],["amount","parameter"]]` for NUMBER and `[]` for number.
- **Suggested fix:** Add one input-type descriptor in the domain, e.g. `domain/inputTypes.ts` with `inputTypeLabels: Record<InputType, string>`, `inputTypes` derived from its keys and an `isInputType(value)` guard. Use it in expressionSymbols (`isInputType(tokens[index + 2]?.text.toUpperCase())`, as node types are already upper-cased there), in arcLanguage (`typeNames = [...nodeTypes, ...inputTypes]`), and in InputParameterCard (map over `inputTypes`; replace the `as InputType` cast with the guard); make `sampleValue` exhaustive (a `Record<InputType, …>` or a `never` check). For Monarch, either add the lower-case spellings to `typeNames` or keep upper-case-only coloring (the next build rewrites canonical upper case) and say so in a comment.
- **Regression test:** `frontend/tests/unit/expression-symbols.spec.ts`: `inputs { amount: number required; }` with `return amount * 2` classifies both `amount` tokens as `parameter`, the same as `NUMBER`. A unit test beside `frontend/tests/unit/node-kinds.spec.ts` asserts `inputTypes` lists every InputType exactly once.
- **Related:** X71b (second verification, logged by another group), X207 (duplicate report, l2#7: `sampleValue` non-exhaustive, dropdown cast), X68, X22 (backend type-name case handling in InputTypes.java:26 depends on the locale).

<a id="x59"></a>

### X59 · medium · bug · The Condition builder joins operands without parentheses, so the stored comparison means something else
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-insp.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/editor/inspector (ConditionFields) + domain/expressions / "Condition builder"
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/editor/inspector/ConditionFields.tsx:38` (+ `frontend/src/domain/expressions.ts:62`, `frontend/src/features/expressions/ValueBinding.tsx:114`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExpressionParser.java:39`)
- **Problem:** `comparisonExpression` stores `[left.trim(), operator, right.trim()].join(" ")` with no parentheses. An operand that contains an operator binding no tighter than the chosen comparison (`||`, `&&`, `==`, `!=`, or `==`/`!=` under `<`/`>`; server priorities: `||`/OR 1, `&&`/AND 2, `==`/`!=`/`=`/`<>` 3, `<`/`<=`/`>`/`>=` 4) is stored as an expression whose meaning differs from what the builder shows, and nothing flags it. The syntax is valid, so diagnostics stay silent, and the editing pin keeps the builder on screen; after the node is reselected it opens as a raw expression, because `simpleComparison` rejects `||`. ValueBinding.tsx:114 always offers the Expression value source that makes this reachable.
- **Trigger → consequence:** Set When=`flag` (BOOLEAN), Equals, and a Comparison value in Expression mode of `a || b`: the builder keeps showing flag / Equals / `a || b` but stores `flag == a || b`, which the server parses as `(flag == a) || b`. With flag=false, a=false, b=true the rule takes the True branch (result 1), while the builder's reading `flag == (a || b)` gives False (result 0). The left operand has the same problem: When=`a || b`, Equals, `false` stores `a || b == false`, which is true for a=b=true, where the intended `(a || b) == false` is false. The only hint is the unparenthesized preview text.
- **Evidence:** Browser probe on :3191: after choosing Expression and pasting `a || b`, the builder showed `when= flag operator= Equals value= a || b`; saved draft `STORED: "flag == a || b"`; /api/preview with {flag:false,a:false,b:true}: stored draft → `200 result=1`, same draft with `flag == (a || b)` → `200 result=0`. Java probe `$SP/probes/v-insp/InspProbe.java`: `a || b == false` → true, `(a || b) == false` → false (a=b=true).
- **Suggested fix:** Move the join into `domain/expressions.ts` as `comparisonText(left, operator, right)`, which wraps an operand in parentheses when its top-level text (outside quotes, brackets and parentheses) contains `||`, `&&`, `and`/`or` or a comparison operator (`==`, `!=`, `<>`, `=`, `<`, `<=`, `>`, `>=`). Extract the masking loop of `simpleComparison` into a shared `topLevelText(expression)` so both functions use one scanner. The pinned builder state keeps the raw operands the user typed; only the stored expression and its preview gain parentheses, and `simpleComparison` reads the parenthesized operand back, so the node reopens in the builder. Stored expressions are not rewritten; add a docs/studio.md "Behavior changes" (Inspector) line that the builder parenthesizes operands containing `||`, `&&` or a comparison.
- **Regression test:** `frontend/tests/unit/expressions.spec.ts`: `comparisonText("flag", "==", "a || b")` is `flag == (a || b)`, `comparisonText("a || b", "==", "false")` is `(a || b) == false`, atoms (`amount`, `order.total`, `$ROUND(x, 2)`, `"a || b"`, `[1, 2]`, `(a || b)`) stay unwrapped, and `simpleComparison` of each result returns the same operands. Browser `frontend/tests/condition-builder.spec.ts`: an Expression value `a || b` saves `flag == (a || b)`, and a preview with {flag:false,a:false,b:true} takes the False branch.
- **Related:** lesson F13 (builder pin, K45), X60 (same editing-pin area).

<a id="x60"></a>

### X60 · low · bug · Add or Remove field remounts every Transform field binding and re-infers its editor
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-insp.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/editor/inspector (TransformFields) / "Transform field mappings"
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/editor/inspector/TransformFields.tsx:61` (+ `frontend/src/features/editor/inspector/TransformFields.tsx:30`, `frontend/src/features/expressions/useEditingPin.ts:14`, `frontend/src/domain/valueBinding.ts:111`, `frontend/src/features/editor/inspector/useInputParameterRows.ts:10`)
- **Problem:** Transform field ValueBindings are keyed by `${index}:${fields.length}` (rows by `key={index}`), so Add field and Remove field remount every field's binding. Each binding loses its useEditingPin state (kept in `useState`, useEditingPin.ts:14) and re-infers its editor from the stored value, which breaks lesson F13 for fields the user did not touch. The key dates from 8712418, before useEditingPin existed; input rows already use stable identities (useInputParameterRows).
- **Trigger → consequence:** Field 1 (`amount`) is switched to Expression, and Field 2's Constant number is cleared and retyped as `-` (stored ""). The user clicks "Add field": Field 1 flips to the "Upstream variable" picker, and Field 2 flips to an empty "Select a variable" picker, losing the `-`. A partial ARRAY constant `[1, 2,` would likewise turn into the Monaco expression editor. Stored values do not change, so the damage is editor swaps and lost partial text, not data loss.
- **Evidence:** valueBinding.ts:111 `if (!value) return "variable";`. Unit probe: `inferBindingMode("", ANY)` = "variable", `("amount")` = "variable", `("[1, 2,")` = "expression". Browser probe on :3191: `BEFORE ADD: field1 source= Expression field2 source= Constant` → `AFTER ADD: field1 source= Upstream variable field2 source= Upstream variable`, with Field 2's picker empty. F13 (review-lessons.md:27): "Only the value-source or constant-type menu, the Builder/Expression toggle, or a value changed elsewhere ... chooses again."
- **Suggested fix:** Give Transform field rows stable UI-only identities (lesson F6) and key both the row and its ValueBinding by that identity, dropping `fields.length` from the key. Extract the WeakMap identity mechanism of `useInputParameterRows` into a shared hook (e.g. `features/editor/inspector/useRowIdentities.ts` with `identity(row)` and `carry(from, to)`) used by `useInputParameterRows` and `TransformFields`; TransformFields carries the identity whenever a field object is replaced by a name or value edit.
- **Regression test:** `frontend/tests/value-binding-editing.spec.ts`: a Transform with Field 1 in Expression mode (`amount`) and Field 2 as a Constant number retyped to `-`; click Add field → Field 1 stays Expression and Field 2 keeps its number field showing `-`; removing a third field, and removing Field 1, keep the remaining fields' editors and partial text with their rows.
- **Related:** lessons F13 and F6, X223 (every Transform row re-renders per keystroke, same component), X59.

<a id="x63"></a>

### X63 · low · bug · Add parameter can pick a name that collides with an existing node result
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-insp.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/editor/inspector (useInputParameterRows) + domain/graph / "Input parameters: Add parameter"
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/editor/inspector/useInputParameterRows.ts:41` (+ `frontend/src/domain/graph.ts:156`, `frontend/src/domain/graph.ts:178`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/validation/NodeValidation.java:57`)
- **Problem:** "Add parameter" computes `uniqueName("input", definition.inputs.map((input) => input.name), definition.inputs.length + 1)`, unique among input names only, and ignores node result variables. `variableNames` (graph.ts:156-160) covers inputs and node outputs, but only `createGraphNode` (:178) uses it. Add parameter can therefore create an input with the same name as an existing result, which makes that node fail validation and goes against lesson F19's rule to generate names from every input and node result.
- **Trigger → consequence:** A rule has input `amount` and a Formula "Calc" that stores `input2`. Clicking Add parameter and saving gives inputs ["amount","input2"], and POST /api/validate returns 422 "Calc: cannot overwrite input input2" at node calc. The error is explicit, not silent; it only triggers when a result is named `input<k>` for the first free k ≥ inputs.length+1, which is uncommon because results default to `result_N`.
- **Evidence:** NodeValidation.java:56-58 `noneMatch(input -> input.name().equals(node.output())), node.label() + ": cannot overwrite input " + node.output()`. Unit probe: `uniqueName` → "input2", while `createGraphNode` on the same definition → "result_1". Browser probe on :3191: `INPUTS: ["amount","input2"]` and `VALIDATE: 422 {"issues":["Calc: cannot overwrite input input2"],"locations":[{"nodeId":"calc",...}]}`.
- **Suggested fix:** Export `variableNames` from `domain/graph.ts` and add a pure `newInputParameter(definition): Input` beside `createGraphNode` that names the parameter `uniqueName("input", variableNames(definition), definition.inputs.length + 1)`; `useInputParameterRows.add` calls it. The same function is the natural place for the 50-input guard that X61 reports missing on Add parameter.
- **Regression test:** `frontend/tests/unit/graph-nodes.spec.ts` (F19 coverage): with input `amount` and a node result `input2`, `newInputParameter` returns `input3`; across add/delete sequences no generated input name equals an input or result name.
- **Related:** lesson F19, X124 (Reuse result name ignores names in use), X61 (Add parameter lacks the 50-input guard; limits restated as literals).

<a id="x87"></a>

### X87 · low · bug · ARRAY constant fields accept arrays that the server rejects for exceeding 256 tokens
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-insp.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / domain (expressions, valueBinding) / "Typed ARRAY constants in Output, Reference/Formula bindings and ANY constants"
- **Location:** `frontend/src/domain/expressions.ts:54` (+ `frontend/src/domain/valueBinding.ts:48`, `frontend/src/domain/valueBinding.ts:77`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/expression/ExpressionParser.java:67`, `frontend/tests/unit/literal-cases.ts:20`)
- **Problem:** `isArrayLiteral`/`inferConstantType` treat any JSON array without objects as an ARRAY constant, but the server's compiler rejects literals over 256 tokens (`Limits.MAX_EXPRESSION_TOKENS`, i.e. 128 or more flat items), and ARRAY constant fields show no error for them, although lesson F16 says "Number and array fields report text the server would reject". The finder's NUMBER example `5e-1000000000000` falls under the documented magnitude exclusion (literal-cases.ts:19-21: magnitude limits are value errors reported by diagnostics), and the 49-level nesting case is moot, because arrays deeper than 8 levels already fail as value errors (`Limits.MAX_VALUE_DEPTH` = 8; a 47-deep array fails with "Value exceeds collection depth or size limit").
- **Trigger → consequence:** An Output, a Reference or Formula ARRAY binding, or an ANY constant holds a 128-item array such as `[1,1,…]` (257 tokens), for example a pasted list of codes. The editor infers Constant/ARRAY and `constantTextError` returns null, so the field shows no error; static diagnostics and publish report "Expression exceeds 256 tokens" only on the node. The failure is visible, but not at the field.
- **Evidence:** expressions.ts:54 `return Array.isArray(value) && !containsObject(value);`; valueBinding.ts:48 `if (isArrayLiteral(text)) return "ARRAY";`, :77 `if (!format || !text.trim() || inferConstantType(text) === type) return null;`. Unit probe: `inferConstantType(128 items)` = "ARRAY", `constantTextError` = null, `inferBindingMode(…, "ARRAY")` = "constant". Java probe `$SP/probes/v-insp/InspProbe.java`: 127 items compile, 128 items → "Expression exceeds 256 tokens". Live POST 8191/api/diagnostics with that Output → `[{"message":"Return: Expression exceeds 256 tokens","locations":[{"nodeId":"out",...}]}]`.
- **Suggested fix:** Mirror the tokenizer bound in `domain/expressions.ts`: add `expressionTokenCount(text)` with the server's token classes (`ExpressionParser.TOKEN`: numbers, names, quoted strings, operators; a unary `-` is its own token) and a `MAX_EXPRESSION_TOKENS = 256` constant (in the shared frontend limits module that X61 asks for). `isArrayLiteral` returns false above the limit, so such text is not offered as a constant (F16: "offer a constant only for text the server reads as that literal"), and `constantTextError("ARRAY", text)` returns a specific message for a JSON array over the limit (e.g. "Array literals are limited to 256 tokens (127 numbers); pass longer lists as an input or a data source") instead of the generic format hint.
- **Regression test:** `frontend/tests/unit/literal-cases.ts`: add a 127-item array (`constant: "ARRAY"`, `server: "literal"`) and a 128-item array (`constant: null`, `server: "error"`), exercised by the existing literal-table tests; `frontend/tests/unit/value-binding.spec.ts`: `constantTextError("ARRAY", <128 items>)` returns the limit message. Browser `frontend/tests/value-binding-editing.spec.ts`: pasting a 128-item array into an ARRAY constant shows the field error.
- **Related:** lesson F16 (K40), X61 (backend limits restated as frontend literals).
