### X84 · medium · bug · OpenAPI spec drift: Switch trace branches, Node.outputName and three served endpoints are missing
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-misc-fe.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Docs / docs/openapi.yaml, docs/api.md / "OpenAPI specification: execution trace, graph JSON, diagnostics and node-code endpoints"
- **Location:** `docs/openapi.yaml:626` (+ `docs/openapi.yaml:721`, `docs/openapi.yaml:593`, `docs/api.md:15`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/model/Definition.java:99`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/api/GraphController.java:33`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/api/StudioController.java:32`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/api/StudioController.java:37`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/GraphExecution.java:183`)
- **Problem:** The published spec no longer matches the API: (1) the trace `branch` enum `[next, 'true', 'false', null]` leaves out the Switch branches `case:<id>` and `default`, and docs/api.md:15 also lists only true/false/next/null; (2) the Definition node schema (nodes at 721-784) has no `outputName` property, which appears only inside the result description at :593; (3) POST /diagnostics, /studio/node/render and /studio/node/build have no paths, although the backend serves them, the frontend calls them (api/studio.ts:19, :51, :62) and docs/architecture.md:178,180 documents all three. All three gaps predate the baseline: Switch came in 8712418, the endpoints in 8389476 and outputName in 4b6a5eb. Nothing in the repo consumes the spec, but README.md:318 links it as "OpenAPI specification".
- **Trigger → consequence:** (1) A client generated from docs/openapi.yaml with enum validation rejects any execute or preview response that contains a Switch step; the live stack returns branch "case:big" for such a step. (2) Some generators build a Node model that keeps only the declared properties; through it, a read-modify-write PUT of a draft drops `outputName`, and after a publish the result changes from {"payable":150} to 150, and multi-Output keys fall back to variable names or node IDs (this part depends on the generator discarding unknown properties). (3) A generated client has no operations for the three diagnostics and node-code endpoints.
- **Evidence:** openapi.yaml:626 `branch: {type: string, nullable: true, enum: [next, 'true', 'false', null]}`; the paths list jumps from /studio/expression/check to /studio/build and /studio/render with no /diagnostics. GraphExecution.java:183-184 `if (matches(option, selector, scope)) return Handles.forCase(option.id()); return Handles.DEFAULT;`, traced as the branch through :167 `yield new Outcome(branch, branch);`. Probe: `curl -X POST localhost:8191/api/preview` with `$SP/probes/v-misc-fe/switch-preview.json` (Input → Switch(case big: amount > 100) → Output(outputName payable), amount 150) returned result={"payable": 150} and trace [('in','INPUT','next'), ('sw','SWITCH','case:big'), ('o1','OUTPUT',None)].
- **Suggested fix:** Bring the spec and docs to the served contract. `branch`: `{type: string, nullable: true, pattern: '^(next|true|false|default|case:[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64})$'}`, the same handle syntax as the edge `sourceHandle` pattern. Node schema: add `outputName: {type: string, nullable: true, pattern: '^([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z_0-9]{0,63})?$', description: OUTPUT only; wraps a single reached Output's value and names its key among several}` (DefinitionShape.java:147 accepts null, empty or a valid identifier). Paths: `/diagnostics` (Definition → array of `{message, locations}`, with the Error schema's location item extracted as a named `Location` schema), `/studio/node/render` (`{definition, nodeId}` → `{source}`) and `/studio/node/build` (`{definition, nodeId, source}` → the same build result schema as /studio/build). docs/api.md:15: a Switch step's branch is `"case:<id>"` or `"default"`. Runtime behavior is unchanged, so no "Behavior changes" entry is needed.
- **Regression test:** New `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/api/OpenApiContractTest.java` that loads `../docs/openapi.yaml` with SnakeYAML (already on the classpath through Spring Boot) and asserts: every `@GetMapping`/`@PostMapping`/`@PutMapping`/`@DeleteMapping` of the `dev.arc.api` controllers has its path and method in the spec; the node schema's property names equal `Definition.Node`'s record components; the trace `branch` schema accepts `Handles.NEXT`, `Handles.condition(true)`, `Handles.condition(false)`, `Handles.DEFAULT` and `Handles.forCase("big")`.
- **Related:** X07 (in-app API reference drift); AGENTS.md compatibility boundary ("HTTP payloads/statuses, graph JSON … are compatibility boundaries"); commits 8712418, 8389476, 4b6a5eb.

### X07 · low · cleanup · The in-app API reference omits DELETE /rules/{id} and its 409 refusal
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-misc-fe.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/execution (ApiReference) / "In-app API reference (#/docs), rule deletion"
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/execution/ApiReference.tsx:11` (+ `frontend/src/features/execution/ApiReference.tsx:84`, `docs/api.md:97`, `docs/api.md:99`, `docs/openapi.yaml:180`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/api/RuleController.java:63`, `frontend/src/styles/api.css:246`)
- **Problem:** The in-app API reference restates the rule endpoints and error statuses of docs/api.md by hand. Commit b8aa4ff added `DELETE /rules/{id}` and its 409 refusal, and neither b8aa4ff nor 1e1ce31 touched this page, which still lists every other /rules and /rules/{id} lifecycle operation (GET, POST, PUT, publish, execute, versions) but no deletion. The heading ("A few clear endpoints") makes it a curated subset, but earlier rule-endpoint changes were copied into this table (a1552af added /rule-summaries and version-summaries, 789591c reworded them), so the missing row is drift, not a curation choice.
- **Trigger → consequence:** An API user reading #/docs finds no way to delete a rule. "Clear failures" gives 409 only for stale revisions or unpublished rules, not for a delete refused because other rules call the rule, although docs/api.md and openapi.yaml document both.
- **Evidence:** ApiReference.tsx:11-50 has rows for GET/POST /rules and GET/PUT /rules/{id} but no `["DELETE", "/rules/{id}", ...]`; :84 `return <code>404</code>. Stale revisions or unpublished rules return <code>409</code>`. docs/api.md:97 "`DELETE /rules/{id}` removes the rule ... returns `204`"; :99 "The request returns `409` "Other rules call this rule: …""; openapi.yaml:180-181 `delete: operationId: deleteRule`; RuleController.java:63 `@DeleteMapping("/rules/{id}")`. `git show --stat b8aa4ff` and `git show --stat 1e1ce31` do not list ApiReference.tsx.
- **Suggested fix:** Add `["DELETE", "/rules/{id}", "Delete a rule that no other rule calls"]` and extend "Clear failures" with "deleting a rule that other rules call returns 409 naming the callers"; add a `.method-delete` badge rule in `frontend/src/styles/api.css` next to `.method-post`/`.method-put` (otherwise DELETE falls back to the GET colors). To stop further drift, move the endpoint rows into a typed data module (e.g. `features/execution/apiEndpoints.ts`) that the page maps over and that a unit test checks against docs/openapi.yaml.
- **Regression test:** New `frontend/tests/unit/api-reference.spec.ts`: read `docs/openapi.yaml` with `fs` and assert that every listed `[method, path]` exists in the spec and that every `/rules` and `/rules/{id}` operation in the spec is listed. Stylesheet change: run `frontend/tests/style-cascade.spec.ts`.
- **Related:** X84 (spec drift), lesson B26 (rule deletion), commits b8aa4ff and 1e1ce31.

### X53 · low · bug · The breadcrumb shows the current section's name but always navigates to the Rule library
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-misc-fe.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / app (WorkspaceHeader) / "Workspace breadcrumb navigation"
- **Location:** `frontend/src/app/WorkspaceHeader.tsx:31` (+ `frontend/src/app/WorkspaceHeader.tsx:4`, `frontend/src/app/Sidebar.tsx:74`, `frontend/src/app/Sidebar.tsx:83`)
- **Problem:** The breadcrumb button shows the current section's name ("Data sources", "API playground", "API reference", "Code studio"), but its handler is always `navigate("/library")`. The sidebar sends the same labels to their own sections (Sidebar.tsx:83-86 "Data sources" → `open("/sources")`, :74-78 "Code studio" → `openCodeStudio()`). The behavior is unchanged since baseline 49bb365.
- **Trigger → consequence:** On #/sources, #/playground or #/docs, clicking the crumb that names that section opens the Rule library instead. In Code studio with an unsaved rule, clicking the "Code studio" crumb shows "You have unsaved changes. Leave this rule and discard them?"; accepting goes to #/library and discards the draft, although the user clicked the name of the view they were in. The confirm prevents a silent loss, and the "Rule library" crumb in graph mode is correct.
- **Evidence:** WorkspaceHeader.tsx:31-33 `<button onClick={() => navigate("/library")}>{sectionLabel(route)}</button>`, labels at :4-15. Browser probe on :3191: `on #/sources: breadcrumb "Data sources" -> #/library`; `on #/playground: breadcrumb "API playground" -> #/library`; `on #/docs: breadcrumb "API reference" -> #/library`; after editing a rule's default value and switching to #/studio/<id>: `breadcrumb "Code studio" -> dialog "You have unsaved changes. Leave this rule and discard them?"`, with `after accept: #/library`.
- **Suggested fix:** Decision needed: the crumb's target must agree with its label. Recommended: replace `sectionLabels` with one table of `{ label, path }` per route in which `path` is null for the view being shown (library → null, rule graph view → `{ "Rule library", "/library" }`, rule code view → `{ "Code studio", null }`, sources/playground/docs → null), and render a null-path crumb as non-interactive text with `aria-current="page"`. Alternative: keep a home link but label it "Rule library" on every page.
- **Regression test:** `frontend/tests/workspace-navigation.spec.ts`: on #/sources, #/playground and #/docs the section crumb is not a button (or clicking it keeps the hash); in Code studio with a dirty draft the "Code studio" crumb shows no discard prompt and keeps the route; on a rule's graph view "Rule library" still navigates to #/library and asks first when the draft is dirty.
- **Related:** lessons F23/F24 (navigation guards and history), X52 (hand-built route paths, no route builder).

### X08 · low · bug · Library filter buttons expose no pressed state, and the search field's aria-label lands on a div
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-misc-fe.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/library (LibraryPage) / "Rule library kind filters and search (accessibility)"
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/library/LibraryPage.tsx:144` (+ `frontend/src/features/library/LibraryPage.tsx:143`, `frontend/src/features/library/LibraryPage.tsx:151`, `frontend/src/features/library/LibraryPage.tsx:153`)
- **Problem:** The kind filter buttons show the active filter only through the "active" CSS class, with no aria-pressed and no tab role or aria-selected; the sidebar does expose its state with aria-current="page" (Sidebar.tsx:151). Separately, MUI 9.4.0 TextField spreads `aria-label="Search rules"` onto the root FormControl div (TextField.js:165-170 passes `...other` to the root slot), so the input's accessible name comes only from its placeholder.
- **Trigger → consequence:** On #/library a screen reader announces four plain buttons ("All rules", "Decision trees", "Formulas", "Condition rules") both before and after choosing one, so the user cannot tell which filter is applied (WCAG 4.1.2 state). `aria-label` on a generic div is prohibited by ARIA 1.2, and `getByLabel("Search rules")` resolves to the div, so `fill` fails. Impact on the search field is small, because the placeholder still names the input "Search rules…".
- **Evidence:** LibraryPage.tsx:143-145 `<button key={type} className={filter === type ? "active" : ""} onClick={() => setFilter(type)}>`; :151-153 `<TextField placeholder="Search rules…" aria-label="Search rules"`. Browser probe on :3191: after clicking Formulas, every button has ariaPressed/role/ariaSelected/ariaCurrent null and only className "active" changes; ariaSnapshot `- button "All rules" … - button "Formulas" … - textbox "Search rules…"`; `getByLabel('Search rules') -> DIV MuiFormControl-root …`; the input has aria-label null; `fill` fails with "Element is not an <input>, <textarea>, <select> or [contenteditable]".
- **Suggested fix:** Add `aria-pressed={filter === type}` to each filter button and wrap the buttons in `role="group" aria-label="Rule kind"`. Move the label onto the input: `slotProps={{ input: { startAdornment: … }, htmlInput: { "aria-label": "Search rules" } }}` (the `htmlInput` slot is already used in NumericDefaultField.tsx:44) and remove the root `aria-label`. The Code studio pane buttons (StudioLibrary.tsx:75) use the same class-only pattern and can take the same treatment.
- **Regression test:** `frontend/tests/catalog-pagination.spec.ts` (library search and filters): `page.getByLabel("Search rules").fill(…)` succeeds on the input; after clicking Formulas, `getByRole("button", { name: "Formulas", pressed: true })` is visible and `getByRole("button", { name: "All rules", pressed: false })` too.
- **Related:** —

### X226 · low · bug · The static nesting-depth check is order-dependent; an accepted, published root fails every execution with the nesting limit
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-sweep.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / source (SourceBindingValidator) / "Rule nesting limit in validate, diagnostics and publish"
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/source/SourceBindingValidator.java:71` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/source/SourceBindingValidator.java:66`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/source/SourceBindingValidator.java:74`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/GraphExecution.java:59`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/execution/Engine.java:95`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/Limits.java:119`)
- **Problem:** The static rule-nesting check, the only one (validate, diagnostics and publish all run it through RuleDefinitionService), walks the dependency DAG with one `visited` set shared by the whole walk, so each pin is checked once, at the depth where depth-first search first reaches it. The verdict therefore depends on the order of the Reference nodes, and a root whose longer call path goes past 16 levels is accepted and published, then fails on every execution. This breaks the Limits.MAX_NESTING_DEPTH javadoc (Limits.java:119-121: "Static source-binding validation rejects deeper reference chains with the same limit"); the only depth checks are in SourceBindingValidator and GraphExecution, and the walk is unchanged since baseline 49bb365.
- **Trigger → consequence:** Publish a chain c0→c1→…→c15 (each rule a Reference to the next; c15 returns 1) and m = Reference c0 (valid alone: its deepest call is 16 levels down). A root with Input → Ref c0 → Ref m → Output gives `/validate` {"valid":true} and `/diagnostics` [], and publish succeeds (v1): c0 is visited at depth 1, and m's own child c0 is skipped as already visited. Every `POST /rules/root/execute` then returns 422 "Rule nesting exceeds 16 levels", because root→m→c0→…→c15 reaches depth 17. Swapping the two Reference nodes (m first) makes validate and publish reject the same graph with 422.
- **Evidence:** SourceBindingValidator.java:66 `if (depth > Limits.MAX_NESTING_DEPTH) throw ArcException.invalid("Rule nesting exceeds " ...)`; :71 `if (!visited.add(dependency.ruleId() + "@" + dependency.version())) continue;`; :74-75 recursion with `visited, depth + 1`; runtime GraphExecution.java:59 `if (depth > Limits.MAX_NESTING_DEPTH) throw ArcException.limit(...)` with Engine.java:95 starting at depth 0. Live probe on :8191 (`$SP/probes/v-sweep/depth_live.py`; the 19 rv-v-sweep-* rules were deleted afterwards): `validate root (c0 first): 200 {'valid': True}` / `validate swapped (m first): 422 Rule nesting exceeds 16 levels` / `diagnostics root: 200 []` / `publish root: 200 1` / `execute root v1: 422 {"issues": ["Rule nesting exceeds 16 levels"], "locations": [{"ruleId": "rv-v-sweep-c14", ...` / `publish swapped root2: 422 Rule nesting exceeds 16 levels`. Finder's Java probe: `$SP/probes/sweep-be/DepthProbe.java`.
- **Suggested fix:** In `validateContracts`, replace `Set<String> visited` with `Map<String, Integer> deepestCheck` (pin → greatest depth at which it was walked) and skip a dependency only when its pin was already walked at a depth ≥ the current one; otherwise record the new depth and walk it again. A deeper arrival leaves less headroom, so the static check then matches the runtime `depth > MAX_NESTING_DEPTH` rule on every path whatever the node order; each pin is walked at most MAX_NESTING_DEPTH + 1 times, the existing `inRule`/`atNode` wrapping keeps the error locations, and repeated source-mapping checks stay cheap (configurations are memoized). Add a docs/api.md "Behavior changes" (Errors and limits) line: validation, diagnostics and publish reject a graph whose longest reference chain exceeds 16 levels regardless of node order; such graphs were accepted before and failed every execution. Already published versions are unchanged and still fail at run time.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/source/SourceBindingValidatorTest.java` with a map-backed `RuleResolver`: chain c0…c15 plus m; the root is rejected with "Rule nesting exceeds 16 levels" in both Reference orders, and a root whose longest path is exactly 16 levels is accepted (both sides of the boundary, lesson B3). Optionally `scripts/smoke.py` publishes the formerly accepted order and expects 422.
- **Related:** X74 (static checks accept parents that always fail at run time), X169 (SourceBindingValidator validate overloads, same class), lesson B7 (nesting is a LIMIT budget), baseline 49bb365.

<a id="x227"></a>

### X227 · low · bug · HTTP source entries are unbounded: {"a":1e5000} makes create/update return 500 instead of 422
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-sweep.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Backend / source.http (HttpSourceAdapter) + persistence (JdbcSourceRepository) / "HTTP data source create and update (API)"
- **Location:** `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/source/http/HttpSourceAdapter.java:29` (+ `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/persistence/JdbcSourceRepository.java:166`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/persistence/JdbcSourceRepository.java:189`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/source/SourceService.java:46`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/source/lookup/LookupSourceAdapter.java:38`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/persistence/JsonCodec.java:17`)
- **Problem:** Corrected by the verification: the finder's consequence (stored version unreadable, GET /api/sources returns 500) is wrong; the source is never stored and GET /api/sources is not broken. The real defect is that the HTTP provider neither rejects nor bounds the `entries` field it does not use (X31): `HttpSourceAdapter.validate` (:29-33) checks only the URL, secret headers and timeout. A POST or PUT whose entries hold a number that expands to more than 1,000 plain digits, such as {"a":1e5000}, passes validation and is inserted; the in-transaction read-back then fails Jackson's number-length limit, so the write rolls back and returns an unclassified 500 instead of 422.
- **Trigger → consequence:** An API client sends `POST /api/sources` {"id":…,"definition":{"kind":"HTTP","url":"https://example.com/a","parameters":[],"entries":{"a":1e5000},"timeoutMs":1000}}. Jackson reads BigDecimal 1E+5000 (use-big-decimal-for-floats), json.encode writes 5,001 plain digits (write-bigdecimal-as-plain) and JSONB stores them; `JdbcSourceRepository.create:166 return get(id, 1)` decodes the row inside the @Transactional `SourceService.create`, which throws "Number value length (5001) exceeds the maximum allowed (1000)" → IllegalStateException, rollback, 500 "An unexpected server error occurred", logged as "Unhandled request error". PUT does the same through update:189 `return get(id, nextVersion)`. Nothing is persisted (version read 404, legacy list 200); the UI cannot trigger it, because `sourceCandidate` (features/sources/model.ts:42) sends only url/secretHeaders for HTTP. A LOOKUP source with the same entries correctly gets 422 "Number exceeds supported precision or magnitude".
- **Evidence:** Java probe `$SP/probes/v-sweep/HttpEntriesProbe.java`: `request entry class=BigDecimal value=1E+5000` / `validate: ACCEPTED` / `decode: IllegalStateException: Could not decode stored ARC definition / cause: JsonMappingException: Number value length (5001) exceeds the maximum allowed (1000 …)` / `lookup validate: ArcException: Number exceeds supported precision or magnitude`. Postgres (read-only): `SELECT length('{"a":1e5000}'::jsonb::text)` → 5008. Live :8191: `POST status=500 {"message":"An unexpected server error occurred"}`, then `GET /api/sources/rv-v-sweep-http-bigexp/versions` → 404 "Source not found", `GET /api/sources` → 200; the API log shows JdbcSourceRepository.get ← JdbcSourceRepository.create:166 ← SourceService.create:50, caused by StreamConstraintsException "Number value length (5001)".
- **Suggested fix:** Apply X31's fix, as maintaining.md:60 requires ("A provider rejects configuration fields it does not use"): `HttpSourceAdapter.validate` throws `ArcException.invalid("HTTP sources do not use lookup entries")` when `entries` is non-null and non-empty, mirroring LookupSourceAdapter's secret-header rule, and LookupSourceAdapter rejects a non-blank `url`. Optional hardening at the storage boundary: `JsonCodec.encode` refuses (422) a number whose plain form exceeds Jackson's read limit, so no other unbounded numeric field can produce a row that cannot be read back. Add a docs/api.md "Behavior changes" (Data sources) line: an HTTP definition with non-empty `entries` returns 422.
- **Regression test:** `backend/src/test/java/dev/arc/source/SourceConfigurationConsistencyTest.java`, next to `aLookupCarryingSecretHeadersIsRejectedBeforeItCanBeTestedOrExecuted`: an HTTP definition carrying `entries` {"a": 1E+5000} is rejected with 422 before storage. `scripts/smoke.py`: POST the raw JSON text (Python's json module cannot write `1e5000`) and expect 422, then `GET /api/sources/<id>/versions` → 404.
- **Related:** X31 (root cause: providers do not reject unused configuration fields), lessons B22 and B17 ("ARC's value bounds keep numbers inside Jackson's plain-writing limit"), X127 (zero of any scale passes the number bounds, another way to reach numbers Jackson cannot handle).

<a id="x225"></a>

### X225 · low · bug · Arrange graph sorts IDs with localeCompare, so the layout depends on the browser locale
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-sweep.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/editor/canvas (graphLayout) / "Arrange graph"
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/graphLayout.ts:17` (+ `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/graphLayout.ts:56`, `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/graphLayout.ts:59`, `docs/architecture.md:76`, `frontend/tests/layout.spec.ts:77`)
- **Problem:** `compareId` orders nodes and edges for ELK with locale-sensitive `String.prototype.localeCompare` (`a.id.localeCompare(b.id)`, used at :56-57 and :59-60). The same graph therefore arranges differently in different browser locales (da-DK sorts "aa" after "z"), and in th-TH IDs that differ only by '-' or '_' compare equal, so the layout depends on the draft's node-array order, which the ID sort is meant to remove. The bug predates the review (49bb365 had the same compareId); node IDs are ASCII `[A-Za-z0-9_-]` (DefinitionShape.java:18-19), so a code-point comparison fixes it.
- **Trigger → consequence:** Input fans out to Outputs aa-review, mm-total and zz-final. In en-US aa-review lands at x=325 and zz-final at x=30; in da-DK aa-review lands at x=30 and zz-final at x=620. Collaborators on different browser locales who press Arrange and save rewrite each other's card positions, giving position-only draft and version diffs. In th-TH, Outputs `calc-total` and `calctotal` swap places when the node array order changes (calc-total x=30 vs 325 for the two orders). This breaks the order-independence that layout.spec.ts:77-85 asserts with its "permuted" check and docs/architecture.md:76 ("Stable input ordering and a fixed seed make repeated arrangements reproducible"); only presentation coordinates are affected.
- **Evidence:** Node 26: under da_DK the sort puts "aa-review" last; under th_TH `"a-b".localeCompare("a_b")` → 0 and `"calc-total".localeCompare("calctotal")` → 0 (en-US gives 1 and -1). Unit probe of the real `arrangeGraph` (spec deleted afterwards): en_US `fan-out [["input",30,30],["aa-review",325,215],["mm-total",620,215],["zz-final",30,215]]`, `array order changes layout: false`; da_DK `fan-out [["input",30,30],["aa-review",30,215],["mm-total",325,215],["zz-final",620,215]]`; th_TH `order A calc-total.x 30 calctotal.x 325` / `order B calc-total.x 325 calctotal.x 30` / `array order changes layout: true`. Locale sort sample: `$SP/probes/sweep-fe/locale-sort.mjs`.
- **Suggested fix:** Replace `compareId` with a named code-unit comparison written with plain `if`s (`if (a.id < b.id) return -1; if (a.id > b.id) return 1; return 0;`) and a comment that IDs are ASCII and the order must not depend on the browser locale. Graphs whose IDs mix case or '-'/'_' may arrange differently than they did under en-US; add a docs/studio.md "Behavior changes" line: Arrange graph gives the same layout in every browser locale.
- **Regression test:** New pure `frontend/tests/unit/graph-layout.spec.ts` (so it runs in `npm run test:unit`; layout.spec.ts is outside tests/unit): run `arrangeGraph` on the aa-review/mm-total/zz-final fan-out and on `calc-total`/`calctotal` in both array orders while `String.prototype.localeCompare` is temporarily replaced by `Intl.Collator("da-DK").compare` and then `Intl.Collator("th-TH").compare` (restored in `finally`); assert positions equal the unstubbed run and do not depend on array order.
- **Related:** X22 (backend locale-dependent lowercase), lesson B9 (an observable order must not follow environment-dependent iteration; sort explicitly); LibraryPage.tsx:189 also sorts with `updatedAt.localeCompare` (noted by the a7b finder, not reported).

## Frontend: duplication and modularity

### X06 · low · cleanup · Test panel and API playground each re-implement execution presentation (trace notices, lazy JSON editor, option defaults, cURL)
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-clean-fe1.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/execution, features/editor/usePreviewExecution.ts / Test panel and API playground: trace notices, execution options (trace, timeout), input JSON editor, cURL example
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/execution/ApiPage.tsx:262` (+ `frontend/src/features/execution/ExecutionResult.tsx:44`, `frontend/src/features/execution/TestPanel.tsx:19`, `frontend/src/features/execution/TestPanel.tsx:88`, `frontend/src/features/execution/ApiPage.tsx:20`, `frontend/src/features/execution/ApiPage.tsx:174`, `frontend/src/features/editor/usePreviewExecution.ts:11`, `frontend/src/features/editor/usePreviewExecution.ts:31`, `frontend/src/features/execution/usePublishedExecution.ts:51`, `frontend/src/features/execution/ExecutionOptionsFields.tsx:45`, `frontend/src/features/execution/TestPanel.tsx:109`, `frontend/src/features/execution/usePublishedExecution.ts:153`)
- **Problem:** The two execution surfaces present the same response contract separately, in four places. (1) The notices for `traceTruncated`/`traceEnabled`/`executedSteps` are written twice with different wording and in opposite order. (2) The lazy `InputJsonEditor` declaration and its `LazyBoundary` wrapper are identical copies, although docs/maintaining.md:153 describes one lazy JSON editor that the two panels share. (3) The execution-option defaults (trace `true`, timeout 30000) are restated in both session hooks, and the offered timeouts are a third literal list. (4) Both build the cURL example with the same `curlExample(ruleApi.executeUrl(..), tryParseExecutionInputs(..), version, { trace, timeoutMs })` call.
- **Trigger → consequence:** No wrong output today. The backend never sets truncated when trace is off (`ExecutionTrace.java:25` `if (!enabled || truncated) return;`), so the reversed notice order is never visible. The cost is drift: any change to trace-flag presentation, or to the default or offered timeouts, must be made in two or three places. Example: change the playground default at usePublishedExecution.ts:52 but not the offered list at ExecutionOptionsFields.tsx:45 (or the reverse); the two panels then start with different timeouts, or the select holds a value that is not one of its options.
- **Evidence:** ApiPage.tsx:262-271 "Trace size limit reached. {n} of {m} executed steps are included; the final result is complete." / "Execution trace is disabled." vs ExecutionResult.tsx:44-55 "Trace disabled. The result includes all executed calculations." / "Trace size limit reached. Showing the first {n} of {m} executed steps. The final result is complete; graph highlights show only the recorded steps." (opposite order). TestPanel.tsx:19 and ApiPage.tsx:20 both `const InputJsonEditor = lazy(() => import("./InputJsonEditor"));`; TestPanel.tsx:88-95 and ApiPage.tsx:174-181 the same `<LazyBoundary label="JSON editor" fallback={<div className="execution-json-editor" role="status">Loading JSON editor…</div>}>`. usePreviewExecution.ts:11 `export const defaultPreviewTimeoutMs = 30_000;` (used at :32), usePublishedExecution.ts:52 `useState(30000)`, ExecutionOptionsFields.tsx:45 `[1000, 5000, 10000, 30000]`; trace `useState(true)` at usePreviewExecution.ts:31 and usePublishedExecution.ts:51. TestPanel.tsx:109-114 and usePublishedExecution.ts:153-158 are the same curlExample call.
- **Suggested fix:** Give each shared piece one owner in `features/execution/` and move both panels to it. (1) `features/execution/executionOptions.ts`: `executionTimeoutChoicesMs = [1000, 5000, 10000, 30000] as const`, `defaultExecutionOptions = { trace: true, timeoutMs: 30_000 }`, and a `useExecutionOptions()` hook returning `{ trace, setTrace, timeoutMs, setTimeoutMs }`; `usePreviewExecution` (drop `defaultPreviewTimeoutMs`, see X54) and `usePublishedExecution` call the hook, and `ExecutionOptionsFields` maps `executionTimeoutChoicesMs`. (2) `features/execution/TraceNotices.tsx` (`{ result: Execution; graphHighlights?: boolean }`): one wording and one order for both notices, keeping the Test panel wording that execution-lifecycle.spec.ts:158/169/202 asserts; ExecutionResult.tsx:44-55 passes `graphHighlights` (which adds "graph highlights show only the recorded steps"), ApiPage.tsx:262-271 renders it without. (3) `features/execution/LazyInputJsonEditor.tsx`: the single `lazy(() => import("./InputJsonEditor"))` plus the `LazyBoundary` label and fallback, forwarding `label`, `value`, `onChange` and `focusRequest`; TestPanel.tsx:87-102 and ApiPage.tsx:174-188 render it. Keep the lazy module named `InputJsonEditor`, so the chunk-failure tests still hit its chunk (execution-catalog.spec.ts:383 routes `**/assets/InputJsonEditor-*.js`; editor-resilience.spec.ts:274 calls `failChunk(page, "InputJsonEditor")`). (4) Optional: a `publishedCurl(ruleId, inputText, version, options)` helper in `features/execution/` for TestPanel.tsx:109-114 and usePublishedExecution.ts:152-158; it cannot live in `domain/`, which must not import `ruleApi`. The playground's notice wording and order change: add one line to docs/studio.md "Behavior changes in this revision" (Library, sources and playground).
- **Regression test:** `frontend/tests/execution-lifecycle.spec.ts`: add playground cases that route `/execute` to return `traceTruncated: true` and `traceEnabled: false`, and assert the same notice text as the Test panel ("Trace size limit reached." … "first 1 of 3 executed steps"; "Trace disabled."). `frontend/tests/unit/execution.spec.ts`: `executionTimeoutChoicesMs` contains `defaultExecutionOptions.timeoutMs`. Keep `frontend/tests/execution-json-editor.spec.ts`, `frontend/tests/execution-catalog.spec.ts:379` and `frontend/tests/editor-resilience.spec.ts:268` green.
- **Related:** F25 (the Test panel and playground already share input-buffer rules through `useInputBuffer`), F26 (cURL endpoint from `ruleApi.executeUrl`), F20 (lazy features render inside `LazyBoundary`); X54 (`defaultPreviewTimeoutMs` export), X172 (endpoint line on the same panel), X182 (the playground's version select).

### X52 · low · cleanup · Routes are parsed in one module but built by hand in 11 places, and ReferenceDialog has a second parser
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-clean-fe1.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / app/routing.ts, app, features/editor / hash routes: rule graph/code links, version links, referenced-rule viewer navigation
- **Location:** `frontend/src/app/routing.ts:26` (+ `frontend/src/App.tsx:157`, `frontend/src/App.tsx:191`, `frontend/src/app/useCodeStudioTarget.ts:39`, `frontend/src/app/useCodeStudioTarget.ts:52`, `frontend/src/features/editor/EditorHeader.tsx:136`, `frontend/src/features/editor/Editor.tsx:236`, `frontend/src/features/editor/VersionHistory.tsx:42`, `frontend/src/features/editor/useRuleDocument.ts:264`, `frontend/src/features/editor/useRuleDocument.ts:313`, `frontend/src/features/editor/useRuleDocument.ts:325`, `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/useGraphFocus.ts:54`, `frontend/src/features/editor/ReferenceDialog.tsx:100`)
- **Problem:** routing.ts says it gives "navigation one owner", but it only parses routes; it has no builder. The inverse is 11 hand-written `/rules|/studio/<id>[?version=]` template literals in 7 files; the version-suffix ternary is copied verbatim between useRuleDocument.ts:325 and useGraphFocus.ts:54, and `?node=` (which parseRoute reads) has no writer. ReferenceDialog re-parses the embedded editor's navigate paths with its own rules: it tests `startsWith("/studio/")`, ignores the rule ID and `?node=`, and falls back to the entry's version.
- **Trigger → consequence:** No misroute today. Renaming a segment, encoding IDs or adding a query parameter such as `node` means editing every literal plus the private parser, and any missed copy misroutes. ReferenceDialog's differences suit the viewer, which only shows pinned versions (`ReferenceTarget.version: number`); its `|| entry.version` fallback is needed for the version-less paths at useRuleDocument.ts:264 and :313. Those two drop `?version=` while :325 keeps it, but neither is reachable for a read-only version: invalid defaults are reported only when the user types, the code buffer is refused for published versions, and the embedded editor hides the controls that would navigate elsewhere (EditorHeader.tsx:133 `readOnly && !embedded`, Editor.tsx:235 `!embedded`, onDelete only with onDeleted).
- **Evidence:** Builders: App.tsx:157 and :191, useCodeStudioTarget.ts:39 and :52, EditorHeader.tsx:136, Editor.tsx:236, VersionHistory.tsx:42 `/rules/${ruleId}?version=${v.version}`, useRuleDocument.ts:264, :313 and :325 `` `/${mode === "code" ? "rules" : "studio"}/${rule.id}${requestedVersion ? `?version=${requestedVersion}` : ""}` ``, useGraphFocus.ts:54 (the same version ternary, verbatim). Parsers: routing.ts:27-37 `/^\/(rules|studio)\/([^/]+)$/ … version: Number(parameters.get("version")) || null, node: parameters.get("node")` vs ReferenceDialog.tsx:100-108 `const [route, query] = path.split("?"); … mode: route.startsWith("/studio/") ? "code" : "graph", version: Number(params.get("version")) || entry.version`.
- **Suggested fix:** Add the inverse of `parseRoute` to `app/routing.ts`, which must stay import-free (scripts/check_architecture.py lists it as pure with no imports): `rulePath({ ruleId, mode = "graph", version = null, node = null }: { ruleId: string; mode?: "graph" | "code"; version?: number | null; node?: string | null }): string`, writing `/rules/` or `/studio/` plus `encodeURIComponent(ruleId)`, then `version` and `node` through `URLSearchParams`. Make `parseRoute` decode the ID (`decodeURIComponent`, treating a malformed escape like any unknown path) so the pair round-trips; IDs are slugs, so every URL produced today stays byte-identical. Migrate all 11 literals: App.tsx:157/:191, EditorHeader.tsx:136 and Editor.tsx:236 → `rulePath({ ruleId })`; useCodeStudioTarget.ts:39/:52 → `mode: "code"`; VersionHistory.tsx:42 → `version: v.version`; useRuleDocument.ts:264/:313/:325 and useGraphFocus.ts:54 pass `version: requestedVersion` uniformly, which removes the :264/:313 vs :325 inconsistency. Replace ReferenceDialog's private parser (ReferenceDialog.tsx:99-111) with `parseRoute(path)` and a named `withRoute(entry, route)` that sets `mode: route.mode` and `version: route.version ?? entry.version` (the viewer's pinned fallback, kept on purpose); this also removes the nested ternary in X176. Optionally add `pagePath(page)` for the `"/library"`, `"/docs"`, `"/playground"` literals. No behavior change; update docs/maintaining.md:125 to say routing.ts builds and parses routes.
- **Regression test:** `frontend/tests/unit/workspace-routing.spec.ts`: `parseRoute(rulePath(r))` equals `r` for graph/code × version null/3 × node null/"input"; `rulePath` reproduces today's strings exactly (`rulePath({ ruleId: "tax", version: 3 }) === "/rules/tax?version=3"`, `rulePath({ ruleId: "tax", mode: "code" }) === "/studio/tax"`). Keep `frontend/tests/node-editor.spec.ts` (nested modal navigation) and `frontend/tests/workspace-navigation.spec.ts` green.
- **Related:** X176 (the nested ternary at ReferenceDialog.tsx:107 sits in the private parser), X97 and X48 (route-driven view switches in useRuleDocument.ts:309-316 use these navigate calls), X53 (breadcrumb route); lesson F24 (history-safe navigation) and the earlier fix "Error navigation needs the whole reference context" (ReferenceDialog's stack, docs/review-lessons.md:90).

### X54 · low · cleanup · Exports with no production caller, kept alive only by tests
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-clean-fe1.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / app/navigationGuards.ts, domain/nodeKinds.ts, features/editor/canvas/graphGeometry.ts, features/editor/usePreviewExecution.ts / navigation guard registry, canvas card summaries, layout geometry, preview timeout
- **Location:** `frontend/src/app/navigationGuards.ts:78` (+ `frontend/src/app/navigationGuards.ts:42`, `frontend/src/domain/nodeKinds.ts:228`, `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/graphGeometry.ts:2`, `frontend/src/features/editor/usePreviewExecution.ts:11`, `frontend/tests/unit/navigation-guards.spec.ts:3`, `frontend/tests/unit/canvas.spec.ts:11`, `frontend/tests/layout.spec.ts:5`)
- **Problem:** Several exports have no production caller and are kept alive only by tests: the guard registry's `first()` and `activeNavigationGuard()`, `nodeSummary()`, and graphGeometry's re-export of `branchHandleX`. `first()` is a second guard query that ignores `GuardScope`. Correction in the record on `defaultPreviewTimeoutMs`: the constant is used inside its own module; only its `export` is unimported, while the playground retypes 30000.
- **Trigger → consequence:** No wrong output. The cost is dead API surface, plus tests pointed at wrappers that production does not call. A future caller of `first()` would prompt for guards scoped out of that change. navigation-guards.spec asserts `first()`, while the app uses `messages(change)`, which is tested separately in workspace-routing.spec. canvas.spec (cited by review-lessons.md:106 for card summaries) tests `nodeSummary`, while GraphNode calls `kind.summary` directly; the wrapper is a pure pass-through, so coverage is equivalent today.
- **Evidence:** navigationGuards.ts:42-46 `first(): string | null {…}` and :78-80 `export function activeNavigationGuard() { return workspaceGuards.first(); }`; grep finds only tests/unit/navigation-guards.spec.ts:3 and :9-29; production imports only `activeNavigationGuards` (useWorkspaceNavigation.ts:2, :43, :92). Both were added in 133ca20 with no caller. nodeKinds.ts:228-231 `export function nodeSummary(…) { return nodeKinds[node.type].summary(node, inputCount); }` is used only by tests/unit/canvas.spec.ts:11 and :176-207, while GraphNode.tsx:33 and :74 use `nodeKinds[n.type]` and then `kind.summary(n, data.inputCount)`. graphGeometry.ts:2 `export { branchHandleX } from "../../../domain/nodePorts";` is imported only by tests/layout.spec.ts:5 and :68 (pre-existing since 49bb365). usePreviewExecution.ts:11 `export const defaultPreviewTimeoutMs = 30_000;` has no importer but is used at :32. The finder's export scan is $SP/probes/s2-simplify-fe/exports.py.
- **Suggested fix:** Delete `first()` (navigationGuards.ts:42-46) and `activeNavigationGuard()` (:78-80), and rewrite the two tests in navigation-guards.spec.ts against `messages()`: active registrations are listed earliest first, releasing a registration twice is harmless, and equal messages from separate owners are released independently. Delete `nodeSummary` (nodeKinds.ts:228-231) and make canvas.spec.ts:176-207 call `nodeKinds[type].summary(node, inputCount)`, the path GraphNode uses, so the "card summaries" link in review-lessons.md:106 still points at real coverage. Remove graphGeometry.ts:1-2 (comment and re-export) and import `branchHandleX` in tests/layout.spec.ts from `src/domain/nodePorts`. Make `defaultPreviewTimeoutMs` module-private, or better, replace it with the shared execution defaults proposed in X06. No behavior change.
- **Regression test:** `npm --prefix frontend run build` (tsc catches stale imports) plus the rewritten `frontend/tests/unit/navigation-guards.spec.ts`, `frontend/tests/unit/canvas.spec.ts` and `frontend/tests/layout.spec.ts`. layout.spec.ts mixes pure and browser tests, so run it with `ARC_WEB_URL` pointing at a disposable stack.
- **Related:** X06 (the timeout default); lesson F23 (the scoped guard registry and `messages(change)`); maintainability row on the node-kind descriptor (review-lessons.md:106, card summaries); introduced in 133ca20 (guard exports) and 49bb365 (re-export).

### X55 · low · cleanup · Catalog query-string encoding copied into four endpoint functions; an explicit undefined is sent as "undefined"
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-clean-fe1.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / api (rules.ts, sources.ts, http.ts) / catalog and version-summary reads: library, playground, Reference picker, data sources
- **Location:** `frontend/src/api/rules.ts:23` (+ `frontend/src/api/rules.ts:32`, `frontend/src/api/sources.ts:15`, `frontend/src/api/sources.ts:24`)
- **Problem:** Four endpoint functions copy the same query-string encoding, `new URLSearchParams(Object.entries(query).map(([key, value]) => [key, String(value)]))`. The copied logic writes an explicit `undefined` field, which the optional query types allow, as the literal text "undefined".
- **Trigger → consequence:** Any change to how query strings are encoded needs four edits. Latent bug: a caller passing `{ search: undefined }` sends `search=undefined`, and the server filters for that literal text and returns an empty page. Every current caller passes defined values (strings from text fields, numbers, booleans, or `kind: ""`, which the API accepts with 200), so nothing triggers it today.
- **Evidence:** api/rules.ts:23 and :32 and api/sources.ts:15 and :24 are the same expression. Probe: `node -e '…Object.entries({offset:0,limit:5,search:undefined}).map(([key, value]) => [key, String(value)])…'` → `offset=0&limit=5&search=undefined`; `curl 'localhost:8191/api/rule-summaries?search=undefined&limit=5'` → `{"items":[],"total":0,…}`; `?kind=&limit=1` → 200.
- **Suggested fix:** Add one named transport helper beside `pathId` in `frontend/src/api/http.ts`: `export function queryString(query: Record<string, string | number | boolean | undefined>): string` that skips `undefined` entries and writes the rest with `String(value)`. Keep empty strings, so `kind=` is still sent as today. The four call sites become `` `/rule-summaries?${queryString(query)}` `` and the like. Keep the literal `?` at the call sites so request URLs stay byte-identical for current callers and existing Playwright route patterns. No behavior change for current callers.
- **Regression test:** `frontend/tests/unit/http.spec.ts`: `queryString({ offset: 0, limit: 5, search: undefined })` is `"offset=0&limit=5"`, `queryString({ kind: "" })` is `"kind="`, and `queryString({ publishedOnly: true })` is `"publishedOnly=true"`.
- **Related:** Maintainability row "Catalog paging was copied into both services…" (docs/review-lessons.md:111; the backend fix gave paging and search normalization one owner, `model.PageRequest`/`api.PageParameters`); no other X ID.

### X61 · low · cleanup · Server size limits restated as bare literals; Add node, Add parameter, connect and Create rule do not check them
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-clean-fe1.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / domain (switchBranches.ts, graph.ts), features/editor/inspector, features/editor/canvas, features/editor/RuleSettings.tsx, app/CreateRuleDialog.tsx / graph and rule size limits: Add node, Add parameter, connect, Add case, Add field, Add default return, Create rule, Rule settings
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/editor/inspector/SwitchDefaultReturn.tsx:88` (+ `frontend/src/domain/switchBranches.ts:46`, `frontend/src/domain/switchBranches.ts:56`, `frontend/src/features/editor/inspector/SwitchFields.tsx:162`, `frontend/src/features/editor/inspector/TransformFields.tsx:80`, `frontend/src/features/editor/RuleSettings.tsx:92`, `frontend/src/app/CreateRuleDialog.tsx:36`, `frontend/src/app/CreateRuleDialog.tsx:109`, `frontend/src/features/editor/inspector/useInputParameterRows.ts:39`, `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/useGraphCommands.ts:41`, `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/GraphToolbar.tsx:100`, `frontend/src/domain/graph.ts:124`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/Limits.java:18`)
- **Problem:** The frontend repeats backend `Limits` values as bare literals: 100/200 twice (the button and the domain guard), plus 20, 50, and 160/2000 (only in RuleSettings), and a 150 derived from the 160 label limit. The add paths enforce the limits inconsistently: Add node, Add parameter and connect have no cap, and Create rule checks neither name nor description length. Correction in the record: these do not fail "only at Save". The editor's live diagnostics flag an over-limit draft on the offending node about 350 ms after the edit, and Create rule shows the server's 422 in its dialog.
- **Trigger → consequence:** At 100 nodes, "Add default return" is disabled but Add node still creates node 101. POST /api/diagnostics (useGraphProblems, 350 ms debounce) then reports "Provide 1 to 100 nodes" on that node, and Save fails with 422. Add parameter at 50 inputs works the same way: the Input node shows "Provide at most 50 inputs" and Save returns 422. A 161-character name in Create rule leaves Create enabled, and the dialog shows the server's 422 message. Drift cost: if the button copy and the domain copy of 100/200 fall out of step, the button stays enabled while `setSwitchDefaultReturn` silently returns the unchanged definition (a click that does nothing), or the reverse. No data loss.
- **Evidence:** SwitchDefaultReturn.tsx:86-90 `disabled={readOnly || rule.draft.nodes.length >= 100 || rule.draft.edges.length >= 200}`; switchBranches.ts:46-48 `definition.nodes.length >= 100 || definition.edges.length >= 200 ||`; switchBranches.ts:56-57 label `slice(0, 150)` with a comment citing 160; SwitchFields.tsx:162 `cases.length >= 20`; TransformFields.tsx:80 `fields.length >= 50`; RuleSettings.tsx:92 `name.length > 160 || description.length > 2000`; CreateRuleDialog.tsx:36 and :109 check only `!name.trim() || !isResourceId(id)`; useInputParameterRows.ts:39-53 add and useGraphCommands.ts:41-52 addNode have no cap. Backend Limits.java:18, :24, :27, :48, :54, :77, :80. Probes against :8191 ($SP/probes/v-clean-fe1/limits_probe.py, $SP/probes/v-clean-fe1/limits_save_probe.py): /api/diagnostics with 101 nodes returned 200 `[{"message":"Provide 1 to 100 nodes","locations":[{"ruleId":null,…,"nodeId":"extra94"}]}]` (the 101st node); with 51 inputs, "Provide at most 50 inputs" located on node "input"; PUT 101 nodes → 422 "Provide 1 to 100 nodes"; PUT 51 inputs → 422 "Provide at most 50 inputs"; POST /rules with a 161-character name → 422 "Name must contain 1 to 160 characters". isCurrentGraphLocation (domain/graph.ts:76) maps ruleId null to the shown graph, so the error appears on the card.
- **Suggested fix:** Add `frontend/src/domain/limits.ts`, the frontend mirror of `engine/Limits.java` (numericDefaults.ts:19-26 and studio/snippets.ts:19-20 already name single backend limits this way): `limits = { inputs: 50, nodes: 100, edges: 200, switchCases: 20, transformFields: 50, labelCharacters: 160, nameCharacters: 160, descriptionCharacters: 2_000, identifierCharacters: 64, resourceIdCharacters: 80, nodeIdCharacters: 80 } as const`, with the predicates `canAddNode(definition)`, `canAddEdge(definition)`, `canAddInput(definition)` and `ruleMetadataProblem({ name, description }): string | null` (returning the server's messages: "Name must contain 1 to 160 characters", and "Description exceeds 2,000 characters" per the finder's probe in findings/r2-reuse-fe.md CANDIDATE 2). Then: (1) export `canAddSwitchDefaultReturn(definition, switchId)` from switchBranches.ts and call it from both `setSwitchDefaultReturn` (switchBranches.ts:46-53) and the button (SwitchDefaultReturn.tsx:86-90); derive the label cut at switchBranches.ts:56-57 from `limits.labelCharacters - " · Default".length`. (2) SwitchFields.tsx:162 and TransformFields.tsx:80 read `limits.switchCases` and `limits.transformFields`. (3) Add node: disable the toolbar button (GraphToolbar.tsx:100) with an explanatory tooltip when `!canAddNode`, and re-check in `useGraphCommands.addNode`, because entry points re-check as the command gate does. (4) Connect: `connectGraphNodes` (domain/graph.ts:124) returns the definition unchanged when `!canAddEdge`, and `useGraphCanvas.connect` tells the user why instead of failing silently. (5) Add parameter: disable the button in InputFields.tsx:41-50 and refuse in `useInputParameterRows.add` when `!canAddInput`. (6) RuleSettings.tsx:92 and CreateRuleDialog.tsx:36/:109 use `ruleMetadataProblem` and show its message as helper text. (7) Optionally fold the other local copies into the module: identifiers.ts:1-2 (`maxIdentifierLength = 64` and `{0,63}` in the regex, plus "Maximum 64 characters" in the guidance), resourceIds.ts:1 (`{0,79}` and "Maximum 80 characters") and snippets.ts:19-20 (`maxNodeIdLength = 80`). User-visible change: add to docs/studio.md "Behavior changes in this revision" (Editor or Inspector) that Add node, Add parameter and new connections stop at 100 nodes, 50 parameters and 200 connections, and that Create rule blocks names over 160 and descriptions over 2,000 characters.
- **Regression test:** New `frontend/tests/unit/limits.spec.ts`: read `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/Limits.java` and assert that each mirrored constant equals its Java value (drift guard); boundaries for `canAddNode` (99 → true, 100 → false), `canAddEdge` (199/200), `canAddInput` (49/50) and `ruleMetadataProblem` (160/161 name characters, 2000/2001 description characters). Browser: `frontend/tests/create-rule.spec.ts`: a 161-character name disables Create and shows the message; `frontend/tests/graph-authoring.spec.ts`: an API-created 100-node draft shows Add node disabled, and a 50-input draft disables Add parameter.
- **Related:** X161 (backend limits retyped as help text), X63 (Add parameter naming, same `useInputParameterRows.add`), X87 and X57 (fields accept values beyond server limits); lessons F34 (validate with the server's range), F15 (enforce a rule in both the domain operation and every control); maintainability row "Editor controls restated the command rules" (command gate, docs/review-lessons.md:108).

### X172 · low · convention · Playground endpoint line hand-builds `/api/rules/{id}/execute` outside src/api
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-clean-fe1.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/execution (ApiPage.tsx, ApiReference.tsx, usePublishedExecution.ts), api (rules.ts, http.ts) / API playground endpoint line, API reference base URL
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/execution/ApiPage.tsx:169` (+ `frontend/src/api/rules.ts:19`, `frontend/src/api/rules.ts:72`, `frontend/src/features/execution/usePublishedExecution.ts:153`, `frontend/src/features/execution/ApiReference.tsx:7`, `frontend/src/api/http.ts:60`)
- **Problem:** The playground's endpoint line writes `/api/rules/{id}/execute` by hand, with no transport base and no `pathId` encoding, instead of using the path owned by src/api. This is the pattern lesson F26 removed from the cURL string on the same panel. ApiReference also retypes the `/api` base, which is a weaker case because it is a static documentation table.
- **Trigger → consequence:** Nothing is wrong today. The base is "/api" (http.ts:60, :94), and rule IDs match `[a-z][a-z0-9-]{0,79}` (Identifiers.java:18-19), so encodeURIComponent leaves them unchanged. If the client base or the execute path changes in src/api, the endpoint label keeps showing the old path while the request and the cURL below it use the new one. This breaks frontend/AGENTS.md:10: "Keep resource paths and HTTP errors in `src/api/`".
- **Evidence:** ApiPage.tsx:167-170 `<code>/api/rules/{id || "{id}"}/execute</code>`; api/rules.ts:19 ``const executePath = (id: string) => `/rules/${pathId(id)}/execute`;``; api/rules.ts:72-73 `executeUrl: … new URL(http.url(executePath(id)), origin).href`; usePublishedExecution.ts:153-154 `curlExample(ruleApi.executeUrl(id || defaultRuleId), …)`; docs/review-lessons.md:40 (F26): the command "receives its endpoint from `ruleApi.executeUrl`, so resource paths stay in `api/`"; ApiReference.tsx:7 `Base URL: <code>{window.location.origin}/api</code>` vs http.ts:60 `baseUrl = "/api"`.
- **Suggested fix:** Compute the endpoint once in `usePublishedExecution`: `const endpoint = ruleApi.executeUrl(id || defaultRuleId)`, pass it to `curlExample` (usePublishedExecution.ts:153) and return `endpointPath: new URL(endpoint).pathname`, which ApiPage.tsx:169 renders; the label, the cURL and the request then share one source. Decision needed (minor): with no rule selected, the label would show the sample rule's path, as the cURL already does, instead of the `{id}` placeholder; recommend matching the cURL. If the placeholder must stay, define the template in api/rules.ts (for example `executePathTemplate = http.url("/rules/{id}/execute")`) rather than in the component. For ApiReference.tsx:7, export `apiBaseUrl(origin = window.location.origin)` (`new URL(http.url(""), origin).href`) from api/http.ts and render it. No compatibility entry needed.
- **Regression test:** `frontend/tests/execution-catalog.spec.ts` (already covers the playground cURL endpoint): for a selected rule, the `.endpoint code` text equals the path of the URL in the cURL example. `frontend/tests/unit/http.spec.ts`: `apiBaseUrl("https://arc.example")` is `"https://arc.example/api"`, beside the existing `executeUrl` test.
- **Related:** F26; X06 (same panel), X07 (the same static ApiReference table lacks DELETE /rules/{id} and its 409).

### X62 · low · cleanup · Decimal-text grammar and zero trimming duplicated in numericDefaults.ts and json.ts
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-clean-fe2.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / domain (json.ts, numericDefaults.ts) / NUMBER parameter defaults and the lossless JSON number comparison
- **Location:** `frontend/src/domain/numericDefaults.ts:16` (+ `frontend/src/domain/json.ts:54`, `frontend/src/domain/numericDefaults.ts:42`, `frontend/src/domain/json.ts:60`, `frontend/src/domain/numericDefaults.ts:74`, `frontend/src/domain/json.ts:62`, `frontend/src/domain/numericDefaults.ts:79`, `frontend/src/domain/json.ts:64`, `frontend/src/domain/numericDefaults.ts:97`)
- **Problem:** The typed-decimal grammar and its digit normalization are written twice in domain/. numericDefaults.ts has `decimalText` and `decimalParts()`; json.ts has a byte-identical regex, also named `decimalParts`, inside `canonicalDecimal`. Both copies destructure the match the same way, run the same empty check and trim leading and trailing zeros the same way. docs/maintaining.md:115 says "`sameDecimalValue` is the shared decimal comparison used by numeric defaults and the codec", so one owner is the intent; the grammar was nevertheless split in 133ca20, when the old `normalizedDecimal` moved into json.ts.
- **Trigger → consequence:** Nothing goes wrong today, because the two copies are identical; the cost is that they can drift. `parseNumericDefault` validates the text with its own copy (`decimalParts(text)`, line 97), then calls `sameDecimalValue(text, double)`, which parses the same user-typed text again with json.ts's copy. If json.ts's copy were narrowed (for example to reject "+1" or ".5") and numericDefaults' copy were not, the field would still accept "+1", `canonicalDecimal` would return null, and the value would silently become `DecimalNumber("1")` instead of the double 1: the draft's number type would change with no error.
- **Evidence:** `grep -F` over frontend/src finds exactly two copies of the regex: json.ts:54 `const decimalParts = /^([+-]?)(\d*)(?:\.(\d*))?(?:[eE]([+-]?\d+))?$/;` and numericDefaults.ts:16 `const decimalText = /^([+-]?)(\d*)(?:\.(\d*))?(?:[eE]([+-]?\d+))?$/;`. Destructuring and empty check: numericDefaults.ts:42-43 and json.ts:60-61 `const [, sign, integer, fraction = "", exponent = "0"] = parts; if (!integer && !fraction) return null;`. Leading-zero trim: numericDefaults.ts:74 `(parts.integer + parts.fraction).replace(/^0+/, "")` / json.ts:62 `(integer + fraction).replace(/^0+/, "")`. Trailing-zero trim: numericDefaults.ts:79-81 / json.ts:64 `digits.replace(/0+$/, "")`.
- **Suggested fix:** Make json.ts, the codec that numericDefaults.ts already imports, the single owner of the grammar: export `interface DecimalParts { negative: boolean; integer: string; fraction: string; exponent: bigint }`, `decimalTextParts(text: string): DecimalParts | null` (the one regex, destructuring and empty check) and `significantDigits(parts): { digits: string; trailingZeros: number }` (the leading and trailing zero trim). Rewrite `canonicalDecimal` (json.ts:57-70) on top of them. In numericDefaults.ts delete `decimalText`, the local `decimalParts` (numericDefaults.ts:39-50) and the trimming in `withinServerLimits` (numericDefaults.ts:71-86), and use the exported helpers in `parseNumericDefault`, `jsonNumberToken` and `withinServerLimits`. Keep the helpers in json.ts rather than a new domain module, so api/http.ts still depends only on `domain/json` (TRANSPORT_FRONTEND in scripts/check_architecture.py). No behavior change. Coordinate with X32 and X33, which change how the same codec treats scale and DecimalNumber equality.
- **Regression test:** `frontend/tests/unit/numeric-defaults.spec.ts` and `frontend/tests/unit/json.spec.ts`: add one shared table of typed texts ("+1", ".5", "5.", "1e3", "-012.50e1" accepted; "1e", ".", "e3", "0x10" rejected) asserted against both `parseNumericDefault(text).valid` and `decimalTextParts(text) !== null`, so the two consumers cannot accept different texts. Keep the existing tests "numeric defaults retain values across decimal and JSON notation" (numeric-defaults.spec.ts) and "decimal comparison ignores spelling but not rounding, overflow or underflow" (json.spec.ts, e.g. `["1.50e1", 15]`, `["+001.2300", 1.23]`, `[".5", 0.5]`, `["5.", 5]`) green.
- **Related:** X32 (json.ts:285 turns 10.50 into a plain double and loses scale), X33 (DecimalNumber compared by token text in `sameDefinition`); lesson F27 (`NumericDefaultField` compares decimal values); the split was introduced in 133ca20.

### X70b · low · cleanup · Colors that must match by design are unlinked literals (symbol roles, edge-label mask, Monaco background, preview arrowhead)
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-clean-fe2.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/studio (arcLanguage.ts, ExpressionColorKey.tsx), styles (tokens.css, expressions.css, library-previews.css), features/editor/canvas/flowElements.ts, features/library/RulePreview.tsx / expression symbol colors and their legend, Available variables list, connection labels, Monaco surface, library preview arrows
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/studio/ExpressionColorKey.tsx:5` (+ `frontend/src/features/studio/arcLanguage.ts:80`, `frontend/src/styles/expressions.css:117`, `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/flowElements.ts:103`, `frontend/src/styles/tokens.css:24`, `frontend/src/features/studio/arcLanguage.ts:89`, `frontend/src/styles/tokens.css:32`, `frontend/src/features/library/RulePreview.tsx:40`, `frontend/src/styles/library-previews.css:22`)
- **Problem:** Colors that must match by design are restated as unlinked literals. The four symbol-role colors appear in the Monaco theme, in the color key's inline styles and (input and result) in the Available-variables CSS. The edge-label mask repeats the `--color-canvas` value inline, even though the token's own comment says connection labels use the token. Monaco's `editor.background` repeats `--color-code-background`, and the preview arrowhead repeats the preview connection stroke. The project's style rule (docs/maintaining.md:157: tokens hold "the values that several rules share by design") says these belong together; there is no "change both" note of the kind tokens.css gives for the theme.ts palette, and the 9e5b177 token migration did not cover them.
- **Trigger → consequence:** Nothing goes wrong today; every pair of copies holds the same value. On the first change: changing the parameter or variable color in the arc-light theme leaves the legend (whose only job is to explain the editor's colors) and the Available-variables list showing the old blue and purple. Changing `--color-canvas` leaves an old-colored box behind every edge label, because the label background is the inline SVG style `labelBgStyle: {fill: "#f8faf8"}`, which RoutedEdge passes to BaseEdge and CSS never reaches; only `.handle-label` actually reads the token. Changing `--color-code-background` leaves the Monaco surface on #fcfdfc inside its restyled container. The preview arrowhead keeps its old color if the connection stroke changes.
- **Evidence:** arcLanguage.ts:80-83 `{ token: "function", foreground: "8B682F" }, { token: "formula", foreground: "007C83" }, { token: "parameter", foreground: "205FA6" }, { token: "variable", foreground: "7B3F98" }`; ExpressionColorKey.tsx:5-8 `style={{ color: "#8B682F" }}` … `"#007C83"` … `"#205FA6"` … `"#7B3F98"`; expressions.css:117-122 `.expression-variable-list code[data-kind="input"] { color: #205fa6; }` and `code[data-kind="result"] { color: #7b3f98; }` (the roles line up: expressionSymbols.ts:177 maps RESULT to "variable", AvailableVariables.tsx:19 maps RESULT to "result"; grep shows these hex values nowhere else). tokens.css:24-25 `/* The graph canvas; connection labels use it to mask the line behind them. */ --color-canvas: #f8faf8;` vs flowElements.ts:103 `const edgeLabelBackground = { fill: "#f8faf8", fillOpacity: 1 };`, applied at :151 and passed at RoutedEdge.tsx:63 `labelBgStyle={labelBgStyle}`. tokens.css:32 `--color-code-background: #fcfdfc` vs arcLanguage.ts:89 `"editor.background": "#fcfdfc"`. library-previews.css:22 `stroke: #879e93` vs RulePreview.tsx:40 `<path d="M 0 0 L 8 4 L 0 8 z" fill="#879e93" />`. The "preserved as found" stylesheet list in docs/reviews/2026-09-27-full-review.md:245-250 does not include these.
- **Suggested fix:** Make `styles/tokens.css` the one owner of these values. (1) Add `--color-symbol-function`, `--color-symbol-formula`, `--color-symbol-parameter`, `--color-symbol-result` and `--color-preview-connection`. (2) expressions.css:117-122 reads the parameter and result tokens; ExpressionColorKey renders role-marked spans (`<span data-role="parameter">Input</span>`) styled by a CSS rule that reads the tokens, with no inline colors. (3) arcLanguage.ts builds the arc-light theme from the tokens when it defines it: `getComputedStyle(document.documentElement).getPropertyValue("--color-symbol-parameter")` with the `#` stripped for `foreground`, and `editor.background` from `--color-code-background`. The module is loaded lazily after index.css, so the tokens are resolved. Decision needed: if a runtime read into `defineTheme` is not acceptable, keep one exported `symbolColors` map in `features/studio/` read by both the theme and the legend, and mirror it in tokens.css with a "change both" note like the theme.ts palette note; recommend the token read, which leaves a single owner. (4) flowElements.ts:103 → `{ fill: "var(--color-canvas)", fillOpacity: 1 }` (custom properties resolve in the SVG style attribute). (5) RulePreview.tsx:40 → `<path className="preview-arrowhead" …/>` with `.preview-arrowhead { fill: var(--color-preview-connection) }` next to `.preview-connection { stroke: var(--color-preview-connection) }` in library-previews.css. No visible change (same values).
- **Regression test:** `frontend/tests/expression-colors.spec.ts`: the computed color of each `.expression-color-key` role and of the Available-variables `code[data-kind="input"]`/`code[data-kind="result"]` entries equals the painted Monaco color of the same role (compare with each other, not with hex literals). A canvas assertion, for example in `frontend/tests/routing.spec.ts`: an edge label's background rect has a computed `fill` equal to the resolved `--color-canvas`. `frontend/tests/style-node-kind-colors.spec.ts`: the preview marker path's fill equals `.preview-connection`'s stroke. Keep `frontend/tests/style-cascade.spec.ts` green.
- **Related:** X70 (first verification in `verdicts/v-studio2.md`, logged by another group); this record is headed "VERDICT X70" in v-clean-fe2.md and listed as X70b in verdicts.tsv. Lesson F12 (painted colors are asserted in the editor); the node-kind color token decision in docs/review-lessons.md:106; the 9e5b177 token migration did not cover these values.

### X71b · low · cleanup · Input type names restated in three files and matched case-sensitively, unlike the backend grammar
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-clean-fe2.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / domain/expressionSymbols.ts, features/studio/arcLanguage.ts, features/editor/inspector/InputParameterCard.tsx / Code studio declaration coloring, Monarch type highlighting, inspector Type dropdown
- **Location:** `frontend/src/domain/expressionSymbols.ts:26` (+ `frontend/src/domain/expressionSymbols.ts:125`, `frontend/src/features/studio/arcLanguage.ts:10`, `frontend/src/features/editor/inspector/InputParameterCard.tsx:80`, `frontend/src/types.ts:10`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/script/ArcScriptParser.java:23`)
- **Problem:** The five input type names are written as three separate string lists besides the `InputType` union, and none of them is tied to it; the backend keeps one `InputTypes.NAMES`. Only `Record<InputType, …>` tables such as `placeholderLiteral` are checked by the compiler. The Script declaration check and the Monarch type rule also match only upper case, although the backend grammar accepts any case.
- **Trigger → consequence:** The first cost is future drift: a new `InputType` member would compile cleanly, but the inspector's Type dropdown, Script parameter coloring and Monarch type highlighting would all leave it out. The second is a cosmetic difference that exists today: typing `amount: number required;` in Code studio builds successfully on the server. Until the next Build rewrites the buffer with the canonical text (the `source/built` reducer sets `source: action.source`), the frontend does not color that declaration, a new input's uses are not colored as a parameter, and the Monarch rule leaves `number` uncolored as a type.
- **Evidence:** expressionSymbols.ts:26 `const inputTypes = new Set(["NUMBER", "STRING", "BOOLEAN", "ARRAY", "OBJECT"]);`, used at :125-128 `inputTypes.has(tokens[index + 2]?.text)`; arcLanguage.ts:10-17 `const typeNames = [...nodeTypes, "NUMBER", "STRING", "BOOLEAN", "ARRAY", "OBJECT"];` (node types come from nodeKinds, input types are literals); InputParameterCard.tsx:80 `(["NUMBER", "STRING", "BOOLEAN", "ARRAY", "OBJECT"] as const).map(`; types.ts:10 holds the union. Backend: ArcScriptParser.java:23-29 builds `TYPES` from `InputTypes.NAMES` with `Pattern.CASE_INSENSITIVE`, and :125 `match.group(2).toUpperCase(Locale.ROOT)`; docs/maintaining.md:34 "`InputTypes.NAMES` is the one list of declared types". Probe 1: `POST :8191/api/studio/build` with `inputs { amount: number required; }` returned `diagnostics: []` and input type `"NUMBER"`, with canonical source `amount: NUMBER required;` (graph payload shape in $SP/probes/v-clean-fe2/def.json). Probe 2 (unit, zz-v-clean-fe2-x.spec.ts, since deleted): `expressionSymbols(script, [], true)` classifies `amount` as `["22:parameter","111:parameter"]` for `NUMBER` and as `[]` for `number`.
- **Suggested fix:** Add `frontend/src/domain/inputTypes.ts`, an exhaustive descriptor in the style of nodeKinds.ts: `const inputTypeFacts: Record<InputType, { label: string }> = { NUMBER: { label: "number" }, STRING: …, BOOLEAN: …, ARRAY: …, OBJECT: … }`, `export const inputTypes: readonly InputType[]` derived from it in menu order, and `export function inputTypeOf(text: string | undefined): InputType | null`, which upper-cases like the backend and looks the name up as an own key. Move the callers: (1) expressionSymbols.ts:26 and :125-128 → `inputTypeOf(tokens[index + 2]?.text) !== null`; (2) arcLanguage.ts:10-17 → node types from `nodeTypes` plus a case-insensitive alternation generated from `inputTypes` (Monarch has no per-rule flags, so generate character classes such as `[Nn][Uu][Mm][Bb][Ee][Rr]` in a small helper); (3) InputParameterCard.tsx:80 → `inputTypes.map(…)` labelled with `inputTypeFacts[type].label`, and `inputTypeOf(event.target.value)` in place of the `as InputType` cast. Visible change: lowercase declarations get parameter and type colors before a rebuild; note it in docs/studio.md "Behavior changes in this revision" (Code studio and completion).
- **Regression test:** `frontend/tests/unit/expression-symbols.spec.ts`: `amount` is classified as a parameter for `NUMBER`, `number` and `Number` declarations. New `frontend/tests/unit/input-types.spec.ts`: `inputTypes` equals the names in `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/InputTypes.java` (read the file), `inputTypeOf` is case-insensitive and returns null for `__proto__`. Browser: `frontend/tests/expression-colors.spec.ts`: a lowercase `number` declaration's parameter is painted with the parameter color.
- **Related:** X71 (first verification in `verdicts/v-studio2.md`, logged by another group; it also notes that `sampleValue`'s switch is not compiler-checked because tsconfig lacks noImplicitReturns); this record is headed "VERDICT X71" in v-clean-fe2.md and listed as X71b in verdicts.tsv. X68 (the same Monarch tokenizer colors fragments inside identifiers); lesson F12; maintainability row on the node-kind descriptor (docs/review-lessons.md:106).

### X38 · low · altitude · Source provider facts are scattered two-way checks with an inconsistent implicit else-branch
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-clean-fe2.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/sources (model.ts, sourceDocument.ts, SourceConfigurationFields.tsx, SourcesPage.tsx, SourceBindingEditor.tsx) / data source providers (HTTP, LOOKUP) and the documented "Adding a data source" extension path
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/sources/model.ts:42` (+ `frontend/src/features/sources/sourceDocument.ts:127`, `frontend/src/features/sources/sourceDocument.ts:212`, `frontend/src/features/sources/SourceConfigurationFields.tsx:25`, `frontend/src/features/sources/SourcesPage.tsx:81`, `frontend/src/features/sources/SourcesPage.tsx:176`, `frontend/src/features/sources/SourceBindingEditor.tsx:110`)
- **Problem:** Per-provider facts on the frontend are seven scattered two-way checks that treat one kind as the implicit "else". There is no exhaustive per-kind table like `domain/nodeKinds.ts`, and the sites do not even agree on which kind the else-branch stands for: most treat any kind other than HTTP as LOOKUP, while the provider switch treats any kind other than LOOKUP as HTTP. The Provider menu is hard-coded and restates the union in a cast.
- **Trigger → consequence:** Nothing goes wrong with today's two kinds. The cost falls on the documented extension path: docs/maintaining.md:60 ("Adding a data source", step 3) says to extend the frontend `SourceConfig` and source form, and warns that "Registering a strategy alone does not create its UI or storage contract". Adding a third kind to `SourceConfig["kind"]` compiles cleanly, and then `sourceCandidate` sends `entries` and drops `url` and `secretHeaders`; `sourceSaveProblem` skips the timeout check; the form shows the lookup-entries field and the lookup help text; the source list shows the Database icon; the Provider menu has no entry for the new kind; and the provider switch treats the new kind as HTTP and installs `customerId`. Every site must be found by grep, because none is an exhaustive `Record`/`switch` the compiler can check (SourceBindingEditor's `|| "LOOKUP"` only fills a summary field that SourceProviderSelect never displays).
- **Evidence:** Kinds other than HTTP fall into the lookup branch at: model.ts:42 `...(configuration.kind === "HTTP" ? { url, secretHeaders: … } : { entries: parseJsonObject(…) })`; sourceDocument.ts:127 `document.source.definition.kind === "HTTP" && parseHttpTimeout(document.timeout) === null`; SourceConfigurationFields.tsx:25 `const http = configuration.kind === "HTTP";` (used at :30, :61 and :67); SourcesPage.tsx:81 `source.kind === "HTTP" ? (<Globe2 …/>) : (<Database …/>)`. Opposite else-branch: sourceDocument.ts:212 `name: action.kind === "LOOKUP" ? "key" : "customerId"`. Hard-coded menu with the union restated in a cast: SourcesPage.tsx:176-181 `event.target.value as "HTTP" | "LOOKUP"` … `<MenuItem value="LOOKUP">` / `<MenuItem value="HTTP">`. Placeholder: SourceBindingEditor.tsx:110 `kind: detail.data?.definition.kind || "LOOKUP"`. Precedent: docs/reviews/2026-09-27-full-review.md:200 describes `domain/nodeKinds.ts` as "one `Record<NodeType, NodeKind>` stating every per-kind fact".
- **Suggested fix:** Add `frontend/src/features/sources/sourceProviders.ts`, a pure `Record<SourceConfig["kind"], SourceProvider>` descriptor. Register it in PURE_FRONTEND in scripts/check_architecture.py with `("types", "domain/")`, and allow model.ts and sourceDocument.ts to import it. Each entry states `label` (menu text: "Local lookup table", "HTTP GET · JSON response"), `starterParameter` ("key" or "customerId"), `usesTimeout`, `parametersHelp`, and `activeFields(definition, buffers)`, which returns the provider's payload fields: HTTP returns `url` and the parsed secret-header aliases, LOOKUP returns the parsed `entries`, each with today's error messages. Move `secretHeaderAliases`/`hasTextValues` (model.ts:54-70) into the HTTP entry so model.ts does not import back into the descriptor. Export `sourceKinds` (menu order LOOKUP, HTTP) and an `isSourceKind(value)` guard. Move the call sites: `sourceCandidate` (model.ts:24-52) spreads `sourceProviders[kind].activeFields(…)`; sourceDocument.ts:127 reads `usesTimeout`; sourceDocument.ts:212 reads `starterParameter`; SourcesPage.tsx:176-181 maps `sourceKinds` and uses `isSourceKind` instead of the cast. Keep React parts in exhaustive tables beside the UI, as the node icons and inspector forms are: a `Record<kind, Icon>` for SourcesPage.tsx:81, and a `Record<kind, ComponentType>` of provider form sections in SourceConfigurationFields.tsx (HTTP: URL, timeout, secret headers; LOOKUP: entries) with the help text from the descriptor. Make `kind` optional in SourceProviderSelect's selected summary and drop the `|| "LOOKUP"` placeholder. Update docs/maintaining.md:60 step 3 to name the descriptor ("add an entry to `sourceProviders`; the compiler then lists the icon and form tables"). No behavior change for HTTP and LOOKUP.
- **Regression test:** New `frontend/tests/unit/source-providers.spec.ts`: every `SourceConfig["kind"]` has a descriptor; `sourceCandidate` builds an HTTP payload with `url`/`secretHeaders` and no `entries`, and a LOOKUP payload with `entries` only; `sourceSaveProblem` checks the timeout only where `usesTimeout`; the `provider` action installs `starterParameter`. Keep `frontend/tests/source-editor.spec.ts` (active provider payloads) and `frontend/tests/unit/source-document.spec.ts` green.
- **Related:** X34 (a provider-switch round trip discards declared parameters; same `provider` action at sourceDocument.ts:212); lessons F7 (payloads from the active provider only) and F34; the node-kind descriptor row in docs/review-lessons.md:106.
