### X205 · low · altitude · Edge styling re-derives "fallback exit" from the literal "false", so Switch Default connections look ordinary; trace badges only for true/false
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-clean-fe4.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/editor/canvas/flowElements.ts, domain/nodePorts.ts, domain/switchBranches.ts, features/execution/ExecutionResult.tsx, features/library (domain/rulePreview.ts, RulePreview.tsx) / connection colors and labels, Test trace branch badges, library preview labels
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/flowElements.ts:108` (+ `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/flowElements.ts:147`, `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/flowElements.ts:121`, `frontend/src/domain/nodePorts.ts:24`, `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/GraphNode.tsx:104`, `frontend/src/features/execution/ExecutionResult.tsx:85`, `frontend/src/domain/rulePreview.ts:85`, `frontend/src/features/library/RulePreview.tsx:51`)
- **Problem:** `flowEdges` re-derives "fallback exit" from the literal "false" and ignores the `fallback` flag on the port it already looks up for the label. A Switch Default connection's stroke and label therefore use the ordinary colours, while the Default caption on the same card is styled `handle-fallback` (GraphNode reads `port.fallback`), and a Condition False edge is brown. Other handle facts are also restated outside `domain/nodePorts`, the documented owner (docs/maintaining.md:79): "default" 4 times, "next" twice, "false" twice. ExecutionResult shows a branch badge only for "true"/"false", so a Switch trace step shows just its raw value `"case:<id>"`/`"default"` with a chevron. The rulePreview `port?.label || edge.sourceHandle` plus RulePreview `label !== "next"` pair is only a code smell with no visible defect, because the server rejects blank case labels (DefinitionShape.java:188-190).
- **Trigger → consequence:** Any Switch with a connected Default: its edge is drawn stroke #a5b4ae with label fill #47765d (ordinary branch), while its card caption is brown fallback; a Condition's False edge is #b7a696/#956a4a. A new fallback-style exit would silently get ordinary edge styling. In the Test panel trace, Switch steps get no branch badge.
- **Evidence:** flowElements.ts:108 `return sourceHandle === "false" ? "#b7a696" : "#a5b4ae";`, :147-150 `labelStyle: edge.sourceHandle === "false" ? edgeLabelStyles.fallback : edgeLabelStyles.ordinary`, while :121-125 already finds the port. nodePorts.ts:24 `{ id: "default", label: "Default", fallback: true }`. GraphNode.tsx:104 `${port.fallback ? "handle-fallback" : ""}`. ExecutionResult.tsx:85 `step.branch === "true" || step.branch === "false" ?`. Backend SWITCH `Outcome(branch, branch)` at GraphExecution.java:166-167 makes value == branch. Unit probe of flowEdges: ports `s [["case:a",false],["default",true]]`; edges `cf false False #b7a696 {"fill":"#956a4a"…}`, `sd default Default #a5b4ae {"fill":"#47765d"…}`. No test pins edge colours. The split predates the recent commits (baseline useGraphCanvas.ts:106-116 styled only "false" edges while its caption treated "false" and "default" as fallback). Handle literals outside nodePorts: switchBranches.ts:10, :50, :79, SwitchDefaultReturn.tsx:63 ("default"), useGraphCanvas.ts:156 and RulePreview.tsx:51 ("next"), flowElements.ts:108 and :148 ("false").
- **Suggested fix:** Make `domain/nodePorts.ts` the one owner of handle facts, as `Handles` is on the backend. (1) Export named handle IDs (`handles.next`, `handles.true`, `handles.false`, `handles.default`, `caseHandle(id)`) and a lookup `sourcePort(node, handleId): SourcePort | undefined`. (2) flowElements.ts: resolve the port once (:121-125) and style from it: `edgeStroke(active, port?.fallback ?? false)` and `labelStyle: port?.fallback ? edgeLabelStyles.fallback : edgeLabelStyles.ordinary`. (3) ExecutionResult: pass the shown draft from TestPanel; for depth-0 steps take the badge text (for example the case label) and a `fallback` class from `sourcePort(node, step.branch)`, renaming `.trace-branch.false` (styles/execution.css:198) to `.trace-branch.fallback`; nested steps from other rules show the raw handle text. (4) rulePreview.ts:85 returns `port?.label ?? ""`, and RulePreview.tsx:51 drops `label !== "next"`. (5) Replace the literals at switchBranches.ts:10, :50, :79, SwitchDefaultReturn.tsx:63 and useGraphCanvas.ts:156 with the named handles, and extend the handle-literal rule in scripts/check_architecture.py (today Java-only, :30-39) to frontend/src outside domain/nodePorts.ts, for example `sourceHandle\s*(===|!==|:)\s*"(next|true|false|default)"` and `"case:"`. Visible change: Switch Default connections get the fallback colours and Switch trace steps get a badge; add both to docs/studio.md "Behavior changes in this revision" (Presentation).
- **Regression test:** `frontend/tests/unit/canvas.spec.ts`: `flowEdges` gives a Switch Default edge and a Condition False edge the fallback stroke and label style, and case and True edges the ordinary ones. `frontend/tests/node-kind-presentation.spec.ts` (already pins the Default caption as `handle-fallback`): the Default edge's path stroke equals the False edge's. `frontend/tests/execution-lifecycle.spec.ts`, or a unit test of the badge helper: a Switch trace step shows its case label as a badge, and `default` gets the fallback class.
- **Related:** X208 (nodePorts also owns card width); maintainability rows on the backend `NodeKind`/`Handles` vocabulary with its architecture rule against handle literals (docs/review-lessons.md:105) and on the frontend node-kind descriptor (docs/review-lessons.md:106).

<a id="x208"></a>

### X208 · low · altitude · Node focus centres on a hard-coded +115/+50 instead of the card's size, so wide Switch cards are off-centre and clipped
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-clean-fe4.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/editor/canvas (useGraphFocus.ts, flowElements.ts, graphGeometry.ts, graphLayout.ts), domain/nodePorts.ts / focusing a node: `?node=` links, the node outline, trace steps, "Show problem", blocked-edge buttons
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/useGraphFocus.ts:41` (+ `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/useGraphFocus.ts:58`, `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/flowElements.ts:17`, `frontend/src/domain/nodePorts.ts:13`, `frontend/src/domain/nodePorts.ts:65`, `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/graphGeometry.ts:3`)
- **Problem:** `focusNode` waits until the card is measured but then centres on `node.position + (115, 50)`, a hard-coded half of the default card. It does not use the card's measured size or `nodeWidth` bounds (`cardBounds` in flowElements.ts:17-31 already combines both). Wide Switch cards are therefore centred off by `nodeWidth/2 − 115` and can be clipped. This is a third copy of the card size, beside `nodePorts.cardWidth` and `graphGeometry.defaultNodeSize`.
- **Trigger → consequence:** A Switch with 6 cases has 7 exits, which makes a 630-px card. Opened via `?node=<switch>` (the same path runs for the outline, trace steps, "Show problem" and blocked-edge buttons): at 1440×1000 the card's centre lands 200 px right of the canvas centre but it still fits (26 px inside); at 1280×800 its right edge is 54 px outside the canvas. With up to 20 cases (1,890-px card) the offset is much larger. The vertical part is minor: a default 94-px card is off by only 3 px.
- **Evidence:** useGraphFocus.ts:41 `void flow.setCenter(node.position.x + 115, node.position.y + 50, { zoom: 1, duration: 350 });`. The effect at :58-71 gates on `measurements.has(pendingFocus)` but never reads the size. nodePorts.ts:13 `const cardWidth = 230;` and :65 `Math.max(cardWidth, sourcePorts(node).length * exitSpacing)`. graphGeometry.ts:3 `export const defaultNodeSize = { width: 230, height: 105 };`. Browser probe on :3191: `1440x1000: pane {"width":1082} card {"width":630,"height":93.6} transform matrix(1,0,0,1,-474,-268)` gives `card center - pane center (x, y): 200 -3, card right beyond pane right by: -26`; `1280x800: pane {"width":922} … transform matrix(1,0,0,1,-554,-368)` gives `200 -3, card right beyond pane right by: 54`.
- **Suggested fix:** Give card geometry one owner in the pure `features/editor/canvas/graphGeometry.ts` (it may import domain/): `cardSize(node, sizes): NodeSize`, the measured size or `{ width: nodeWidth(node), height: defaultCardHeight }`, and `cardCenter(node, sizes)`. `cardBounds` (flowElements.ts:17-31) and graphLayout.ts:27 use `cardSize`; `focusNode` becomes `const center = cardCenter(node, measurements); void flow.setCenter(center.x, center.y, { zoom: 1, duration: 350 });` with `measurements` in its dependencies. Replace `defaultNodeSize` with a height-only `defaultCardHeight`: its `width: 230` has no production reader, and tests/layout.spec.ts:119-120 (which uses it as a size fallback) can use `cardSize`; the width then has one owner, `nodePorts.cardWidth` through `nodeWidth`. Optionally, for cards wider than the pane at zoom 1 (a 20-case Switch is 1,890 px), fit the card's bounds (capped at zoom 1) instead of clipping both sides. No docs entry needed.
- **Regression test:** `frontend/tests/unit/canvas.spec.ts` (or `frontend/tests/layout.spec.ts`): `cardCenter` uses the measured size when present and `nodeWidth` otherwise (630 px for a 6-case Switch). Browser, for example in `frontend/tests/routing.spec.ts`: open `#/rules/<id>?node=<6-case switch>` at 1280×800 and assert that the card's horizontal centre is within 1 px of the pane centre and its right edge is inside the pane.
- **Related:** X89 (the same useGraphFocus effect: an initial fitView overrides "Show problem" from the code view), X205 (nodePorts owns card width); the earlier fix "UI measurements must survive without entering the saved graph" (docs/review-lessons.md:92).

## Frontend: efficiency

<a id="x217"></a>

### X217 · medium · efficiency · A new Monaco `options` object on every render fires a page-wide configuration change for each inline editor
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-eff-fe1.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/expressions (InlineExpressionEditor); the same inline `options` pattern is in features/studio, features/editor and features/execution / Inline expression editors in the inspector (Switch cases, Transform fields), Code studio with the Test panel open
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/expressions/InlineExpressionEditor.tsx:104` (+ `frontend/src/features/expressions/InlineExpressionEditor.tsx:120`, `frontend/src/features/studio/CodeStudio.tsx:114`, `frontend/src/features/editor/NodeExpressionDialog.tsx:96`, `frontend/src/features/expressions/ExpressionDialog.tsx:116`, `frontend/src/features/execution/InputJsonEditor.tsx:60`, `frontend/src/features/studio/useArcEditor.ts:5`)
- **Problem:** InlineExpressionEditor passes `options={{ ...arcEditorOptions, readOnly, ariaLabel: label, … }}` inline, so every render hands Monaco a new object. When the options identity changes, @monaco-editor/react 4.7.0 calls `editor.updateOptions(options)`. monaco-editor 0.56's `StandaloneEditor.updateOptions` writes those options into the page-wide `StandaloneConfigurationService`, which compares values with `===`. Because `quickSuggestions: { other: true, comments: false, strings: false }` is an object-valued top-level key, every call fires a global `onDidChangeConfiguration`, and `ModelService` then processes that event for every model. The cost per keystroke therefore grows with the square of the number of mounted editors. The finder also blamed `padding`, `scrollbar` and `suggest`; the verifier corrected this, because Monaco registers only their dotted sub-keys, so `quickSuggestions` alone causes the change. CodeStudio, NodeExpressionDialog, ExpressionDialog and InputJsonEditor have no object-valued top-level key. They fire a global change only when two mounted editors write different primitive values for the same key.
- **Trigger → consequence:** Measured on minified HEAD builds with render and event counters, against the same build with `useMemo(options, [readOnly, label])`. Chromium, 10 characters typed at 120 ms each, CDP metrics plus Event Timing, 3 runs at 1x. (1) Switch with 20 condition cases (the product maximum), typing into "Case 1 label": median keystroke latency 72–80 ms at HEAD against 40–48 ms with the fix. At HEAD every keystroke was a long task of 57–74 ms; with the fix there were none. Main-thread time was 810–841 ms against 472–485 ms. There were 440 `updateOptions` calls and 440 global configuration changes (20 editors × 22 renders), against 0. (2) Transform with 40 expression fields, typing into "Field 1 name": median 176 ms per keystroke, all 10 keystrokes over 100 ms, long tasks of 159–179 ms, against 64 ms with the fix. Main-thread time was 2.75 s against 0.75 s. (3) Typing inside the Monaco "Case 1 condition" editor of the 20-case Switch: 80 ms against 40 ms. (4) Switch with 10 cases at 4x CPU throttling: 156 ms (all 10 keystrokes over 100 ms) against 92 ms. Main-thread cost per keystroke attributable to this issue at 1x: about 3 ms with 5 editors, 9 ms with 10, 35 ms with 20 and 200 ms with 40, which is roughly quadratic. X219's double render doubles these event counts. Extra case: in Code studio with the Test panel open, 9 typed characters fired 17 global changes, all `editor.lineHeight`, flipping between CodeStudio's 23 and the Test panel JSON editor's 20. Renaming with only the JSON editor mounted fired 0.
- **Evidence:** InlineExpressionEditor.tsx:104-123 contains `options={{ ...arcEditorOptions, readOnly, ariaLabel: label, … quickSuggestions: { other: true, comments: false, strings: false }, … }}`. In @monaco-editor/react dist/index.mjs: `l(()=>{o.current?.updateOptions(x)},[x],s)`. In monaco-editor 0.56: standaloneCodeEditor.js:217-218 `updateOptions(newOptions) { updateConfigurationService(this._configurationService, newOptions, false);`; standaloneServices.js:427 `if (this.getValue(key) === value) { continue; }` and :436 `this._onDidChangeConfiguration.fire(configurationChangeEvent);`; modelService.js:78 `onDidChangeConfiguration(e => this._updateModelOptions(e))` and :166-167 loop over `Object.keys(this._models)`; editorOptions.js:1758 registers `quickSuggestions` with a single anyOf schema, while :1660 `editor.padding.top`, :2002 `editor.scrollbar.vertical` and :2546 `editor.suggest.insertMode` are sub-keys only. Counter probe: switch20 at HEAD gave `{"updateOptions":440,"configChange":440}`, and 0 with the fix. The Code studio log showed `17x editor.lineHeight <= StandaloneConfigurationService.updateValues <= StandaloneEditor.updateOptions`. Harness: `$SP/probes/v-eff-fe1/make_variants.py` (`fix_x217`; variants v0, v1 and v123), `$SP/probes/v-eff-fe1/run-matrix.sh` and `$SP/probes/v-eff-fe1/run-matrix2.sh`. Results are in `$SP/probes/v-eff-fe1/results.jsonl` and `results2.jsonl`; fixtures are `$SP/probes/v-eff-fe1/draft-sw20.json` and `draft-tf40.json`.
- **Suggested fix:** Give every Monaco host a referentially stable options object that changes only when a real option changes. In `features/studio/useArcEditor.ts`, next to `arcEditorOptions`, define module-level presets: `inlineExpressionEditorOptions`, holding everything InlineExpressionEditor sets today except `readOnly` and `ariaLabel`, plus one preset each for Code studio, node code, the expression dialog and the JSON input editor. Add one hook, `useEditorOptions(preset, readOnly, ariaLabel)`, that returns `useMemo(() => ({ ...preset, readOnly, ariaLabel }), [preset, readOnly, ariaLabel])`. Use it in InlineExpressionEditor, CodeStudio, NodeExpressionDialog, ExpressionDialog and InputJsonEditor instead of inline objects. `updateOptions` then runs only when `readOnly` or the label actually changes, so read-only transitions still reach Monaco. This is a pure performance change, with no behavior change and no docs entry.
- **Regression test:** Add a shared browser render probe, `frontend/tests/helpers/renderProbe.ts`. It installs through `page.addInitScript` a `__REACT_DEVTOOLS_GLOBAL_HOOK__` whose `onCommitFiberRoot` walks the committed fibers; the verifier used the same technique. The Playwright suite runs against the minified production build, so the probe must identify fibers by their props, not by component names. In `frontend/tests/inline-expression.spec.ts`, create a 20-case Switch through the API and type 10 characters into "Case 1 label". Among committed fibers whose props carry `options` and `onMount` (the @monaco-editor/react component), assert that none received an `options` object different from its previous render (HEAD: 440 such changes), and that those fibers did re-render, so the assertion is meaningful. Add the same assertion for Code studio with the Test panel open while typing into "ARC code editor" (HEAD: 17 `lineHeight` flips over 9 characters).
- **Related:** X219 (its double render doubles the `updateOptions` count); X223 (every row re-renders, which multiplies this cost: 277 ms per keystroke at 40 fields with both); X64 (the same CodeStudio `options.readOnly`, so keep `readOnly` in the memo dependencies and coordinate the edits); lesson F31 (a new identity on every render re-registered Monaco providers, the same failure class); previous review E02/E04 (docs/reviews/2026-09-27-full-review.md:150)

<a id="x218"></a>

### X218 · low · efficiency · Inline React Flow handlers and an unmemoized GraphNode re-render every card on every Editor render
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-eff-fe1.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/editor/canvas (GraphCanvas, useGraphCanvas, GraphNode) / Graph canvas while typing in the inspector, dragging or selecting cards
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/GraphCanvas.tsx:189` (+ `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/GraphCanvas.tsx:194`, `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/GraphCanvas.tsx:203`, `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/GraphCanvas.tsx:207`, `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/GraphCanvas.tsx:208`, `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/GraphCanvas.tsx:209`, `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/GraphCanvas.tsx:213`, `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/useGraphCanvas.ts:143`, `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/useGraphCanvas.ts:147`, `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/GraphNode.tsx:31`, `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/flowElements.ts:33`, `docs/maintaining.md:143`)
- **Problem:** On every Editor render, GraphCanvas creates new inline `onNodeClick`, `onNodeContextMenu`, `onPaneClick`, `onPaneContextMenu`, `onMoveStart`, `onEdgeClick` and `onEdgeContextMenu` handlers, and useGraphCanvas creates new `onEdgesChange` and `connect` functions, neither wrapped in `useCallback`. React Flow 12.11.6's memoized NodeRenderer and EdgeRenderer pass these handlers to every memoized NodeWrapper and EdgeWrapper. GraphNode is not wrapped in `memo`, so every card (MUI Tooltip, Handles, icons) re-renders on every Editor render. React Flow's StoreUpdater also calls `store.setState` for the `onConnect`, `onEdgesChange` and `onMoveStart` fields. This defeats the flowElements design: its own comment (flowElements.ts:33-36) says a card re-renders only when its user object changes, and docs/maintaining.md:143 claims that "a text edit re-renders one card".
- **Trigger → consequence:** HEAD compared with `memo(GraphNode)` plus stable handlers, on a rule with 100 nodes (the product maximum). Typing 7 characters into "Node name": 1500 GraphNode renders against 7. At 1x: main-thread time 314 ms against 172 ms, and median keystroke latency 40 ms against 24 ms, which is not perceptible. At 4x CPU throttling: median 116 ms against 48 ms; at HEAD all 7 keystrokes were over 100 ms (long tasks up to 127 ms), and with the fix none were. A rule with 30 nodes at 4x: 76 ms against 44 ms. Dragging one card 20 steps on the 100-node rule: 2120 GraphNode renders against 22; script time 426 ms against 252 ms at 1x (about 9 ms per frame), and 1.69 s against 1.01 s at 4x (21 long tasks with a 92 ms maximum, against 4). Selecting 4 cards: 408 GraphNode renders against 8, about 13 ms per click at 1x. The cost is perceptible only on large graphs or slower CPUs; at 30 nodes or fewer on a fast machine it is 10 ms or less per keystroke.
- **Evidence:** GraphCanvas.tsx:189 `onNodeClick={(_, n) => {`, :194 `onNodeContextMenu={(event, node) => {`, :203 `onPaneClick={() => {`, :207-208 `onPaneContextMenu={() => setContextMenu(null)}` / `onMoveStart={() => setContextMenu(null)}`, and :209 and :213 for the edge handlers. useGraphCanvas.ts:143 `const onEdgesChange = (changes: EdgeChange[]) => {` and :147 `const connect = (…) => {`. GraphNode.tsx:31 is `export default function GraphNode(...)` without `memo`. @xyflow/react index.mjs:2407 renders `jsx(NodeWrapper$1, { …, onClick: props.onNodeClick, … onContextMenu: props.onNodeContextMenu, … })`, and NodeWrapper renders `jsx(NodeComponent, { id, data: node.data, … })`. At :3058, EdgeWrapper receives `onClick: onEdgeClick, onContextMenu: onEdgeContextMenu`. `reactFlowFieldsToTrack` includes `onConnect`, `onEdgesChange` and `onMoveStart`. Harness: `$SP/probes/v-eff-fe1/make_variants.py` (`fix_x218`: `memo(GraphNode)` plus `useCallback` handlers that read the latest props through a ref; variants v0, v2, v12 and v123). Scenarios chain100-name, chain30-name, chain100-drag and chain100-select are in `$SP/probes/v-eff-fe1/run-matrix.sh` and `run-matrix2.sh`. The fixture is `$SP/probes/v-eff-fe1/draft-n100.json`; results are in `results.jsonl` and `results2.jsonl`.
- **Suggested fix:** (1) Export `memo(GraphNode)` from GraphNode.tsx; flowElements already keeps the same `data` object for unchanged cards. (2) Make every ReactFlow callback stable. Wrap `onEdgesChange` (dependencies `[setSelectedEdge]`) and `connect` (dependencies `[edit]`) in `useCallback` in useGraphCanvas. Build `onNodeClick`, `onNodeContextMenu`, `onPaneClick`, `onEdgeClick`, `onEdgeContextMenu`, and one `closeMenu` for both `onPaneContextMenu` and `onMoveStart`, with `useCallback`. These handlers should read `setSelected` and `setSelectedEdge` through a latest-value ref, because `selectNode` changes identity with the selection; this is what the verifier's patch did. Preferably move these handlers into useGraphCanvas, so that one hook owns all ReactFlow wiring. (3) Keep `nodeTypes`, `edgeTypes`, `proOptions` and the fit options at module level. The flowElements comment and docs/maintaining.md:143 then hold. This is a pure performance change with no behavior change.
- **Regression test:** Use the render probe proposed under X217 (`frontend/tests/helpers/renderProbe.ts`) in a browser test, either in `frontend/tests/graph-authoring.spec.ts` or in a new `frontend/tests/canvas-render-cost.spec.ts`. Create a 100-node chain through the API. Count function-component fibers that performed work (the PerformedWork flag) and whose parent host element has class `react-flow__node`; these are the cards NodeWrapper renders. Typing 7 characters into "Node name" must render at most about 2 cards per keystroke (HEAD 1500; fixed 7). A 20-step drag of one card must render at most about 2 cards per step (HEAD 2120; fixed 22). Selecting 4 cards must render at most 2 cards per click (HEAD 408; fixed 8). Keep "editing one card rebuilds only that card's React Flow node" in `frontend/tests/unit/canvas.spec.ts`.
- **Related:** X219 (the second render on each key change doubles these renders); X222 (during the same drag, the remaining per-frame cost is edge routing); X223 (the inspector side of the same keystroke); previous review E03 (docs/reviews/2026-09-27-full-review.md:130), which fixed element identity but not the handler props; useGraphProblems.ts:55-56 comments "so memoized canvas nodes … keep their error lists", assuming a memoized card

<a id="x219"></a>

### X219 · low · efficiency · useAsyncResource stores a redundant loading state on every key change and mount, so every consumer renders twice
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-eff-fe1.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / hooks (useAsyncResource) / All async reads; in the graph editor, the per-keystroke diagnostics and variables reads
- **Location:** `frontend/src/hooks/useAsyncResource.ts:32` (+ `frontend/src/hooks/useAsyncResource.ts:64`, `frontend/src/features/execution/useExecutionRequest.ts:36`, `frontend/src/features/editor/useGraphProblems.ts:33`, `frontend/src/features/editor/useGraphProblems.ts:40`, `frontend/src/features/editor/inspector/useNodeVariables.ts:30`)
- **Problem:** On every key change, and on mount, the effect always stores a new `{key, enabled, data, error: "", loading}` object. The render (lines 64-69) has already derived exactly that view whenever the stored key or `enabled` flag differs from the current one. Every consumer therefore commits a second full render per key change. In the graph editor, both the diagnostics key (EditorContent, through useGraphProblems) and the variables key (Inspector, through useNodeVariables) change on every semantic keystroke. This doubles the canvas and inspector render work, and with it the X217 and X218 costs. useExecutionRequest.ts:36-39 already avoids this pattern: "an idle state is kept to avoid a render for every keystroke that changes the key".
- **Trigger → consequence:** On a rule with 100 nodes, typing 7 characters into "Node name" gives 15 GraphCanvas renders at HEAD against 9 with an updater that returns `previous` when the key differs or the state already has the loading shape. Inspector renders fall from 16 to 13, and inline-editor `updateOptions` calls from 16 to 10. EditorContent's function still runs 15 times with this change, because useGraphProblems' `clearCommandProblem()` effect makes a same-value setState that triggers a bailout render; only full subtree renders drop. User-visible effect of this fix alone at HEAD: on a 10-case Switch at 4x CPU throttling, median keystroke latency drops from 156 ms to 84 ms, and main-thread time from 2.07 s to 1.23 s per 10 characters, because the fix halves X217's configuration events (310 against 140); on a 30-node rule at 4x, 76 ms against 48 ms. With X217 and X218 already fixed, the remaining gain is small: on the 100-node rule, 178 ms against 164 ms per 7 characters at 1x (about 2 ms per keystroke) and 468 ms against 393 ms at 4x (about 11 ms per keystroke); on the 20-case Switch at 1x, a median of 40 ms against 32 ms.
- **Evidence:** useAsyncResource.ts:30-38 `useEffect(() => { const controller = new AbortController(); setState((previous) => ({ key, enabled, data: keepData ? previous.data : initial, error: "", loading: enabled, }));`, compared with :64-69 `if (state.key === key && state.enabled === enabled) return state; return { data: keepData ? state.data : initial, error: "", loading: enabled };`. useExecutionRequest.ts:36-39 keeps an idle state "to avoid a render for every keystroke that changes the key". The measured updater is `fix_x219` in `$SP/probes/v-eff-fe1/make_variants.py` (variants v3 and v123); it returns `previous` when the key or `enabled` differs, or when the state already has the loading shape. Counts and timings come from the instrumented HEAD and patched builds (v0/v3/v12/v123) in `$SP/probes/v-eff-fe1/results.jsonl` and `results2.jsonl`.
- **Suggested fix:** Make the effect's updater a no-op whenever the rendered view is already correct. Extract it as a pure function, for example `pendingResourceState(previous, { key, enabled, keepData, initial })` in `hooks/useAsyncResource.ts`, with three cases: (1) return `previous` when `previous.key !== key || previous.enabled !== enabled`, because lines 64-69 already derive exactly the pending view; (2) return `previous` when the stored state already equals the pending view (`loading === enabled`, no error, same data), which is the mount case; (3) otherwise return the new loading state. Fetching, aborting and stale-response suppression stay unchanged. One behavior difference: when a consumer returns to the stored key and `enabled` pair before the new read settles (A → B → A, or disable → enable), the data already stored for that same key shows for one render, until the effect stores the loading view. If that is unwanted for `enabled` toggles, apply the shortcut only when `previous.key !== key`. Optionally, call `clearCommandProblem()` in useGraphProblems.ts:40-42 only when a command problem exists, which removes the remaining bailout render. If X187 lands in the same change, fold this into its signature cleanup.
- **Regression test:** Add `frontend/tests/unit/async-resource.spec.ts` for the pure updater. It must return the same object (`toBe(previous)`) for a stored state of another key and for the mount-shaped state, and a new loading state for the same key when `enabled` flips or after a completed read of that key. Add a browser guard with the X217 render probe: typing 7 characters into "Node name" of a 100-node rule renders the ReactFlow fiber (the one whose props include `nodeTypes`, which re-renders with GraphCanvas) fewer than 2 × typed times (HEAD: 15 GraphCanvas renders; fixed: 9). The existing test "pending and failed variable reads do not invent input choices or erase the current expression" in `frontend/tests/available-variables.spec.ts` and `frontend/tests/resources.spec.ts` must still pass, because a new key must still show the loading view.
- **Related:** X217, X218 and X223 (costs this double render multiplies); X187 (the same hook's positional `delay`/`enabled` cleanup); X220 (the variables key that changes on every keystroke); previous review E05, "Clearing runtime problems caused a second render per keystroke" (docs/reviews/2026-09-27-full-review.md:131)

<a id="x220"></a>

### X220 · low · efficiency · The variables read is keyed by the whole draft, so label edits refetch /api/variables and blank the scope, and bindings flicker "unavailable"
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-eff-fe1.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/editor/inspector (useNodeVariables), domain/graph / Inspector variable choices (bindings, Available variables, completions) while renaming nodes, cases or fields
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/editor/inspector/useNodeVariables.ts:31` (+ `frontend/src/domain/graph.ts:11`, `frontend/src/hooks/useAsyncResource.ts:64`, `frontend/src/features/expressions/ValueBinding.tsx:156`, `frontend/src/features/expressions/ValueBinding.tsx:162`, `frontend/src/features/editor/inspector/Inspector.tsx:74`, `frontend/src/features/editor/NodeEditDialog.tsx:108`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/engine/graph/BranchScopes.java:17`, `backend/src/main/java/dev/arc/rule/RuleDefinitionService.java:62`, `docs/maintaining.md:143`)
- **Problem:** The /api/variables read is keyed by `semanticGraphKey`, which covers every node field except position. The scope answer depends only on input names, node kind, handles (a Switch's case IDs), stored result names and edges (BranchScopes). A keystroke in a label, case label or field name therefore changes the key. useAsyncResource, which is used here without `keepPrevious`, resets the scope to `{}` at once, and the whole draft is posted again after 150 ms. While that read is pending, the inspector shows valid bindings as unavailable. Two neighbouring behaviors are intended: refetching after expression and connection edits is documented (docs/maintaining.md:143, "A different expression or connection does"), and showing an empty scope while a read is pending is required by lesson F10 and its test "pending and failed variable reads do not invent input choices…". The defect is that scope-irrelevant edits blank the scope. Any projection key must still surface shape-limit failures, because `variables()` runs `validator.shape` first. Also confirmed from the code: NodeEditDialog's own Inspector sends a second read of the same draft, and the Inspector recomputes `semanticGraphKey` on every render.
- **Trigger → consequence:** On the HEAD stack (3191), with rule Input(amount) → Formula(price) → Switch in "Match a value" mode and the value bound to `price`. One character typed into "Case 1 label": the "Value to match" select immediately shows "price · unavailable" with the helper "No compatible upstream variables are available at this node." for about 150 ms (11 painted frames), then recovers, with 1 extra POST. Three characters typed into "Node name" at 250 ms each: 3 POSTs, and the unavailable state appeared in 60 frames over about 680 ms. Ten characters typed continuously into "Case 1 label" at 120 ms each: the binding showed as unavailable for the whole burst plus the debounce, 255 frames over about 1.4 s. Every read returned the same body, and available-variable lists and completions are empty over the same window. The effect is a visible, transient and misleading flicker on a common path (renaming nodes or cases); no data changes and it corrects itself.
- **Evidence:** useNodeVariables.ts:30-35 `useAsyncResource(semanticGraphKey(definition), (signal) => studioApi.variables(definition, { signal }), noScopes, 150,)`. graph.ts:11-15 `stringifyJson({ ...definition, nodes: definition.nodes.map(({ position: _position, ...node }) => node) })`. useAsyncResource.ts:64-69 returns `initial` for a new key. BranchScopes.java:17-60 reads only `kind()`, `handles()`, incoming edges, `storesResult()`/`output()` and input names. RuleDefinitionService.java:62-64 `validator.shape(definition); return new GraphPlan(definition).available();`. The unavailable state is rendered at ValueBinding.tsx:156 and :162. Probe output: `{"readsOnOpen":1,"caseLabelOneChar":{"reads":1,"selectorTextRightAfterKeystroke":"price · unavailable","framesShowingUnavailable":11,"unavailableMs":149},"nodeNameThreeChars":{"reads":3,"framesShowingUnavailable":60,"unavailableMs":682},"caseLabelTenChars":{"reads":1,"framesShowingUnavailable":255,"unavailableMs":1399},"distinctBodies":["{\"input\":[\"amount\"],\"calc\":[\"amount\"],\"choose\":[\"amount\",\"price\"],\"out\":[\"amount\",\"price\"]}"]}`. The fixture is `$SP/probes/v-eff-fe1/draft-scope.json`.
- **Suggested fix:** Add `scopeGraphKey(definition)` to `domain/graph.ts`, beside `semanticGraphKey`. It serializes exactly what BranchScopes and GraphTopology read: input names in order; per node, in document order, its id, its type, its `output` when `nodeKinds[type].storesResult`, and a Switch's case IDs in order; per edge, its source, `sourceHandle` and target. Key useNodeVariables by it (memoized per draft), so label, case-label, field-name, expression and position edits keep the scope and send no read. Decision needed: /api/variables runs `validator.shape` over the whole draft first, so today its failures depend on fields outside the projection. A read that failed because of a blank label would not be retried when the label is fixed. The recommended option is to narrow `RuleDefinitionService.variables` to the structural checks that scope analysis needs (node, edge and input limits, IDs, handles, cycles). This also resolves X56's editor-wide loss of variables. It changes when POST /variables returns 422, so it requires updating docs/api.md:83 and adding a docs/api.md "Behavior changes" entry. If the endpoint must keep full shape validation, keep the `semanticGraphKey` read instead, but while a read is pending whose `scopeGraphKey` equals that of the last successful read, keep showing that successful scope. That scope is authoritative for the unchanged projection, so F10 still holds. This removes the flicker but not the extra POSTs. In both options, never put the read's own error state into its key (lesson F5). Rewrite docs/maintaining.md:143 ("A different expression or connection does") to describe the projection. Optionally, let the NodeEditDialog Inspector reuse its parent's scope, or a small projection-keyed cache of successful reads, to drop its duplicate read.
- **Regression test:** Add a unit test in `frontend/tests/unit/inspector-variables.spec.ts`. `scopeGraphKey` must stay the same across label, case-label, Transform field-name, expression and position edits. It must change for an input rename, a node type change, a result-name change, a case add, remove or reorder, and any edge change. Add a browser test in `frontend/tests/available-variables.spec.ts` with the Input(amount) → Formula(price) → Switch "Match a value" = `price` fixture. Count `POST /api/variables` with `page.on("request")` and delay that route by about 1 s. Type into "Case 1 label" and "Node name", then assert 0 new reads and that "Value to match" keeps showing `price`, never "price · unavailable". Adding a connection must still send one read and show no scope while it is pending. Keep the existing test "pending and failed variable reads do not invent input choices or erase the current expression".
- **Related:** X56 (whole-draft shape validation in /api/variables, which decides whether the projection key can be exact); X215 (in the backend, variables and diagnostics both run shape validation and GraphPlan on every edit, so fewer reads reduce it); X219 (the reset to `initial` on every key change); lesson F10 (a pending scope offers no variables); lesson F5 (keep state learned from a response out of that response's key); lesson F1 (derived identities must survive semantic no-ops)

<a id="x221"></a>

### X221 · low · efficiency · Each source-bound input card re-reads its source's version list and immutable pin, and a Reference re-reads its pin on every selection
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-eff-fe2.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/sources (SourceBindingEditor), features/editor/inspector (InputFields, ReferenceFields) / Input parameters bound to data sources; Reference node inspector
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/sources/SourceBindingEditor.tsx:39` (+ `frontend/src/features/sources/SourceBindingEditor.tsx:45`, `frontend/src/features/editor/inspector/InputFields.tsx:26`, `frontend/src/features/editor/inspector/Inspector.tsx:130`, `frontend/src/features/editor/inspector/ReferenceFields.tsx:29`, `frontend/src/features/editor/inspector/ReferenceFields.tsx:44`, `frontend/src/features/studio/formulaMetadata.ts:15`, `frontend/src/components/PagedAutocomplete.tsx:66`)
- **Problem:** Each InputParameterCard mounts its own SourceBindingEditor with two uncached reads: the first page of the source's version summaries (`usePagedResource`), and the pinned version through `useAsyncResource` → `sourceApi.source(id, version)`, which is immutable. N inputs bound to one source therefore send 2N identical GETs every time the Input fields mount. That happens on open and again on every reselection, because `<Fields key={node.id}>` remounts. ReferenceFields likewise re-reads the immutable pinned child version on every Reference selection. It also reads GET /api/rules/{child} (the full rule) whenever the child is not on App's cached library page. There is no HTTP caching: http.ts sends no cache headers and the backend sets none. A page-wide cache for immutable pins already exists (formulaMetadata.ts:15-26, lesson F31) but is not used here, and nothing in the docs describes per-card reads as intended. PagedAutocomplete loads only while open, so it adds no reads.
- **Trigger → consequence:** A rule has 5 STRING inputs bound to source S v1, with pointers /name, /tier, /limit, /country and /segment. Opening /#/rules/<id> (Input is the default selection) sends 5× GET /api/sources/S/version-summaries?offset=0&limit=20 and 5× GET /api/sources/S/versions/1, with identical bodies. Clicking Output and then Input again sends the same 10 GETs. For a Reference node pinned to child v1, selecting it 3 times (alternating with Input) sends 3× GET /api/rules/child/versions/1 and 3× GET /api/rules/child. The cost is 2N small GETs and DB reads per Input selection; the UI does not break.
- **Evidence:** SourceBindingEditor.tsx:39-51 `usePagedResource(JSON.stringify([source?.id, catalogRevision]), … sourceApi.versionSummaries(source!.id, …))` and `useAsyncResource<DataSource | null>(JSON.stringify([source?.id, source?.version, catalogRevision]), (signal) => sourceApi.source(source!.id, source!.version, { signal }), …)`. InputFields.tsx:26-27 renders one card per input. Inspector.tsx:130 renders `<Fields key={node.id} {...fieldProps} />`. ReferenceFields.tsx:29-35 reads the rule when `!!node.ruleId && !knownRule`, and :44-50 calls `useAsyncResource<Version | null>(`${node.ruleId}:${node.version}`, … ruleApi.version(…))`. Browser probe on 3191 (`page.on('request')`; `$SP/probes/v-eff-fe2/zz-v-eff-fe2-requests.spec.ts`, test "X221 source-bound input cards and reference pin reads"): `{"onOpen":{"GET …/version-summaries?offset=0&limit=20":5,"GET …/versions/1":5},"reselect":{same 5+5},"identicalBodies":true,"refOnOpen":{},"refReselect":{"GET /api/rules/<child>":3,"GET /api/rules/<child>/versions/1":3}}`.
- **Suggested fix:** (1) Extract the bounded LRU from `FormulaMetadata` into one shared cache for immutable pinned reads, for example `PinnedReads<T>` with `load(key, read, signal)`, a capacity, and completed reads only. Add in-flight sharing, so cards that mount together share one request: count subscribers, and abort the shared read only when every subscriber has aborted. Route `sourceApi.source(id, version)` in SourceBindingEditor, `ruleApi.version(id, version)` in ReferenceFields, and FormulaMetadata's own reads through it. Source pins are immutable, because the API has no source delete. Rule pins are not immutable after delete and re-create, so the cache needs `forget(ruleId)`, called from App's `forgetDeletedRule`, and must follow X44's resolution for deletions made in another tab. (2) Load the mutable version list only when the "Source version" control opens, as PagedAutocomplete does. This is the shared paged version control proposed in X182; with it, mounting N cards reads no version lists. (3) The child-rule name read (`ruleApi.get`, which returns the full rule) is not a pin; resolve it together with X201. This is a pure performance change, with no user-visible behavior change.
- **Regression test:** Add a unit test for the shared cache, modeled on `frontend/tests/unit/formula-metadata.spec.ts`. It must show that two concurrent loads of one key make one read, that aborting one subscriber keeps the other subscriber's result, that aborted reads are not cached, that the LRU capacity holds, and that `forget(id)` drops every version of that ID. Add a browser test in `frontend/tests/source-provider.spec.ts` with 5 inputs bound to one source v1. With `page.on("request")`, opening the rule must send exactly 1× GET …/versions/1 and 0× version-summaries until "Source version" is opened, and switching Output → Input must send no reads. In `frontend/tests/reference-picker.spec.ts`, selecting a Reference node 3 times must send 1× GET /api/rules/child/versions/1.
- **Related:** X182 (the paged version select is written 3×; the shared control should load on open); X44 and X01 (id:version and id:revision caches survive delete and re-create, and a shared pin cache inherits that); X201 (ReferenceFields uses the library page as a name cache); lesson F31 (one bounded page-wide cache for immutable `id:version` metadata)

<a id="x222"></a>

### X222 · low · efficiency · Dragging a card re-routes every edge on every frame
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-eff-fe2.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/editor/canvas (useGraphCanvas, RoutedEdge, edgeRouting) / Dragging cards on large, edge-dense graphs
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/useGraphCanvas.ts:60` (+ `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/useGraphCanvas.ts:63`, `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/useGraphCanvas.ts:128`, `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/RoutedEdge.tsx:15`, `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/RoutedEdge.tsx:35`, `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/edgeRouting.ts:262`, `docs/maintaining.md:143`)
- **Problem:** The RoutingContext value is memoized on `JSON.stringify(bounds)` of every card, and a drag writes the new position into the draft on every move (useGraphCanvas.ts:128-139). Each drag step therefore gives every RoutedEdge a new `nodes` array, and every edge re-runs `routeEdge`: the simple-route check, plus the A* `search` wherever the simple route is blocked. Each neighbour step of that search calls `clear(p, q, rects)` over all rects. All of this happens although only edges near the moved card can change. The finder's claim that "only edges attached to or crossing the moved card can change" is approximate, because the A* grid lines come from every rect, but the full re-route on every frame is real. Previous finding E03 covered only non-geometry edits, which no longer re-route.
- **Trigger → consequence:** Drag one card 20 steps after Arrange, on an unminified HEAD build with a CPU profile. At 100 nodes/199 edges (a chain plus skip-2 edges): 4080 RoutedEdge renders, meaning all 199 edges on every step, and routeEdge 205 ms (search 157 ms). That is about 10 ms of routing in about 34 ms of total work per step. At 100 nodes/199 edges including 100 random long-span edges: routeEdge 2843 ms, about 142 ms per step and about 170 ms of total work per step (roughly 6 fps); the production build on 3191 gives the same total of about 171 ms per step. At 30 nodes/45 edges, or on a plain 100-node chain, routing costs about 1 ms per step, which is negligible. The cost matters only for large, edge-dense graphs near MAX_NODES=100 and MAX_EDGES=200.
- **Evidence:** useGraphCanvas.ts:59-66 `const bounds = cardBounds(definition.nodes, measurements); const geometry = JSON.stringify(bounds); … const routing = useMemo(() => ({ nodes: bounds, reportBlocked }), [geometry, reportBlocked]);`. RoutedEdge.tsx:15 `useContext(RoutingContext)` and :35-43 `useMemo(() => routeEdge(…, nodes), [source, target, sourceX, sourceY, targetX, targetY, nodes])`. edgeRouting.ts:262-264 falls back to `search(start, end, rects)`. Probe (DevTools-hook fiber walk plus CDP Profiler, 20-step drag; `$SP/probes/v-eff-fe2/zz-v-eff-fe2-timing.spec.ts`, tests "X224/X222 dense/wide/random-span/mid/chain 100-node/30-node graph"): dense100 `{"routeEdge":204.7,"search":157.4,"renders":{"RoutedEdge":4080,"GraphNode":2020}}`; random100 `{"routeEdge":2842.7,"search":2690}`; mid30 `{"routeEdge":21.2}`; chain100 `{"routeEdge":21.1}`.
- **Suggested fix:** Route incrementally while a drag is in progress, and route fully once the drag settles. In useGraphCanvas, compare `bounds` with the previous routing bounds by card id to find the moved cards and their old and new rects, and publish them with the routing context. Give routing a per-edge route cache, either in `edgeRouting.ts` (for example `EdgeRouteCache.route(edgeId, source, target, nodes, moved)`) or as a ref inside RoutedEdge. The cache recomputes an edge in three cases: an endpoint moved, the edge is attached to a moved card, or a moved card's old or new rect, expanded by the 20 px routing margin, intersects the bounding box of the edge's cached path. Otherwise it returns the cached route object. When the drag ends (a position change with `dragging: false`, or `onNodeDragStop`), route every edge once more, so the settled drawing equals a full `routeEdge` pass; this matters because the A* grid depends on every rect. Keep full routing for Arrange, measurement changes and non-drag geometry changes. Update docs/maintaining.md:143 to describe drag routing. Optionally, stop re-rendering unaffected RoutedEdges by turning the routing context into a subscription (`useSyncExternalStore` with a per-edge selector) instead of one value that changes on every frame. This is a pure performance change; the drawing after a drag is unchanged.
- **Regression test:** Add unit tests in `frontend/tests/unit/canvas.spec.ts`, next to "routing obstacles depend on positions and sizes only", on a 100-node/199-edge fixture. Moving one card must recompute only the edges attached to it or whose path box meets its old or new rect; count the calls through an injected router. Unaffected edges must keep the identical route object, and after settling, every route must equal a fresh `routeEdge` result. As an optional browser guard with the X217 render probe, check that routing work per step during a 20-step drag stays proportional to the nearby edges, not to all 199.
- **Related:** X218 (every card also re-renders during the same drag); previous review E03 (docs/reviews/2026-09-27-full-review.md:130), the geometry-keyed routing this refines for drags

<a id="x223"></a>

### X223 · low · efficiency · Transform fields and Switch cases re-render every row on each keystroke (unmemoized rows, new callbacks and a new `variables` array on every render)
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-eff-fe2.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/editor/inspector (TransformFields, SwitchFields, useNodeVariables), features/expressions (ValueBinding, ExpressionField) / Editing Transform fields and Switch cases
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/editor/inspector/TransformFields.tsx:29` (+ `frontend/src/features/editor/inspector/TransformFields.tsx:61`, `frontend/src/features/editor/inspector/SwitchFields.tsx:65`, `frontend/src/features/expressions/ValueBinding.tsx:31`, `frontend/src/features/expressions/ExpressionField.tsx:12`, `frontend/src/features/editor/inspector/useNodeVariables.ts:36`, `frontend/src/features/editor/inspector/Inspector.tsx:73`, `frontend/src/features/expressions/InlineExpressionEditor.tsx:48`)
- **Problem:** TransformFields (up to 50 fields) and SwitchFields (up to 20 cases) render every row inline with fresh `onChange` closures. They also pass a new `variables` array on every render, because useNodeVariables recomputes `nodeVariables(...)` each time. The Inspector's `patch` (Inspector.tsx:73) is also a new closure on every render. None of ValueBinding, ExpressionField, AvailableVariables or InlineExpressionEditor is memoized. A keystroke in one row therefore re-renders every row, about 2–3 times per keystroke (see X219). At HEAD this also multiplies X217's Monaco `updateOptions` cost on each render. The finder also noted that InlineExpressionEditor JSON-stringifies the variable names and about 200 function names on every render (InlineExpressionEditor.tsx:48-51).
- **Trigger → consequence:** Type 10 characters into "Field 1 name" of a Transform with expression fields. With only the Monaco options memoized, which isolates this finding, 40 fields cost 71.3 ms of script per keystroke (66 ms of React work), against 12.7 ms with 2 fields. That is about 55–59 ms per keystroke spent re-rendering 38 unchanged rows (880 InlineExpressionEditor renders = 40 × 22). With 10 fields, 26.1 ms (+13 ms). Typing into "Case 1 label" of a 20-case Switch costs 36.7 ms per keystroke. At HEAD, with options not memoized, the same typing costs 277 ms per keystroke at 40 fields (1240 editor renders; `updateOptions` takes 1656 of 2773 ms), 36.5 ms at 10 fields, 14.5 ms at 2 fields and 79 ms for the 20-case Switch.
- **Evidence:** TransformFields.tsx:29-76 `{fields.map((field, index) => (… <TextField … onChange={(e) => patch({ fields: fields.map(…) })} /> <ValueBinding key={`${index}:${fields.length}`} … variables={variables} onChange={(value) => patch({…})} />`. SwitchFields.tsx:65-159 has the same shape. ValueBinding.tsx:31 and ExpressionField.tsx:12 are plain function components, and useNodeVariables.ts returns `nodeVariables(definition, nodeId, scopes)` on every render. The probes ran on an unminified HEAD build served locally with a proxy to 8191, and on a copy that differs only by a `useMemo` around the InlineExpressionEditor options (`$SP/probes/v-eff-fe2/zz-v-eff-fe2-timing.spec.ts`, tests "X223 transform 40/10/2 fields" and "X223 switch 20 cases"; builds in `$SP/probes/v-eff-fe2/dist-dev` and `dist-patched`). HEAD tf40 `{"perKeystrokeScriptMs":277.3,"updateOptions":1656.3,"renders":{"InlineExpressionEditor":1240,"TransformFields":31}}`. Options-memoized tf40 `{"perKeystrokeScriptMs":71.3,"performWorkOnRoot":659.9,"renders":{"InlineExpressionEditor":880,"TransformFields":22}}`; tf10 26.1; tf2 12.7 (performWorkOnRoot 115.2); sw20 36.7.
- **Suggested fix:** Extract one memoized row component per list, `TransformFieldRow` and `SwitchCaseRow`, each wrapped in `memo`. A row receives its value, its index or ID, `variables`, `readOnly` and stable callbacks. Make the callbacks stable with a node-scoped functional update built on the document's stable `edit` (passed down as `onDefinitionChange`), for example `updateNode(nodeId, (node) => ({ fields: node.fields.map(…) }))` through `patchGraphNode`. A row callback then reads the current fields instead of closing over the render's `fields` array. In useNodeVariables, return the previous `variables` array when the recomputed options are equal by name, type and label. This is structural sharing, the approach flowElements uses for cards, and it means rows see an unchanged prop when the scope did not change. Give Transform rows a stable identity key instead of `${index}:${fields.length}` (X60), reusing the `useInputParameterRows` pattern. Because rows then stay mounted, ValueBinding keeps its `useEditingPin` state (lesson F13). Land this after X217 and X219, which remove the multipliers. This is a pure performance change.
- **Regression test:** Use the X217 render probe in `frontend/tests/graph-authoring.spec.ts` or in a new inspector render-cost spec. Create a 40-field Transform through the API and type 10 characters into "Field 1 name". Count fibers that performed work and whose nearest host ancestor is a `.node-mapping-card` other than the first; expect 0 (the options-memoized HEAD made 880 editor renders). Do the same for "Case 1 label" of a 20-case Switch, where the other `switch-case-<id>` cards must render 0 times. Keep `frontend/tests/value-binding-editing.spec.ts` (F13 editing pins) and X60's add/remove-field case passing.
- **Related:** X217 (Monaco options on every render; with both, 277 ms per keystroke at 40 fields); X219 (a double render on every keystroke); X60 (the Transform ValueBinding key includes the field count, so fix row identity in the same change); lesson F13 (editing pins must survive); lesson F31 (a new `variables` array on every Inspector render)

<a id="x224"></a>

### X224 · low · efficiency · Arrange runs ELK (thoroughness 20) on the main thread, causing one long task of up to ~1.3 s
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-eff-fe2.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/editor/canvas (graphLayout, useGraphCommands) / Arrange graph
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/graphLayout.ts:5` (+ `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/graphLayout.ts:14`, `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/graphLayout.ts:87`, `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/graphLayout.ts:93`, `frontend/src/features/editor/canvas/useGraphCommands.ts:68`, `frontend/src/features/studio/arcLanguage.ts:6`, `frontend/tests/layout.spec.ts:2`, `docs/architecture.md:76`, `docs/architecture.md:84`)
- **Problem:** Arrange imports `elkjs/lib/elk.bundled.js`, which is ELK plus an in-thread fake worker. It creates the instance at module scope and lays out with `elk.layered.thoroughness` 20 on the main thread. Each Arrange is therefore one main-thread long task whose length grows with graph size and edge span. The first use adds about 75–100 ms for chunk evaluation and warm-up. The finder's single ~720 ms figure depends on graph structure and lies inside the measured range. docs/architecture.md:76 documents the layout options but says nothing about main-thread execution.
- **Trigger → consequence:** Clicking Arrange graph on the production build at 3191, measured with a PerformanceObserver long-task observer and a CDP profile. With 100 nodes/199 short edges, the first Arrange gives long tasks of [136, 121] ms (164 ms in ELK code), and a warm Arrange gives one 65 ms task. With 100 nodes/199 edges including 100 random long-span edges, the first Arrange gives [1288, 395] ms, and a warm Arrange gives one 1276 ms task (1247 ms in ELK code). With 30 nodes/45 edges, the first Arrange gives [72, 55] ms, mostly chunk evaluation; a warm Arrange takes 22 ms with no long task. While the task runs, the page cannot paint, scroll or accept input.
- **Evidence:** graphLayout.ts:1-5 `import ELK, {…} from "elkjs/lib/elk.bundled.js";`, :14 `const elk = new ELK();`, :87 `"elk.layered.thoroughness": "20",`, :93 `const result = await elk.layout(graph);`. useGraphCommands.ts:68-69 imports the module dynamically, then calls `arrangeGraph(draft, measurements)` on the main thread. A worker alternative exists: node_modules/elkjs/lib ships elk-worker.min.js, elk-api.d.ts:134-135 declares `workerUrl?`/`workerFactory?`, and arcLanguage.ts:6 already uses `import EditorWorker from "monaco-editor/editor/editor.worker?worker";`. Probe: `$SP/probes/v-eff-fe2/zz-v-eff-fe2-timing.spec.ts` (tests "X224/X222 dense/wide/random-span/mid/chain …").
- **Suggested fix:** Run ELK in a Web Worker, in these steps. (1) Split graphLayout.ts. The pure part builds the ELK graph and maps the result, as `arrangeGraph(definition, sizes, elk)` with an ELK layout port. A browser-only module creates the worker-backed instance, for example `import ELK from "elkjs/lib/elk-api.js"` with `workerUrl` from `import elkWorkerUrl from "elkjs/lib/elk-worker.min.js?url"` (elk-api then calls `new Worker(url)`), or through a `workerFactory`. Create the instance on the first Arrange, inside the existing lazy `graphLayout` chunk. (2) Handle worker failure. elk-api's `PromisedWorker` sets only `onmessage`, so a worker that fails to load or throws would leave `elk.layout` pending forever and keep the document's command lock (lesson F8). Listen for the worker's `error` and `messageerror` events, reject the pending layout, then call `terminateWorker()` and drop the instance, so the next Arrange starts a new worker. (3) Keep the layout identical: the options and seed stay the same. X225's locale-independent ID sort is a separate fix in the same file. (4) Keep the Node tests running: `frontend/tests/layout.spec.ts` imports `arrangeGraph` in Node, so pass it the in-thread `elk.bundled.js` instance. (5) Mention off-thread layout at docs/architecture.md:84. Users see no behavior change.
- **Regression test:** In `frontend/tests/layout.spec.ts`, keep the Node layout tests, using an injected in-thread ELK. In `frontend/tests/editor-commands.spec.ts`, Arrange on a 100-node/199-edge random-span fixture must produce no main-thread long task of 200 ms or more (PerformanceObserver `longtask`; HEAD 1276–1288 ms) and must request the ELK worker script. Also route the worker script to fail (`page.route` abort or 404): Arrange must report an error, and Save and Arrange must become enabled again. Follow the pattern of "leaving the graph while Arrange waits for its layout module cannot keep the editor locked" (F8/F20).
- **Related:** X225 (the same file sorts IDs with `localeCompare`); X90 (node code can open during Arrange, and an asynchronous worker keeps that window open); lesson F8 (no command may wait on a promise that may never settle); lesson F20 (a failed chunk must not block the app)

<a id="x05"></a>

### X05 · low · efficiency · The playground's catalog refresh key includes the hidden library page, so a direct visit reads the published catalog twice
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-eff-fe2.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/execution (usePublishedExecution, ApiPage), app (App, useRuleLibrary) / API playground and API docs pages (direct visit or reload)
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/execution/usePublishedExecution.ts:69` (+ `frontend/src/features/execution/usePublishedExecution.ts:54`, `frontend/src/features/execution/usePublishedExecution.ts:59`, `frontend/src/features/execution/usePublishedExecution.ts:81`, `frontend/src/App.tsx:60`, `frontend/src/App.tsx:151`, `frontend/src/app/useRuleLibrary.ts:18`, `frontend/src/app/useRuleLibrary.ts:28`, `frontend/src/features/execution/ApiPage.tsx:120`)
- **Problem:** The published catalog's refresh key includes `libraryReleases`, which is built from App's library page. That page always loads, but it reloads only while visible (`if (!visible || !stale) return;`). While the playground is mounted, the page therefore changes only once, when its first load settles. As a result, the refresh described at usePublishedExecution.ts:59-60 ("a library change or Retry reloads the page that is shown") only ever produces a duplicate read, on a direct visit to or reload of /#/playground or /#/docs, and the Rule selector is disabled during that second read. In-app navigation from the library reads the catalog once.
- **Trigger → consequence:** Load /#/playground or /#/docs directly with any rules in the database. The page sends GET /api/rule-summaries?offset=0&limit=20&search=&publishedOnly=true, then GET …&kind= (the library). When the library page arrives, it sends the same publishedOnly read again. While that read loads, `disabled={running || request.catalog.loading || …}` disables the Rule select.
- **Evidence:** usePublishedExecution.ts:54-58 `const libraryReleases = rules.map((rule) => [rule.id, rule.revision, rule.publishedVersion]);` and :69 `{ refresh: JSON.stringify([libraryReleases, retry]), keepPrevious: true }`. App.tsx:60 `useRuleLibrary(view.page === "library")` and :151 `<ApiPage mode={view.page} rules={library.rules} …/>`. useRuleLibrary.ts:18-32 keeps the page always enabled and reloads it only past `if (!visible || !stale) return;`. ApiPage.tsx:120-126 disables the Rule select while the catalog loads. Browser probe on 3191 (`$SP/probes/v-eff-fe2/zz-v-eff-fe2-requests.spec.ts`, test "X05 direct visits to playground and docs"): "/#/playground": [publishedOnly, kind=, publishedOnly]; "/#/docs": [publishedOnly, kind=, publishedOnly]; "library -> playground (in-app)": [publishedOnly] (one read).
- **Suggested fix:** Remove `libraryReleases` from the catalog refresh key and keep `retry`. Stop passing the library page into the playground at all: remove `rules` from the `usePublishedExecution` and `ApiPage` props; compute `listedVersion` (usePublishedExecution.ts:81-84) only from the playground's own `selectedRule` and version history, since history page 0 holds the newest release; and render `<ApiPage mode={view.page} notify={setNotice} />` at App.tsx:151. This follows X201's direction for ApiPage and also removes the stale library input behind X02. Update the comment at usePublishedExecution.ts:59-60. The only visible change is that the second read disappears.
- **Regression test:** Extend `frontend/tests/execution-catalog.spec.ts`, which already asserts "a single first-page history read". With `page.on("request")`, a direct `page.goto("/#/playground")` and a direct `page.goto("/#/docs")` must each send exactly one `rule-summaries…publishedOnly=true` request, counted after the library read (`kind=`) has also completed. Once the Rule select is enabled, it must not become disabled again. Keep the existing F5 history and selection tests.
- **Related:** X201 (the library page is threaded into ApiPage as a refresh signal; same root cause); X02 (`listedVersion` takes the maximum with the stale library page); X04 (ApiPage shows "no published rules" while the catalog loads); lesson F5 (a value learned from another read, placed in a paged read's key, caused a duplicate read); lesson F33 (a view must not depend on another view's filter or page)

<a id="x122"></a>

### X122 · low · efficiency · Code studio Reuse search sends a catalog request per keystroke and blanks the result list
- **Status:** CONFIRMED — record: `verdicts/v-eff-fe2.md`
- **Area / module / feature:** Frontend / features/studio (StudioLibrary), hooks (usePagedResource, useDebouncedValue) / Code studio Reuse pane search ("Find reusable rule")
- **Location:** `frontend/src/features/studio/StudioLibrary.tsx:34` (+ `frontend/src/features/studio/StudioLibrary.tsx:32`, `frontend/src/features/studio/StudioLibrary.tsx:122`, `frontend/src/app/useRuleLibrary.ts:15`, `frontend/src/features/execution/usePublishedExecution.ts:53`, `frontend/src/features/sources/useSourceEditor.ts:43`, `frontend/src/hooks/useDebouncedValue.ts:4`, `frontend/src/hooks/usePagedResource.ts:18`)
- **Problem:** The Code studio Reuse pane keys `usePagedResource` by the raw search text, with no debounce and no `keepPrevious`. Every keystroke therefore sends a catalog search and blanks the result list. The library, playground and sources searches all use `useDebouncedValue(search, searchDelayMs)` (250 ms) instead; the earlier fix for undebounced library search (previous review E01) reached only useRuleLibrary. The finders claimed "four variants" of the search delay, but only the missing debounce is a real divergence: the 180 ms in useAutocompletePages and the documented 150 ms for `@`-completion (docs/maintaining.md:75) belong to different controls.
- **Trigger → consequence:** In Code studio, open reuse and type "discount" into "Find reusable rule" at about 90 ms per key. This sends 8 GET /api/rule-summaries?…publishedOnly=true requests (d, di, …, discount). The client aborts each earlier request, but the server still runs it. The same typing sends 1 request in the library and 1 in the playground.
- **Evidence:** StudioLibrary.tsx:32-42 `const [search, setSearch] = useState(""); … const catalog = usePagedResource(search, (offset, limit, signal) => ruleApi.catalog({ offset, limit, search, publishedOnly: true }, { signal }), pane === "reuse");` and :122 `onChange={(event) => setSearch(event.target.value)}`. Compare useRuleLibrary.ts:15, usePublishedExecution.ts:53 and useSourceEditor.ts:43, each of which calls `useDebouncedValue(search, searchDelayMs)`, with useDebouncedValue.ts:4 `export const searchDelayMs = 250;`. Browser probe on 3191 (`$SP/probes/v-eff-fe2/zz-v-eff-fe2-requests.spec.ts`, test "X122 studio reuse search vs library search"): `{"studioReuse":["d","di","dis","disc","disco","discou","discoun","discount"],"library":["discount"],"playground":["discount"]}`.
- **Suggested fix:** Add one shared catalog-search hook in `hooks/`, for example `usePagedSearch(search, load, { enabled, refresh, keepPrevious })`. The hook applies `useDebouncedValue(search, searchDelayMs)`, keys `usePagedResource` by the settled query plus the caller's own key parts (such as kind or catalog revision), and returns `searching: query !== search` alongside the page. Use it in StudioLibrary with `keepPrevious: true`, so the shown cards stay until the settled search answers. Also use it in useRuleLibrary, in usePublishedExecution (which, as today, treats `searching` as loading for its Rule selector) and in useSourceEditor. Leave useAutocompletePages' 180 ms and the 150 ms `@`-completion delay alone. This is a user-visible change: the Reuse list now waits for a pause in typing and no longer blanks. Add a bullet under "Code studio and completion" in docs/studio.md "Behavior changes".
- **Regression test:** In `frontend/tests/studio-reuse.spec.ts`, type "discount" into "Find reusable rule" with `pressSequentially(…, { delay: 90 })`, and assert that the catalog requests with a non-empty `search` are exactly `["discount"]`. With the route delayed, assert that a card from the empty-search page stays visible until the settled search answers.
- **Related:** previous review E01 (docs/reviews/2026-09-27-full-review.md:172); lesson F31 (search once typing pauses); X50 (usePagedResource restores an older key's offset; it is in the hook family the shared search hook wraps); X66, X136 and X124 (other Reuse-pane bugs in StudioLibrary and its snippets; coordinate the edits)
